# customers — Business Rules
> Reconciled 2026-08-08 against PO rulings (BR-CUS-046..059) — propagation sweep.

Format: BR-XXX-### · rule · source (spec/decision §) · status (CONFIRMED / PLACEHOLDER / OPEN) · test ref.
Rules CITE B-docs; they never copy values out of them (one source of truth).

## Accounts pack (EP-CUS-01..06, 2026-08-08) — StorySmith draft, PO ratification pending on all PROPOSED rows
| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-015 | S17 list contract: search account #/customer name/service address; status segments All/Current/Past due/Closed; one service tag per active service agreement; Balance = **the ledger's own position for the account** (⚠ AMENDED 2026-08-15, DEF-127 — see below); columns/sort/export per the EP-CUS-01 capability matrix (B9) — superseded by BR-CUS-053 where they differ. ⚠ **THE CACHE'S PRECISION IS NOW A STATED RULE (recorded 2026-08-12 under the PO's *"offset first"* ruling, OQ-135):** `accounts.balance` is **good enough to answer *is there a debt on another account* and stop a payout leaving** — and **NOT good enough to subtract from one**. So a refund blocked by an arrear elsewhere **REFUSES**, and the offset is applied as a **balance transfer through the reviewed writer** (BR-PAY-014 / BR-CUS-054), never netted off this number. `apps/billing/final_bill._prior_balance` draws the same line for the same reason. This is an Eng-Lead judgment inside a PO ruling, not a PO ruling itself ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-15 (DEF-127, CRITICAL) — THE S17 BALANCE IS THE LEDGER'S FIGURE, NOT THE CACHE.** This rule read *"Balance = cached `accounts.balance` (ledger = truth)"* and the product did exactly that — while **nothing in this product has ever written that column**. Measured in a browser: account `E2E-10001` holding an ISSUED, UNPAID **108.88**, the books carrying `1200 AR` debit **108.880000**, and S17 rendering the row as **$0.00** and the header as **Outstanding $0.00**. A customer who owed money was told they owed nothing, on the one screen a clerk opens to find out. **The Balance cell and the header roll-up now come from `apps.ledger.reporting.ar_positions`** — the per-account slice of the same Σ over posted journal lines that `ar_control` publishes as the AR tie-out and `revenue_and_ar` publishes as `still_owed`, reached through the customers seam (B1: customers touches no other module's ORM). One derivation, grouped three ways; BR-REP-002 is the law being kept. **AND AN ABSENCE IS NOT SPELLED LIKE ZERO:** when the books cannot be asked — the read is unpublished, or this tenant has designated no AR account — the figure is **null** with its reason on `balance_unavailable`, and the screen renders an awaiting cell. **$0.00 is a claim.** The `balance_gt` filter moved out of SQL for the same reason: it filtered the empty column and matched nothing. **THE CACHE IS NOT NOW WRITTEN AND MUST NOT BE** — a second source of truth for one figure is what BR-REP-002 forbids; it is served as `balance_cached`, labelled as what it is, and read by nobody for a money decision. **The 2026-08-12 cache-precision reading below still stands unchanged** and is about a different question (`arrears_on_other_accounts`, which asks *is there a debt elsewhere at all*). ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-24 (CR-030 W2) — THE SEVEN-COLUMN PROTOTYPE LIST IS NOW A TAB WITH NINE DEFAULT COLUMNS, AND THIS ROW'S OPENING CLAUSE IS WHAT IS SUPERSEDED.** This rule opens *"S17 list contract: search account #/customer name/service address; status segments All/Current/Past due/Closed; one service tag per active service agreement"* and describes **the prototype's seven columns**. B18 §5.1 rebuilt that screen and five things in the sentence moved. Each is named with what replaced it, so a reader can tell a CHANGE from a gap: **(1) THE LIST IS A TAB, NOT A PAGE.** S17 is now the first of **eight** tabs on one Accounts page — Accounts · Requests · New connects · Move-ins · Move-outs · Transfers · Disconnects · Imports (B18 §4, task 2.1). Only **Accounts** and **Imports** have a body; the other six render a stated absence naming the wave that brings them, never an empty table, because *"you have no move-ins"* is a false claim about a utility's data when the product cannot hold a move-in at all. Everything else in this row is the **Accounts tab's** contract from here on, not the page's. **(2) THE HEADER ROLL-UP LINE IS REPLACED BY A FIVE-TILE KPI STRIP** (task 2.2) — `ACCOUNTS` · `OUTSTANDING` · `PAST DUE 60+` · `CLOSED THIS YEAR` · `PENDING ACTIVATION`. **Replaced, not joined:** the old `.sub` sentence carried the first three figures and one screen stating *"8 accounts"* twice is the drift this row's own DEF-127 clause was written about. Two of the five are honest absences with DIFFERENT reasons — `PAST DUE 60+` because a bucket-derived count would undercount every account whose 60+ position came from an issued bill, and `CLOSED THIS YEAR` because `ACCOUNT_FILTERS` carries no date dimension, so *closed* is askable and *closed since January* is not. Neither may become `0`. **(3) `STATUS` AND `AGEING` ARE TWO COLUMNS.** The prototype draws ONE cell holding `Pending` beside `30d past`, and those answer two different questions about an account: what the utility SET it to, and where its money sits. `STATUS` is the stored lookup (BR-CUS-055); `AGEING` is derived and never stored (BR-CUS-016 / BR-CUS-048's four bands), and a row with no derivable position renders `—` with its reason, never `Current`. **(4) THE SERVICE CELL CAPS AT FIVE CHIPS PLUS A `+N`.** *"One service tag per active service agreement"* still holds as the DATA rule and no longer describes the cell: this product sells **ten** services (BR-RAT-024), so an uncapped cell wraps and makes every row on the page taller for the one account that takes them all. Five squares then one overflow square carrying the REMAINDER — eight services give `+3`. The hidden names are the overflow chip's accessible label, so the cap is a layout decision and never a data one. **(5) `CUSTOMER #` IS A COLUMN, AND THE SEARCH SET IS FIVE — PO RULING 2026-08-24**, asked as a question about work they expected to be done: *"we were suppose to add customer # comlume what happened to that"*. This row's opening *"search account #/customer name/service address"* is superseded by **BR-CUS-053 as amended the same day**: `account_no` · **`customer_no`** · contact name · service address · meter #, all in ONE `Q(...)` OR-group so one box finds a person by either number. **Nine default-visible columns now** — `ACCOUNT` · **`CUSTOMER #`** · `NAME` · `PREMISE` · `SERVICES` · `BALANCE` · `LAST BILL` · `STATUS` · `AGEING` — with `AUTOPAY` and `SINCE` declared and hidden, **eleven declared in total**, all reachable through the shared column selector. ⚠ **`CUSTOMER #` IS NOT `external_ref`**: that is the LEGACY system's id, this is ours, and a customer created by the S18 wizard has no legacy ref and still needs a number to quote down a phone. On a jointly-held account the CELL shows the first link's number (so S17 and S21 cannot name two different people) while the SEARCH matches every holder's. ⚠ **AND ONE THING B18 SPECIFIED IS CUT RATHER THAN OWED — PO RULING 2026-08-24, verbatim: *"okay dont link it"*.** §5.1.2 put a `4 accounts` sub-link on the `NAME` cell (BR-CUS-054's shape). It is **not built and will not be built**, and neither are the two server halves it needed — `account_row` serves no customer block and `ACCOUNT_FILTERS` declares no `customer_id`, so following it could not have filtered anything either. `TC-ACC-3` is closed **WON'T BUILD**, not left as a todo that reads as work owed. **The capability it carried is served instead by (5):** the customer's number is on the row and typing it returns every account they hold. ⚠ **WHAT DID *NOT* CHANGE, stated so the next reader does not mistake a standing gap for W2 work:** the four **status** segments (All/Current/Past due/Closed) are still NOT rendered — only *All* and *Closed* are answerable without invoices. The AGEING segments that DID ship are a different vocabulary (BR-CUS-048's four bands) and render as a filter chip rather than `[shot]`'s radio control, because the shared table framework has no segment capability. There is still no `Plan` column and no `plan` filter (BR-CUS-029 has no DDL), and still no CSV `Export` (`apps/customers/` registers no export resource). **The BALANCE clause above — the whole DEF-127 ruling — is untouched by every one of these.** | prototype S17 · B2 §customers · B9 · cache-precision reading 2026-08-12 (under OQ-135) · **ledger-derived balance 2026-08-15 (DEF-127)** · **B18 §5.1 + PO rulings 2026-08-24 (CR-030 W2 tasks 2.1/2.2/2.3/2.6/2.9, T2.5 CUT)** | CONFIRMED (PO closure batch final 5, 2026-08-08) · **AMENDED 2026-08-15 — the balance is DERIVED, never cached** · **AMENDED 2026-08-24 — eight tabs, a five-tile KPI strip, STATUS/AGEING split, the `+N` chip overflow and `CUSTOMER #`; the sub-link CUT** | US-CUS-001/TC-1..4 · US-CUS-002 · `06-backend/tests/test_def_127_one_account_one_figure.py` · `07-frontend/e2e/DEF-127-one-figure.spec.ts` · **`06-backend/tests/test_cr030_w2_customer_no_search.py`** · **`06-backend/tests/test_cr030_w2_ageing.py`** · **`07-frontend/src/features/customers/AccountsPage.test.tsx` (TC-ACC-1..7 · TC-UI-1..8)** |

| BR-CUS-017 | One account ↔ one unit (accounts.unit_id); never premises-attached; at most ONE active account per unit (occupancy derives, BR-MET-036); enforcement = PROPOSED-SCHEMA partial unique index | B2 DDL · BR-MET-036 · S15 copy | CONFIRMED (PO closure batch, 2026-08-08) | US-CUS-001/TC-5 · US-CUS-004/TC-6 | ⚠ **NARROWED 2026-09-12 BY PO RULING — AN ACCOUNT MAY BE RE-POINTED AT ANOTHER UNIT AS A CORRECTION, AND ONLY AS A CORRECTION (`BR-CUS-1047`).** PO verbatim, answering `OQ-CUS-1769`: ***"(a) Narrow the rule to allow exactly this correction: a reason from a fixed list, a supervisor's approval, effective today, never backdated, issued bills untouched."*** **Everything else in this rule STANDS**: still one account to one unit, still at most one active account per unit, still never premises-attached, and **a genuine MOVE still goes through move-out and move-in** because the bills, meters and reads belong to the unit and must not follow a person. What opens is the case the product had no path for at all — a record that named the WRONG unit from the start, where nobody moved. ⚠ **AND IT HAS A DOOR SINCE 2026-09-13: `POST /accounts/{id}/correct-unit`.** `PATCH /accounts/{id}` still refuses `unit_id` — an edit is the wrong door and always was — but the refusal now NAMES the right one instead of saying the unit can never change, which stopped being true the day this narrowing was ruled (`REFUSED_FIELDS`, hard-won law 1's second form).

| BR-CUS-021 | Initial reads at new-account/move-in: per active meter, source AMI/manual/estimate, prior-final prefill, committed via the metering service (append-only BR-MET-017); reads below prior route to the metering exception path | prototype S18/S19 · BR-MET-015/017 | CONFIRMED (PO closure batch, 2026-08-08) | US-CUS-003/TC-7 · US-CUS-004/TC-5  ⚠ **"PREFILL" MEANS THE BOX, NOT THE HINT — CONFIRMED AS WRITTEN (PO ruling 2026-08-27, closes `OQ-394`).** PO verbatim: *"(a) Box opens with 42,864 in it"*. **This rule did not change; a divergence from it was reversed.** The CR-038 W9 lane read *"prior-final prefill"* as ambiguous, built an EMPTY input with the prior read in the help line, and flagged that it had chosen (`OQ-394`, and the human-review row on `customers/migrations/0026`). The PO chose the other way, so the wording above stands and the code now matches it. **THE COST WAS PUT TO THE PO AND THEY RULED WITH IT IN FRONT OF THEM, so it is recorded here rather than lost in the closure:** a clerk in a hurry presses Next, an eight-day-old number closes the account, bills the final invoice and becomes what the next occupant is measured from — and those eight days bill as **zero**. **WHAT THE BUILD THEREFORE CARRIES, and it is the merge lane's stated reading of how to implement the ruling safely rather than an amendment to it:** the reading's DATE travels with the number ON THE CONTROL, so what a person skims is *"Last read 42,864 kWh · read 8 days ago"* and not a bare figure. **Two things the build settles that the rule does not state and which are judgments, flagged as such:** (1) the prefilled value is TRIMMED of BR-MET-019's `NUMERIC(18,6)` storage zeros — `42864`, not `42864.000000` — and is never GROUPED in the input, because `42,864` would reach the server inside the decimal the clerk submits (DEF-646's rule, one derivation, `UnitCell.trimQuantity`); (2) a register with NO committed read opens EMPTY and is still never invented. Built in `07-frontend/src/features/customers/moveReadsCapture.tsx` for BOTH move wizards through one hook, so the rule cannot mean two things again. ⚠ **AMENDED FOR THE MOVE-IN ONLY — THE BOX OPENS EMPTY (PO ruling 2026-09-23, `DEF-CUS-6362`; decision log *"THE MOVE-IN READING TABLE (ACC-05 STEP 3), APPROVED AS MOCKED"*).** PO verbatim, on a mock-up of the table with *W-5041 · Last reading 2,530 kgal · Move-in reading [empty]*: *"this is correct fix it"*. **So *"prior-final prefill"* now means, AT A MOVE-IN, the prior final shown in its own *Last reading* column beside an EMPTY box** — a prefilled box nobody touched records a reading nobody took, and an empty one records nothing. **The worked case:** before, a move-in on a unit with two read meters and one never-read sub-meter posted TWO readings when the clerk typed nothing; now it posts NONE, and typing `2541` on one meter posts exactly ONE (measured in a browser on 2026-09-24). **`OQ-394` IS NOT REVERSED:** it was asked about a MOVE-OUT's closing figure and the move-out wizard keeps its prefilled box through `useReadsDraft`. The move-in holds a plain draft and mounts `MoveInReadingsTable.tsx`. |

| BR-CUS-024 | Deposit carry-forward from a prior closed account of the same customer replaces new collection at move-in; linkage = the customer entity (`customer_accounts`). ⚠ **THE MECHANICS ARE RULED — PO 2026-09-01, `CR-059` §6 Q2, option (b) CHOSEN, verbatim: *"offer it forward: the move-in screen says the deposit is held on the old account and the clerk accepts it"*.** Option (a) *"refund the old one, collect a new one"* DECLINED; option (c) *"carry forward only when the two accounts are close in time, say within 60 days"* **DECLINED — so there is NO time limit and its absence is the ruling, not an omission.** The cost the PO was shown with it: *"money has to move between two accounts, which nothing in the product does today"*. `CR-059` CS-22 states the consequence for this register in its own words: *"the rule that carried this as proposed on mechanics **can be CONFIRMED**"*. **THE MOVEMENT, AS BUILT 2026-09-03 (task 33):** ONE balanced movement recorded on both accounts through the single cross-account writer `apps/payments/transfer.carry_deposit_forward` — **never a refund on one plus a fresh collection on the other** — posting `DR 2300 deposit liability (SOURCE) / CR 2300 deposit liability (DESTINATION)` with **receivables untouched**, and moving the customer deposit register (`BR-CUS-670`) by the same figure on both sides. **WORKED: $150.00 held on the closed account → register old 150.00 → 0.00, new 0.00 → 150.00; the tenant's 2300 balance UNCHANGED and BR-LED-007's tie-out AGREED at 150.00 = 150.00.** ⚠ **WHAT MOVES IS THE PRINCIPAL, AND `BR-CUS-057`'s INTEREST TRAVELS AS THE CLOCK RATHER THAN AS MONEY.** The offer states the customer's POSITION — **$153.50** — and $153.50 cannot move: interest is booked only at the final bill (`DR 6950 / CR 2300`), so neither the register nor 2300 holds it. The **ORIGINAL receipt date moves with the money** (`BR-CUS-050`), so the same **$3.50** stands on the new account the day it lands and goes on accruing; a confirmation of 153.50 is REFUSED naming both figures. ⛔ **THIS DOES NOT TOUCH `OQ-365` AND MUST NOT BE READ AS CLOSING IT.** `OQ-365` is the SERVICE-TRANSFER case (`BR-CUS-085`, one customer moving address without closing), and this ruling is the CLOSE-AND-REOPEN case. **The two flows are different and collapsing them would move money on a rule that never mentioned it.** `BR-CUS-085`'s row still reads *"BR-CUS-024 PROPOSED"*; that clause is now stale for the close-and-reopen case only, and the amendment there is OWED. | prototype S19 step 4 · **PO ruling 2026-09-01 (`CR-059` §6 Q2)** · `CR-059` §8 CS-22 · task 33 · decision log 2026-09-03 | **CONFIRMED-AS-AMENDED 2026-09-01 (mechanics — PO ruling, transcribed and built 2026-09-03) / linkage CONFIRMED → BR-CUS-054 (OQ-057 closed)** | US-CUS-007/TC-4 · `06-backend/tests/test_cr059_cs22_deposit_carry_forward.py` · `06-backend/tests/test_cr059_cs22_deposit_carry_pg.py` |
| BR-CUS-025 | Deposit is a liability, never revenue; at move-out it (+ accrued interest, BR-CUS-057) applies to ~~the final bill FIRST~~ **EVERYTHING OUTSTANDING**, remainder refunds via payments `refunds`; the prototype's contradictory static sample (applied $0.00) is mock data — the step-3 hint is the rule. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-12 (PO, OQ-158 CLOSED → BR-BIL-013): THE DEPOSIT SETTLES EVERYTHING OUTSTANDING, NOT JUST THE FINAL BILL.** Verbatim: *"everything outstanding"*. A customer who leaves owing a final bill **AND an older arrear** has the deposit applied against the **whole closing position**; applying it to the final bill alone would **refund a customer who still owes the utility money**. **Worked (built with the ruling, 2026-08-12):** a **$120.84** arrear is inside the settled base, so the product stops refunding **$90.14** to a customer who is in debt to it. **AND THIS IS THE ONLY TIME THE DEPOSIT MOVES (PO 2026-08-12, OQ-179 → BR-CUS-042/062):** it is settled ONCE, at closure — a plan change moves no deposit money | S20 step-3 hint · B2 r2 · settlement scope PO 2026-08-12 (OQ-158) | PROPOSED (apply-first mechanics) · **scope AMENDED 2026-08-12: everything outstanding** / interest CONFIRMED → BR-CUS-057 | US-CUS-005/TC-1 · US-CUS-007/TC-5 · WE-7 |

| BR-CUS-029 | ~~Account-side snapshot storage: APPEND-ONLY `account_plan_assignments` (account, rate_plan_id, plan_version, assigned_from/to, source new_connection\|move_in\|change_plan, actor, reason) mirroring r5 #13~~ — **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-11 (Product Owner), replacing the 2026-08-08 confirmation. THE ACCOUNT-SIDE PLAN TABLE IS NOT BUILT.** Verbatim: *"from unit you will derive the plan, you will also need to maintain attributes that are important at account level - A"*. **The plan lives once, on `units.rate_plan_id`**, with its dated history and version snapshot in `unit_plan_assignments` (rating-owned, and genuinely written since 2026-08-11 — until then it could not be, because that table's FK wants a plan and the unit held a tariff). The account DERIVES its plan through the unit, so the two can never disagree: there is only one of them. Reprint fidelity — the reason a snapshot was wanted — comes from the BILL's own locked-at-issue payload, not from a live assignment row, so a second table would add a thing that can drift without adding a fact. **What SURVIVES this supersession:** the second and third clauses stand unchanged — scalar attributes snapshot to `accounts` (deposit_on_file · deposit_received_on · credit_tier · autopay · tax_exempt +ref +expiry · bad_address · nsf_count · preferred_language · type/subtype · status · currency · balance, all shipped columns), capacity units stay effective-dated in `account_capacities`, and `service_agreements.tariff_id` remains superseded. That is the "attributes that are important at account level" half of the ruling. | BR-CUS-014 sc.1/6 · BR-CUS-011 · B2 r5 #5/#13 | **SUPERSEDED (PO 2026-08-11)** — was CONFIRMED (PO closure batch, 2026-08-08) | US-CUS-006/TC-1..5 · tests/test_us_met_003_plan_history.py |
| BR-CUS-030 | Move-out: final reads committed; `closed_at` = move-out date, status → closed; unit derives Vacant (BR-MET-036) keeping its plan (BR-MET-044); ~~dunning stops~~; close-out documents queue via notifications. ⚠ **THE `dunning stops` CLAUSE IS SUPERSEDED FOR THE CLOSED-WITH-BALANCE CASE 2026-08-24 (PO, OQ-346 CLOSED → BR-CUS-082).** Verbatim: *"(b) continue, ladder restarts"*. **A debt that survives closure is STILL CHASED, and the dunning ladder RESTARTS FROM TIER 1** rather than resuming the tier it had reached. **Worked, on the seed fixture OQ-346 was put with:** `A-11024 · Ridge Coffee Co.` closes owing **$328.70** with **$153.50** of deposit and interest against it — the **$175.20** shortfall was recorded and then pursued by nothing under this clause, and is now pursued with the ladder starting again at TIER 1; a customer already at TIER 2 at closure does not stay at TIER 2. **WHAT SURVIVES UNCHANGED:** every other clause of this rule, and `dunning stops` ITSELF for the ordinary case — **an account that closes owing nothing has nothing to chase and no ladder runs.** The supersession is narrow, and reading it wider would start a ladder against a settled customer. ⚠ **A closed account cannot be escalated to disconnection** — there is nothing left to disconnect — **so the restarted ladder is a COLLECTIONS sequence, not a service one**; its tiers, intervals and protections all apply again from the beginning. W7 owns the shape, per BR-CUS-082. ⚠ **AND A MOVE-OUT NOW RECORDS WHY THE TENANCY ENDED — ADDED 2026-09-20 (PO ruling, option A, decision log *"TEN MOVE-OUT RULINGS IN ONE SITTING"* item 3).** This rule named every effect of a move-out EXCEPT its reason, because there was nowhere to put one: `accounts.close_reason` had been PROPOSED-SCHEMA since 2026-08-08 and `reason` was a REFUSED field on every door. The blueprint's own dashed panel on p110 is why that stopped being acceptable — *"Five options are named on screen and none of them is stored. There is no column for it, so the wizard asks and forgets - which is worse than not asking."* **THE CLAUSE:** a move-out MAY record one of five reasons — `lease_ended` · `owner_sold` · `downgrade_or_hardship` · `deceased` · `other` — written to `accounts.close_reason` in the close's own transaction (migration `customers/0058`, `ck_accounts_close_reason`). **REQUIRED ON THE SCREEN, OPTIONAL AT THE DOOR:** the S20 wizard will not continue without one, and `services.move_out` still accepts a move-out with none, because a caller with no clerk in front of it — an import, a script — must still be able to close an account. **NULL MEANS *NOBODY WAS ASKED* AND IS NEVER FOLDED INTO `other`:** they are two different facts about the utility's own record, and the move record states which of the two silences a row carries. ⚠ **NO MIGRATION WRITES A REASON FOR AN ACCOUNT ALREADY CLOSED** — hard rule 4, ruled explicitly; on the beta that is exactly ONE account and it keeps none. **AN UNKNOWN CODE IS REFUSED BY NAME, NEVER COERCED ONTO `other`**, because silently mapping a typo is how a closure count becomes a lie. **WHETHER THE FIVE BECOME TENANT-EDITABLE IS `OQ-CUS-12512` AND IS OPEN; WHETHER A MISTYPED REASON MAY BE CORRECTED IS `OQ-CUS-12513` AND IS OPEN** — today it cannot be, because `close_reason` is refused on every door but the move-out. | prototype S20 · BR-MET-036/044 · **`dunning stops` clause SUPERSEDED PO 2026-08-24 (OQ-346 → BR-CUS-082)** · **reason clause ADDED PO 2026-09-20 (item 3)** | CONFIRMED (PO closure batch, 2026-08-08) · **`dunning stops` clause SUPERSEDED for the closed-with-balance case 2026-08-24 (→BR-CUS-082)** · **reason clause CONFIRMED 2026-09-20 (PO, option A) — BUILT `v2.314.0`** | US-CUS-005/TC-1 |

| BR-CUS-036 | Documents: customers-owned `account_documents` metadata (name, type lookup System/Contract/KYC/Payment/Correspondence, uploader, size, storage ref); system documents attach automatically; system/contract docs audit-locked (IS-1 🔒); 7-yr retention (OQ-017 closure). ✅ **IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-12** (`CR-094-BRC01` wave I, `ACC-S8`, `2.239.10`) — **thirty-five days after it was CONFIRMED, during which nothing implemented it and the tab said so on screen.** `customers/0042` creates the table, `0043` puts RLS `FORCE` on it, `GET|POST /accounts/{id}/documents` are served. ⚠ **`name` AND `size` ARE NOT COLUMNS ON THIS TABLE AND THAT IS THE RULE BEING MET, NOT MISSED:** they are `stored_documents.display_name` and `byte_length`, written by the same call that stores the bytes and frozen by the same write-once trigger — a second copy of a file's own name is a second answer to what it is called. ⚠ **THE *storage ref* IS A FOREIGN KEY TO `tenancy.stored_documents`** (`CR-049` task 1's store, reused — no second storage path), and the document's `source_type` is the **ninth** value `account_document` rather than `customer_account`, because `uq_stored_documents_filed` would otherwise refuse a clerk's SECOND pdf on one account (`tenancy/0044`, the same finding `tenancy/0040` recorded for a technician's ninth photograph). ⚠ **`system` IS A VALUE A ROW MAY HAVE AND NOT ONE A CLERK MAY CHOOSE** — *system documents attach automatically*, so the upload refuses it by name. ⚠ **AUDIT-LOCKED WAS BUILT AS AN ABSENCE UNTIL 2026-09-13, AND IT IS NOW BUILT AS A REFUSAL.** This clause read *"there is no delete column, no delete endpoint and no row action, so nothing in this product can remove a filed document of any type. Whether a mis-filed document may ever be withdrawn is put to the PO in the review-ledger row of 2026-09-12 and is not decided here."* **The PO decided it** — `BR-CUS-1052`, verbatim *"We should have this functionality"* — and the answer changes what *audit-locked* means in code without changing what it means in law. **WHAT A WITHDRAWAL DOES:** a `kyc`, `payment` or `correspondence` filing leaves the account — unlisted, uncounted, unopenable — and an audit row names who and when. **WHAT IT DOES NOT DO:** it erases no byte (`NFR-35`, `BR-SHL-031`, untouched), and it is **REFUSED BY NAME for `system` and `contract`** with 409 `BIZ-LOCKED` and its reason on screen (`BR-SHL-025`). So *audit-locked* is still exactly what this row has always said it is: **nothing in this product can edit or remove a system or contract document**, and that sentence is now enforced by a named refusal rather than by the absence of a route. | prototype Documents tab | CONFIRMED (PO closure batch, 2026-08-08) · **BUILT 2026-09-12** | US-CUS-011/TC-4,6 · `06-backend/tests/test_cr094_i_account_documents.py` (18 cases) |

| BR-CUS-042 | ~~Deposit differential on plan change = `plan_deposit_rules` category amount (new − old); positive billed, negative credited/refunded~~ — ⚠ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-12 (PO, OQ-179 CLOSED): THERE IS NO MID-PLAN DEPOSIT DIFFERENTIAL AT ALL — THE DEPOSIT SETTLES ONLY AT CLOSURE.** Verbatim: *"we dont have to give depoiste back in between it will be settled only at the time of closure"*. **The ruling did not choose between this rule and BR-CUS-062 — it removed the question.** A plan change **moves no deposit money**, so **neither −$18.50 nor −$68.50 is ever shown to a customer** (decision log, 2026-08-12, verbatim). The deposit is settled **once, against everything outstanding, at closure** — which is OQ-158's ruling of the same day, and the two now agree. BR-CUS-062's differential clause is superseded by the same sentence. **Consequence for the built S23 preview:** it states no deposit figure and the confirm registers no deposit instruction — `preview_plan_change` returning `deposit_differential: null` and `change_plan` reporting `deposit_differential_not_registered` were the parked behaviour and are now **the rule**, not a stub. ⚠ **FLAGGED, NOT CHOSEN (reported to the PO rather than decided here):** **−$18.50 is the BASE delta** (RES-STD **$30.50** → Senior **$12.00**), not a deposit figure, and **−$68.50** was the deposit-inclusive net (−18.50 + −50.00); the ruling's sentence bans both. Whether US-CUS-016 §7 may still state a **base-only** net impact is not settled by the ruling's words and is left for the PO | OQ-011 closure ("differential follows category amounts") · B4 addendum · **SUPERSEDED PO 2026-08-12 (OQ-179)** | **SUPERSEDED (PO 2026-08-12)** — was CONFIRMED (amount) / PROPOSED (bill-vs-credit mechanics) | US-CUS-016/TC-2 · §7 |
| BR-CUS-043 | ⚠ **SUPERSEDED IN PART 2026-10-17 — THE LATE-FEE CLAUSE ONLY (PO, asked *"Should a customer on a payment plan be charged late fees?"* — verbatim *"no"*; decision log 2026-10-17 *"Same day, PO verbatim on payment plans"*).** **While a plan is LIVE the account is charged NO late fee at all** — not on the plan's arrears and not on the current month's bill; *"late-fee suspension is a per-plan election"* and *"including its late fee"* below no longer decide the fee. **Worked:** Ana on a live 6-instalment plan, October bill $62 unpaid past grace → **$0.00** late fee (was $5.00). The schedule (❄️ GT-47), the dunning-and-disconnect pause and its edge are UNCHANGED. Payment-plan schedule: installment = (total − down) ÷ n, 2dp half-up (B4.4), remainder on FINAL (A7 EXPLICIT); step-2 edits must re-sum exactly; while active + current, dunning and disconnect pause (A7/A19 §3); late-fee suspension is a per-plan election; one live plan per account. ⚠ **THE PAUSE IS SCOPED (PO ruling 2026-08-11, OQ-138 CLOSED → BR-BIL-073).** Verbatim: *"no there is monthly charges that thye have to pay nad it is due every month"*. **The dunning and disconnect pause covers the ARREARS THIS PLAN SPREADS and nothing else** — the current month's bill is due every month and is chased like anybody else's, including its late fee under BR-BIL-001 as amended. **Worked, on the frozen PE-13 plan:** instalment **$36.22** + October's bill **$184.20** = the customer owes **$220.42** in October, not $36.22. `suspend_late_fees` remains a per-plan election over the plan's own arrears. The SCHEDULE half of this rule is unchanged and is ❄️ frozen as GT-47 (PE-13: 267.30 − 50 over 6 → **5 × 36.22 + 36.20**). ⚠ **THE PAUSE NOW HAS ITS EDGE (PO ruling 2026-08-12, OQ-170 CLOSED): A MISSED INSTALMENT LIFTS IT IMMEDIATELY.** Verbatim: *"Immediate"*. This rule pauses dunning and disconnect *"while active **AND current**"*; the ruling fixes when **current** ends — **the day the instalment is missed, with no grace period**. **Worked, on the frozen PE-13 plan:** the 1 Oct instalment of **$36.22** is unpaid; on **2 Oct** chasing resumes over the arrears this plan spreads. It does **not** remove the customer from the plan — nobody is dropped (PO 2026-08-11) — the plan stays `active` and the chase resumes **while the plan stands**. ⚠ **AND THE INSTALMENT IS MET FIRST (PO 2026-08-12, OQ-139 CLOSED → BR-PAY-012):** an instalment payment reaches **the PLAN before the oldest invoice** — a NAMED EXCEPTION to oldest-invoice-first, scoped to instalment payments only — and **short of the instalment, nothing reaches the current bill**, so a part-paid instalment is still a missed one. **Worked on the same plan:** **$36.22** is met before October's **$184.20** (the customer still owes **$220.42** that month, unchanged by this) | A7 · A19 §3 · B4.4 · A3; pause SCOPED by PO 2026-08-11; pause EDGE + instalment-first PO 2026-08-12 (OQ-170, OQ-139) | CONFIRMED (pause clause amended 2026-08-11 and 2026-08-12) | US-CUS-018/TC-1,1b,3,4,5,7 · `tests/test_us_cus_018_payment_plan.py` |
| BR-CUS-044 | Payment-plan agreement: renders from the S67 contract template (A19 §3 content, BR-NOT-004 type ①); e-sign via DocuSign seam (B7); states proposed → agreement_sent → active(on sign) → closed; ~~PROPOSED `defaulted` on the template default clause ({missed_count} missed → full balance due, fees resume; default 2 — tenant/template config, flagged for PO)~~; activation ONLY on signature; executes via CUSTOMER E-SIGNATURE ALONE — no utility countersignature or any other signature required (supersedes the A19 §3 countersign block). ⚠ **THE `defaulted` STATE AND THE {missed_count} DEFAULT CLAUSE ARE SUPERSEDED (PO ruling 2026-08-11, OQ-141 CLOSED).** Verbatim: *"nodrop we chase for payments until write off"*. **Nobody is dropped off a plan.** A missed instalment is RECORDED so collections can chase it and changes no state; the plan stays `active` at the tenth miss exactly as at the first, and an account that never pays reaches write-off through the ordinary collections path (BR-BIL-006) — a decision with a human on it. The state set is therefore FOUR values: proposed → agreement_sent → active → closed. ~~A plan ends by being PAID, never by being broken.~~ The template's default clause must not be rendered onto a new agreement; BR-PAY-016's *"2nd miss VOIDS plan"* and J13 step 6 are superseded by the same sentence. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-12 (PO, OQ-171 CLOSED) — A WRITE-OFF CLOSES A PAYMENT PLAN, AND THE PRODUCT'S JOB IS THE MECHANISM AND THE REPORT, NOT THE DECISION.** Verbatim: *"Write-off closes - these are user business decision, they will make a decision, we have to provide a mechanism and report it"*. **So a plan ends by being PAID *or* by having its arrears WRITTEN OFF — never by being broken.** **Worked:** an account agrees a **$267.30** plan, pays the **$50.00** down payment and nothing else; **eighteen months on the plan is still live** because nobody is dropped; when the utility decides to write the arrears off, that write-off **closes the plan** and the account can take a new plan tomorrow. Before this, `closed` was reachable only by paying every instalment, and *one live plan per account* (BR-CUS-043) locked an account whose arrears the utility had already given up on **out of the arrangement that might help it next time**. **THE PRODUCT NEVER WRITES ANYTHING OFF** — `close_plan_on_write_off` has **no automatic caller** and counts nothing toward itself; that absence is as much the ruling as the closure is. The FOUR-value state set is unchanged; what changes is that `closed` is now reachable by a human's write-off as well as by payment. ⚠ **AND TWO COLLECTIONS EDGES RULED THE SAME DAY LAND ON THIS RULE'S LADDER (PO 2026-08-12 → detail in BR-PAY-016):** after a **bounced cheque the ladder RESTARTS AT ITS FIRST RUNG** (*"Restart from the beginning"* — a **$1,842.10** bill due 6 Jul, cheque posted 5 Jul, bounced three weeks later → the full ladder again), and **a partial payment pauses the ladder only if it meets a configured percentage of the outstanding balance** (*"at least 25% or 50%"*, a TENANT SETTING in Settings → Configure — **$150** against **$948** is **15.8%**, so the ladder continues; 25% would need **$237.00**, 50% **$474.00**) | A19 §3 · A5 · A6 · BR-NOT-004; default clause SUPERSEDED PO 2026-08-11; write-off closure + ladder edges PO 2026-08-12 (OQ-171, OQ-133, OQ-136) | CONFIRMED (in part SUPERSEDED; amended 2026-08-12) | US-CUS-018/TC-1,2,9 · `tests/test_us_cus_018_payment_plan.py::test_us_cus_018_tc9` |
| BR-CUS-045 | Contact PII: per B2 contacts columns (citext email, dob, gov_id_last4 tokenized); display always masked; high-sensitivity columns app-layer encrypted per the OQ-016 closure | B2 §customers · OQ-016 closure | CONFIRMED (PO closure batch, 2026-08-08) | US-CUS-009/TC-2,5 |

## PO Accounts onboarding rulings (2026-08-08) — EP-CUS-07 approved
| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-046 | **EP-CUS-07 "Account onboarding (brownfield)" approved**: bulk account upload with template per AC-1 (account# auto-option, customer+contact, REQUIRED premises/unit ref, category/subtype, ~~plan override~~, status, deposit $+date, tax-exempt, autopay enrollment, opening balance, since date). ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-12 (PO, OQ-166 CLOSED — the PO said *"decide for me"* and the decision is recorded with its reasoning): A CUTOVER ROW CARRYING THE RETIRED PLAN COLUMN IMPORTS, THE COLUMN IS NOT APPLIED, AND THE ROW REPORTS WHAT WAS DISCARDED.** The template's plan-override column **lost its destination on 2026-08-11** — the plan derives from the UNIT (BR-CUS-029 as superseded), `account_plan_assignments` does not exist, and customers may not write metering's `units.rate_plan_id` (B1 / DB-16). **Rejecting the row** would fail a whole cutover over a legacy column that means nothing; **ignoring it silently is worse** — a utility would believe they had set a plan and would find out at the first bill run, at **a price nobody chose**. So: **the row IMPORTS, the override is NOT applied, and the importer raises a per-row notice naming the unit's actual plan and the value it discarded** — silent only when the two already agree. **Worked:** row **412** says *unit `2205 Cedar Ln · Unit B`, plan `C1`*; the unit is on **RES-STD**; the account is created on **RES-STD** and the row reports **"plan C1 ignored — this unit is on RES-STD"** | PO AC-1 · retired plan column PO 2026-08-12 (OQ-166, delegated) | CONFIRMED (amended 2026-08-12) | US-CUS-019 (row 412 notice) |
| BR-CUS-047 | Migration order: Premises → Units+Plans → Assets → **Accounts** → opening reads; account rows referencing missing units error. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-12 (PO, OQ-165 CLOSED) — A RE-IMPORTED CUTOVER FILE MATCHES ON SOMETHING IN THE FILE, NEVER ON AN ID THE SYSTEM MINTED.** Verbatim: *"Match on something in the file"*. **Worked:** a utility imports its accounts, **fixes one misspelt street name** and re-uploads — today that creates every account a second time, because the rows whose account number the **SYSTEM** allocated carry no natural key. Correcting one cell changes the bytes, so the `file+tags` idempotency key (B2 DB-12) no longer matches: on `bbu-accounts-cutover.csv` the **1,144** rows carrying explicit numbers all error as duplicates (BR-CUS-051 working as intended) while the **96** blank-number rows **quietly create 96 new accounts** on units that already have one — and a row imported as *Pending activation* or *Closed* is caught by BR-CUS-017's occupancy guard not at all. **The importer must therefore find its key IN the file** — the customer reference, the unit reference, or a stated combination — rather than relying on an id it minted. The `file+tags` key still catches the identical-bytes re-upload; this closes the CORRECTED-file case underneath it | PO AC-2 · re-import key PO 2026-08-12 (OQ-165) | CONFIRMED (amended 2026-08-12) | US-CUS-019/TC-4,5 |
| BR-CUS-048 | **Opening balances = AGED BUCKETS** (Current / 31–60 / 61–90 / 90+ per account) — aging, collections, and late fees correct from day one. Lump-sum and invoice-level rejected/deferred | PO AC-3 (option B) | CONFIRMED | GT-16 (PROPOSED fixture) · US-CUS-021 |
| BR-CUS-049 | Opening balances post as **balanced opening journal entries** (AR ↔ opening-balance clearing), dated at cutover, immutable; corrections via J6 only | PO AC-4 | CONFIRMED | GT-16 (PROPOSED fixture) · US-CUS-022 |
| BR-CUS-050 | Deposits import with their ORIGINAL receipt dates (interest accrual from true date per OQ-061 outcome) | PO AC-5 | CONFIRMED | US-CUS-023/TC-1,2 |
| BR-CUS-051 | Import mechanics = the standard: staged → per-row errors → skip-errors ON → "N created · N failed" summary → 10,000-row cap | PO AC-6 | CONFIRMED | US-CUS-019/TC-1,3,6 |
| BR-CUS-052 | Category change on live account: allowed w/ warning + audit, forward-only. **PLUS (PO addition): imports include a SOURCE→TARGET VALUE-MAPPING step** — legacy system codes (statuses, categories, charge codes…) map to this tenant's reference-data values before acceptance; unmapped values error per the rules-based import standard. [PM interpretation of "attribute data from source to target mapping will be required since DB values will change" — applied to the import wizard; flag if you meant category-change mechanics instead] | PO AC-7 | CONFIRMED (interpretation flagged) | US-CUS-020/TC-1..4 |
| BR-CUS-053 | S17 capability row per AC-8 (search acct#/name/address/meter#; filters status·category·plan·autopay·balance>0; default sort name A→Z; IS-5 columns). ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-24 (PO ruling, CR-030 W2 task 2.9) — THE SEARCH SET IS FIVE, NOT FOUR, AND `CUSTOMER #` IS A COLUMN AS WELL AS A SEARCH TERM.** PO verbatim, asked as a question about work they expected to be done: *"we were suppose to add customer # comlume what happened to that"*. The stored column exists and has since W0 — `customers.customer_no`, migrations `0013_cr030_w0_customer_no` + `0014_cr030_w0_backfill_customer_no`, allocated from the tenant's numbering series and unique per tenant case-insensitively over its non-null values. **Three things were missing and all three are now required:** (a) `selectors.account_list` MATCHES `customer_no` — `Q(customer__customer_no__icontains=term)` joined into the same OR as `account_no`, so one box finds a person by either number; (b) the list renders `CUSTOMER #` as a column, available through the shared column selector; (c) the search placeholder names it. **Order matters and B18 already ruled it — *do not ship the placeholder before the column*:** the W2 lane correctly withheld the prompt because a placeholder promising a search the server does not perform is hard-won law 1 in its purest form (see `AccountsListPage.tsx` exception E3, T2.9). `TC-ACC-7` is the test that goes green when the selector learns the column. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-09 (`CR-089` wave K, `PUJ-51`) — THE ADDRESS LEG WALKS THE PROPERTY, NOT ONLY THE UNIT, AND UNTIL IT DID THIS RULE WAS TRUE OF EVERY ACCOUNT EXCEPT ONE.** *"service address"* was implemented as `seams.unit_ids_matching`, which walks *unit → premises* — correct for an occupant and unreachable for `BR-CUS-1010`'s owner Master Account, which names **no unit** and hangs off the ADDRESS. Measured on the demo utility: **`500 Main St` returned five of the six accounts standing there** and left out **`AC-2026-00051`, the account that owns the building**. The leg now also ORs `Q(id__in=account_ids_at_matching_address(term))`, which walks `accounts.premises_id` (backfilled on every row by migration `0037`) into metering's own `premises_list(q=…)`. ⚠ **THAT PREDICATE IS METERING'S AND IS WIDER THAN THE WORD *ADDRESS*** — it is the one the Properties screen searches with, so **town, property number and owner** match too. Deliberate: one definition of *does this property match what I typed* for the whole product, rather than one per screen. **LAZY, AND THAT IS PART OF THE RULE** (`NFR-3`): it is handed to `Q(…__in=…)` unevaluated and renders as a nested subquery, so the leg costs **no extra round trip**. The same read is what `apps/payments` and `apps/billing` resolve by name for `PUJ-52`/`PUJ-53`. | PO AC-8 · **PO 2026-08-24 (CR-030 W2 T2.9)** · **PO 2026-09-08 (`CR-089`, *"you will ahve changes when … Accounts, Bills, Payments are search by address"*)** | CONFIRMED · **AMENDED 2026-09-09 — the address leg reaches the owner's account** | US-CUS-001/TC-1,4 · US-CUS-002/TC-2 · TC-ACC-7 | ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-12 — THE `default sort name A→Z` CLAUSE IS SUPERSEDED BY `BR-CUS-1046`.** PO ruling 2026-09-10, verbatim: ***"Whenever any account is created or any entry is created in the system, it automatically sorts it by the alphabetical order. It should always sort it by the creation order. The new newly created entries should always show on the top."*** The default becomes **`created_at` descending**. **Every other clause of this rule — the five search terms, the filters, the columns — is UNTOUCHED**; this moves one clause and nothing else. ⚠ The ruling was made on 2026-09-10 and reached no file in this repository until 2026-09-12, surviving only in the chat and in `05-prototype/02 Accounts Menu Blueprint.pdf`; a lane reading this row in between would have built the behaviour the PO had already rejected.

## PO Accounts final rulings (2026-08-08)
| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-054 | **Customer is a first-class entity** (option b): one customer (person/business) links to MANY accounts (landlord case); one edit updates all; "all accounts for this customer" view enabled. PROPOSED-SCHEMA: `customers` table + `customer_accounts` link (Lane C batch #3). ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-12 (PO, OQ-135 CLOSED) — A CUSTOMER OWING ON ANOTHER ACCOUNT IS OFFSET FIRST, AND THIS RULE'S ENTITY IS WHAT "ANOTHER ACCOUNT" MEANS.** Verbatim: *"offset first"*. The same person holding a credit on one account and an arrear on another has **the credit applied against the arrear before any refund is paid**; the refund, if any, is what remains. **Worked:** **$57.13** credit on one account against **$948.20** overdue (91 days, final notice sent) on another → **no refund**. **THE SCOPE, written down because the ruling did not contain it (Eng-Lead, 2026-08-12):** the `customer_accounts` link is **N:M**, so a **jointly-held account counts every holder's other accounts**; a shared surname or a shared address counts for **nothing**, because offsetting across those would move money between strangers. Execution and the cache caveat live in BR-PAY-014 / BR-CUS-015 — the refund REFUSES and the offset is a balance transfer, never a subtraction from a cached balance | PO OQ-057 (b) · offset-first PO 2026-08-12 (OQ-135) | CONFIRMED (amended 2026-08-12) | US-CUS-019/TC-2 · US-CUS-008/TC-2 · `apps/customers/selectors.arrears_on_other_accounts` |
| BR-CUS-055 | Account statuses (option b): **Pending activation · Active · Marked disconnect · Vacation hold/Suspended · Closed** — seeded in Reference data, tenant-extensible, behavior-class mapped — SEED: Pending activation=non-billable · Active=billable · **Marked disconnect=billable** (consuming until cut; PO delegated, PM ruled) · Vacation hold/Suspended=billable-base-only · Closed=terminal (OQ-064 CLOSED) ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-25 — THERE ARE SIX, NOT FIVE (BR-CUS-107, PO ruling closing DEF-701).** The sixth is **`Disconnected` = non-billable** — the state a CUT account is in, which none of the five could express: a cut account read `Marked disconnect / billable`, and that row is the rung BEFORE the cut, saying *"consuming until cut"* about a customer who has stopped consuming. Read BR-CUS-107 for the three classes that were ruled OUT and why. **The seed order is now Pending activation · Active · Marked disconnect · Vacation hold/Suspended · Closed · Disconnected**, mirrored in `apps/customers/services.SEED_ACCOUNT_STATUSES`, in the platform fixture at **V2.48.0.0** and in migration `customers/0025_br_cus_107_disconnected_status.py`, all three pinned equal by `06-backend/tests/test_br_cus_107_disconnected_status.py`. ✅ **AMENDED 2026-08-31 — THE ROW IS SEEDED **AND ASSIGNED**; DEF-701's screen symptom is CLOSED.** This clause read *"THE ROW IS SEEDED AND NOTHING ASSIGNS IT YET"* and listed two consequences of the `non_billable` class that had to close first. Both closed: the OCCUPANCY one was RULED CORRECT AS WRITTEN (BR-CUS-110, PO 2026-08-26 — a cut ends occupancy and the debt follows the person) with its terminal-path fallout fixed by BR-CUS-430/431, and the BILLING one was OQ-1170, closed by **PO ruling 2026-08-30 — THE DISCONNECT IS THE WHOLE EVENT**, verbatim: *"when we disconnect - we generate final bill at that time itself - so later bill is not required ... entire workflow including all settlement and status update is taken care at time of disconnect or move out - so account status changes and outstanding is pending on account if any"*. `commit_cut` now raises the final metered bill for the days up to the cut, records what is outstanding, and writes `Disconnected` in the same act — see BR-CUS-107 and BR-CUS-103 below for the two rules that meet there and the ONE case where the status is deliberately not written. | PO OQ-058 (b) · **BR-CUS-107 (PO 2026-08-25, DEF-701)** | CONFIRMED incl. class seed (2026-08-08) · **AMENDED 2026-08-25 — six statuses, the sixth `Disconnected` / non_billable** | US-CUS-020/TC-2,3 · **`06-backend/tests/test_br_cus_107_disconnected_status.py`** |

| BR-CUS-067 | **Account sub-types are a TWO-LEVEL tree under the platform `account_types`, TENANT-scoped and tenant-extensible, seeded 12 per tenant.** ⚠ **THIRTEEN SINCE 2026-09-07 — PO ruling, verbatim: _"please add Governtment in it"_.** `government` / **Government**, under the `government` platform type, appended LAST because this list's order is the order the S8 card prints. **It is not a duplicate of the account TYPE of the same name**: the sub-type is the tenant-editable level beneath the type, which is this rule's whole shape, and a utility that bills a federal depot differently from a city hall needs somewhere to put it — the three existing government rows name specific kinds of public body and this is the general one. The ratified tree (CR-010 "Part-10 confirmations" 10.0, PO verbatim *"add them"*, 2026-08-17): Residential (Single-family · Multi-family · Senior/Low-income) · Commercial (Small commercial · Large commercial · **Institutional & non-profit** · **Healthcare** — the PO's item-1 home for *"Hospital, Church etc"*) · Industrial (Light · Heavy) · Municipal/Government (Municipal buildings · Schools · Parks & facilities). `account_subtypes.account_type_id` is the parent FK (customers 0011, PROTECT, nullable ONLY for rows that predate the ruling); the lookup handler REQUIRES a parent on every row created or edited, so the tree stays exactly two levels. Rows are the tenant's own vocabulary (r5 #12 [D5]'s tenant-defined half) — seeded rows `is_system` (BR-TEN-036: deactivatable, never deletable), full CRUD on the S8 `account_subtypes` card beside the READ-ONLY platform `account_types` card (the pair is S65 "Account types & sub-types"). DISCRETIONARY, not structural: `accounts.account_subtype_id` is NULL by r5 #19, so no schema path requires a sub-type to exist and the S111 toggle governs the list (BR-TEN-067). ⚠ **SUPERSEDES the "no parent-type link" reading of r5 #12** (recorded in apps/customers/models.py + lookups.py lifecycle notes since 2026-08-10, REPORTED not chosen): the PO's 2026-08-17 sentence IS the answer to that reported question — the link exists and points at `account_types`. | CR-010 (PO 2026-08-17: "add them" + A-section item 1) · r5 #12 [D5] | CONFIRMED | tests/test_cr010_subtypes_unit_types.py |
| BR-CUS-068 | ⚠⚠ **SUPERSEDED IN ITS ANCHORING 2026-09-01 (CR-056 R1+R2, and the PO ruling of 2026-08-31 that ratifies R2 in substance) — A MASTER ACCOUNT HANGS OFF THE ADDRESS, NOT OFF A UNIT INSIDE IT. THE CLAUSE *ATTACHED TO THE PREMISES' COMMON-AREA UNITS* IS THE PART THAT IS REVERSED; THE ROW'S SHAPE — AN ORDINARY `accounts` ROW WITH `kind='master'`, NO NEW TABLE — IS UNCHANGED AND STILL CONFIRMED. See `BR-CUS-1010`.** ⚠⚠ **AND NARROWED IN ITS COVERAGE 2026-09-21 — A MASTER ACCOUNT MAY COVER NOTHING AT ALL. PO, verbatim: *"common area is option"*.** This row's clause *over the premises' COMMON AREAS* is what is narrowed: a build with no shared space at all now commits and **still opens the owner's master account**. It had not: `metering.connections.create_owner_account` refused such a build by name (`connection_no_common_area_for_master`, `BR-MET-116`), which with `BR-MET-040`'s plan check on the OTHER side made a pincer — **eight New Connect builds raised in a browser and eight refused at the final press**, with a common area and without. **NOTHING ABOUT THE ANCHOR MOVES, BECAUSE THERE IS NOTHING LEFT TO MOVE:** `BR-CUS-1010` put the row on the ADDRESS on 2026-09-01, so `accounts.unit_id` is NULL and `accounts.premises_id` is set on a master with common areas and on one without, identically. The common areas were never the anchor; they were the COVERAGE, carried by `service_agreements`. **With none, the account covers nothing, bills nothing and produces no document** — which is `CR-056` W4's *no lines, no document* and not a new state. `_master_attachment_units` has answered `[]` for a premises with nothing shared since `CR-056` W1 task 9, so the whole change was the DELETION of metering's refusal and **zero new lines in `customers`**. ⚠ **ANCHORING ON A DWELLING WAS MEASURED AND REJECTED** — see `BR-MET-116`'s row for the three reasons, the load-bearing one being that it would put the developer's coverage on a unit a resident's own account must later occupy, which `BR-CUS-017` refuses. **The money is unmoved:** `1408 Elm` still bills `$41.20 + $28.40 = $69.60`. ⚠ **AND THE PER-UNIT PLAN GUARD THIS ROW INHERITS IS NARROWED THE SAME DAY:** `_require_plan_on_unit` no longer fires on a COMMON AREA (`BR-MET-040`, *"common area dont need rate plan as of now; make this optional"*), so a master over an unplanned hallway is created rather than refused. It goes on firing for a DWELLING, and `COMMON_AREA_REQUIRES_A_PLAN` is the one constant that decides. **A MASTER ACCOUNT IS AN ORDINARY `accounts` ROW, `kind='master'`, PAYER = THE PREMISES' OWNER/MANAGER, ~~ATTACHED TO THE PREMISES' COMMON-AREA UNITS THROUGH THE SAME ACCOUNT–UNIT LINKAGE EVERY ACCOUNT USES~~ **ATTACHED TO THE PREMISES ITSELF THROUGH `accounts.premises_id`** — NO NEW TABLE.** (PO ruling 2026-08-22, closing OQ-314's second half; PO verbatim: *"build it and also tell me how you will strore it can it go into account table as master accounts"* — the PO's own suggestion, confirmed as the design.) Worked figure, unchanged from BR-MET-101: **1408 Elm, 3 units + 2 common areas — MA-1408 covers both common areas and one bill to the owner carries $41.20 + $28.40 = $69.60.** **HOW THE SCHEMA EXPRESSES IT (lane judgment 2026-08-22, decision-logged):** `accounts.kind` is a NEW COLUMN (`regular` default · `master`), NOT a fifth `account_types` row — the platform catalog is the SIGNED four-code consumer-category vocabulary (B2 r5 #12 [D5], migration 0003: *"if they ever diverge, B2 r5 #12 [D5] is the tiebreaker"*), and a master account still HAS a consumer category (a residential building's shared hallway is residential); orthogonal facts get orthogonal columns. **HOW ONE ROW COVERS SEVERAL UNITS — ⚠ SUPERSEDED AT THIS SENTENCE 2026-09-01 (CR-056 W1 task 5): the master row now names the PREMISES (`accounts.premises_id`) and names NO unit at all (`accounts.unit_id` is NULLABLE from migration `customers 0036`), so every covered common area — the first included — is reached through `service_agreements`, and a common area is something the account is CHARGED FOR rather than the thing it hangs off. Master accounts created BEFORE that migration keep the anchor they were created with and bill exactly what they billed before it; the backfill only fills `premises_id` in.** The superseded text, kept because the pre-2026-09-01 rows still have this shape: `accounts.unit_id` holds exactly ONE unit (BR-CUS-017), so the master row ANCHORS on the premises' first common-area unit (lowest label, deterministic) and covers the REST through `service_agreements` over each common area's service points — the same rows `_open_service_agreements` writes for every account, which is what "the same account–unit linkage" buys: billing linkage with no new table. ⚠ **THE MASTER PATH ATTACHES ONLY COMMON-AREA UNITS AND REFUSES ANYTHING ELSE BY NAME** — an ordinary unit in the list, a unit of another premises, a deactivated unit, a common area with no plan (BR-MET-040), or one already occupied/covered (BR-CUS-017's family). `kind` is a REFUSED input on every other account path: the ONLY door to `kind='master'` is `POST /accounts/master`. ⚠ **WHAT THIS RULE DOES NOT BUY:** BR-BIL-013's balance-TRANSFER path stays un-offered — OQ-069's closure requires a LANDLORD-AGREEMENT FLAG that still has no column, so `apps.customers.selectors.master_billing_account_for_unit` is deliberately NOT published; and the screens (PRM-02 Common-areas tab's `MA-1408` cell, an Accounts-list kind chip) are the FRONTEND half, not built with this rule. r10's `MA-` number prefix is layout/copy; the account number comes from the tenant series (BR-TEN-035, B2 governs data). ⚠ **STAMPED 2026-08-22 — THE BILL-ASSEMBLY HALF IS BUILT AND THE WORKED FIGURE COMES OUT OF THE PRODUCT: one document, $41.20 + $28.40 = $69.60** (`apps/billing/gather.covered_units` prices every covered common area; `runs.readings_for_account` reads every covered meter; `tests/test_br_cus_068_master_bill.py` pins it and keeps the anchor-only inversion beside it). **Until that date the owner's bill carried the ANCHOR's services alone (`DEF-571`) and the run reported every other covered area as having nobody to bill (`DEF-572`).** ⚠ **THREE SHAPES THIS RULE DOES NOT DECIDE, AND THE BILL REFUSES BY NAME ON EACH RATHER THAN CHOOSING:** two covered areas taking the SAME service (`OQ-334` — base per connection $64.00 or per bill $52.00), covered areas on DIFFERENT plans (`OQ-335` — whose taxes, fees, discounts and plan snapshot: $0.00 / $1.39 / $0.57 on the same $69.60 bill), and a period holding a mid-period rate or plan boundary (`OQ-336` — $69.60 vs $70.62). A coverage of one plan and distinct services — the ruled worked case — bills unblocked. | PO 2026-08-22 (OQ-314 second half) · BR-MET-101 · decision log 2026-08-22 · **CR-056 R1 (PO verbatim) + R2 · PO 2026-08-31 · decision log 2026-09-01** | **CONFIRMED in its shape / SUPERSEDED in its anchoring (2026-09-01, → BR-CUS-1010)** | tests/test_br_cus_068_master_accounts.py · tests/test_br_cus_068_master_bill.py |
| BR-CUS-075 | **THE ACCOUNTS IMPORT TARGET SHIPS A SEEDED SYSTEM DEFAULT MAPPING TEMPLATE, EXACTLY AS THE THREE ASSETS TARGETS DO (PO ruling 2026-08-24).** PO verbatim, pointing at Settings → Reference data → Finance → **Import mappings** on a live tenant showing `0`: *"you might hav to add account import mapping tempalte to this settings"*, and — asked whether this was a precedent or a new idea — *"we did that for other improt also"*. This is the ruling BR-AST-048 was missing: that rule is written for the **assets** targets and names three defaults (Meters · Sub-meters · Devices), and CR-030 W1 removed a fourth it had briefly added precisely **because no rule named an accounts default** (decision log 2026-08-24, and the tell was that `test_the_three_defaults_seed_for_a_fresh_tenant` went red on `3 != 4` — a new row on a shipped Settings screen needs a rule behind it). The PO has now supplied it. **The MECHANISM is BR-AST-048's, unchanged and not re-litigated:** the template is **GENERATED from the same declared column contract that generates the CR-025 sample CSV** — never hand-written, so the sample's header row and the default template cannot drift (DEF-521's shape, prevented by construction); it is a system row under the reference-list convention (renamable, deactivatable, **never deletable**, never mutated — BR-TEN-036); a tenant's "Save mapping as template" writes a NEW tenant row (copy-on-write), so the seeded default stays the known-good path; and auto-detect order is unchanged (exact header match wins · tenant template beats system default · ties to most-recently-used). **Code:** `accounts_default` / *"Accounts default"*, appended LAST to `import_runs.DEFAULT_MAPPING_TEMPLATES` — that dict's order is the order the SET-04 list prints, so a new entry goes at the end rather than moving three rows on a screen a tenant already reads (the same lesson as `core.numbering.SERIES_CATALOG`, CR-030 W0). **What this rule does NOT extend to:** `premises` and `units` still have no default and this ruling does not give them one — they were not raised and a signature covers only what was shown. ⚠ **AND IT DOES NOT REACH A TENANT PROVISIONED BEFORE IT EXISTED BY ITSELF.** `import_mappings` is in tenancy's `RERUN_REFERENCE_LISTS`, so a version-sweep replay inserts what is missing — but only for a tenant carrying a `reference_seed_runs` row for that list (OQ-088's recorded opt-in: *"declined"* and *"did not exist yet"* must not be the same absence). A tenant with no such row receives nothing, and that is the ruled behaviour, not a bug. | PO 2026-08-24 (screenshot of SET-04 Finance → Import mappings at `0`, Silver Creek Water) · adopts BR-AST-048's mechanism · decision log 2026-08-24 | CONFIRMED | tests/test_cr028_mapping_templates.py (`test_the_three_defaults_seed_for_a_fresh_tenant`, `test_defaults_are_generated_not_hand_written`, `test_reseeding_is_idempotent_and_backfills`) |
| BR-CUS-076 | **ROLLING BACK AN ACCOUNT IMPORT REVERSES ITS OPENING JOURNAL ENTRIES WITH A MIRROR ENTRY DATED THE DAY THE ROLLBACK RUNS — NEVER BACK-DATED TO CUTOVER (PO ruling 2026-08-24, closing OQ-352 on reading (A) with its date settled).** ⚠ **The question OQ-352 asked was not whether a mirror is lawful — `posting.reverse_entry` exists and hard rule 5 is satisfied — but WHAT DATE IT CARRIES**, and the two candidate dates put the same money in different months. **What was put to the PO, in plain English with the figure:** account `A-12103` migrated on **31 Aug** carrying **$267.30** of receivables; someone rolls the import back on **15 Oct**. Dated at the rollback (the ruling): the $267.30 comes off the books on 15 Oct, September and early October still show it as owed — *which is true, it WAS on the books then* — and nobody reopens a month already closed and reported. Dated at cutover (rejected): the books read as though the $267.30 was never there, which rewrites a period that may already have gone to a board or a regulator, makes every September report retrospectively wrong, and **BR-TEN-038 would push the entry to the earliest OPEN period anyway**, so the back-date is not even reliably obtainable. **Also rejected: (B) refusing the rollback outright** — a utility that migrates 1,247 accounts from a bad file would have had no undo at all; **(C) rolling back the sub-ledger only** — closing the account and deleting its buckets while the entry stands, which breaks BR-LED-007's tie-out (AR GL = Σ account balances) silently and is what a naively written branch does; **(D) one reversing entry for the whole batch** — symmetric with how it went in, but it cannot express the PARTIAL rollback the billed check already creates, and partial is the normal case (an account already billed is KEPT under hard rule 4, so a real rollback is 1,246 reversed and one kept). **Mechanism:** ONE mirror per reversed account, DR opening-balance clearing / CR AR for that account's own opening total, posted through the ledger posting service (hard rule 5 — customers writes no journal line), dated the rollback day, and the original entry is untouched (hard rule 4 — corrections are new rows, never edits). An account KEPT by the billed check posts no mirror, because its receivable is real. Every mirror writes `audit_log`. | PO 2026-08-24 (OQ-352, four readings put with the $267.30 worked case) · BR-CUS-049 · BR-LED-007 · BR-TEN-038 · hard rules 4 and 5 | CONFIRMED | tests/test_cr030_w1_accounts_rollback.py |
| BR-CUS-077 | **A MIGRATED ACCOUNT THAT IS BOTH IN CREDIT AND IN ARREARS CARRIES BOTH, SEPARATELY — THE CREDIT AND THE AGED DEBT ARE NEVER NETTED (PO ruling 2026-08-24).** This settles the case BR-CUS-061 left open: that rule says an opening CREDIT balance imports as a carried account credit (BR-PAY-010 overpayment pattern) and that aging buckets stay debit-only, but it does not say what happens when ONE ROW carries both. **What was put to the PO, in plain English with the figure:** a customer with **$45.00** credit in the current period and **$121.00** owed from over 90 days ago. **The ruling:** the account shows **$121.00 in the over-90 ageing band** and separately holds a **$45.00 credit** that applies to the next bill. **Netting to $76.00 was REJECTED, and the reason is the reason the ageing bands exist:** netting loses the AGE of the debt — $76 sitting in one band when $121 is genuinely over ninety days old — and everything that keys off ageing (collections, disconnection notices, the `PAST DUE 60+` tile, BR-CUS-016's derived display) would then understate the problem on a real delinquent account. **Refusing the row was also rejected**: a large migration may carry thousands of these and each refusal is manual work in the utility's old system for a case the rules can now answer. **Consequence for the importer:** a negative bucket value is NOT written as a bucket row (BR-CUS-061's debit-only half stands) — it becomes the carried credit; the positive buckets load as buckets and age normally. A row carrying only a credit still loads. | PO 2026-08-24 · BR-CUS-061 · BR-CUS-048 · BR-CUS-016 · BR-PAY-010 | CONFIRMED | tests/test_def620_opening_credits.py |
| BR-CUS-078 | **A CARRIED OPENING CREDIT POSTS DR OPENING-BALANCE CLEARING / CR CUSTOMER CREDIT, IN ITS OWN ENTRY — NEVER INSIDE THE CUTOVER DEBIT BATCH (PO 2026-08-24, closing OQ-353).** BR-CUS-061 makes an opening credit a carried **account credit** rather than a receivable, so BR-CUS-049's `AR ↔ opening-balance clearing` shape does not fit it: the money is not owed to the utility. The PO ruled the credit leg verbatim — *"keep it as customer credit"* — so it books to the customer-credit position the BR-PAY-010 overpayment pattern already uses, and the debit leg is opening-balance clearing, the mirror of the debit entry's credit leg. ⚠ **AND IT IS A SEPARATE ENTRY, WHICH THE PO DID NOT RULE AND THE FROZEN CASE REQUIRES.** Posting it inside the cutover batch would DEBIT clearing in a batch whose ❄️ FROZEN GT-16 line asserts `Σ clearing credits = Σ imported buckets` (batch **$184,320.55** across 1,213 accounts); changing that identity is a **finance re-sign via HUMAN-GUIDE §6, not an inline edit**, and no re-sign was given. So the credit posts in its own entry, GT-16's figures are untouched, and a migration that carries both debits and credits produces two balanced entries rather than one unbalanced batch. ⚠ **BR-PAY-010's WRITER DOES NOT EXIST YET:** `payments.record_payment` hardcodes `type='payment'` and books a cash leg, and at cutover no cash is received — so `payments.services.record_opening_credit` is an owed story, and until it lands a carried credit is measured, audited (`account.opening_credit_imported`) and reported, never silently materialised. | PO 2026-08-24 (OQ-353) · BR-CUS-061 · BR-CUS-077 · BR-PAY-010 · BR-CUS-049 · GT-16 ❄️ | CONFIRMED | tests/test_def620_opening_credits.py |
| BR-CUS-079 | **SUBMITTING A SERVICE REQUEST SENDS THE CUSTOMER AN ACKNOWLEDGEMENT BY EMAIL OR SMS (PO 2026-08-24, settling CR-031 §3).** The PO's clarification, verbatim: *"when you take and submit SR - we have to send email or text messge to customer - im talimg about that"*. ⚠ **THIS IS NARROWER THAN EITHER READING CR-031 §3 OFFERED**, and the difference matters: it is **not** a general rule that every notice about an application is filed against the SR. One acknowledgement, at submission, to the applicant, carrying the SR number. Nothing here says what happens to notices sent later during a move-in or a new connect — that remains unruled and must not be assumed. **The acknowledgement is a NOTIFICATION, so it is subject to the same absence rule as every other:** no delivery adapter exists in this product yet (see BR-CUS-072's neighbours and the `LAST NOTICE` posture in W7 task 7.10), so the send must render its own absence rather than fabricate a `sent` state — hard-won law 1. ⚠ **BUILT 2026-09-15, AND IT HAD NEVER ONCE WORKED (`DEF-CUS-5391`).** The code to honour this rule was written the same week it was confirmed — `submit` calls `send_acknowledgement`, which asks the queue for a `service_request` message — and `service_request` was not one of the kinds the platform queued, so **every call in three weeks came back refused and not one applicant was told their request had arrived.** The kind exists now and the acknowledgement reaches the queue. ⚠ **EMAIL ONLY**, which narrows this rule's *"email or SMS"* in practice and is named rather than hidden: the wording is written as an email and an SMS carries 160 characters, so an applicant who gave a phone and no email is recorded as `applicant_supplied_no_email_address` and gets nothing. Writing the SMS wording is real work and is not done. **The absence half of this rule is untouched** — there is still no delivery adapter on any channel (`BR-NOT-013`), so QUEUED remains the truest thing the product can say and `sent` is still never written. | PO 2026-08-24 (CR-031 §3) · CR-031 statement 4 | CONFIRMED | W4 · built `CR-094-BRC01` lane C |
| BR-CUS-080 | **A SERVICE REQUEST AND THE MOVE-IN OR NEW-CONNECT IT CREATES ARE LINKED BY `origin_service_request_id` ON THE CHILD; THE SR KEEPS `outcome` AND `outcome_ref`; DOCUMENTS MOVE BY REFERENCE, NOT BY COPY (PO 2026-08-24, closing CR-031 §5).** Ratified in the PO's own words: *"origin_service_request_id; SR keeps outcome + outcome_ref so it alone answers 'what happened to this application'; documents move by reference, not copy"*. Consequences, each load-bearing: **the child points at the parent**, so a child is never orphaned and one SR cannot silently produce two live children for one decision; **the SR alone answers *"what happened to this application"*** without scanning two other tables, which is what makes a refused, a moved-in and a connected application all readable from one row; and **documents move by reference**, so BR-CUS-073's *"carry over to the account and are not re-uploaded"* holds literally — one uploaded file stays ONE row and the audit trail of who supplied it survives the hand-off, where a copy would fork it. ⚠ **THE FOURTH CLAUSE OF THE PROPOSAL WAS NOT QUOTED BACK AND IS NOT RULED:** that SAVE AND EXIT is a `draft` state on the CHILD rather than a fourth outcome on the SR. The REQUIREMENT it serves IS ruled — *"all these request can happen in 1 go or dur to pratical cases only step will be executed in such case we have to give save and exit opition t utility clark"* — so save-and-exit is built; the draft-on-child mechanism remains an engineering judgment, chosen because it makes "all in one go" and "one step at a time" the same code path instead of two. | PO 2026-08-24 (CR-031 §5) · BR-CUS-073 · CR-031 statements 6–9 | CONFIRMED | W4 |
| BR-CUS-081 | **A CLOSING BALANCE THE DEPOSIT MORE THAN COVERS IS SETTLED FROM THE DEPOSIT AND THE DIFFERENCE IS REFUNDED — THE BILL DOES NOT STAND (PO 2026-08-24, closing OQ-347).** Worked case as put to the PO: deposit **$153.50**, owed **$142.30** → settle the $142.30 and refund **$11.20**. The rejected reading was to refund the whole $153.50 and leave the $142.30 outstanding, which would send a final bill to a customer whose own money the utility is simultaneously returning. Settlement and refund are BOTH money actions and both write `audit_log`; the settlement is a new row, never an edit of the issued bill (hard rule 4). | PO 2026-08-24 (OQ-347) · BR-CUS-026 · hard rules 4 and 7 | CONFIRMED | W7 |
| BR-CUS-082 | ✅ **CONFIRMED AND WIDENED 2026-10-12 (PO): move-out never writes the debt off — *"we dont do write off at the time of move out; these charges remain outstanding on the account and followup is done as per outstanding rules"*.** Write-off is not a move-out step-4 path; a later write-off goes through Payments under BR-PAY-020. **A DEBT SURVIVING ACCOUNT CLOSURE IS STILL CHASED, AND THE DUNNING LADDER RESTARTS FROM TIER 1 (PO 2026-08-24, closing OQ-346).** Worked case: a customer leaves owing **$175.20** more than their deposit covers. Today BR-CUS-030 stops dunning at close, so that shortfall is recorded and pursued by nothing. The PO ruled *"(b) continue, ladder restarts"* — the ladder does **not** resume from whatever tier it had reached before closure. ⚠ **THIS AMENDS BR-CUS-030**, whose stop-at-close clause is superseded for the closed-with-balance case; the amendment is dated here and BR-CUS-030 carries its supersedes line. **What restarting means and why it is not merely gentler:** the tiers, their intervals and their protections all apply again from the beginning, so a closed account cannot be escalated to disconnection — there is nothing left to disconnect — and the ladder for a closed account is a collections sequence, not a service one. W7 owns the shape. ⚠⚠ **AND AS OF 2026-08-27 THE PAIR RESOLVES ITSELF — `OQ-366` IS CLOSED AND THE LADDER CHASES THE MASTER (PO ruling).** PO verbatim: *"Sub account is closed when debt is trasnfer hence we now follow with owner for debt"*. **The reasoning is STRUCTURAL, not a balance of fairness:** the sub-account is **closed** at the moment the residue transfers, so there is no open account on the tenant side to chase at all — which also disposes of the *both, differently* reading, because a second ladder would have to run against a closed account and this product does not do that. **Worked, end to end, on the case both rules were written against:** `A-11024 · Ridge Coffee Co.` closes owing **$328.70**, deposit and interest cover **$153.50**, **$175.20** survives, moves to the master, and **TIER 1 fires on the MASTER's account**. ⚠ **IT INHERITS THE SAME DAY'S QUALIFICATION AND THE TWO MUST BE READ TOGETHER:** the transfer is clerk-initiated (BR-CUS-097 as qualified), so the ladder follows the master **only for a residue a clerk actually tied back** — a residue nobody tied back does not silently become the landlord's problem and therefore does not generate the landlord's letters either. ⚠ **ONE FOLLOW-ON IS RAISED AND NOT ANSWERED (`OQ-580`):** if the master is already at TIER 3 on its own arrears, does an arriving residue RESTART its ladder to Tier 1, JOIN its existing position, or run as its OWN sequence? Not put to the PO; **no fixture may answer it by existing.** | PO 2026-08-24 (OQ-346) · BR-CUS-030 (amended) | CONFIRMED | W7 |
| BR-CUS-083 | **CONNECTION CHARGES ON A MULTI-UNIT BUILD ARE INVOICED TO THE DEVELOPER THROUGH A MASTER ACCOUNT (PO 2026-08-24, closing OQ-349).** Worked case, and it is the seed fixture: `SR-26-0043` → `CON-26-0117`, 500 Main Street, **25 units**, raising **$14,390.00**. The PO ruled *"a) the developer, via a master account"*, so BR-CUS-068's master account becomes the payer and the charges do **not** attach to the first account opened on each unit. Consequence worth stating before W6 builds it: the master account exists before any resident account does, so the connection charge is raised against a party who may never take service at the premises — which is exactly why it needs a master account rather than a resident one. | PO 2026-08-24 (OQ-349) · BR-CUS-068 | CONFIRMED | W6 |
| BR-CUS-090 | **THE COUNTER ACCEPTS WHAT THE CUSTOMER HANDS OVER; A SHORTFALL AGAINST THE QUOTED FEES IS A FINANCE RECONCILIATION ITEM, NEVER A REFUSAL AT THE DESK (PO ruling 2026-08-24, settling DEF-637 fault 1).** PO verbatim: *"we acept it and in finance we will reconcile ; here segregation of responsibilitys, accept it and mark it as cash"*. **Three clauses, and the third is a copy fix the same ruling forces.** **(1) The clerk is never blocked by a mismatch.** The request records `fee_quoted`, `fee_collected`, `method` and ONE receipt number; `fee_collected < fee_quoted` is a lawful, storable state and the difference stays an **open fee balance** on the request. It does not silently become a waiver — a waiver is BR-CUS-073's explicit act with a reason, and this is not that. **(2) The two roles are separate, which is the reason the PO gave.** The person who takes the money records it; finance agrees it against the cash count. So the request never adjusts its own quote to match the cash, and finance never reaches into the request to change what the clerk recorded — the reconciliation is a finance act against both numbers. **Worked, on the screen that raised the defect:** quoted **$130.00** (CC-REQFEE $25.00 + CC-EXPED $60.00 + CC-METERT $45.00), taken **$25.00** cash, receipt `RCP-26-4471` → the request shows **$25.00 collected of $130.00 quoted · $105.00 outstanding**, and the review step reads that outstanding figure rather than assuming the fee is settled. **(3) THE ACKNOWLEDGEMENT MAY NOT SAY *"Nothing is charged"* WHEN MONEY WAS TAKEN.** The acknowledgement BR-CUS-079 sends currently prints *"Nothing is charged and no service changes until we have confirmed what is needed"* directly beneath a panel confirming a cash fee was collected. Where a fee WAS taken the sentence states the amount, the method and the receipt number; where none was, it may stand. It is the FIRST thing an applicant receives and it is customer-facing, so a false sentence there is a money defect, not a copy nit. ⚠ **CLAUSE (3) BUILT 2026-09-15, AND NOT AS A SENTENCE SOMEBODY HAS TO REMEMBER.** A letter template has no *if* in it, so one acknowledgement class could only ever carry one of the two sentences and the other would be a rule waiting to be broken. There are **two** classes now, and the one whose wording states the amount, the method and the receipt number is chosen when and only when a receipt exists. The class that says *nothing is charged* has **no money token in its vocabulary at all**, so a utility editing its own wording cannot put an amount there and the product cannot reach it over money taken. Worked: `$25.00` cash against receipt `RCP-26-4471` queues the letter reading **`25.00 USD`** and the receipt number — two decimals in the letter, four in the database (hard rule 2).  ⛔⛔⛔ **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-24 FOR APPLICATION FEES — THE DESK HALF TOO: THE COUNTER TAKES THE WHOLE FEE OR THE FEE IS WAIVED; A SHORT PAYMENT IS REFUSED (`OQ-CUS-12624`).** PO, verbatim: *"p4 - i already calrified this - all or waived - why you ask again and again - are we missing something erhe?"*, restating `OQ-CUS-12617`'s *"either fee is paid or waived - no other condition"*. Read by date, it supersedes clause (1)'s *"we acept it and in finance we will reconcile"* for an application fee: a part-paid fee is the one state that sentence rules out, and a short payment is the only way to reach it. Worked: $130.00 quoted, $25.00 offered → refused, *"Take the full $130.00, or waive it."* ⛔⛔ **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-23 — THE 2026-09-21 NARROWING BELOW NO LONGER HOLDS: APPROVAL WAITS UNTIL THE FEE IS PAID IN FULL OR WAIVED (`OQ-CUS-12617`).** PO, verbatim: *"there are no unpaid fees either applcition fee is paid or it is waived - so this is not applicable anymore"*, then *"we already told you - either fee is paid or waived - no other condition"*. **The desk half of clause (1) STANDS** — the counter still accepts a short payment and is never refused; what returns is the gate at APPROVAL, on the open balance: paid in full → clear · waived → clear · no fee lines → no condition · part-paid → waits on the remainder. Worked on this row's own figures: quoted **$130.00**, taken **$25.00** → Approve is off and reads *$105.00 unpaid*; take or waive the $105.00 and it approves. Built by lane APPROVE-CHECKS. ⛔ **CLAUSE (1) IS NARROWED 2026-09-21 AND THE NARROWING IS THE WHOLE OF WHAT MOVED — PO, verbatim: *"make application fees optional - so that we can approve it"*.** This row said the open fee balance **blocks APPROVAL** and nothing else; it now **blocks nothing and is REPORTED**. The fee is optional: an application may be approved with its fee unpaid, part-paid or waived. ⚠ **READ IT NARROWLY — THE MONEY IS UNTOUCHED.** It does not abolish the fee, cancel what was collected or hide anything: the quote is still quoted, the collection still collected, the open balance still totalled and still printed on the request AFTER the approval, and a shortfall still never becomes a waiver (clause (1)'s own sentence, which stands). **Worked, on the same figures:** quoted **$130.00**, taken **$25.00**, receipt `RCP-26-4471` → the review step reports **$105.00 outstanding**, names it, and the request approves; before 2026-09-21 it could not be approved at all and neither door out of it worked (`DEF-CUS-6096`, `DEF-CUS-6097`).
