The condition's STATE stays `blocked` and only `blocking` goes False — exactly the shape the documents ruling of 2026-09-16 took, because `Condition.clear` reads the state and calling this clear would print *"4 of 4 conditions clear"* over an open balance. **Not mandatory is not the same as not mentioned.** ⚠ **AND THIS CLOSES `DEF-CUS-5399`**, the two signed screens that disagreed: `REQ-03.5`'s *"the review step lists the unpaid fee as a blocking condition … it simply cannot be approved yet"* is **SUPERSEDED in its second sentence**, and the Accounts blueprint p083's *"The money does not gate approval and the documents do"* is what the product now does. ⚠ **AND THE PART-PAYMENT CASE THAT PROMPTED IT WAS OURS, NOT THE WORLD'S — PO the same day: *"in practical this does not ahppen - it is application fees - customer will pay it once or clark will wavie it off - $20 or $0 - some utilities will not take applciation fees also"*.** Three shapes and only three: **paid once**, **waived**, or **no fee at all**. Clause (1) still holds for the shortfall it describes — it is simply rarer than the row's worked example implies. **The third shape is a REQUIREMENT THIS ROW DOES NOT YET MEET:** a utility configured to take no application fee must meet no fee screen, and **no per-tenant application-fee setting exists** — `$0` is the absence of an act, not a configured posture (`DEF-CUS-6102`, OPEN). ⛔ **THAT LAST SENTENCE IS SUPERSEDED 2026-10-06 — THE PO RULED THAT THERE IS NO SETTING AND THE FEE IS ADDED AUTOMATICALLY (`DEF-CUS-9819`).** PO, verbatim, offered a Settings switch *"We charge an application fee: Yes/No"*: *"No - we wil ahve application fees as zero and pprovide option to waive off"*; then, asked whether every new request should get the fee automatically: *"Should the application fee be added to every request automatically? I recommend (a)."* So **the posture IS a price**: the utility marks one one-time charge code as its application fee (`charges.is_application_fee`), every new request — staff capture, the counter, a portal Start service application — arrives with that code's line at its live price, and a utility that charges nothing prices the code at `$0.00`. A request owing `$0.00` reads *Nothing to collect.* and offers no *Record what was handed over*; the per-line waive stays. **Worked:** Kyle Mark's portal request arrives with *Application fee $10.00* already on it and the clerk takes it or waives it; at a `$0.00` utility the request reads *Nothing to collect* with no collect button. The approval half above is unchanged — paid or waived, part-paid blocks, and a `$0.00` line owes nothing. ⊕ **AMENDED 2026-10-23 FOR PORTAL APPLICATIONS ONLY (`CR-191-BRC10`).** PO, verbatim: *"if there is a application or service fee then consumer will have to pay it online; they can't say pay later"* and *"That is application fee it is not refunded"*. A portal application whose fee is above `$0.00` is paid by card on its last step and is created only when the payment succeeds; the line is then collected in full (method `card`, receipt number = the request number) and earned at once, so approval asks nothing. **Worked:** `$10.00` → collected `$10.00`, outstanding `$0.00`. Staff-entered requests are unchanged. | PO 2026-08-24 (DEF-637) · **PO 2026-09-21 (clause (1) narrowed; decision log, two rows of that date)** · BR-CUS-073 · BR-CUS-079 · `DEF-CUS-5399` · `DEF-CUS-6096` · `DEF-CUS-6097` · `DEF-CUS-6102` | **CONFIRMED AS AMENDED 2026-09-21** — clauses (2) and (3) untouched; clause (1)'s *blocks approval* is SUPERSEDED by the PO's own words and the fee no longer gates the decision | W4 · clause (3) built `CR-094-BRC01` lane C · the 2026-09-21 narrowing built by lane APP-8 (`06-backend/tests/test_po20260921_application_fee_optional.py`, `10-scripts/po20260921_application_fee_walkthrough.mjs`) |
| BR-CUS-091 | **A CUSTOMER MAY REQUEST A DISCONNECT, SO THE SERVICE REQUEST IS THE FRONT DOOR FOR COLLECTIONS-INITIATED WORK AS WELL AS CUSTOMER-INITIATED WORK (PO ruling 2026-08-24, settling DEF-638 fault 1).** PO verbatim: *"Yes customer can request it"*. Two artefacts disagreed and this says which was right: the Requests list draws `SR-26-0048 · DISCONNECT · Ridge Coffee Co. · 500 Main` under `Under review` — **that is correct** — while REQ-02's decision panel disables Disconnect with the reason *"A customer asking to end service is a move-out … a disconnect leaves the account open and accruing, and is only ever started by collections, never from a customer request"*. **That reason is withdrawn and the control is enabled.** ⚠ **What the withdrawn reason got RIGHT is still true and must survive the change:** a disconnect and a move-out are **different outcomes** — a move-out closes the account and settles the deposit (BR-CUS-030), a disconnect leaves it open and accruing (BR-MET-105's base charge keeps running). So the capture form must not quietly convert one into the other; where an applicant describes ending service, the clerk picks the outcome and the request records which was picked, exactly as `decide_outcome` already does for every other type. **The consequence to design against:** a request may now ORIGINATE from staff (collections) rather than from an applicant, so `channel` must distinguish them and the applicant-identity fields are not required on a staff-raised disconnect. ⚠⚠ **SUPERSEDED IN PART 2026-09-22 — THE APPROVE MODAL NO LONGER OFFERS MOVE-OUT, TRANSFER OR DISCONNECT AT ALL.** PO ruling, verbatim: *"Option (b) — remove all three permanently"*, answering a lettered table that set out (a) *hide an outcome that cannot apply to this request* against (b) *remove move-out, transfer and disconnect from the modal for good*. **So the sentence above — *"That reason is withdrawn and the control is enabled"* — IS ITSELF WITHDRAWN**, and `REQ-02`'s *What kind* list is `A new connection · A move-in`, on every request, not greyed. **THE COST WAS SHOWN TO THE PO BEFORE THEY ANSWERED**, with two real requests side by side — John Doe at 77 Foundry Row Unit 1 (empty unit, no live account) and Ridge Coffee Co. at 500 Main St (live account, 90 days in arrears, collections wanting it cut) — and they were told that under (b) collections can no longer start a disconnect from a request and a clerk can no longer close an account as a move-out from here. They chose it knowing that. ⚠ **EVERY OTHER CLAUSE OF THIS ROW STANDS, AND THE ONE THAT MATTERS MOST IS THE SECOND:** a disconnect and a move-out are still DIFFERENT OUTCOMES and the capture form still *"must not quietly convert one into the other"*. **Removing the place where that choice was made does not remove the need to make it**, so each of the three has its own door and all three were walked before this was written: a TRANSFER starts at `/transfers/new` (the Accounts page's Transfers tab carries `+ New transfer` as its one primary); a DISCONNECT starts at `/disconnects/new` (same, on the Disconnects tab); a MOVE-OUT starts on the ACCOUNT — `/accounts/{id}/move-out` — because the move-out endpoints are account-scoped and a header with no row selected would have to guess which account was meant. **No capability is lost and none is orphaned.** ⚠ **THE VOCABULARY IS UNTOUCHED.** All five values are still lawful, `decide_outcome` still accepts all five, every stored `outcome` still renders, and the tenant's document checklist (`ServiceRequestDocumentSettingsPage`, `BR-CUS-095`) still carries a column per request type — cutting three values out of the shared constant would have deleted three columns of that grid with no ruling behind it, so the modal reads a constant of its own (`APPROVE_OUTCOMES`). ⚠ **AND THE STAFF-RAISED-DISCONNECT CONSEQUENCE IN THE LAST SENTENCE ABOVE IS NOW DEAD LETTER FOR THIS PATH** — a disconnect cannot be the outcome of a request any more, so nothing raises one here for `channel` to distinguish. The `channel` column and the staff-raised draft (`BR-CUS-069`, a draft from NOTHING on a staff channel) are unaffected and are used by other work. | PO 2026-08-24 (DEF-638) · **PO 2026-09-22 (decision log, *TWO PO ANSWERS*)** · BR-CUS-030 · BR-MET-105 | **CONFIRMED-AS-AMENDED (the disconnect/move-out distinction stands; the modal control is removed, PO 2026-09-22)** | W4 · W7 |
| BR-CUS-092 | 🔒 **THE SSN AND THE DRIVER'S LICENCE ARE CAPTURED, ENCRYPTED AT THE APPLICATION LAYER, AND SHOWN AS THE LAST FOUR DIGITS ONLY — AND NO LAW IS BEING OVERRIDDEN TO DO IT (PO ruling 2026-08-24, settling DEF-632).** PO verbatim: *"we have to take SSn and Driver lience and follow PII rules - if you ahve to override any law - please do Im permitting it"*. ⚠ **The permission is recorded and DECLINED as unnecessary, because accepting it would misrepresent the position.** `OQ-016` is already **CLOSED** — *"app-layer encryption for high-sensitivity columns, disk for rest (PO R6)"*, B2 §6 — so this ruling does not reverse a rule; it asks for the rule to finally be **implemented**. **What exists today:** nothing. A grep for `def encrypt` / `def decrypt` across `06-backend` returns only integration and notification SECRETS, never customer PII, and the gap is self-documented in the tree — `apps/tenancy/services.py:48`: *"ein stored as plain 9 digits for now — the OQ-016 ruling (app-layer encryption)"*. A tax identifier is ALREADY stored in plaintext against a closed ruling; an SSN is the same shape with worse consequences. **So the rule is:** (a) both identifiers are captured on the service request; (b) they are written through the app-layer encryptor OQ-016 requires — which must be BUILT, and `tenancy.ein` migrates onto it as the second caller, because a helper with one caller is how the first one was skipped; (c) every read path renders **last four only** and the full value is never in a list response, an export, a log line or an audit `details` blob; (d) the screen may only claim *"stored encrypted"* once (b) is live — until then the claim comes OFF the screen, because a false security promise to a customer over a government identifier is worse than an absent one (hard-won law 1). **This is a 🔒 sensitive path**: the encryptor file carries `HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING` and a row in `12-governance/09-human-review-ledger.md` naming what was shown, and reaches RATIFIED only on a recorded sign-off. ✅ **BUILT 2026-08-27 FOR THE SOCIAL SECURITY NUMBER, AND THE SCREEN'S PROMISE IS NOW TRUE — DEF-632 CLOSED.** PO verbatim: *"We tell customers their Social Security number is stored encrypted - Verify and keep the promise ... We need to capture SSN and encript it; we have to ensure it is working"*. Clause (b)'s precondition is met (`core/crypto.py`, AES-256-GCM, a fresh 96-bit nonce per write, AAD binding a ciphertext to its column); `ServiceRequest.ssn` is an `EncryptedTextField`, `ssn` moved from `IDENTIFIER_FIELDS` into `CAPTURE_FIELDS`, `service_request_reads.applicant_tab` serves `identifier.ssn_last4` only, `_audit` masks at the boundary, and clause (d)'s hold on the *"stored encrypted"* sentence is LIFTED for this field. Proofs in `06-backend/tests/test_br_cus_092_ssn_capture.py` — the column is read back RAW through a second cursor and contains no spelling of the digits; the list, detail and `dumpdata` payloads carry `6789` and nothing more; the audit sink carries `•••••6789`; a wrong key RAISES. ✅ **CLAUSE (a) IS FINISHED — PO RULING 2026-09-15, BUILT THE SAME DAY (`customers 0050`).** *"ensure user is able to inputs both these on step 1 - Date of birth and driver's licence on step 1"*. `service_requests.drivers_licence` is an `EncryptedTextField` with `drivers_licence_bidx` beside it and `service_requests.date_of_birth` is a plain `date`; `service_request_reads` serves `identifier.drivers_licence_last4` and the date of birth in full; `_audit` masks the licence; `identity_match.evidence_from_service_request` lifts all three facts, which is what makes `BR-CUS-089` assessable on a request for the first time. **ELEVEN identifier names still answer `BIZ_STATE`** and six spellings of a name this table DOES have are refused as aliases with the canonical spelling in the message. ⚠ **AND THE SENTENCE THIS CLAUSE REPLACED WAS WRONG IN ONE WORD FOR TWO WEEKS (`DEF-CUS-5410`)** — it read *"twelve identifier field names still answer `BIZ_STATE`"* and named the licence among them; no spelling of the licence was ever in `IDENTIFIER_FIELDS`, so it answered *"unknown field"* instead. The distinction is the whole point of that list. ⚠ **THERE IS NO `ssn_last4` COLUMN** — the last four is a read projection; a stored one would put four of the nine digits back in the clear. ⚠ **NO BLIND INDEX ON `service_requests.ssn`** — that column still has none, so it cannot be searched. **`service_requests.drivers_licence` HAS one** (`drivers_licence_bidx`, 2026-09-15), as `customers.ssn`, `customers.driving_licence` and `customers.phone` have since `customers 0036`. Whether the SSN gains one is the lane that needs it; a token nothing queries is surface without a caller. Two questions were minted rather than answered: **OQ-780** (does this fold into B19 §14.1's one typed identifier column?) and **OQ-781** (is a nine-digit format check wanted, and would it refuse an ITIN?). 🔒 `HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING`. | PO 2026-08-24 (DEF-632) · **OQ-016 (CLOSED, PO R6)** · B2 §6 | CONFIRMED | W4 |
| BR-CUS-093 | **A SERVICE REQUEST IS NUMBERED THE MOMENT IT IS CREATED — A DRAFT CARRIES ITS NUMBER, AND ABANDONED DRAFTS LEAVE GAPS IN THE SERIES (PO ruling 2026-08-24, closing DEF-634).** The PO was shown three readings and chose *"at creation — every draft is numbered"*, over numbering-at-submit and over the screen's own claim that the number arrives when the fee is collected. **The reason the PO's option gave, and it is the requirement:** the clerk can quote the number to the customer on the phone while the form is still half-filled. **Worked:** a clerk opens the form on Tuesday, the request is `SR-26-0048` from that moment, they read it out, the applicant rings back Thursday quoting it, the clerk finds it in `Draft` and finishes it. **Consequences, all of them accepted with the ruling:** **(a) GAPS ARE NORMAL AND MUST NOT BE TREATED AS LOSS** — an abandoned draft burns its number permanently; nothing renumbers, backfills or reuses, and no report may present a gap as a missing record. **(b) THE FOOTER COPY IS NOW FALSE AND CHANGES**: every step reads *"Step N of 6 · nothing is created until you submit"* and something IS created — it becomes *"Step N of 6 · saved as draft SR-26-0048"*, naming the number the clerk can quote. **(c) THE HEADER COPY IS FALSE TOO** — *"the request is created and numbered when the fee is collected"* comes out entirely. **(d) B18's PROPOSED `BR-CUS-069` (*numbered at submission, not at draft*) IS SUPERSEDED BEFORE RATIFICATION** and must be restated wherever it is cited. **(e) IDEMPOTENCY GETS SIMPLER, NOT HARDER (hard rule 6):** `submit` no longer allocates anything, so a repeated submit is a state transition on a row that already has its identity — the idempotency key is the request itself. **(f) THE COLLECT-LATER INVOICE HAS SOMETHING TO ATTACH TO from the first keystroke**, which was the sharpest edge in the defect: a $130.00 fee invoice raised before the request existed had no parent. | PO 2026-08-24 (DEF-634) · supersedes proposed BR-CUS-069 | CONFIRMED | W4 |
| BR-CUS-094 | **THE CONSUMER CATEGORY VOCABULARY EXPANDS FROM FOUR TO SEVEN, AND `government` BECOMES A LEGACY CODE NO NEW RECORD MAY PICK (PO ruling 2026-08-24, closing DEF-635).** The PO was shown that the four are a **signed** vocabulary (B2 r5 #12) that plans, deposits and tax treatment key off, and chose *"Expand the signed list to seven"* — so Agricultural, Institutional, Municipal and Builder-or-developer become REAL categories a plan can be priced for, not display labels. **The seven are:** Residential · Commercial · Industrial · Agricultural · Institutional · Municipal · Builder or developer. ⚠ **`government` IS NOT ONE OF THE SEVEN, AND THIS IS THE PART THE RULING COULD NOT SETTLE FOR US.** The seeded fourth code is `government`, and **Municipal and Institutional are both plausible readings of it** — a city water department and a state university are different things and the stored value cannot tell them apart. **So no row is re-labelled by guess.** The migration ADDS the four new codes and marks `government` **legacy**: it stops being selectable on any form, every existing record keeps it and keeps billing exactly as it does today, and a settings screen lists them for a human to re-point. **Nothing breaks while they sit there** — a legacy code is still a valid FK, still filterable, still priceable. **Re-labelling is a human act per record, and it is now a tracked task, not an assumption.** ⚠ **This CHANGES A SIGNED VOCABULARY**, so it carries its supersedes line to B2 r5 #12 and to `customers/migrations/0003_seed_account_types.py`, and the seed is additive — the four existing rows are never rewritten. | PO 2026-08-24 (DEF-635) · **supersedes B2 r5 #12's four-code list** | ⚠ **CONFIRMED AS TO THE SEVEN · SUPERSEDED AS TO THE PLATFORM POSTURE — PO ruling 2026-09-17, verbatim: *"utility can get their own account types"* (`BR-CUS-1079`).** The seven and the legacy treatment of `government` STAND and are untouched; what is reversed is that they were *the* vocabulary. They are now the **SEED** a new tenant's list starts from, `account_types` carries `tenant_id` with RLS `FORCE` (`customers.0054`/`0055`), and a utility may add, rename, deactivate and delete its own rows on S8. **Do not read this rule as a closed list.** | W4 |
| BR-CUS-095 | **EACH UTILITY DECIDES WHICH DOCUMENTS ARE COMPULSORY, PER REQUEST TYPE, FROM A SETTINGS CHECKLIST THAT SHIPS ENTIRELY UNTICKED (PO ruling 2026-08-24, closing OQ-351).** PO verbatim, answering the one half of the question only they could: *"who decides which of those eight are compulsory? If the answer is each utility decides for itself, then Settings grows a checklist — for each request type, tick which documents are required. We ship with nothing ticked, and a utility that configures nothing blocks nothing."* This is reading **(A)**, the tenant-set checklist on `service_request_form_templates`; the fixed-list reading **(B)** is rejected, and the reason the PO gave is the design constraint: **the product ships with NO OPINION about any jurisdiction's law.** **The eight types stay as designed** — proof of identity, proof of address or occupancy, tenancy agreement or deed, authority to act, site plan or unit schedule, assistance evidence, signed service agreement, completion paperwork — and the checklist is `(request_type × document_type) → required`, tenant-scoped like every other setting. **THE DEFAULT IS THE RULING'S SHARPEST CLAUSE AND MUST NOT BE SOFTENED: nothing is ticked, so a utility that never opens the screen never has a request blocked.** No helpful pre-tick of *proof of identity*, however reasonable it looks — a product that blocks approvals out of the box for a rule its customer never chose is worse than one that blocks none. **This joins, and does not replace, the behaviour half already ruled (B19 §5): attaching never blocks CREATION; an unverified required document blocks APPROVAL. Two gates.** ⚠⚠ **SUPERSEDED IN ITS SECOND HALF ON 2026-09-16 — `BR-CUS-1078`, PO verbatim: *"lets not make it mandetory"*. AN UNVERIFIED REQUIRED DOCUMENT NO LONGER BLOCKS APPROVAL.** The sentence above, and the worked example below it — *"`approve` refuses by name until both are attached and verified"* — are **no longer true and must not be shipped**; they are left standing rather than deleted because they are quoted in the code and in B19, and a reader who followed them needs to find out that they moved. ⚠ **EVERY OTHER CLAUSE OF THIS RULE SURVIVES INTACT AND IS LOAD-BEARING:** each utility still decides, per request type, from a checklist that **ships entirely unticked**; the eight types are unchanged; and the checklist is still what the product reads to say WHICH documents are outstanding. What changed is only what happens when one is — the clerk is told instead of refused. **Worked, end to end:** a utility ticks *proof of identity* and *signed service agreement* for MOVE-IN only → a move-in request submits and sits happily in `Under review` with neither attached, and `approve` refuses by name until both are attached **and verified**; the same tenant's TRANSFER requests tick nothing and approve with no documents at all. ✅ **THIS UNBLOCKS THE SEED**, which was the only thing OQ-351 was actually blocking: the demo fixture can now show a request sitting in `More info needed` and say what it is waiting for. | PO 2026-08-24 (OQ-351) · B19 §5 · design B18 §5.3.6 | CONFIRMED | W4 |
| BR-CUS-096 | ⏸ **SUSPENDED 2026-10-11 (PO): while nothing records a disputed amount, no dispute is open and the excess is refunded** — PO, verbatim: *"today we are not handling any disppute case not recodingt he dispute anywhere. hence dispute check doe snot work, I think we should remove it fo time being and process the refund"* (decision log of that date). The hold below returns unchanged the day a dispute store is published. ⏸ **AN OPEN DISPUTE HOLDS THE EXCESS — `BR-CUS-081` GAINS A CONDITION (PO 2026-08-24, closing DEF-653).** PO verbatim: *"hold until dispute close"*. Where the deposit is worth more than everything owed, BR-CUS-081 settles and refunds the difference — **unless any part of the outstanding amount is DISPUTED, in which case the excess is HELD until the dispute closes**, because the amount owed can still move. **Worked, the case put to the PO:** deposit + interest **$153.50** against everything outstanding **$140.38** leaves **$13.12**, and **$48.00** of that $140.38 is disputed — so the $13.12 is held, not refunded, and the move-out reads `Disposition · Excess $13.12 pending decision`. ⚠ **BR-CUS-081 IS NOT REVERSED.** Where nothing is disputed it still settles and refunds exactly as signed — $153.50 against $142.30 refunds **$11.20**, unchanged. This is a condition added to a rule confirmed the same morning, which is the process working: the rule was signed on the case it was shown, and the case it was not shown returned to the agenda. ⚠ **NOT SETTLED:** who releases a held excess when the dispute closes, and on what clock. Nothing chases it today. | PO 2026-08-24 (DEF-653) · BR-CUS-081 (amended) · B19 §15.9.6 | CONFIRMED | W5 · W7 |
| BR-CUS-097 | **A SUB-ACCOUNT'S UNPAYABLE RESIDUE TRANSFERS TO ITS MASTER ACCOUNT (PO 2026-08-24, from DEF-651).** PO verbatim: *"master account gets debt - if sub accoun tis unable to pay it ; even aftr deposite settlement"*. When a sub-account closes still owing after its own deposit and interest have been applied to everything outstanding (BR-CUS-025 as amended), the residue does not simply become an uncollectable — **it moves to the master account the sub-account hangs off**. ⚠ **THIS WAS RULED IN ANSWER TO A NARROWER QUESTION AND IS RECORDED AS THE WIDER RULE IT IS.** DEF-651 asked which of two account numbers printed on one panel was the typo; the PO answered where a shortfall GOES. **The identifier discrepancy is therefore STILL OPEN** and a transfer cannot be built against an ambiguous target. ⚠ **AND IT INTERACTS WITH `BR-CUS-082`, RULED THE SAME DAY**, which says a debt surviving closure is still chased with the dunning ladder restarting at TIER 1. If the residue moves to the master, **who is chased — the departed sub-account holder or the master account?** The two rulings do not resolve that between them, and W7 must not build both readings. ⚠ **ALSO UNSETTLED:** what happens when there is no master account (a standalone account has none), and whether the master may refuse. ⚠⚠ **QUALIFIED 2026-08-27 — THE TRANSFER IS CLERK-INITIATED AND MUST NOT FIRE ON ITS OWN (PO ruling, OQ-531 part 5).** PO verbatim: *"if U1 leave and dont pay $500 then $500 Debt is shifted to Owner A, **if clark tied back to A during move out process**"*. This row's text reads as AUTOMATIC — *"the residue does not simply become an uncollectable, it moves to the master account"* — and that reading is now wrong. The residue moves **only when a clerk ties it back during move-out**: a deliberate act, in the S20 wizard, on one named account, with a person behind it. **Why the qualification matters more than it looks:** an automatic transfer would move a departing tenant's debt onto a landlord who never agreed to it, silently, on every move-out in the product — **the clerk's action is the consent.** So the transfer needs a control in the move-out wizard and an audit row NAMING WHO TIED IT BACK. `move_out_settlement.shortfall_decision` already refuses to act without an explicit `path="transfer"` from a clerk and writes `account.move_out.shortfall_decided` with the actor; as of 2026-08-27 the decision record itself carries `tied_back_by` so the consent is readable without joining the audit row. ⚠ **THIS DOES NOT CLOSE `OQ-366`** — who the dunning ladder chases once the residue has moved was not put to the PO and stays open, and nothing may be seeded against it. ⚠⚠ **AND AS OF 2026-08-27 THE PAIR RESOLVES ITSELF — `OQ-366` IS CLOSED AND THE LADDER CHASES THE MASTER (PO ruling).** PO verbatim: *"Sub account is closed when debt is trasnfer hence we now follow with owner for debt"*. **The reasoning is STRUCTURAL, not a balance of fairness:** the sub-account is **closed** at the moment the residue transfers, so there is no open account on the tenant side to chase at all — which also disposes of the *both, differently* reading, because a second ladder would have to run against a closed account and this product does not do that. **Worked, end to end, on the case both rules were written against:** `A-11024 · Ridge Coffee Co.` closes owing **$328.70**, deposit and interest cover **$153.50**, **$175.20** survives, moves to the master, and **TIER 1 fires on the MASTER's account**. ⚠ **IT INHERITS THE SAME DAY'S QUALIFICATION AND THE TWO MUST BE READ TOGETHER:** the transfer is clerk-initiated (BR-CUS-097 as qualified), so the ladder follows the master **only for a residue a clerk actually tied back** — a residue nobody tied back does not silently become the landlord's problem and therefore does not generate the landlord's letters either. ⚠ **ONE FOLLOW-ON IS RAISED AND NOT ANSWERED (`OQ-580`):** if the master is already at TIER 3 on its own arrears, does an arriving residue RESTART its ladder to Tier 1, JOIN its existing position, or run as its OWN sequence? Not put to the PO; **no fixture may answer it by existing.** ⚠ **AND THE TRANSFER NOW MOVES THE BOOKS — PO 2026-10-14 (D5), `OQ-1250` CLOSED → (a), `CR-185-BRC10`.** PO verbatim: *"D5 … option 1; property is always under landload agreement"* — so every property with an owner (master) account counts as under agreement (`OQ-069`'s condition, met with no agreement record), and the residue a clerk ties back is posted as ONE entry through the ledger posting service: **DR accounts receivable on the owner's account / CR accounts receivable on the tenant's account**, same control account, the account dimension differs; no revenue, no bad debt, total AR unchanged. Worked: `$1,053.78` leaves AC-2026-00041 and lands on owner AC-2026-00053. Idempotent (one entry per departing account), dated the stated move-out day or the account's close date (never a clock), refused by name where no owner account resolves — exactly as before. Built in `apps/ledger/balance_transfer.py` 🔒 HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING. ⚠ **ONLY MONEY IN THE BOOKS MOVES** (coordinator rulings 2026-10-14; this clause read *"POSTED AT THE MOVE-OUT COMMIT … drafted final bill included"* until the second of them): the wizard's step 3 records the tie-back and previews the figure; when the move-out DRAFTS a final bill, its commit records the tie-back PENDING in `balance_tie_backs` and posts nothing; the entry posts when that final bill is ISSUED, for what the closed account then owes per the ledger (prior + the bill − deposit and interest; payments are already in it), once per departing account. A voided draft posts nothing and the tie-back stays pending. A move-out with nothing left to bill posts at commit; a refused move-out moves no money. A tie-back made on the move-out record (account already closed) posts at once unless a final draft still waits. Decision log *"BALANCE TO OWNER MOVES ONLY MONEY THAT IS IN THE BOOKS"*. | PO 2026-08-24 (DEF-651) · BR-CUS-025 · BR-CUS-082 · BR-CUS-068 (the master account shape) | CONFIRMED (the transfer) / **OPEN (who is chased · no-master case · the A-11968 vs 11902 identifier)** | W5 · W7 |

## 2026-08-24 · W5 BUILD NOTE — the move-out settlement, and what it could not answer

Written by the CR-030 W5 move-out lane (B18 tasks 5.7 · 5.8 · 5.9 · 5.14) at the
moment the code was written, per hard-won law 9/10: *the test is not "was a
decision made" — it is "would the next agent, with only the repo, build the same
thing?"* Everything below is either a rule already signed above, or a **residue**
this lane refused to resolve. **No rule is created here.**

**Task 5.14 is SATISFIED and was already satisfied before this lane started.**
`BR-CUS-030` above carries its amendment, dated 2026-08-24, with the PO's verbatim
*"(b) continue, ladder restarts"*, the `$328.70 / $153.50 / $175.20` worked figure,
the supersedes pointer to `BR-CUS-082`, and the narrow-reading warning. Nothing was
added; it is recorded as checked so the next lane does not amend it twice.

### What was built — `06-backend/apps/customers/move_out_settlement.py`

B19 §15.9's **five money landings** derived from **one subtraction**, not five
branches:

    final bill net = final bill − unbanked export credit    (BR-MET-107)
    outstanding    = prior balance + final bill net         (BR-CUS-025, amended)
    applied        = deposit held + deposit interest + this account's credit
    net            = outstanding − applied

    net > 0 → `short` · net = 0 → `settled` · net < 0 → `refused_excess`
    (BR-CUS-096) / `refused_other_debt` / `refund_due` (BR-CUS-081)

Every figure in B19 §15.9.1 and every worked figure of BR-CUS-025, BR-CUS-081,
BR-CUS-082 and BR-MET-107 is asserted in
`06-backend/tests/test_w5_move_out_settlement.py`.

### Three orderings that are DERIVATIONS, not choices — recorded so they are auditable

1. **The export credit comes off the FINAL BILL, before the deposit settles
   anything.** BR-MET-107's own noun (*"apply it to the final bill"*) and its own
   sentence (*"only what remains after it follows the ordinary settlement path"*).
2. **A signed hold beats an unsigned seizure.** Where an account has BOTH an open
   dispute and a debt on a sibling account, `BR-CUS-096` (signed) holds the excess
   and the sibling seizure (B19 §15.9.5, drawing only) does not run. Transferring
   money that may still be owed elsewhere is the irreversible act BR-CUS-096 exists
   to prevent.
3. **Every unreadable term fails CLOSED.** A missing deposit interest understates
   what is applied and biases the answer toward `short`; a missing final bill
   understates what is outstanding and biases it toward `refund_due`. One of those
   two directions posts a cheque, so no landing is stated at all while either is
   unknown. An unreadable dispute store or an unreadable set of sibling accounts
   gaps **only where there is an excess** — neither can change a `short` or a
   `settled` close, and gapping those would refuse settlements the rules settle.

### Residues — raised, not answered. Each needs a PO ruling or another lane.

| # | What is unresolved | Why this lane would not decide it |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | **A sibling CREDIT is shown and never applied; a sibling DEBT is applied.** B19 §15.9.4 draws `Credit on A-09984 · $61.40` beside a `$170.74` shortfall it would cut by a third, and §15.9.5 seizes a sibling debt. | Nothing signed states either half. Implemented as drawn, flagged `sibling_credit_rule_unsigned` on every settlement that holds one. **MINTED AS `OQ-362` on merge, 2026-08-24** — this lane asked for the row and the merging lane wrote it, with the $170.74 / $61.40 / $109.34 figures and three readings (symmetric · as-drawn · offer-do-not-take). |
| R2 | **`BR-MET-105a` names `premises.owner_customer_id` and that column does not exist.** Measured 2026-08-24: `apps/metering/models.Premises` carries only the free-text `owner_manager`; no migration and no code names `owner_customer`. | The rule is CONFIRMED and, on the code, still unbuildable for exactly the reason DEF-650 was filed. The migration is metering's. The owner transfer target refuses with the measurement rather than posting to a string. |
| R3 | **`BR-CUS-096` does not say who releases a held excess, or on what clock** — its own text records this. Nothing in the repository chases one: no release path, no timer, no queue. | `hold_excess()` records the hold and says so on every call. A release clock would be answering a question the PO has not been asked. |
| R4 | **`BR-CUS-097` and `BR-CUS-082` do not resolve each other:** if the residue moves to the master, is the restarted TIER 1 ladder run against the departed sub-account holder or against the master? | Both rules say W7 owns the ladder and must not build both readings. The shortfall decision carries the restart fact and the unresolved subject side by side, and names no subject. |
| R5 | **`BR-MET-107` does not say what happens when the export credit EXCEEDS the final bill** — refundable, carried, or held. | Treated as a gap, never floored at zero: flooring silently confiscates the difference, which is the confiscation the ruling was made to stop. |
| R6 | **`BR-CUS-057`'s deposit-interest accrual is NOT BUILT** (`apps.customers.selectors.deposit_interest_accrued` is published by nothing; US-CUS-023 §7). | An account that HOLDS a deposit therefore cannot be settled at all today — the same wall `apps/billing/final_bill.deposit_credit_for` hits. Principal-only would keep WE-7's `$3.50` (and GT-61's `$22.25`) of the customer's money on a bill that foots. |
| R7 | **`apps/ledger/deposit_legs.post_deposit_applied` still REFUSES the over-credit case naming `OQ-248`** — the question `BR-CUS-081` closed on 2026-08-24. | The refusal is correct code written against a question that has since been answered. Lifting it is the ledger lane's, under 🔒 ledger posting. Until then the books record neither the settlement nor the refund. |
| R8 | **B19 §15.9.6's `$13.12` and §15.11's read-back `Excess $13.12 pending decision` are now short by `$4.10`.** Those screens were drawn while an unbanked export credit was *"forfeited on close"*; under `BR-MET-107` the same account's final bill nets to `$90.35`, everything outstanding to `$136.28` and the excess to **`$17.22`**. | The prototype is the reference and it predates the ruling. Recorded rather than silently reconciled. |
| BR-CUS-089 | **THE IDENTITY-MATCH TIER IS STORED WITH ITS CONFIRMER, AND MONEY MOVES ONLY ON `strong` — WRITTEN INTO THE REGISTER 2026-08-25 (DEF-673), HAVING BEEN CITED AS A RULE SINCE B18 WAS DRAFTED AND DEFINED NOWHERE.** B18 §11 listed it in a NEW-rules table and B19 §7 then wrote *"✅ Confirms BR-CUS-089"*, which reads as citing a rule that exists. It did not: no status, no source, no worked figure, no supersedes chain — a **money** rule living only in a design document. G02 code-trace found it the moment a lane wrote the id into `apps/customers/discounts.py`, and only because DEF-672 had just given that gate a working entry point. **THE CONSEQUENCE, which is what B18 actually states and what is CONFIRMED here:** the tier (`strong` · `probable` · `weak`) and the person who confirmed it are STORED, and **a deposit carry-forward runs only on `strong`.** Absent, `weak` or `probable` refuses, with the reason named. **Worked:** Rosa Alvarez closed an account holding **$150.00** of deposit; on `strong` the offer carries it, on anything else the offer is refused **and shows no figure at all** — a number on a refused offer is an invitation to type it in by hand. ✅ **THE CRITERIA ARE RULED (PO 2026-08-25, closing `OQ-364`): NAME + DATE OF BIRTH + ONE GOVERNMENT ID.** PO verbatim: *"Name + date of birth + one government ID"* — the strictest of the three offered. It refuses some genuine returners, and the PO chose that over the risk of handing a stranger someone else's deposit. On the worked case, Rosa Alvarez's **$150.00** (**$153.50** with BR-CUS-057's interest, once that is built) moves only when all three agree. ⚠ **AND IT IS NOT ENFORCEABLE TODAY — the ruling names evidence this product never collects.** `REQ-03.1` captures no **date of birth**; `DEF-638(2)` is open on exactly that. So the criteria are signed and inert: until the request form captures a DOB, no match can be assessed against them. **`carry_forward_offer` therefore refuses everything not already stored as `strong`, which is STRICTER than the ruling rather than looser** — the safe direction for a rule about moving somebody else's money. ⚠ Historic note, kept: before 2026-08-25 nothing anywhere said what evidence earned `strong`. **The tier names existed; the test that assigned them did not**, and there is no matcher — `service_request_service.link_customer` states in its own words that *"`tier` is the clerk's, not ours … there is no automatic matcher here"*. `DEF-638(2)` is separately open on the date of birth such a match would be made on, which REQ-03.1 never collects. **WHAT IS BUILT TO THIS BOUNDARY:** `apps/customers/discounts.carry_forward_offer` **reads** a stored tier and can neither compute nor accept one — `tier` is not a parameter, and a test asserts that against the signature, so a later lane cannot quietly start deciding it. Every non-`strong` case carries `identity_match_strength_has_no_rule`. | B18 §11 · B19 §7 · DEF-673 (the id existed nowhere) · **OQ-364 (the criteria)** · DEF-638(2) · `apps/customers/discounts.py` | **CONFIRMED (consequence, B18/B19) · CONFIRMED (criteria, PO 2026-08-25, OQ-364 CLOSED) — ⚠ criteria NOT ENFORCEABLE until DEF-638(2) gives the form a date of birth** | W5 |
| BR-CUS-070 | **THE ADDRESS AS THE APPLICANT GAVE IT IS KEPT VERBATIM AND NEVER OVERWRITTEN BY THE RESOLVED RECORD — WRITTEN INTO THE REGISTER 2026-08-25 (DEF-673, second instance), having been cited by five files and defined nowhere.** Quoted verbatim as r10 draws it, a footnote on the Location tab: *"The address as the applicant gave it is kept verbatim and never overwritten by the resolved record."* The resolved premise and unit ids sit **BESIDE** it in their own columns, and the service **refuses an edit** to `address_as_given` after capture. ⚠ **THIS ROW IS A TRANSCRIPTION, NOT A NEW RULING.** It records what is already cited, already quoted and already ENFORCED — `service_requests.address_as_given` (`models.py`, its own docstring and the column comment), `service_request.py`, `service_request_api.py`. Nothing about the behaviour changes; only the register catches up. It is written now because G02 code-trace, given a working entry point by DEF-672, reported five files citing a rule id that resolved to nothing. **Why it matters, in one line:** an applicant who writes *"the blue house behind the Shell station"* has told you something the resolved address cannot; overwriting it destroys the only record of what they actually said, and a disputed connection later turns on exactly that. ⚠ **NO OPEN HALF.** Unlike `BR-CUS-089` — the other phantom found in the same sweep, whose CRITERIA were never stated and are now `OQ-364` — this rule is fully determined: keep it, never overwrite it, refuse edits, put the resolved ids elsewhere. There was nothing left to decide, which is why it could be transcribed rather than routed to the PO. | r10 Location-tab footnote · B19 §4 · `apps/customers/models.ServiceRequest.address_as_given` · `service_request.py` · `service_request_api.py` · DEF-673 | CONFIRMED (transcribed 2026-08-25 from the drawing and the built refusal) | W4 · shipped |
| BR-CUS-674 | **AN ADDRESS CHANGES WHEN THE ACCOUNT HOLDER TELLS US, AND BY NO OTHER ROUTE.** PO ruling 2026-08-31, closing `OQ-1171`, verbatim: *"(a) Ignore it — mailing only, our record unchanged - we cannot make chnges until account confirm it"*. **The case as put:** a customer leaves `1408 Elm Street` without telling the utility; the print vendor's licensed mover lookup (NCOA) returns their new address; the bill is forwarded and paid. **We do not write it.** ⚠ **THE SECOND CLAUSE IS THE RULE AND IT IS BIGGER THAN THE OPTION IT CAME ATTACHED TO.** Three options were offered — *ignore*, *write it automatically*, *record it as a staff suggestion for a clerk to confirm* — and the suggestion was recommended. The PO chose *ignore* **and gave a reason that disposes of the suggestion too**: a suggestion queue is still us proposing a change to a person's address on a third party's say-so. So the rule is stated as a GENERAL one and not as a decision about NCOA: **no third party's answer about where a customer lives may become our record. The account holder says so, or it does not change.** This sits directly on `BR-CUS-070`'s instinct one step further out — that rule keeps the address the applicant GAVE verbatim and never overwritten by the RESOLVED record; this one says the same of an address a vendor hands back. **What it does NOT forbid:** enriching a mailing for delivery (a mail house may CASS-certify or NCOA-process the file it mails — `BR-NOT-029` already puts those standards on the vendor), and holding a returned fact as EVIDENCE somewhere that is not the address, which is what `outbound_messages.returned_at` already is. **What it forbids is the WRITE, and any queue whose purpose is to propose one.** ⚠ **AND NOTE WHAT A MOVER RESULT PROBABLY MEANS, because it is the more useful reading:** a customer who has moved away has probably also stopped taking service, so the interesting consequence is not a new mailing address — it is an unreported move-out with a final bill owing. That is a move-out question and it goes through the move-out flow, where the account holder is the one who confirms. **Measured, not assumed, on the day this was written: nothing in the product writes an address from a vendor return.** There is no inbound vendor path at all (`apps/notifications/provider_seam.py` — no vendor, no credentials, no connection to make); `apps/notifications/delivery_ops.mark_returned` is a CLERK marking a paper letter come-back and its `update_fields` is `returned_at, returned_by, updated_at`; and `apps/metering/geocode.geocode_missing` — the one third-party lookup that DOES answer about an address — writes `update_fields=["lat", "lng", "updated_at"]` and no address column. **`G60` enforces the absence** so it stays true when the print hand-over is built. | **PO 2026-08-31** (`OQ-1171` closure) · `BR-CUS-070` · `BR-NOT-029` clause (d) · `10-scripts/gates/g60_no_address_from_a_vendor_return.py` | CONFIRMED | W4 · the rule and its guard are shipped; there is no vendor return to refuse yet |
| BR-CUS-086 | **A TRANSFER IS ONE JOB AND APPEARS IN NEITHER MOVE-INS NOR MOVE-OUTS — written into the register 2026-08-25 (DEF-673, THIRD instance).** Quoted from B18 §11's NEW-rules table and §5.7.3's callout. A transfer closes an account at one unit and opens one at another; **listing it in both queues would show one customer's single move as two jobs and let two clerks work it independently.** The two halves are one row and one commit. ⚠ **A TRANSCRIPTION, NOT A RULING** — same treatment as BR-CUS-070 an hour earlier. B18 states the behaviour unambiguously and there is no open half, so it is recorded rather than routed to the PO. ⚠ **AND THE EXCLUSION HAS NOWHERE TO LIVE YET, WHICH IS THE USEFUL PART.** Measured 2026-08-25: **neither list exists.** `AccountsListPage.tsx` renders the `move-ins` and `move-outs` tabs as stated absences (`tracks="OQ-350"` / `"OQ-346"`), and B18 §6.2 marks both endpoints ❌. So `transfers.py` publishes the contract — `transfer_half_account_ids()`, `is_transfer_half()`, `exclude_transfer_halves()` — and `TC-TRF-1` asserts it in two halves: the predicate's behaviour on a real transfer, **and a STATIC case that scans `apps/customers/*.py` and fails, naming the file, the moment a move list appears that calls none of the three.** The contract is enforced before the thing it constrains exists. ⚠ **ONE LEAK VECTOR IS NAMED AND NOT CLOSED:** `services.move_out` writes `account.closed` and `create_account` writes `account.created`, so a Move-outs list built from `audit_log` on `account.closed` **would** pick up a transfer's outgoing half. Renaming those actions is another module's call and probably wrong; the trap is recorded so the list's author sees it first. **And it covers OPEN transfers, not just committed ones** — B18 §5.7.6's Jenna Park sits at step `3 of 5` and is correctly absent from the Move-outs five. | B18 §11 · §5.7.3 · §5.7.6 · DEF-673 · `apps/customers/transfers.py` (`EXCLUSION_CONTRACT`) | CONFIRMED (transcribed 2026-08-25 from B18's own statement) | W7 |
| BR-CUS-098 | **WHEN A PROTECTION LAPSES, THE DUNNING LADDER RESTARTS AT TIER 1 (PO 2026-08-25, closing DEF-689).** The PO was shown that B18 §5.8.3's ladder diagram draws `HOLD -.->|"protection lapses"| T1` while `TC-DSC-3` beside it says *"the hold lifts and the ladder resumes **where it was**, not from tier 1"* — the same document, opposite behaviours — and ruled **"Back to tier 1"**. ⚠ **THE BUILD RECOMMENDED THE OTHER READING AND THE PO CHOSE THIS ONE; IT IS BUILT AS RULED AND NOT RE-ARGUED.** The argument put was that a protection pauses collections rather than forgiving a debt, and that under a restart anyone can hold the ladder open indefinitely by renewing a certificate. The argument the ruling accepts is the one that outweighs it: **going straight to a 48-hour cut notice on a household just out of a medical hardship is what a regulator asks about**, and fresh notice before a cut is the defensible position. **THE WORKED COST, ON THE RECORD, BECAUSE IT WAS PUT BEFORE THE RULING:** a customer at **`TIER 3 · 48-HOUR`** — certified letter served, two days from a cut — produces a medical certificate. Three months later it expires. Under this rule they receive **`TIER 1 · REMINDER` (email), then `TIER 2 · 10-DAY` (posted), then `TIER 3 · 48-HOUR` (certified)** — **three more notices** — before the cut can be scheduled again. On the drawn ladder's own intervals (Aug 04 → Aug 18 → Aug 22) that is roughly **eighteen further days**, and the arrear keeps accruing throughout. ✅ **AND IT MAKES THE PRODUCT CONSISTENT WITH ITSELF**, which the alternative did not: `BR-CUS-082` (PO 2026-08-24) already rules that a debt surviving account closure is chased on and **the ladder RESTARTS at TIER 1**. Two different interruptions — a closure and a lapsed protection — now resume the same way, so there is one rule for *"the ladder was interrupted"* rather than two that differ by accident. ⛔ **`TC-DSC-3` IS WRONG AND MUST BE CORRECTED, NOT LEFT AS A SECOND OPINION** — a test case stating the opposite of a signed rule is how the wrong one gets built. ⚠ **What this does NOT decide:** whether a SECOND protection filed during the restarted ladder holds it again from wherever it has reached (nothing says it would not), and whether a tenant who has already received six notices for one debt is entitled to anything different from one who has received three. Neither was raised; neither is inferred. | PO 2026-08-25 (DEF-689) · B18 §5.8.3 diagram · TC-DSC-3 (now wrong) · BR-CUS-082 · task 7.7 | CONFIRMED | W7 |
| BR-CUS-099 | **THE TRANSFERS LIST DOES NOT SEARCH BY REQUEST NUMBER (PO 2026-08-25, closing DEF-688).** B18 §5.7.2 draws the search box as *"Customer, account #, either address, or **request #**…"* and §5.7.6 links Park's transfer to `SR-26-0047`, but **`transfers` carries no service-request column and no join exists**. Put the two ways — add the link, as New Connect already has it, or drop the leg — the PO ruled **"Drop the leg — remove 'or request #' from the box"**. ✅ **SO THE SEARCH OFFERS EXACTLY WHAT IT CAN DO: customer · account # · either address.** *"Either address"* stays and is the one that earns its place, because a transfer has two and a clerk may know only the one the customer is leaving. ⚠ **THE CONSEQUENCE, STATED SO IT IS A CHOICE AND NOT A SURPRISE:** there is now **no way to get from a service request to the transfer it became**. A customer ringing about `SR-26-0047` cannot be answered from this screen, and the request's own detail page has no link forward either. That is acceptable today because **a transfer can be raised without a request at all** — a clerk simply does one — so the link would be empty on most rows and a search that finds only the minority is its own kind of misleading. **If the front door becomes the normal way transfers arrive, this decision is worth revisiting**, and it is cheap to reverse: one nullable column, the same shape `connections.service_request_id` already has. ⚠ **`B18 §5.7.2`'s drawn placeholder is now STALE** and is stamped rather than rewritten — the prototype is the record of what was handed over. | PO 2026-08-25 (DEF-688) · B18 §5.7.2 · §5.7.6 · `apps/customers/transfer_reads.py` · contrast `connections.service_request_id` | CONFIRMED | W7 |
| BR-CUS-100 | **A DISCONNECT NOTICE TRAVELS THE NOTIFICATION QUEUE AND IS DELIVERED IN-APP; CERTIFIED MAIL IS RECORDED AS PROOF OF SERVICE, NEVER CLAIMED AS A SEND (PO 2026-08-25, closing DEF-692).** PO verbatim: *"you amke the provision and send in app messages - we discussed this before"*. ✅ **THEY HAD, AND THE EARLIER REASONING IS THE BETTER HALF OF THIS RULE.** `EP-NOT-05` records the PO on 2026-08-17: *"We can confirm it whether it is working on not if we get in APp Notifcaition, which does not require any integration and it is proof that notification is working"*, then *"can you build the in-app?"* — and the epic's own finding is that **in-app is not a second delivery path**: the message travels *"the same queue, the same dispatcher, the same ONE provider seam and the same rendered-artifact contract every other channel will travel — the only difference is that the adapter behind the seam is this application."* **So *"make the provision"* means: raise the notice as an ordinary notification event, and let the in-app adapter deliver it.** Email, SMS and print plug into the same seam when their adapters exist; nothing about the notice changes when they do. ⛔ **BUT IN-APP DOES NOT SATISFY CERTIFIED MAIL, AND THE RULE MUST NOT BE READ AS IF IT DID.** `DSC-02.3` marks certified mail **`Required at tier 3 — proof of service`**, and proof of service is a **legal artefact produced outside this product**: a posted item, a tracking number, a signature that came back. No adapter can manufacture one. **So the two halves are separated and neither pretends to be the other:** the SEND is in-app (and later email/SMS/print), and the PROOF is a **record a person enters** — the channel used, the reference, and the date it was signed for. That is what a regulator asks to see, and recording it is honest where claiming to have sent it is not. ⚠ **`disconnect_notices` CANNOT HOLD THE PROOF TODAY.** It carries `tier`, `delivery_state`, `delivery_unavailable_reason`, `issued_on`, `actor` and `detail` — enough to say a notice was raised, **not enough to say it was served, by what means, or that a signature came back.** Owed: a proof reference, a served-at date and the serving channel, entered by a human, on the same discipline `BR-MET-110` applies to a field result — somebody decided, and the record must name them. **Until those columns exist, `delivery_state` stays NULL with its reason** (the W7 lane's behaviour) and **a tier-3 cut must not be schedulable on an unrecorded service**, because that is the one thing on this screen whose absence is not merely untidy. ⚠ **`B18 §5.8.2` IS NOW HALF-STALE and is stamped rather than rewritten:** its warning that *"there is no email, SMS or print adapter in this release — the in-app bell is the only channel that delivers anything"* remains exactly true; what it did not say is that in-app is the channel disconnect notices USE, which this rule settles. | PO 2026-08-25 (DEF-692) · PO 2026-08-17 (`EP-NOT-05`) · BR-NOT-020 · B18 §5.8.2 · `DSC-02.3` · BR-MET-110 (the same human-record discipline) | CONFIRMED (the channel) · **schema owed (proof of service)** | W7 |
| BR-CUS-101 | **THREE PROTECTIONS ARE THE RELEASE; THE OTHER SEVEN ARE A CLERK'S JOB UNTIL THEY ARE BUILT (PO 2026-08-25, closing DEF-691).** PO verbatim: *"3 is okay to start with"*. `DSC-02.2` draws **ten** checks and `disconnects.py` implements **three** — medical certificate, active payment plan, weather moratorium, each with its own lifetime (task 7.7). The PO was shown the list of seven and the reason the gap was raised as HIGH, and ruled the three sufficient to start. **Built as ruled.** ⚠ **ONE RESIDUAL, AND IT IS NARROW ENOUGH TO STATE EXACTLY.** The tier-3 letter drawn on `DSC-02.3` promises the customer three things: the right *"to dispute this balance"*, *"to request a payment plan"*, and *"to be protected from disconnection if somebody at this address depends on medical equipment"*. **Two of those three are among the built protections.** The third — **an open dispute** — is one of the seven, so the letter names a right the system does not check. It is not a broken promise, because a customer who rings to dispute is answered by a person and a clerk can hold the case by hand; it IS a promise with no machine guard behind it, and that distinction belongs on the record rather than in somebody's memory. ⚠ **THE SEVEN ARE DEFERRED, NOT DISMISSED**, and each is a reason a cut must not proceed: life support registration · age or disability protection · open dispute · assistance application pending · recent partial payment · notice period served · landlord-billed premises. **`DSC-02.2` must not draw a ✓ against a check the product did not perform** — a tick that means *"a clerk looked"* and a tick that means *"the system verified"* are different claims to the person authorising the most serious action this product can take, and the screen must say which it is. | PO 2026-08-25 (DEF-691) · `DSC-02.2` · `DSC-02.3` (the letter) · task 7.7 · B18 §5.8.7 | CONFIRMED (three is the release) · **the seven deferred, the tick's meaning owed** | W7 |
| BR-CUS-102 | **THE PROTOTYPE'S NUMBERS ARE SAMPLES; THE PRODUCT COMPUTES (PO 2026-08-25, closing DEF-690).** PO verbatim: *"Those are sample only from Prototype; you calcualte correctly"*. Raised because three cells of `TRF-02.1` disagree with the rest of the transfer wizard — a **`60+ past due`** chip over a **$0.00** balance, **`$150.00 + $4.20 interest`** where four other screens say **$153.50** (and step 4 shows the working: `150.00 + 3.50`), and a *"no sub-meter measures through it"* claim that step 2 contradicts by reading one. ✅ **THE RULING IS GENERAL AND IT RETIRES A WHOLE CLASS OF DEFECT.** `DEF-676` is the same shape (one build's charge lines footing to `$705` under a drawn total of `$2,340`), and every future capture will meet more. **A figure in an r10 fixture is illustrative. It is not a specification, it is not a golden test, and a mismatch between two of them is not a defect to fix.** ⚠ **THE BOUNDARY MATTERS AND IS NARROW.** CLAUDE.md's standing ruling — *"for this session we will be refering to r10 - as default"*, and where A1–A17 contradict r10, **r10 is right** — is **unchanged**. r10 remains authoritative for **layout, copy, flows, states, affordances and the rules written on the screens**. What this ruling removes from its authority is **the sample DATA only**. *"Deposit held $150.00 + $4.20 interest"* is not a rule about interest; *"the deposit moves with them, nothing refunded and re-collected"* on the same screen **is** a rule and still binds. ✅ **AND THE MONEY CONTRACT IS UNTOUCHED:** `B4`/`B5` are ❄️ FROZEN and `GT-1..61` are the billing-math contract. **Where code disagrees with GT-16 (`267.30`, batch `$184,320.55`), the code is wrong** — that has not changed and a prototype figure never had that standing in the first place. ⚠ **WHAT A CAPTURE SHOULD DO INSTEAD OF FILING:** re-derive every figure, say plainly which screens agree, and **prefer the screen that shows its working** — step 4 of the transfer wizard is the arbiter precisely because it prints `150.00 + 3.50 = 153.50` rather than asserting a total. A capture that records the arithmetic is doing the useful half; filing the mismatch is not. | PO 2026-08-25 (DEF-690) · CLAUDE.md (r10 as default reference) · DEF-676 · B4/B5 ❄️ · GT-16 · B18 §5.7.7 | CONFIRMED | all waves |

## B18 §6.4's proposals `BR-CUS-069 · 071 · 072 · 073 · 074` — written into the register 2026-08-25 (CR-030 task 4.17)

> ⚠ **TASK 4.17 ASKED FOR THESE *"BEFORE THE CODE"* AND THE CODE SHIPPED FIRST.**
> W4 landed on 2026-08-24 and the register still carried only `BR-CUS-070` out of
> the six. The other five were cited by the design, by two CONFIRMED rules and by
> six source files, and **an id that resolves to nothing is DEF-673's exact
> class** — the sweep that found `BR-CUS-070` and `BR-CUS-086` phantom found them
> the same way. So they are written now, late, and the lateness is the finding.
>
> ⚠ **NOT ONE OF THESE IS A NEW RULING, AND NONE IS RATIFIED HERE.** Each row
> below records **what the repository already determines** — a PO ruling that
> superseded the proposal, a CONFIRMED rule that adopted it, or a refusal already
> enforced in code — and where nothing determines it, the row says **PROPOSED,
> NOT RULED** and names what is missing. Filling a number with an invented rule
> is the one thing task 4.17 must not do.
>
> ⚠ **`BR-CUS-069…074` KEEP THESE NUMBERS.** B18 §6.4's renumbering note
> (*"these six were proposed as `BR-CUS-075…080` … they are now
> `BR-CUS-084…089`"*) sits directly beneath this table and is about the **second**
> six — park · parked-holds-no-money · transfer-is-one-job · move-out-shortfall ·
> import-rollback · identity-match-tier — which is why `BR-CUS-086` and
> `BR-CUS-089` are already in this register carrying exactly those statements. It
> is placed where it reads as though it governs this block. **It does not.**
>
> ⚠ **AND THAT ORDINAL LIST IS NOW HALF TRUE — `085` IS NO LONGER THE SECOND
> ITEM (PO ruling 2026-08-25, DEF-711).** Counting *park · parked-holds-no-money ·
> transfer-is-one-job · …* onto `BR-CUS-084…089` gives **085 = parked-holds-no-money**,
> and B18 §5.7.7 **also** cited `085` for the transfer wizard's *"the deposit stays
> with you"* while this register held neither. The PO ruled **`BR-CUS-085` is the
> DEPOSIT rule** (its row is at the foot of this table) **and the parked-wizard
> statement took `BR-CUS-108`**, because 085 was already cited in shipped code
> paths for the deposit meaning. `084`, `087` and `088` keep their proposed numbers
> and are **still unregistered** — the same DEF-673 class, reported here, not minted.

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-069 | ~~**A SERVICE REQUEST IS NUMBERED AT SUBMISSION, NOT AT DRAFT, AND THE NUMBER IS IDEMPOTENT.**~~ **SUPERSEDED BEFORE RATIFICATION — `BR-CUS-093` (PO 2026-08-24, closing DEF-634) numbers a request AT CREATION.** BR-CUS-093 clause (d) reverses this proposal by name and says it *"must be restated wherever it is cited"*; this row is that restatement. The PO's reason is the requirement: *"the clerk can quote the number to the customer on the phone while the form is still half-filled"* — worked, `SR-26-0048` exists from the moment the form opens, and an abandoned draft **burns its number permanently** (clause (a): gaps are normal and must not be reported as loss). ⚠ **THE IDEMPOTENCY HALF SURVIVES THE SUPERSESSION AND IS STRONGER, NOT WEAKER** (clause (e)): `submit` allocates nothing at all now, so a repeated submit is a state transition on a row that already holds its identity — the idempotency key is the request itself (CLAUDE.md rule 6). ✅ **THE HALF OF BR-CUS-093 THAT WAS NOT HONOURED BY THE CODE IS HONOURED NOW — PO ratification 2026-09-20 (decision log, *"SEVEN PO RATIFICATIONS FROM ONE SITTING"*, §3; `DEF-CUS-6006`).** This clause read: *"`_clean_capture(partial=False)` requires `address_as_given` (step 3) and `applicant_name` before a row may exist, so the number does **not** arrive at the first keystroke — the capture screen says 'not saved yet' rather than naming a number that does not exist, and B18 row 4.10 reports it as owing a defect row and a backend change."* **The defect row is `DEF-CUS-6006` and the backend change is made.** A draft is created from the applicant NAME ALONE — from NOTHING on a staff-raised channel (`BR-CUS-091`) — and the address requirement MOVES to SUBMIT (`_require_submit_minimum`). So BR-CUS-093's worked example is literally true: `SR-26-0048` exists from the moment the name is typed, and a clerk can read it down the phone while the form is half-filled. Measured in Chromium on the deployed join at `2.317.4`: a name alone on step 1 creates `SR-26-0053`. **`BR-CUS-070` is untouched** — the address is still verbatim and still immutable after capture, which is implemented as *after the row leaves DRAFT*, so a draft opened without one can still be given one on step 3. | B18 §6.4 (proposal, `NEW`) · **superseded by** BR-CUS-093 (PO 2026-08-24, DEF-634) · CR-031 §4 (*"unaffected"* — written before BR-CUS-093 existed) | **SUPERSEDED (proposal → BR-CUS-093, numbered at creation)** | tests/test_cr031_service_request.py (`test_a_request_opens_as_a_draft_and_is_numbered_at_creation`, `test_no_transition_allocates_a_number`, `test_br_cus_093_a_repeated_submit_is_idempotent`, `test_br_cus_093_an_abandoned_draft_burns_its_number_and_the_gap_stays`) |
| BR-CUS-071 | ~~**THE SYSTEM CLASSIFIES, BY ADDRESS RESOLUTION: vacant unit → move-in · nothing → new connection · live account → refused.**~~ **SUPERSEDED BEFORE RATIFICATION — CR-031 statement 3: THE CLERK DECIDES.** The requester's own words are *"the clerk decides: move-in · new connect · refusal"*, after reviewing the documents and verifying the address. CR-030's change record states why this needed no re-sign: *"BR-CUS-071 is `NEW` — a proposal in a design document stamped DRAFT FOR PO REVIEW, and it is NOT in `02-business-rules.md`. So this is not a reversal of a signed rule … it is the PO ruling on a proposal before it was ratified, which is the process working."* **WHAT REPLACES IT, and it is not nothing:** B19 §4 reconciles the two — *"the resolution is EVIDENCE, the decision is a JUDGMENT"*. The system RESOLVES the address (`resolution_kind` · `lookup_found`, both STORED so the sentence a clerk read survives) and adapts the form; the clerk DECIDES the outcome afterwards, with **nothing preselected**, one option badged `Fits the evidence` and every `not_possible` carrying the PO's own sentence. A clerk who decides against the evidence is **recorded, never refused** (`decided_against_evidence`). ⚠ **THE UNDRAWN VERDICT IS `OQ-356` AND IS NOT INVENTED:** B19 draws the five verdicts for exactly ONE evidence state, and inverting a drawn impossibility into a possibility would be manufacturing a rule out of a sentence about one case. | B18 §6.4 (proposal, `NEW`) · **superseded by** CR-031 statement 3 (PO 2026-08-24) · B19 §4 · `12-governance/11-change-requests.md` CR-031 §4 | **SUPERSEDED (proposal → CR-031 statement 3, the clerk decides; the resolver serves evidence only)** | tests/test_cr031_service_request.py (`test_nothing_is_preselected`, `test_the_clerk_may_override_the_evidence_and_it_is_recorded_not_refused`, `test_a_served_vacant_unit_fits_a_move_in`, `test_an_unknown_address_resolves_to_no_premise_and_fits_a_new_connection`) |
| BR-CUS-072 | **A REFUSED OR WITHDRAWN REQUEST IS KEPT, NEVER DELETED; REOPENING REQUIRES A REASON — transcribed 2026-08-25, not newly ruled.** ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-10-05 (lane MVI-FLOW): THE REOPEN HALF WAS RETIRED ON 2026-09-19 (*"refuse is terminal"*) AND IS REINSTATED FOR MOVE-IN REQUESTS ONLY** by the PO's ruling closing `OQ-CUS-12789` → (a), verbatim *"Reopen: SR-26-0014 goes back to \"To approve\". Same number, her documents stay, and the decline stays in its history."* As built: `MOVE_IN_TRANSITIONS[reopen] = (refused → submitted)` (not the `(refused, withdrawn) → under_review` described below, which is the pre-2026-09-19 table), reached only through `service_request_service.reopen_declined_move_in`, **reason still required**; a non-move-in refusal stays terminal. The sentences below describing `TRANSITIONS[REOPEN]` are history. Same treatment as `BR-CUS-070` and `BR-CUS-086`: the behaviour is stated unambiguously by the design, adopted verbatim by CR-031 statement 5 (*"refusal closes the SR — it ends there"*) and **already ENFORCED**, so it is recorded rather than routed to the PO. What is built: `REASON_REQUIRED = (refuse, manual_close, reopen, request_info, hold)` and `REOPEN: ((refused, withdrawn) → under_review)` in `service_request.py`; **no service function and no route deletes a `service_requests` row**; and `service_request_events` is append-only, so the refusal and its reason survive the reopen and every reopen after it. **Why it matters, in one line:** B18 §1.2 cost #4 — *"someone turned down for arrears at a previous address simply reapplies"* — and a deleted refusal is a customer with no history at the one moment history is the whole point. ⚠ **NO OPEN HALF ON THE KEEPING; ONE ON THE COUNTING.** Nothing states whether a reopened request is a NEW request for a KPI window (`REFUSED THIS MONTH 3` counts `state=refused` with `closed_at` in the month, so a refusal reopened the next day leaves that tile silently). Reported, not decided. | B18 §6.4 (proposal, `NEW`) · CR-031 statement 5 · B18 §1.2 cost #4 · `apps/customers/service_request.py` (`REASON_REQUIRED`, `TRANSITIONS[REOPEN]`) · CLAUDE.md hard rule 4 | **CONFIRMED (transcribed 2026-08-25 from the stated design and the built refusal)** | tests/test_cr031_service_request.py (`test_refuse_needs_a_reason_and_keeps_the_request`, `test_a_refusal_survives_a_reopen_in_the_event_log`, `test_the_timeline_is_append_only_and_internal`) |
| BR-CUS-073 | **DOCUMENTS CAPTURED ON A REQUEST CARRY OVER TO THE ACCOUNT AND ARE NOT RE-UPLOADED.** **ADOPTED VERBATIM BY A CONFIRMED RULE**, which is why this is a transcription and not a ratification: `BR-CUS-080` (PO 2026-08-24) rules that *"documents move by reference, not by copy"* and states the consequence in these words — *"so BR-CUS-073's 'carry over to the account and are not re-uploaded' holds literally — one uploaded file stays ONE row and the audit trail of who supplied it survives the hand-off, where a copy would fork it."* CR-031 statement 6 strengthens it again: *"move-in → all documents transfer to it."* ⚠ **THE MECHANISM IS BUILT AND THE DESTINATION IS NOT.** `attach_document` writes one row and nothing copies it; there is **no account-document table in this product** (`service_request_documents` is the request's, and B18 §5.2.2 marks the account detail's Documents tab ❌ with task 3.7 owning it). So the rule is honoured by the half that exists — never re-uploaded, never copied — and the carry-over itself cannot be exercised end to end until that tab has a table. Stated, not worked around. ⚠⚠ **AND THIS ID IS CITED FOR A SECOND, DIFFERENT STATEMENT — REPORTED AS A DEFECT, NOT RESOLVED HERE.** Six places call `BR-CUS-073` the **WAIVER** rule (*"a waiver is BR-CUS-073's explicit act with a reason"*) — `service_request_reads.py`, `service_request.py`, `service_request_service.py`, `service_request_api.py`, `models.py`, and the CONFIRMED text of `BR-CUS-090` itself. **B18 §6.4 is the only place this id is DEFINED and it says nothing about waivers.** One id, two statements, both cited from CONFIRMED rules: the fee-waiver rule has never been allocated a number. **A lane may not pick one** — the citations stand until an id is allocated, and this row exists so the next reader is not misled by them. | B18 §6.4 (proposal, `NEW`) · **adopted by** BR-CUS-080 (PO 2026-08-24, CR-031 §5) · CR-031 statement 6 | **CONFIRMED (documents half — adopted verbatim by BR-CUS-080; destination table NOT BUILT, task 3.7)** · ⚠ **the waiver citations under this id are UNRESOLVED** | tests/test_cr031_service_request.py (`test_documents_move_by_reference_and_are_never_copied`, `test_attaching_is_not_verifying`) |
| BR-CUS-074 | **APPROVAL IS THRESHOLD-CHECKED; REFUSAL IS SUPERVISOR-ONLY AND REQUIRES A REASON CODE.** ⛔ **PROPOSED, NOT RULED — AND IT IS THE ONLY ONE OF THE SIX THAT NOTHING IN THIS REPOSITORY DETERMINES.** Three separate things are unstated and **no lane may pick any of them**: (1) **what the threshold is measured in** — an outstanding fee balance? a connection quote? a unit count? — (2) **its figure**, which nothing anywhere states, and (3) **who the supervisor is**: the identity model has `system_admin` and `billing_clerk` and **no supervisor tier**, so *"supervisor-only"* names a role that does not exist. **WHAT IS BUILT, and it is the part that IS ruled:** `approve` refuses on a missing outcome and on any of the four approval CONDITIONS (documents · fees · arrears · eligibility) with the blocking condition named, for every role that may reach the call — *"Nothing is refused — it simply cannot be approved yet"* — and `refuse_request` requires a free-text **reason** (TC-REQ-9) while `reason_code` is optional and uncontrolled. **CONSEQUENCE, RECORDED SO IT IS NOT MISTAKEN FOR AN OVERSIGHT: `TC-REQ-8` (*"a clerk under the approval threshold → `approve` → refused; a supervisor may"*) CANNOT BE WRITTEN AS DESIGNED.** It is pinned as an ABSENCE instead — `test_tc_req_8_there_is_no_approval_threshold_and_br_cus_074_is_unruled` asserts a plain billing clerk approves once conditions clear, so the day a threshold appears that case goes RED and whoever added it must name the ruling that authorised it. **What closes this row:** the PO stating the measure, the figure and the supervising role — in plain English with a concrete figure, never as an ID (HUMAN-GUIDE §6). | B18 §6.4 (proposal, `NEW`) · B18 §5.3.9 TC-REQ-8 · `apps/customers/service_request_service.approve` / `.refuse_request` | **PROPOSED — NOT RULED, NOT BUILT** (three unstated: measure · figure · supervising role) | tests/test_cr031_service_request.py (`test_tc_req_8_there_is_no_approval_threshold_and_br_cus_074_is_unruled` — pins the ABSENCE; `test_refuse_needs_a_reason_and_keeps_the_request` pins the reason half that IS built) |
| BR-CUS-103 | **BASE CHARGES STOP AT THE CUT (PO ruling 2026-08-25, closing OQ-380).** Asked in plain English with a figure — *"when a service is cut, does the base charge keep running?"* — the PO answered **"no"**. This is reading **(B)**: a customer with no service pays nothing for it, and the cost of coming back is recovered by the reconnection fee (BR-CUS-104), not by billing a standing charge against a dead connection. Reading **(A)**, base keeps running because the connection and the meter are still theirs, is **REJECTED**. ⚠ **THE PRODUCT DOES THE OPPOSITE TODAY, AND IT DOES IT BY ACCIDENT:** `commit_cut` never touches billing, so base runs on because nothing was written to stop it — the behaviour was an omission, never a decision, which is exactly why this had to be asked rather than assumed from the code. **Worked, end to end:** `A-11488` was cut on **2026-08-18** owing **$612.75**. Under this rule its balance on 2026-09-18 is still **$612.75** — no base accrues for the month it had no service. Under the rejected reading it would have grown by one month's standing charge. **This is a MONEY rule and it is finance-signed, so it is not a lane's to soften**: a bill run that emits a base line for a period a service was cut is a defect, not a rounding question. **The boundary is the cut date, not the notice date** — a service is billed base right up to the moment it is cut, because until then it was available. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-30 — THE WORDS ARE UNCHANGED AND *WHEN* THEY ARE HONOURED HAS MOVED (OQ-1170, CLOSED).** This rule worked only because a cut account stayed BILLABLE and stayed in the September run, where `gather` subtracted the dark days. BR-CUS-107 writes `Disconnected` at the cut, which takes the account out of `customers.selectors.accounts_for_bill_run` — so the days it used BEFORE the cut would have been billed to NOBODY: on `A-11488`, cut 18 August on a **$40.00** monthly base, **$23.23** (18 days of 31) plus every gallon and every kWh. Two signed rules met and one had to give. **PO ruling 2026-08-30 — THE DISCONNECT IS THE WHOLE EVENT**, verbatim: *"when we disconnect - we generate final bill at that time itself - so later bill is not required ... entire workflow including all settlement and status update is taken care at time of disconnect or move out - so account status changes and outstanding is pending on account if any"*. **So neither gives way: this rule keeps its words exactly, because those days are now billed AT the cut instead of a month later**, by the SAME engine on the SAME B4.3 ❄️ proration (`apps/billing/cut_bill.py` → `single.prepare_single_bill` → `calculate_single_bill` → `issue.issue_invoice`; there is no second pricing path). **The one exception, and it is a safety rule not a softening:** when billing REFUSES that account — no tariff version in force at the service date, no plan, no recorded move-in date — the status is NOT written, the account stays billable and stays in the run, and the ordinary run bills those days once a human fixes the configuration (`disconnects.CUT_BILL_BLOCKED_SO_STATUS_HELD`). The days before a cut are never billed by nobody. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-21 — THE EXCEPTION IS NOW A CHOICE AND THE LAST SENTENCE IS KEPT BY A DIFFERENT MEANS.** PO ruling 2026-09-21 allows the cut request to be COMPLETED with no bill, which DOES take the account out of the run with those days unbilled — so *never billed by nobody* is preserved by **stating them** rather than by refusing: the window travels on the case record and the audit row until a bill covers the cut day (`disconnects.unbilled_days_at_cut`, `billing.selectors.unbilled_service_window`). **This rule's own words — base stops at the cut — are untouched.** ⚠ **AND THE WINDOW'S START IS RULED THE SAME DAY:** *"you create the bill from last bill date - if last bill was generated in feb and data is 28 feb you start the bill from 1 mar - for arreras - it is previous balance"*. The first unbilled day is the day after the last ISSUED bill that priced SERVICE, falling back to the day after a migration's `as_of` and then to the move-in date — PRECEDENCE, never a maximum — and arrears are **never re-billed**: they print as `Previous balance` (BR-BIL-035 / BR-BIL-160). | PO 2026-08-25 (OQ-380) · B18 §5.8 · CR-030 W7 | CONFIRMED | W7 |
| BR-CUS-104 | **THE RECONNECTION FEE LANDS AT THE CUT, NOT AT RESTORATION (PO ruling 2026-08-25, closing OQ-381).** `DSC-02.4` said both, and the PO was shown the two amounts the difference produces and confirmed the larger: *"does reconnection need $8,315.00 — this is correct"*. So the fee becomes owed the moment the service is cut, and **a customer must clear the arrears PLUS the fee before `mark_reconnecting` will accept them.** **Worked, on the account the question was put with:** `A-11340` owes **$8,240.00**; the reconnection fee is **$75.00**; so the amount that must be cleared to be reconnected is **$8,315.00**, not $8,240.00. The rejected reading put the $75.00 on their next bill and let them be reconnected at $8,240.00. ⚠ **THE FEE IS RECORDED ON THE CASE AND POSTS NOWHERE TODAY** — the reconnection gate reads AR only, so under the code as it stands the fee is not part of the balance it checks. **That is now a defect, not a design**: the ruling makes the fee a receivable at the cut, and until it posts, the gate lets a customer through $75.00 short. **The fee's AMOUNT is never a literal** — it is read from the tenant's own `CC-RECON` charge code (BR-BIL-007) and `commit_cut` refuses when it is absent or is not a flat amount. $75.00 is Brazos Bend's configured figure, not the product's. | PO 2026-08-25 (OQ-381) · B18 §5.8.7 DSC-02.4 · CR-030 W7 | CONFIRMED | W7 |
| BR-CUS-105 | **TOGETHER, BR-CUS-103 AND BR-CUS-104 MAKE A CUT ACCOUNT'S BALANCE STATIC — AND THAT IS A TESTABLE PROPERTY, NOT A SIDE EFFECT (derived 2026-08-25 from the two rulings above, same day, same PO session).** Base stops at the cut; the fee lands at the cut. So from the cut date onward **nothing the passage of time does can change what a cut customer owes** — the balance moves only when they pay, when a correction is posted, or when service is restored. **Worked:** `A-11488`, cut 2026-08-18 owing **$612.75** with a **$75.00** fee, owes **$687.75** on the day of the cut and **$687.75** on any later day until it is paid. **This is written down as its own rule because it is the cheapest guard either ruling has:** a single test that runs a bill cycle over a cut account and asserts the balance is unchanged catches a violation of EITHER rule — a base line that should not exist, or a fee that landed late — without knowing which. A property that falls out of two rules is still a property somebody must not break. | derived from BR-CUS-103 + BR-CUS-104, PO 2026-08-25 | CONFIRMED | W7 |
| BR-CUS-106 | **A SERVICE REQUEST'S AGE IS ACCENTED AT SEVEN DAYS — ONE WEEK (PO ruling 2026-08-25, closing decision D4).** `REQ-01`'s `AGE` column accents a row once the request is **7 days old or more**; below that it renders plain. The question could not be derived and was never guessed: B19 §12.1's drawing accents `15d` and `11d` and leaves `5d` plain, which bounds the threshold to **6–11 days inclusive** and no further, and B18 *proposed* 7 without authority to confirm it. Put to the PO with that bound and its consequence, the PO chose **7**, on the reading that a request older than a week is one staff would say *"has been sitting over a week"*. **Both drawn rows keep their meaning under this figure** — `11d` still accents and `5d` still does not — so the ruling contradicts no part of the design it came from. **Worked, on the eight seeded requests:** at ages 15 · 11 · 5 · 4 · 3 · 2 · 1 · 0 days, exactly **two** accent (15 and 11) and six do not; the nearest row to the line is `5d`, two days clear of it. ⚠ **`list_row` REFUSED TO SERVE `age_is_accented` UNTIL THIS RULING and TC-REQ-14 was deliberately asserted INVERTED** — it proved a 15-day and a 2-day row were styled IDENTICALLY, so that no lane could quietly pick a number. **That inversion is now obsolete and must be replaced by the real assertion**, not merely deleted: a 15-day row accents, a 5-day row does not, and a row at exactly 7 days accents (the boundary is inclusive, which is the half a "greater than" reading would get wrong). | PO 2026-08-25 (D4) · B19 §12.1 · B18 | CONFIRMED | W4 |
| BR-CUS-107 | **A CUT ACCOUNT GETS A SIXTH STATUS, `Disconnected`, IN CLASS `non_billable` (PO ruling 2026-08-25, closing DEF-701).** BR-CUS-055 seeds five — Pending activation · Active · Marked disconnect · Vacation hold/Suspended · Closed — and **none of them means "the service is off"**. A cut account reads `Marked disconnect / billable` today, which is the rung BEFORE the cut and says *consuming until cut* about a customer who has stopped consuming. The other three classes were each ruled out for a stated reason, not by preference: **`billable`** bills a service that no longer runs; **`billable_base_only`** was eliminated by the PO's OWN answer the same day (BR-CUS-103 — there is no base to bill); **`terminal`** is what `Closed` means, and a cut account is not closed — it holds a debt, it can pay, and it comes back. **Worked:** `A-11488`, cut 2026-08-18 owing **$612.75**, reads `Disconnected / non-billable`;
