nothing accrues (BR-CUS-103), the $612.75 stays owed, and `mark_reconnecting` can still take it back. ⚠ **ONE THING TO VERIFY BEFORE BUILDING, and it is the clause that could make this ruling wrong in practice: a `non_billable` account MUST still accept a PAYMENT.** Paying is how a cut customer gets reconnected (BR-CUS-104), so if `non_billable` suppresses payment anywhere in this product, that path is a defect to fix — not a reason to pick a different class. ✅ **AMENDED 2026-08-30 — THE STATUS IS ASSIGNED AT THE CUT, AND A RESTORED ACCOUNT COMES BACK TO WHAT IT WAS (OQ-1170, CLOSED).** For five days this rule was seeded and assigned by nobody, because writing the word drops the account out of `customers.selectors.accounts_for_bill_run` and the days before the cut would then be billed to nobody — **$23.23** of a $40.00 base on `A-11488` (18 days of 31), plus the consumption. **PO ruling 2026-08-30 — THE DISCONNECT IS THE WHOLE EVENT**, verbatim: *"when we disconnect - we generate final bill at that time itself - so later bill is not required ... entire workflow including all settlement and status update is taken care at time of disconnect or move out - so account status changes and outstanding is pending on account if any"*. **So `commit_cut` now does four things in one transaction, in this order:** (1) raise the final metered bill for the account's first unbilled day through the cut day INCLUSIVE, through billing's own door — customers may not write an invoice (BR-CUS-037 / B1) — and FIRST, because billing refuses to price an account that is not billable; (2) raise the reconnection fee (BR-CUS-104, unchanged); (3) write `Disconnected`, recording on the case the status code the account held before the cut; (4) settle — read the position and write NOTHING off. **`DSC-02.4` is untouched: no close, no deposit settlement, no meter removal, and whatever remains is OUTSTANDING on an account that is still open** — on `A-11488` that is the **$612.75** arrears plus the **$75.00** fee plus the final bill. **THE STATUS IS WRITTEN ONLY WHEN THE DAYS ARE BILLED, OR PROVABLY HAVE NOTHING LEFT TO BILL** — a blocked bill holds it and leaves the account in the run (`disconnects.CUT_BILL_BLOCKED_SO_STATUS_HELD`). ⚠⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-21 — THAT SENTENCE IS NO LONGER THE WHOLE RULE, AND THE PART THAT MOVED IS A REFUSAL RATHER THAN A FIGURE. PO ruling 2026-09-21, verbatim: *"in single bill we have final bill workflow - so if clark want to complete the bill he can do it from there - we will need to allow clark to complete the cut request later without bill."*** **COMPLETING THE CUT REQUEST AND RAISING ITS FINAL BILL ARE TWO ACTS.** A clerk may now complete the cut request **with no bill at all** — `disconnects.finish_cut(..., without_bill=True)`, offered on the case record as `The final bill · Complete without it` — and `Disconnected` is written. The hold above **stays available and stops being compulsory**: a clerk who asks for the bill and is refused still gets nothing written and billing's own sentence. ⚠ **WHAT THE RULING DID NOT SAY, AND A LANE MAY NOT QUIETLY DROP IT:** the reason for the hold is real — a `disconnected` account leaves `accounts_for_bill_run`, so no ordinary run will pick those days up. The PO ruled WHO raises the bill, not that the days stop mattering. So the unbilled days are STATED — `disconnects.unbilled_days_at_cut`, on the completion payload, on `audit_log` and as a row on the case record reading *Days not billed · 09/21/2026 – 09/21/2026 · 1 day* — and they are DERIVED on every read, so the statement closes itself the day billing's single-bill workflow raises the bill. **This supersedes the refusal built 2026-09-20 under `DEF-CUS-6063`, which kept the hold in `finish_cut`.** **AND A RESTORED ACCOUNT RETURNS TO THE BILLABLE STATUS IT HELD BEFORE THE CUT** — derived, not asked (CLAUDE.md law 13): `mark_reconnecting` refuses with `not_settled` while a cent is owed and records `settled_balance` when it succeeds, so `RECONNECTING → RESTORED` is only reachable on a settled account and the debt that would justify `Marked disconnect` cannot exist by then. Where nothing was recorded (a case cut before this ruling, or a status code a tenant has since deleted) the account keeps the status it has and the reason is NAMED — restoring a `vacation_hold` customer to `active` would write a status nobody decided. ⛔ **SUPERSEDED 2026-10-29 — A RESTORED ACCOUNT RETURNS TO `Active`, NOT TO THE STATUS IT HELD BEFORE THE CUT (PO, verbatim *"2A"*; decision log *"Five open questions answered in one message (PO)"* item 2; `DEF-CUS-10668`).** The derivation above died on 2026-09-25 with `BR-CUS-1097` (an owing account may be reconnected) and was left unargued; the PO has now ruled it: the mark clears, exactly as S148 step 2 promises (*"The mark on the account clears"*), and if the customer still does not pay **the ladder starts again**. **Worked, as shown to the PO:** `A-11340` was `Marked disconnect` → cut → reconnected owing **$8,315.00** and went back to `Marked disconnect`; it now reads **`Active`**. `Active` is the tenant's seeded `active` row, chosen by CODE (BR-CUS-055) — never by behaviour class alone, because `Marked disconnect` shares class `billable`. `disconnect_cases.status_before_cut` is still recorded at the cut and is now a historical fact that decides nothing. Where the tenant has no live `active` row (a provisioning gap BR-TEN-036 forbids) the account keeps its status and `RESTORED_STATUS_NOT_RECORDED` names why. | PO 2026-08-25 (DEF-701) · **PO 2026-08-30 (OQ-1170)** · **PO 2026-10-29 (DEF-CUS-10668)** · BR-CUS-055 · BR-CUS-103 · `DSC-02.4` | CONFIRMED · **AMENDED 2026-08-30 — assigned at the cut, with the final bill and the settlement** | W7 · `06-backend/tests/test_cr052_w0_disconnected_at_the_cut.py` · `06-backend/tests/test_cr052_w0_the_23_23_case.py` |
| BR-CUS-108 | **A PARKED WIZARD NEVER HOLDS MONEY — A DEPOSIT TAKEN IS A PAYMENT ROW (PO ruling 2026-08-25, resolving DEF-711).** B18 proposed this statement as `BR-CUS-085` while B18's own §5.7.7 **also** cited `BR-CUS-085` for the transfer wizard's *"the deposit stays with you"* — one id, two claims, and the register held neither. The PO ruled: **085 belongs to the DEPOSIT rule; the parked-wizard rule takes a new number**, which is this one. The reasoning recorded with it: 085 was already cited in shipped code paths for the deposit meaning, so moving it would have required finding and re-pointing live citations to settle a proposal that had none. **The rule itself:** a wizard parked mid-flight holds no money. If a deposit was taken, it is a **payment row** against the account like any other payment — never a value cached on the wizard's own state — so parking, resuming or abandoning the wizard cannot create, move or lose money. Hard rule 4's discipline: money exists as rows, never as screen state. | PO 2026-08-25 (DEF-711) · B18 §5.7.7 + §6.4 | CONFIRMED | W7 |
| BR-CUS-085 | **THE DEPOSIT STAYS WITH THE CUSTOMER ON A TRANSFER — IT IS NOT REFUNDED AND RE-COLLECTED (id allocated by PO ruling 2026-08-25, resolving DEF-711; statement TRANSCRIBED from B18 §5.7.7, not newly ruled).** **The statement, verbatim from `TRF-02.1`'s `STAYS WITH YOU` badge:** *"This customer is not leaving. Their deposit, their discounts and their history move with them — nothing is refunded and re-collected."* B18 §5.7.7 then names it: *"That is `BR-CUS-085`'s reason stated to a clerk, and it is the sentence the whole wizard exists to make true."* The same sentence is drawn three more times — the wizard frame's sub *"Five steps · one job, not two. The deposit moves with them."*, the Transfers KPI caption `DEPOSITS CARRYING` *"not refunded and re-collected"*, and `TC-TRF-3`: *"both carried; **the deposit is not refunded and re-collected**"* — and B18 task 7.3 states it as work: *"Deposit carries **without** a refund-and-recollect; arrears follow"*. **WHY THIS ID CARRIES THIS MEANING AND NOT THE OTHER ONE (DEF-711).** B18 cited `BR-CUS-085` twice for two different statements — §5.7.7 for this one, and its §6.4 NEW-rules table for *"a parked wizard never holds money — a deposit taken is a payment row"* — and this register held neither, running 083 straight to 086. **PO ruling 2026-08-25: 085 is the DEPOSIT rule; the parked-wizard rule takes a new number**, which is `BR-CUS-108`. The recorded reasoning: **085 was already cited in shipped code paths for the deposit meaning, so moving it would mean re-pointing live citations to settle a proposal that had none.** B18's §6.4 row keeps its wording and is stamped `SUPERSEDED (PO 2026-08-25, DEF-711 → BR-CUS-108)`, the `disconnects.CUT_WILL` posture. ⚠ **WHAT THIS RULE BINDS IS THE PROHIBITION, NOT A MOVEMENT.** The sentence B18 signs off is a statement about what must NOT happen, and it is guaranteed exactly: on the outgoing account `deposit_on_file` is **not touched**, so no refund is raised and no `payments.refunds` row is created; the incoming account is opened with **no deposit collected**, because `deposit_on_file` is in `services.REFUSED_FIELDS` (BR-CUS-023) and there is no collection door to walk through by accident. `apps/customers/transfers.py` commits with `"refund_raised": False` and a test asserts it. ⛔ **AND IT DOES NOT SAY THE POSITION MOVES — DO NOT READ IT AS CLOSING `OQ-365`.** Whether the `$153.50` crosses gross or the close settles it against the `$542.10` first is **OPEN** (`OQ-365`, non-blocking): `BR-CUS-024`'s carry-forward mechanics are **PROPOSED**, and `BR-CUS-025` says in as many words that closure is *"THE ONLY TIME THE DEPOSIT MOVES"*. So nothing is settled, refunded, chased or moved, and `transfers.DEPOSIT_HAS_NO_TRANSFER_PATH` / `TRANSFER_MONEY_RULE_MISSING` state the absence on every money statement. ⚠ **THE BADGE'S OTHER TWO NOUNS ARE DRAWN COPY WITH NO MECHANISM — measured 2026-08-25:** nothing in `apps/customers/transfers.py` carries a **discount** or a **history** across a transfer, and no rule anywhere states how one would. The sentence is rendered as drawn; only its deposit half has a stated guarantee, and inventing the other two here is the one thing this row must not do. **WHERE IT IS RELIED ON:** `07-frontend/src/features/customers/TransferWizard.tsx` renders the sentence as drawn copy (its §1a comment records that it cited no id while the register held none — that block may now cite this row, see DEF-711 follow-up) · `TransferWizard.test.tsx` asserts *"Stays with you"* and *"nothing is refunded and re-collected"* · `EP-CUS-10` §2 row *"the drawn STAYS-WITH-YOU sentence"* · `06-backend/apps/customers/transfers.py` §3. | B18 §5.7.7 (`TRF-02.1` badge, the naming sentence) · §5.7.1 KPI caption · §5.7.5 `TC-TRF-3` · task 7.3 · **id allocation: PO 2026-08-25 (DEF-711)** · BR-CUS-023 (the refused input) · BR-CUS-024 (PROPOSED mechanics) · BR-CUS-025 · **OQ-365 (OPEN)** · `apps/customers/transfers.py` | **CONFIRMED (the prohibition — transcribed 2026-08-25 from B18's own statement; id allocated by PO ruling 2026-08-25) · ⛔ the deposit POSITION moving is NOT part of this rule — BR-CUS-024 PROPOSED, OQ-365 OPEN · ⚠ the "discounts and history" clause is DRAWN COPY, no mechanism** | US-CUS-029 / `TransferWizard.test.tsx` *"the drawn badge states the mechanism the whole flow exists for"* · `TC-TRF-3` |
| BR-CUS-109 | **A SERVICE ADDRESS BELONGS TO THE UNIT; AN ACCOUNT CARRIES A BILLING ADDRESS (PO ruling 2026-08-26, answering the address half of OQ-386).** PO verbatim: *"service address are part of unit, unit are physical entity, hence they will always retain address. customer has billing address and service address. servcie address is coming from unit, billing address can be same or diferent. after account is clsoe you can keep the billing address on account - does not make a difference"*. **A unit is a PHYSICAL ENTITY and its address is a property of the place, not of whoever is billed for it** — so a customer never "holds" or "loses" a service address, and nothing about a cut, a close or a move changes it. The account's BILLING address is a separate fact, may differ from the service address, and **may be kept on the account after it closes** — the PO ruled that harmless in as many words. ⚠ **THIS RULE ANSWERS ADDRESS AND NOTHING ELSE. IT MUST NOT BE READ AS SETTLING OCCUPANCY**, which is the different question of whether the utility may sign a NEW customer up at that unit while a cut account is still open and still owing — see OQ-386, still open on that half. The two were conflated in the question as originally put; they are separated here so no lane reads one off the other. | PO 2026-08-26 (OQ-386, address half) | CONFIRMED | — |
| BR-CUS-110 | **A CUT ENDS OCCUPANCY, AND THE DEBT FOLLOWS THE PERSON (PO ruling 2026-08-26, closing OQ-386's occupancy half).** PO verbatim: *"B Yes, a cut ends occupancy and the debt follows the person"*. So the moment a service is cut, **the utility may sign a new customer up at that unit** — the premises is released even though the old account stays open and still owes. **Worked, on the account the question was put with:** `A-11488` was cut on **2026-08-18** owing **$612.75**; 12 Foundry Row is available to another move-in from that date, and the **$612.75 stays with `A-11488`** rather than attaching to the address. **The debt is the PERSON'S, not the premises'** — which is the same direction BR-CUS-097 already runs when a sub-account's unpayable residue moves to its master rather than becoming the building's. ⚠ **THIS MAKES `services.OCCUPYING_BEHAVIOR_CLASSES` CORRECT AS WRITTEN** — occupancy is granted to `billable` and `billable_base_only` only, so a `Disconnected` account releases its unit by construction, and what was reported as a hazard is the intended behaviour. ⚠⚠ **AND IT LEAVES ONE THING THAT MUST BE BUILT BEFORE THE STATUS IS ASSIGNED: A CUT CUSTOMER MUST STILL BE ABLE TO REACH A TERMINAL STATE.** `services.move_out` refuses a non-occupant by name (`not_an_occupant`), so under this ruling the only close-out path the product has stops working for exactly the customers who most need to be closed out — settled, written off, or gone. **A close-out that does not require occupancy is owed (DEF-724).** Ruling (A), which I recommended and the PO rejected, is recorded here so the reasoning is not lost: `DSC-02.4` cuts *"leaving the account open"* and BR-MET-036 vacates a unit when the account is CLOSED. Those sentences are about the ACCOUNT staying open, which this ruling preserves — it is occupancy, not the account, that the cut ends. | PO 2026-08-26 (OQ-386) · BR-CUS-097 · BR-MET-036 | CONFIRMED | — |

## CR-039 wave D tasks 17–19 — a move-in and a move-out ARE records (2026-08-27)

PO ruling, verbatim and in full: *"you cna keep them as records for audit perpuses"*. The four rules below are the record, its contents, its immutability and its number. **Only the first three are the PO's**; `BR-CUS-153` is the BUILD's and says so.

| Rule | Statement | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-150 | **A MOVE-IN AND A MOVE-OUT EACH LEAVE A RECORD, AND THE RECORD IS OF THE JOB (PO ruling 2026-08-27, closing CR-039 wave D task 17).** PO verbatim and in full: *"you cna keep them as records for audit perpuses"*, answering *"what ARE a move-in and a move-out, as records?"*. Before this, neither flow left anything: the wizard wrote occupancy and account state and discarded everything else, which is CR-039's own trace finding 5 — *"move-in and move-out have NO RECORD AT ALL"*, consequence *"A clerk cannot quote a reference; nothing on a bill can point at the job that caused a change."* **The purpose the PO named is what decides the shape**: *for audit purposes* is a requirement about PROVENANCE, so the row records **that somebody did the job, who, when, and on what terms** and records nothing about its effects. ⚠ **A ROW EXISTS ONLY FOR A COMPLETED JOB.** There is no status, no step and no state machine: a `transfers` row exists while a job is IN FLIGHT, and this is written by a flow that has already committed, in the same transaction. `move_reads.STEP_HAS_NO_STORE` is therefore **NOT closed by this rule** — it asks for *"a move-in/move-out case row carrying step + state"*, and B18 §5.5.2's own footnote rules that one out (*"a move-in is short enough that the wizard is the record"*). | PO 2026-08-27 (CR-039 wave D task 17) | CONFIRMED | `06-backend/tests/test_cr039_wd_move_records.py` |
| BR-CUS-151 | **THE RECORD CARRIES PROVENANCE AND NOTHING THAT ALREADY HAS AN OWNER (derived 2026-08-27 from BR-CUS-150's own stated reason, same ruling, same session).** The ruling's words: *"a record that duplicated them would be a second copy of a fact that already has an owner, which is how two numbers come to disagree."* **WHAT IT CARRIES:** the acting clerk · the moment · the effective date · **a COUNT of the readings the job committed** · and, on a chained move-in, the OUTGOING account it followed. **WHAT IT MUST NOT CARRY, each with the row that owns it:** occupancy (DERIVED from the status class, BR-MET-036 — there is no column to copy) · the account's status / `opened_at` / `closed_at` (`accounts`, written by the same transaction) · the readings themselves (`meter_reads`, through the BR-CUS-021 event door) · the agreement dates (`service_agreements`) · any money figure at all (`Transfer`'s own reason: *"a stored `deposit_carried` would read as money that moved on a path where nothing moved"*). ⚠ **`from_account` IS THE ONE FACT THIS RULE ADDS RATHER THAN RELOCATES.** `services.move_in` has always returned the outgoing account as `moved_in_after_account_id` and stored it NOWHERE, so the chain from the tenancy that ended to the one that began was lost the moment the response was closed. ⚠ **`performed_by` / `performed_at` DUPLICATE THE AUDIT ROW ON PURPOSE** — hard rule 7 keeps `account.moved_in` / `account.closed` in `audit_log` whatever this table does, and `audit_log` is keyed by entity and action and is not a list a clerk can filter, sort or quote a reference from. | derived from PO 2026-08-27 · BR-MET-036 · BR-CUS-021 · CLAUDE.md rule 7 | CONFIRMED | `test_cr039_wd_move_records.py::test_the_record_copies_nothing_that_already_has_an_owner` |
| BR-CUS-152 | **A MOVE RECORD IS APPEND-ONLY IN SPIRIT: CORRECTING ONE IS A NEW ROW (PO ruling 2026-08-27, third clause of BR-CUS-150's ruling).** Verbatim: *"a move-out that happened is a thing that happened. Correcting one is a new row, the same posture as every other financial record (CLAUDE.md hard rule 4)."* So **nothing in this product updates a `move_records` row** — `record_move` inserts and no service function edits. ⚠ **THE CONSEQUENCE A READ SIDE MUST HANDLE:** an account can hold more than one record of a kind, and the NEWEST by `performed_at` is the current statement of the job while the earlier ones stay on file. `move_reads.move_records_by_account` orders by `-performed_at` for exactly that reason. ⚠ **`updated_at` EXISTS AND IS NEVER WRITTEN AFTER THE INSERT** — it is there for parity with every other customers table and for the sample-data sweep, and a lane finding it should not read it as permission. | PO 2026-08-27 · CLAUDE.md hard rule 4 | CONFIRMED | `test_cr039_wd_move_records.py` |
| BR-CUS-153 | **EACH FLOW GETS ITS OWN NUMBER SERIES — `MVI-26-0001` AND `MVO-26-0001` — AND THIS IS AN EXTENSION OF A RULING RATHER THAN A RULING (build decision 2026-08-27, CR-039 wave D task 18).** ⚠ **THE PO RULED THE RECORD AND NOT THE NUMBER**, and the decision log says so in as many words: *"IT DOES NOT SETTLE D18's NUMBERING, and that is not a quibble … a signature covers only what was shown."* **What made it buildable anyway is that the argument against it died the same day.** The transfer's format was ruled with a reason — *"it is one job spanning two accounts, and neither account number names it"* — which does not carry to a job belonging to ONE account whose number already names it. Then a DISCONNECT, a one-account job, got its own series on *"it needs its own series"* (OQ-427). **It cannot be true both that a one-account job needs no number of its own and that a disconnect, a one-account job, needs one.** Leaving these unnumbered would rest on an argument the PO has just overturned. **ONE WORD FROM THE PO REVERSES THIS.** **TWO SERIES AND NOT ONE SHARED `MV-`, and that is the build's call:** a clerk reading `MV-26-0004` back down a phone could not say whether it named an arrival or a departure, and the two are separate flows on separate tabs with separate wizards. **Allocated BY NAME INSIDE the flow's own transaction** (BR-TEN-035); where the tenant has no series row the writer REPORTS `numbering_series_missing` and writes NULL rather than raising — a household must not be refused a move-in for a numbering gap. | BUILD (CR-039 wave D task 18) extending PO 2026-08-27 (OQ-427) · BR-TEN-035 | **CONFIRMED as a BUILD decision, not a PO ruling** — flagged as an EXTENSION | `test_cr039_wd_move_records.py::test_the_first_move_in_and_move_out_on_a_new_tenant_are_mvi_and_mvo_26_0001` |


## DEF-724 — a disconnected customer gets a CLOSE-OUT ACT (2026-08-27)

PO ruling, verbatim and in full: *"A disconnected customer can never be closed - Add a separate close-out act — Cleaner in the record"*. `BR-CUS-430` is the PO's. **`BR-CUS-431` is the BUILD's and says so** — the ruling settles that the act is separate; it does not say which column the date lands in, and that had to be derived from two CONFIRMED rules rather than picked.

| Rule | Statement | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-430 | **A CUT CUSTOMER REACHES A TERMINAL STATE THROUGH A SEPARATE CLOSE-OUT ACT, AND NEVER THROUGH A MOVE-OUT (PO ruling 2026-08-27, closing DEF-724).** PO verbatim: *"A disconnected customer can never be closed - Add a separate close-out act — Cleaner in the record"*. **The problem it fixes, measured:** `BR-CUS-110` ends occupancy at the cut while leaving the account open, and `services.move_out` refuses a non-occupant by name (`customers.account.not_an_occupant`) — so from 2026-08-26 the only terminal path in the product stopped working for exactly the customers who most need it: settled, written off, or gone. `A-11488`, cut **2026-08-18** owing **$612.75** with 12 Foundry Row already re-let, could not be finished by anybody. **THE REASONING IS PART OF THE RULE AND AN IMPLEMENTATION MAY NOT DROP IT:** a close-out writes a date **nobody can un-write** — once an account is closed, correcting it means new rows and never an edit (CLAUDE.md hard rule 4). Relaxing `move_out`'s occupancy check to admit a cut customer would file **a disconnection as a move-out** in the permanent record, and to a regulator those are not the same event. **So the two doors are DISJOINT IN BOTH DIRECTIONS:** `move_out` keeps refusing a non-occupant, and `close_out` refuses an OCCUPANT (`customers.account.close_out_still_occupying`) — an occupant leaving is a move-out and must not be filed as a close-out either. ⚠ **AN OUTSTANDING BALANCE DOES NOT BLOCK A CLOSE-OUT AND THIS RULE ADDS NOTHING NEW ABOUT IT:** `BR-CUS-082` (CONFIRMED, PO 2026-08-24) already rules that a debt surviving closure is STILL CHASED with the ladder restarting at TIER 1 as a COLLECTIONS sequence, never a service one — closure with a balance is the case that rule was written for. The close-out therefore records the surviving balance and hands it to `disconnects.restart_after_closure`, and **ties nothing back to a master**: `BR-CUS-097` as qualified 2026-08-27 makes the tie-back a clerk's act in the S20 move-out wizard, and this act does not collect that consent. | PO 2026-08-27 (DEF-724) · BR-CUS-110 · BR-CUS-030 · BR-CUS-082 · BR-CUS-097 | CONFIRMED | 06-backend/tests/test_def724_close_out.py |
| BR-CUS-431 | **A CLOSE-OUT WRITES THE DATE SERVICE ENDED — THE CUT DATE — INTO `accounts.closed_at`, NOT THE DAY THE CLERK PERFORMED IT (build decision 2026-08-27, derived; NOT the PO's).** ⚠ **THE PO RULED THE ACT AND NOT THE COLUMN.** `DEF-724`'s own triage names the hazard exactly: *"the wrong choice writes a `closed_at` for a tenancy that actually ended at the cut, which is a date on a customer's record that no correction row can un-say."* **The derivation, and it is a measured consequence rather than a preference:** `accounts.closed_at` is the ONLY thing `BR-CUS-056` measures the next occupant's start day against — `selectors.last_closed_account_for_unit` takes the greatest `closed_at` among terminal accounts on the unit and `services._refuse_if_start_lands_on_the_prior_move_day` refuses any start on or before it. `BR-CUS-110` releases the unit **at the cut**, so the next occupant may lawfully start the day after. **Worked:** `A-11488` is cut **2026-08-18** and closed out **2026-09-30**. Writing `2026-09-30` would refuse a lawful start on any day from **2026-08-19 to 2026-09-30** with `earliest_start 2026-10-01` — forty-three days of a unit the utility was entitled to re-let. Writing `2026-08-18` gives `earliest_start 2026-08-19`, which is what `BR-CUS-110` grants. `BR-CUS-103` agrees from the money side: base charges stop at the cut, so the cut day IS the last day the outgoing account is charged for, which is precisely `BR-CUS-056`'s *"the outgoing account pays the move day"*. **Every open `service_agreements.ended_on` takes the same date, for the same reason.** ⚠ **THE ACT'S OWN DATE IS NOT LOST — IT IS RECORDED WHERE IT IS TRUE:** `disconnect_cases.closed_out_on` carries the day the account was closed out and `closed_out_by` carries who did it, so the record reads *the service ended on the cut date and the account was closed out on this later day, by this person* — which is what *"cleaner in the record"* asks for. **NOTHING IS WRITTEN TO `move_records`**: a close-out is not a move, and a `kind='move_out'` row would be the very falsification `BR-CUS-430` forbids. | derived 2026-08-27 from BR-CUS-056 + BR-CUS-110 + BR-CUS-103 · DEF-724 | CONFIRMED (build decision — **one word from the PO reverses it**) | 06-backend/tests/test_def724_close_out.py |
| BR-CUS-171 | **AN OCCUPYING ACCOUNT IS A CHILD OF THE PREMISES' OWNER ACCOUNT (PO 2026-08-27, closing `OQ-531`) — AND SINCE THE SAME DAY THE OWNER IS **DECLARED**, NOT DERIVED FROM THE BUILDING'S SHAPE (`DEF-650`).** ⚠ **THIS ROW IS WRITTEN LATE AND SAYS SO.** The rule was ruled and BUILT on 2026-08-27 (`master_account_for` · `sub_accounts_for_master` · the `master_account`/`sub_accounts` keys on the account read) and no rule row was ever written for it — it lived in a docstring and a decision-log paragraph. It is written here by the lane that SUPERSEDED half of it, because a rule with no row cannot be found by the next lane to change it. **THE LINK ITSELF — UNCHANGED AND CONFIRMED.** A master answers its children; every other account answers its master; both are `[]`/null for a standalone account, which is a real answer and not a gap. The link is DERIVED and stored nowhere, so it needs no maintenance: "occupying" keys off the status's BEHAVIOUR CLASS and never its label (BR-CUS-055), so a closed tenant drops out on the day they close and a pending one never appears. A master is never its own child. ⚠⚠ **THE MECHANISM IS SUPERSEDED 2026-08-27 (PO ruling, decision 4).** It used to find the owner GEOMETRICALLY: walk the premises' COMMON AREAS and take the `kind='master'` account that pays for one of them. PO verbatim, after the question was restated in plain English for them: *"to find a building's master today, the code does not look at the owner at all — it looks at the building's COMMON AREAS (the hallway, the car park) and finds the account that pays for them. Whoever pays for the hallway is treated as the owner. … The ruling replaces all of it: the premises names its owner, the owner is the master, and nothing is inferred from hallways."* `master_account_for` now reads `premises → owner → premises_owners.master_account_id` (`BR-MET-400`) and no longer calls `common_area_units_for_premises`. **THE TWO STATED WEAKNESSES ARE GONE BY CONSTRUCTION, not by a better tie-break:** a premises with **no common area** — a single-family home, the PO's own next question — had no master at all; and where **two masters** covered two common areas this refused by answering `None` (*"naming the first would make the owner of one building's hallway the parent of another's tenants on a tie-break nobody chose"*). There is no tie to break once the owner is declared. ⚠ **RETIRED, NOT KEPT AS A FALLBACK**, and the ruling gives the reason: *"a fallback that disagrees with the declared owner would silently bill a different party."* A premises whose owner is not declared answers `None` — honest, visible on PRM-02, and fixed by declaring the owner rather than by inferring one. ⚠ **ONE READING IS KEPT AND IT IS NOT A FALLBACK ABOUT THE OWNER:** `sub_accounts_for_master` still falls back to the master's own ANCHOR unit's premises where nothing declares that account as an owner. Those are the pre-ruling master accounts, still covering the common areas `create_master_account` attached them to; it answers *which premises this account covers*, never *who the owner is*. | PO 2026-08-27 (OQ-531) · PO 2026-08-27 (DEF-650, decision 4) · BR-CUS-055 · BR-CUS-068 · BR-MET-400 | **CONFIRMED (the link) / SUPERSEDED IN ITS MECHANISM (how the owner is found)** | `06-backend/tests/test_br_cus_171_master_children.py` · `06-backend/tests/test_def650_owner_is_the_master_account.py` |
| BR-CUS-172 | **AN ACCOUNT NUMBER IS THE UTILITY'S — TAKEN FROM THE FILE VERBATIM, REQUIRED, AND NEVER ISSUED BY US — AND THE ACCOUNT GETS AN `external_ref` OF ITS OWN, WHICH IT DID NOT HAVE (PO ruling 2026-08-27, `CR-046` C4/C5).** ⚠ **RENUMBERED FROM `BR-CUS-171` AND REHEADLINED 2026-08-27 (`CR-046` wave H3), AND BOTH HALVES WERE REAL DEFECTS.** `BR-CUS-171` was minted twice in one day for two different rules — the master/child link (the row above, `OQ-531`) and this one — so **G17 was RED on the tip** and `common`'s id-keyed lookups had two rows to choose from; the later definition is renumbered, which is what G17's own banner instructs and the reason it refuses a baseline entry (`DEF-1780`). And this row's HEADLINE still read *"issued from the tenant's `account` series, never taken from an uploaded file"* — **the exact opposite of the ruling it carries** — because clause 1 was reversed eleven hours after the row was written and the reversal was appended INSIDE the body while the headline stayed. A headline is what a grep returns and what a reader stops at; a wrong one in a signed rule is worse than a missing one (`DEF-1781`). **The body below is unchanged and was already correct.** The original headline, for the supersedes chain: *"AN ACCOUNT NUMBER IS ISSUED FROM THE TENANT'S `account` SERIES, NEVER TAKEN FROM AN UPLOADED FILE"* — **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-27 by the PO's own reversal, quoted in full below.** ⚠ **READ THIS FIRST, BECAUSE `CR-046` SAYS THE OPPOSITE AND THE CHECK IT ASKED FOR IS WHAT FOUND IT.** C4 warns *"Read `BR-CUS-054`'s `external_ref` first — accounts already have the legacy-reference column this change gives the other three"*, and C5 adds *"so C5 may be a no-op there"*. **It is not a no-op. `BR-CUS-054`'s `external_ref` is a column on `customers` — the PERSON — and this is the `accounts` table.** One landlord is **one** customer row with **one** `customers.external_ref` and **fourteen** account rows with **fourteen** legacy account numbers; fourteen values do not fit in one column, so under the ruling thirteen of them would simply be lost. ⚠⚠ **AND CLAUSE 1 WAS REVERSED BY THE PO ELEVEN HOURS AFTER IT WAS BUILT — ACCOUNTS ARE THE ONE UPLOAD OF SIX THAT DOES *NOT* CALL SETTINGS › NUMBERING.** PO verbatim, 2026-08-27: *"we use utility provdied account number here - we dont create our own number - since it will create lot of change management for utility ; if row dont have account number reject it - please update the logic; here we dont call settings -> account number logic"*. **THE ACCOUNT NUMBER IS TAKEN FROM THE FILE, VERBATIM, AND A BLANK CELL IS A REJECT** (`ACC.NUMBER_REQUIRED`, structural). **IT IS AN EXCEPTION, NOT AN INCONSISTENCY, AND THE PRINCIPLE UNDER BOTH RULINGS IS ONE PRINCIPLE: THE SIDE THAT OWNS THE IDENTIFIER ISSUES IT.** A meter number is OURS and the customer has never seen it, so issuing it removes collisions at no cost. An account number is THEIRS — it is on every bill they kept, in their bank's payee list, on the standing order — so renumbering is not a data change, it is a letter to every customer, a reprint of every form, and a year of people quoting a number the staff can no longer find (`BR-TEN-035`'s own instinct). **THERE IS NOTHING SAFE TO INVENT:** a number we made up is one the customer has never seen and the utility's own old records cannot match. **DO NOT ADD A BLANK-MEANS-ALLOCATE PATH HERE**, and do not "harmonise" this target with the other three. ⚠ **AND THE RULING CLOSES A MEASURED DEFECT** — `BR-CUS-051`/`OQ-165`'s worked case on `bbu-accounts-cutover.csv`: the **1,144** rows carrying explicit numbers all errored as duplicates *(working as intended)* while the **96** blank-number rows **quietly created 96 new accounts on units that already had one**. Those 96 are exactly what `ACC.NUMBER_REQUIRED` now rejects. **SO CLAUSE 1 READS: THE NUMBER IS THE UTILITY'S, THE COLUMN IS REQUIRED, THE SERIES IS NOT CALLED, AND THE DRY RUN PROJECTS NOTHING.** **(2) THE COLUMN IS THE MATCH KEY**, and an UPDATE never changes it — it is the key we matched on, and a re-run that rewrote it would make the next re-run match nothing. **(3) THE WRITE MODE DECIDES — *Insert new only* → **REJECTED** (`ACC.NUMBER_ON_FILE` becomes an ERROR for the run); *Insert & update* → the DETAILS are updated through `update_from_import`. ⚠ **AN UPDATE RE-POSTS NO OPENING BALANCE, AND THAT IS HARD RULE 4 RATHER THAN A SCOPE CHOICE**: the four `ob_*` buckets became journal entries at the first load (GT-16 ❄️), and a journal entry is corrected by a NEW entry, never an edited one — so `IMPORT_UPDATE_FIELDS` excludes every money field, `unit_id` (BR-CUS-017) and `account_no` (the key we matched on). A number that is **not** on file is INVENTED and is refused in both modes (`ACC.NUMBER_NOT_ON_FILE`). **(4) `accounts.external_ref` IS UNIQUE PER TENANT**, case-insensitively and partially (`uq_accounts_tenant_external_ref_ci`, customers 0033) — the `customers.external_ref` posture on the row one table along, and it is what makes a corrected re-run match. **Worked:** 1,213 accounts migrate carrying **the utility's own numbers, verbatim** — `AC-2026-00001 … AC-2026-01213` in this fixture only because that is what the file holds, **not because a series issued them** (⚠ this sentence read as though we had numbered them until wave H corrected it; the series is not called on this target at all) — with **$184,320.55** of opening AR (GT-16). The operator corrects twelve deposit dates and re-runs the SAME file. Without a unique legacy key every row reads as new — and `BR-CUS-017` rejects all 1,213 at the unit seat, so the operator meets **1,213 refusals on a file the product told them to correct**. With it: **1,213 matches, 12 updates, and NOT ONE new journal entry.** | PO 2026-08-27 · `CR-046` C4/C5 · `DEF-1660` · BR-AST-049 (the meters precedent) · BR-CUS-054 (the CUSTOMER's legacy id — a different fact) · BR-CUS-017 · GT-16 ❄️ · hard rule 4 | **CONFIRMED · CLAUSE 1 REVERSED 2026-08-27 (the number is the utility's, not ours)** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr046_identifiers.py` |
| BR-CUS-510 | **A BALANCE CELL MAY READ *AWAITING* ONLY WHERE NOTHING **CAN** BE OWED YET — AND NEVER OVER A FIGURE (build decision 2026-08-27, closing `DEF-628`; NOT a PO ruling).** `DEF-127` established that an ABSENCE must not be spelled like a zero. This is its neighbour and a different fact: here the zero is **real** — the ledger was asked and answered nothing — but on a `Pending activation` account it is the wrong answer to render, because that account **cannot be billed yet**. B18 §5.1.5 requires the shape to show a balance reading *awaiting* and `LAST BILL` = `—`, never `$0.00`. Measured on a live seeded row by the CR-030 W2 seed lane: `balance='0.000000'`, `balance_unavailable=()`, `last_bill_at=None`, `ageing_segment=None` — every check in the repo green, and a fabricated-looking zero on the screen. ⛔ **`DEF-628`'s OWN PRESCRIBED FIX MUST NOT BE IMPLEMENTED AND THIS ROW EXISTS BECAUSE OF IT.** Its `prevention` column (2026-08-24) reads *"the cell should key off `non_billable` rather than off the amount"*. **`BR-CUS-107` landed the next day** (PO 2026-08-25, migration `customers/0025`) and put a SIXTH status into that same behaviour class: `Disconnected`. That migration's own worked row is **`A-11488`, cut 2026-08-18 owing $612.75**, reading `Disconnected / non-billable`. Keying the cell off `non_billable` alone would render *awaiting* over that **$612.75** — hiding the arrears on precisely the account the disconnect ladder exists to chase, which is `DEF-127` again pointed at real money. The note was correct when written and was overtaken twenty-four hours later; r11 step 0 is what catches that. **SO THE RULE IS FOUR CLAUSES, AND A FIGURE IS NEVER HIDDEN: (1)** the ledger **ANSWERED** (`positions.available`) — where it could not, `balance_unavailable` governs and says a different thing; **(2)** the derived amount is **exactly zero** — any figure at all is rendered, and this is the clause that saves `A-11488`; **(3)** the status **BEHAVIOUR CLASS** is `non_billable` — the class and never a code, because `BR-CUS-055` lets a tenant add and rename statuses; **(4)** **no bill has ever been issued** (`last_bill_at is None`) — a cut account that has been billed has a history, and calling its settled zero *awaiting* would invent a future for a relationship that is over. ⚠ **THE FIGURE IS STILL SERVED AND IS NEVER NULLED.** `balance` keeps its `0.000000`; the conjunction is served BESIDE it as `balance_not_yet_billable`. Nulling it would claim the books could not be asked — false — and would move the row into the `balance_gt` filter's *"kept because unreadable"* branch on a premise that branch does not hold. ⚠ **AND NO SCREEN RE-DERIVES IT.** The obvious client-side guess is `status_behavior_class === "non_billable"`, which is the trap above; the server publishes the conjunction and the S17 row, the S21 header and any later surface read the one fact. **Worked, three rows:** pending-activation `A-10001` (answered `0.000000`, `non_billable`, never billed) → **Awaiting**; cut `A-11488` (`612.750000`) → **$612.75**; active `A-10003` (`0.000000`, `billable`) → **$0.00**, because settled IS an answer. | `DEF-628` (CR-030 W2 seed lane, measured) · B18 §5.1.5 · `BR-CUS-107` + `customers/0025` (the overtaking ruling) · `BR-CUS-055` (the class is read, never a code) · `BR-CUS-015`/`DEF-127` (the sibling rule) | **CONFIRMED — a build decision, made under CLAUDE.md law 13 (*"everything else is ours to decide and write down"*); it collides with no signed rule, moves no ❄️ FROZEN figure and needs no fact only the PO holds** | `06-backend/tests/test_def_628_awaiting_balance.py` (5 cases, 3 proved RED against the prevention-as-written) · `07-frontend/src/features/customers/AccountsPage.test.tsx` (the row cell, both directions) |


## DEF-2030 / DEF-2031 — the transfer and the ladder get their doors, and they get OPPOSITE shapes (2026-08-28)

`G52` measured **1,838 lines of built, tested, role-gated write behind no door**, and eighteen of the twenty-nine offenders were these two files. Both wizards were finished and mounted in `App.tsx`, posting to addresses that 404'd. Opening them forced one question — *does the wizard's one door run the staged service calls server-side, or does each stage get a route?* — and **the two workflows get opposite answers.** Both rows are **BUILD decisions under law 13** and say so; neither collides with a signed rule, moves a ❄️ FROZEN figure, or needed a fact only the PO holds.

| Rule | Statement | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-590 | **THE TRANSFER WIZARD COMMITS THROUGH ONE DOOR THAT RUNS ALL FIVE STAGED WRITES INSIDE ONE `transaction.atomic()`; THE FIVE STAGES GET NO ROUTES OF THEIR OWN (build decision 2026-08-28, closing `DEF-2031`).** `POST /transfers/commit` takes the wizard's whole plan and runs `start_transfer` → `set_target_unit` → `set_dates` → `elect_money` → `commit_transfer`. **THE REASON IS NOT TIDINESS AND AN IMPLEMENTATION MAY NOT DROP IT.** `uq_transfers_open_from` allows **ONE open transfer per outgoing account**, and `US-CUS-027 §8` records that **no screen shows a transfer in flight**. Five routes means five transactions, so a failure at stage 4 strands an `in_progress` row that **blocks that account from ever being transferred again** and that nobody can find to cancel — a silent, permanent, per-account loss needing a DBA. Under one transaction the same failure rolls back the `Transfer.objects.create` with everything else. **Worked:** a clerk moves `A-10650` to unit `U-4021`, out **2026-09-01**, in **2026-09-01**, deposit **$153.50** elected to carry, arrear **$542.10** elected to follow. A colleague takes `U-4021` 200 ms earlier, so `BR-CUS-017`'s advisory lock inside `_open_account` refuses at stage 5. **Rows surviving: ZERO**, and the clerk picks another unit. Under five routes: **one** `transfers` row on `A-10650`, un-transferable for the life of the database. ⚠ **BOTH MONEY ELECTIONS ARE PASSED EXPLICITLY, INCLUDING `False`** — `elect_money` falls back to the statement's own reading when a key is ABSENT, so a wizard that omitted them would have the server silently re-tick a box the clerk had cleared, and record that a human elected to carry a deposit they had just declined. ⚠ `money_statement` and `cancel_transfer` DO get routes and **neither is a stage**: the first is `TRF-02.4`'s read, the second is the only way out of an open transfer the **demo seeder** leaves behind. ⚠ **Django nests `atomic()` as savepoints**, so each service keeping its own block is correct and unchanged; no engine code was touched to mount this. | BUILD (lane DOORS, `DEF-2031`) · `BR-SHL-019` · `BR-CUS-017` · `uq_transfers_open_from` · `US-CUS-027 §8` · `12-governance/02-decision-log.md` 2026-08-28 | **CONFIRMED as a BUILD decision** — one word from the PO reverses it | `06-backend/tests/test_def2030_2031_wizard_doors.py::test_a_failure_at_any_stage_leaves_zero_rows_behind` (proved RED: removing the outer transaction leaves the row and the test names the harm) |
| BR-CUS-591 | **THE DISCONNECT LADDER IS *NOT* ORCHESTRATED: EACH OF ITS ELEVEN ACTS GETS ITS OWN ROUTE, AND `BR-SHL-019` IS SATISFIED BY `schedule_cut` ALONE (build decision 2026-08-28, closing `DEF-2030`).** The opposite answer to `BR-CUS-590`'s question, from the same test, and **the code proves it rather than an opinion**: `schedule_cut` refuses unless `case.tier == TIER_3` **and** `assert_service_proved(case)` passes. Tier 3 is reached by `advance` after statutory waits, and `assert_service_proved` demands `record_proof_of_service` — **a certified-mail receipt, signed by the recipient, that arrives days later in the post.** Orchestrating the ladder into one door would therefore mean **manufacturing, inside one HTTP request, a signed receipt for a certified letter that was never sent** — which is exactly what `BR-CUS-039` (*certified mail is REQUIRED on the final tier, and SMS never stands alone*) and `BR-CUS-100` (*a tier-3 cut must not be schedulable on an unrecorded service*) exist to prevent, and it ends with a household losing water on a letter nobody posted. **THE DECIDING TEST, STATED SO THE NEXT LANE CAN APPLY IT: not the shape of the service layer, but how much real-world time separates the stages and whether a precondition of a later stage is a document that arrives in the post.** A transfer is one clerk at one screen for ninety seconds; a ladder is a **case file**, not a wizard. **`BR-SHL-019` IS NOT VIOLATED:** the disconnect WIZARD's one commit is `schedule_cut`, one payload to the one address `DisconnectWizard.tsx` already posts to; the other ten acts are not its steps. **Worked:** Bramble Bakery `A-11812`, owing **$2,410.00** — `open_case` **2026-08-14** · `advance` to `TIER 2 · 10-DAY` **2026-08-24** · `advance` to `TIER 3 · 48-HOUR` and `record_notice` **2026-09-03** · the green card returns signed and `record_proof_of_service` records it **2026-09-08** · only then does `schedule_cut` accept a cut date of **2026-09-14**. **Five acts, twenty-five days, five doors.** ⚠ **`DisconnectRuleError` IS TRANSLATED AT THE BOUNDARY into one catalogue code (422 `BIZ_COMPLETENESS`) carrying `br_ref` and the service's own sentence**, which that exception's own docstring asks for BY NAME; without it every ladder refusal is a 500 and a clerk meets `BR-CUS-100` as a stack trace. ⚠ **NO OPEN QUESTION IS RESOLVED AND NONE IS SOFTENED:** `OQ-374` · `OQ-382` · `OQ-383` · `OQ-384` · `OQ-385` are all OPEN, the services already refuse correctly on each, and **nothing in these routes reads `new_service_block_for()`** — `OQ-384` has no subject yet, and consuming a three-valued block with no ruled subject would be choosing one. ⚠ **NO DATE IS COMPUTED HERE:** `DSC-02.3`'s earliest lawful cut is the notice date plus the statutory period **rolled past weekends and public holidays**, nothing in this product computes that roll, and a layer adding 48 hours would implement *"+2 days"* — a different rule that agrees with B18's fixture by luck, because Aug 22 2026 is a Saturday. | BUILD (lane DOORS, `DEF-2030`) · `BR-CUS-039` · `BR-CUS-100` · `BR-CUS-103`/`104`/`105` · `BR-BIL-007` · `BR-SHL-019` · `12-governance/02-decision-log.md` 2026-08-28 | **CONFIRMED as a BUILD decision** — one word from the PO reverses it | `06-backend/tests/test_def2030_2031_wizard_doors.py` (24 cases; the cut refusal, the boundary translation and the eleven distinct doors each asserted) |

## The deposit register and the books (2026-08-28, DEF-177 + DEF-1713)
| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-670 | **THE CUSTOMER DEPOSIT REGISTER MOVES WITH THE MONEY, IN BOTH DIRECTIONS — `accounts.deposit_on_file` is written when a deposit-purpose payment CLEARS and unwritten when one is REVERSED, by the same amount, in the same transaction as the ledger leg.** ⚠ **THIS IS NOT A HOLE IN `BR-CUS-023` AND THE DISTINCTION IS THE WHOLE RULE.** BR-CUS-023 refuses `deposit_on_file` on every door a person types into, because *how much deposit an account SHOULD hold* is a LOOKUP from the plan's deposit schedule (US-CUS-007, still unbuilt) and is nobody's free text. This rule is about a different fact — *how much the utility ACTUALLY HOLDS* — derived from a money event that has already reached the books, exactly as `nsf_count` is derived from a bounce that has already been reversed. Neither is an input, and `services.REFUSED_FIELDS` is untouched. **THE DEFECT IT CLOSES, DEF-177, OPEN SINCE 2026-08-16:** `apps/payments/ledger_seam.post_payment_cleared` had credited the **2300** deposit-liability account on every cleared deposit for a fortnight and **nothing had ever written the register column**, so the first utility to take a **$150.00** deposit got *books $150.00 / register $0.00* on the S36 tie-out and BR-LED-007 blocked their release — correctly, because the register really was not being kept. **PAYMENTS ASKS; CUSTOMERS WRITES** (`apps.customers.services.record_deposit_movement`, resolved BY NAME, the `record_nsf` shape): `accounts` is customers' table and payments may not write another module's (B1 / DB-16). **THE MOVEMENT IS THE AMOUNT THAT CLEARED, NEVER THE PLAN'S FIGURE** — a $150.00 deposit against a $300.00 schedule moves the register by $150.00, because the register states what is HELD and the gap between held and required is a collections question. That is also what makes the tie-out hold: **both sides are written from ONE figure.** **THE RELEASE IS EVERY REVERSAL REASON, NOT ONLY NSF** — BR-PAY-013's count is a judgement about the customer and belongs to a bounce alone; this is a statement about where the money IS, and a duplicate, an error or a wrong-account reversal moves it back just as a bounce does. **A RELEASE LARGER THAN WHAT IS HELD IS REFUSED, NEVER CLAMPED**: the ledger reversal takes the full amount out of 2300, so a register clamped at 0.00 would differ from the books by exactly the excess — the drift this rule exists to prevent, created by the code written to prevent it. ⛔ **IT DOES NOT DECIDE WHETHER A DEPOSIT WAS DUE.** US-CUS-007's plan-schedule lookup is still unbuilt and still owns that question. ⛔ **AND THE INTEREST HALF IS 0.00 BY ABSENCE, NOT BY CHOICE:** US-LED-004 §5 signs the register as *`deposit_on_file` + interest* and BR-CUS-057's accrual engine is unbuilt, so the interest contributes nothing to EITHER side today and will move BOTH the day it lands. | DEF-177 · BR-LED-007 (PO LF-7, 2026-08-08) · BR-CUS-023 · BR-CUS-025 · BR-CUS-050 · B1/DB-16 · `12-governance/02-decision-log.md` 2026-08-28 | **CONFIRMED as a BUILD decision (Eng, not a PO ruling)** — the RULE it serves, BR-LED-007, is PO-signed; where the register is written was ours and is recorded, and it is flagged in `12-governance/09-human-review-ledger.md` as NOT yet shown to the PO | `06-backend/tests/test_def177_def1713_deposit_register_and_books.py` (the cleared deposit, the second deposit that must not restamp the receipt date, the reversal, the over-release refusal) · `06-backend/tests/test_cr040_seeded_books_tie_out.py` |
| BR-CUS-671 | **A MIGRATED DEPOSIT POSTS A BALANCED OPENING ENTRY — DR opening-balance clearing / CR deposit liability — DATED AT CUTOVER, keyed per (batch, account), immutable.** **THE DATE IS BR-CUS-049 AND IS NOT A NEW RULING.** That rule is CONFIRMED (PO AC-4, 2026-08-08) and says opening balances post *"as balanced opening journal entries … **dated at cutover**, immutable"*; a deposit the utility already held at cutover IS an opening balance — the same row of the same spreadsheet as the four ageing buckets — so it takes the same date from the same batch in the same handoff. **And "a date the operator supplies" is the SAME date in this product**: the 2026-08-27 changeover ruling makes the cutover the operator's own answer to *"the last day your old system was in charge of the money"*, and while it is unanswered the whole batch is HELD and nothing posts. ⚠ **THE RECEIPT DATE IS KEPT AND IS NOT THE BOOK DATE.** BR-CUS-050's original receipt date stays on the account and BR-CUS-057 accrues from it; the ENTRY is dated at cutover. Collapsing them either way loses one: book at the receipt date and a 2024 liability lands in a book that opens in 2026; accrue from the cutover and the longest-standing customers are quietly under-paid. **THE CONTRA LEG IS THE JUDGMENT AND IT WAS OURS.** BR-CUS-049 signs ONE pair (*AR ↔ clearing*) and US-LED-001's contract table holds one opening row; neither states the other side of a migrated DEPOSIT. The opening clearing account is chosen because it is where the AR half of the very same row goes and is what *"the other side of a migration"* means here; **cash was not received today**, so no cash account may move — the money reached the old system years ago and debiting 1000/1010 would double-count the bank balance the same cutover brings across. **THE DEFECT IT CLOSES, DEF-1713:** `create_from_import` wrote `accounts.deposit_on_file` (US-CUS-023, `IMPORT_ACCOUNT_FIELDS`) and posted **nothing**, so the seeded estate carried a deposit register of **$750.00** against a 2300 GL of **$0.00** — and the books still footed, because the entry was never made rather than made wrong. Both figures now read **$750.00**. ⚠ **IT IS ONE HANDOFF, NOT TWO** — same batch id, same cutover, same transaction as the receivables, because a migration that moves one and not the other is the defect. ⚠ **AND IT DOES NOT JOIN ❄️ GT-16's TIE-OUT**, which is finance-signed as *Σ opening JEs = Σ imported BUCKETS*; the deposit batch ties out against its own two sides, so no frozen figure moves. A NEGATIVE migrated deposit is refused by name (a utility cannot hold less than none of a customer's money — BR-CUS-025), and a ZERO one posts nothing. | DEF-1713 · **BR-CUS-049 (CONFIRMED, PO AC-4, 2026-08-08 — the date)** · BR-CUS-050 · BR-CUS-025 · US-LED-001 · GT-16 ❄️ · PO changeover ruling 2026-08-27 · `12-governance/02-decision-log.md` 2026-08-28 | **CONFIRMED as to the DATE (BR-CUS-049, PO-signed) · the CONTRA LEG is an Eng judgment**, recorded in the decision log with its worked figure and flagged in `12-governance/09-human-review-ledger.md` as NOT shown to the PO | `06-backend/tests/test_def177_def1713_deposit_register_and_books.py` (the entry date, the receipt date, the re-run, the key collision, the negative refusal, the deposit-only account) · `06-backend/tests/test_cr040_seeded_books_tie_out.py` |
| BR-CUS-672 | **AN ACCOUNT CREATED BY THE IMPORT INHERITS ITS UNIT'S SERVICES WHATEVER ITS STATUS — AND EVERY INHERITED FACT IS HELD BY REFERENCE, NEVER COPIED (PO ruling 2026-08-29, `CR-047`, closing `DEF-2518`).** PO verbatim: *"if account is created then we need to inherit details form units - otherwise upadling it later will be big tasks"*. ⚠ **WHAT WAS WRONG.** `_open_service_agreements` is the ONLY writer of `service_agreements` on the import path and it sat inside `_make_occupying`, behind `if is_occupying(status)`. `OCCUPYING_BEHAVIOR_CLASSES` is (`billable`, `billable_base_only`); a row whose Status cell is EMPTY resolves to `pending_activation`, which is `non_billable`. The Status column is OPTIONAL in the published template and `ACC.STATUS_IN_LOOKUP` warns only on an UNKNOWN value, never an absent one — **so the easiest file a utility can produce loaded accounts with no service, no route, no cycle, no plan and no meter, and the receipt counted every one of them under "Accounts created" beside the good ones.** ⚠ **THE RULE SPLITS TWO THINGS `_make_occupying` HAD BUNDLED, AND THE SPLIT IS THE WHOLE DESIGN.** **(a) OCCUPANCY GUARDS** — `BR-CUS-017` (one LIVE account per unit), `BR-CUS-056` (the boundary day) and `BR-CUS-022` (a start date to activate) — stay tied to a billing status, because `BR-CUS-018` is explicit that a draft never blocks the unit. **(b) INHERITANCE** — the `service_agreements` rows — becomes **unconditional on the import door**. `create_account` (S18) and `move_in` (S19) are UNCHANGED: this ruling is about a migration, and widening it to the hand-typed doors would be a scope the PO did not widen. ⚠ **WHY A DRAFT MAY HOLD LINKS SAFELY, CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** The bill run selects accounts by billing status, so a draft's agreements are read by nothing until its status changes; and `service_agreements` carries NO unique constraint on (tenant, service_point) — only `ix_sa_tenant_account_started` on (tenant, account, started_on) — so a draft linked to a service point a live account also holds is not refused by the database. **That is the property the ruling buys: switching a draft on is a STATUS CHANGE AND NOTHING ELSE** — no load, no backfill, no second pass over every migrated account. ⚠ **NOTHING IS COPIED, AND THAT HALF WAS ALREADY RIGHT** (`BR-CUS-029`, PO 2026-08-11 — *"from unit you will derive the plan"*). The chain is `accounts` → `service_agreements(service_point_id)` → `service_points(unit_id, service_id, route_id, cycle_id)` → `units(rate_plan_id)` and `assets(service_point_id)`. Every arrow is a foreign key resolved live. **DO NOT ADD A PLAN COLUMN TO THE ACCOUNT** — a second copy of a fact with one owner is how two numbers come to disagree, and an old bill reprints from its own locked-at-issue snapshot regardless. | PO 2026-08-29 · `CR-047` · `DEF-2518` · BR-CUS-029 · BR-CUS-018 · BR-CUS-017 | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr047_import_inheritance.py` |
| BR-CUS-673 | **AN IMPORT REFUSAL SKIPS ITS OWN RECORD AND NOTHING ELSE, AND EVERY RUN HANDS BACK TWO FILES — WHAT LOADED, AND WHAT DID NOT AND WHY (PO ruling 2026-08-29, `CR-047`, closing `DEF-2519`).** PO verbatim: *"if it is success then you read next record, if it is fail - you display on screen shich record failed and why (dont write to dB) and continue to uplaod next record; at the end we need 2 CsV file 1 for success record and other for failed records"*. ⚠ **CLAUSE 1 IS NOT NEW LAW — IT IS `BR-CUS-051` FINALLY IMPLEMENTED**, and the row is written that way on purpose. `BR-CUS-051` has been CONFIRMED since the import was specified and reads *"staged → per-row errors → **skip-errors ON** → **\"N created · N failed\" summary**"*. Stage 4 shipped as ONE `transaction.atomic()` around the whole file, so a refusal raised inside the customers writer escaped the row loop and rolled back every account that had already landed — **1,213 rows in, row 63 refused, ZERO accounts on file, one error message, no receipt.** Filing that as a *change* would have converted our own unbuilt rule into the customer's new requirement (`13-change-process.md` step 1), so it is a defect and this row says so. **(1) A SAVEPOINT PER RECORD.** A business refusal undoes THAT RECORD and lets the loop continue. A non-business exception still aborts — the transaction property the runner rests on is not abandoned, it is narrowed to one row. **(2) THE REASON IS WRITTEN ON THE ROW**, in the operator's words, with a concrete fix — the same `problem` / `fix_hint` pair the four already-handled rejections fill in. "Row 63 failed" is not a reason; it is a referral to us. **(3) TWO FILES, AND THE SECOND ONE'S SHAPE IS THE RULE.** `failures.csv` is **THE ORIGINAL ROW, BYTE-IDENTICAL, plus exactly two appended columns** — `why_it_failed` and `how_to_fix_it`. Not re-derived, not re-quoted, not re-ordered. A failure file that has been reformatted is a file the operator must reconcile before they can use it; the point is that they fix two cells in the spreadsheet they already know and upload that file on its own. It carries the balance columns too, so the money in it reconciles against the batch that did post. **(4) THE MONEY FOLLOWS THE ACCOUNTS** (PO verbatim, same day: *"if account is created then money is also lands"*). Opening balances post as ONE batch for exactly the accounts that landed. **`GT-16` ❄️ DOES NOT MOVE and no finance re-sign is entered:** the buckets are written only for an account that was created, so both sides of *Σ opening JEs = Σ imported buckets* shrink together and the identity holds exactly; a re-run of `failures.csv` is a DIFFERENT batch id and posts its own entry set, so the idempotency clause is untouched. The books therefore foot after every run, not only after the last. | PO 2026-08-29 · `CR-047` · `DEF-2519` · BR-CUS-051 (the rule this implements) · GT-16 ❄️ (re-read, unmoved) | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr047_per_record_load.py` |

## CR-056 — the owner's account hangs off the ADDRESS (2026-09-01)

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-1010 | **A MASTER ACCOUNT IS THE OWNER'S ACCOUNT AT AN ADDRESS. IT NAMES A PREMISES (`accounts.premises_id`) AND NEED NAME NO UNIT (`accounts.unit_id` IS NULLABLE).** ⛔ **NARROWED AGAIN 2026-10-02 BY `CR-162-BRC10` (PO, Q1): *every address* NOW MEANS EVERY ADDRESS WHOSE PREMISES TYPE TAKES A MASTER ACCOUNT** — the *Account type* setting on the utility's own premises-type row (`BR-CUS-1114`, amended); the two-unit count below survives only for a type nobody has answered. ⛔ **NARROWED 2026-10-02 BY `BR-CUS-1114` (PO ruling, decision log top row): *every address* NOW MEANS EVERY ADDRESS OF TWO OR MORE UNITS.** PO verbatim: *"we have to raise master account on multi pairtment building where we can have multiple units. single family hime, single unit, we should not do master account"*. A single-unit address takes no master account; its applicant gets a REGULAR account on the one unit. Existing masters on single-unit addresses are counted and reported, never changed. This is the whole of `CR-056`'s shift, and it replaces `BR-CUS-068`'s anchoring clause at that rule's own sentence. **SOURCE, AND ITS FORM:** `CR-056` **R1**, PO verbatim — *"Duplex should be linked to master account of Ajinkya → link this account to customer # that already exist"* — and **R2**, which is RECONSTRUCTED rather than quoted (`CR-056` §0) but is ratified in substance by the PO ruling of 2026-08-31: **"every address gets its owner a Master Account, whether or not anything is shared, and whether or not the owner lives there."** An account that must exist for an address with nothing shared cannot hang off a shared unit, because there is not one. **WHAT CHANGES, MECHANICALLY:** `create_master_account` writes `premises_id` and leaves `unit_id` NULL; a common area is reached through `service_agreements` like every other covered unit and is therefore something the account is CHARGED FOR, not the thing it hangs off; and a premises with **no common area at all** now gets its owner's account instead of being refused. **WHAT DOES NOT CHANGE:** the row is still an ordinary `accounts` row with `kind='master'` and no new table (`BR-CUS-068`, still CONFIRMED in its shape); `kind` is still a REFUSED input on every door except `POST /accounts/master`; and **coverage still rides `service_agreements`**, which is why the ruled figure comes out unchanged — **`1408 Elm Street` bills `$41.20` + `$28.40` = `$69.60` on one owner document, before and after.** **EXISTING ROWS KEEP THEIR ANCHOR.** The `customers 0036` backfill fills `premises_id` from each account's own unit and moves no `unit_id`, so every Master Account created before 2026-09-01 bills exactly what it billed before it (`CR-056` task 6). Two shapes coexist on purpose: re-anchoring live accounts would change coverage on a money path to tidy a column. | `CR-056` R1 (PO verbatim) · R2 (reconstructed, ratified in substance by PO 2026-08-31) · decision log 2026-09-01 · BR-CUS-068 (superseded in its anchoring) · BR-MET-400 | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_br_cus_068_master_bill.py` · `06-backend/tests/test_cr056_w1_account_on_the_address.py` |
| BR-CUS-1011 | **ONE OWNER ACCOUNT PER ADDRESS — A SECOND ACTIVE MASTER ACCOUNT ON A PREMISES IS REFUSED BY NAME.** Once a Master Account IS the owner's account at an address, two of them at one address are two owners of one building, and every read that asks *who owns this premises* would have to pick one. **THIS IS A LANE JUDGMENT, NOT A PO RULING**, and it is recorded rather than asked because it follows from `BR-CUS-1010` and law 13 leaves it to us: nothing about it is a frozen figure, a collision with a signed rule, or a fact only the PO holds. **SERVICE-LAYER ONLY, NO DATABASE HALF** — the same posture `OQ-092` ruled for `BR-CUS-017` (option (a), *"the check runs inside the create/update transaction with row locking"*), and for a second reason here: **premises that already carry two masters exist**, `BR-CUS-171` records that state by name, and a database constraint would refuse to migrate them. The guard binds new creations; it does not rewrite history. | `BR-CUS-1010` · `BR-CUS-017`/`OQ-092` (the enforcement posture) · `BR-CUS-171` | **CONFIRMED (lane judgment, decision log 2026-09-01)** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr056_w1_account_on_the_address.py` |
| BR-CUS-1012 | **BR-CUS-017's ONE-OCCUPYING-ACCOUNT-PER-UNIT IS A RULE ABOUT UNITS, AND AN ACCOUNT THAT NAMES NO UNIT IS OUTSIDE IT — IT NEITHER OCCUPIES NOR BLOCKS ONE.** With `accounts.unit_id` nullable this needs saying out loud, because the failure it prevents is silent in both directions: a `NULL = NULL` reading would make every unit-less Master Account occupy the same imaginary unit and the SECOND address in the product would be refused; the opposite slip — dropping the NULL check — would let a unit-less row satisfy the guard and a real unit be double-let. **A `regular` account must still name a unit**, enforced by a CHECK constraint (`ck_accounts_regular_has_unit`) as well as by the door, because that is the direction where a missing unit means a bill with nothing to price. | `BR-CUS-017` · `OQ-092` · `BR-CUS-1010` · migration `customers 0036` | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr056_w1_account_on_the_address.py` |
| BR-CUS-1040 | **A MOVE-OUT STATES, IN WORDS AND BEFORE IT IS COMMITTED, WHICH ACCOUNT THE UNIT FALLS TO AND THAT ITS STANDING CHARGE CONTINUES THERE — AND IT STATES THE REVERSE EDGE IN THE SAME BREATH.** PO ruling 2026-08-31, verbatim: *"it is taken care during move out step - we have in impact section, we need to cover it there - expliciately"*, and the decision log of that day makes the adverb a REQUIREMENT rather than an emphasis: *"The unit falling to the owner's account is a new consequence of that act and must be drawn there in words — which account it moves to, and that the standing charge continues on it — rather than happening correctly and invisibly. A clerk who has to discover a money movement afterwards has been given a surprise, and a surprise on a money path is a defect whatever the arithmetic did."* **THE MECHANISM IS NOT NEW AND MUST NOT BE REBUILT:** the move-out sets the terminal status, `BR-MET-036` derives the unit back to vacant, and `base_charge_only_coverage` puts every unit with no occupying account onto the owner's bill — the SAME function a cut reaches, which is why `$69.60` of common areas becomes `$81.60` whichever event emptied the unit. What this rule adds is the SENTENCE. **THREE THINGS IT MUST CARRY:** (1) the account, by number; (2) that the standing charge is billed there while the unit is empty; (3) that it comes off again the day the next tenant takes the unit. **AND AN HONEST BRANCH WHERE THERE IS NO OWNER ACCOUNT**, which is a FAULT and not a blank — PO 2026-08-31, ruling 1 consequence 3: *"'no lines because nothing falls to it' and 'no lines although the premises HAS an unoccupied unit' are different facts and only one of them is good news."* ⚠ **IT RIDES THE ADVISORY RAIL AND NOT A PANEL ROW**, because a panel is a `Disclosure` and everything inside one folds away (B19 §15.7): a money movement a clerk has to expand a panel to discover is still the surprise this rule forbids. **NO PRICE IS STATED** — a single *what the empty unit will cost* figure is the rating engine's and nothing publishes a per-unit standing charge outside a bill run; the plan's own configured per-service base charges are shown VERBATIM instead, with no minimum added (`OQ-1166`, PO 2026-08-30). | PO 2026-08-31 (decision log, `CR-056` ruling 3 and ruling 1) · `BR-MET-036` · `BR-CUS-170` · `BR-CUS-1010` · `OQ-1166` · B19 §15.7 · B14 rule 32 | **CONFIRMED · BUILT 2026-09-01** (`CR-056` W6 task 26) | `06-backend/apps/customers/move_out_settlement.close_impact_for` · `07-frontend/src/features/customers/moveShared.tsx` · `06-backend/tests/test_cr056_w6_the_move_out_story.py` tc1–tc3 · `07-frontend/src/features/customers/MoveOutWizard.test.tsx` |

## CR-056 waves W2 and W3 — the customer becomes a person, and every door writes the link (2026-08-31)

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-1020 | 🔒 **IDENTITY LIVES ON THE CUSTOMER, ENCRYPTED, AND IT IS WHAT MAKES `BR-CUS-089`'s SIGNED CRITERIA ASSESSABLE FOR THE FIRST TIME.** Until 2026-08-31 the `customers` row held a number, a name, a person-or-company flag and a legacy reference and **nothing that identifies a human being** — `CR-056` §4 states the cost in one line: *"What makes an owner one person today is his NAME, unique per utility, case-insensitive. A. Kulkarni and Ajinkya Kulkarni are two landlords and nothing objects."* The row now carries a **date of birth** and an app-layer-encrypted **Social Security number**, **driving licence** and **phone**, each identifier with a keyed blind index beside it (`bix1:<kid>:<HMAC-SHA256>`) so a randomised ciphertext can be MATCHED without being readable. **No new encryption rule is minted:** `OQ-016` is CLOSED (*"app-layer encryption for high-sensitivity columns, disk for rest"*, PO R6) and `BR-CUS-092` already names both identifiers; this row is those rules reaching the customer table. **The date of birth is a plain `date` and deliberately so** — it must be COMPARABLE, `contacts.dob` is a plain date too, and `BR-CUS-092` names two identifiers of which it is not one. **WHAT THIS UNBLOCKS, and it is the whole point:** `BR-CUS-089`'s criteria were **signed and inert** — its own row says *"until the request form captures a DOB, no match can be assessed against them"*. They are now assessable on a CUSTOMER. **THE MATCHER EMITS TWO OF THE THREE TIER WORDS AND NEVER `probable`:** the PO ruled what earns `strong` and ruled nothing about where `probable` begins, so a machine handing one out would be minting a second threshold. **A PHONE MATCH ALONE IS `weak`** — `CR-056`'s R3 names the phone as identity evidence and it is indexed, but `BR-CUS-089` says *one government ID* and a telephone number is not one. ⚠ **NO UNIQUE CONSTRAINT ON THE SSN INDEX, and the reason is a ruling's FORM rather than a judgement about SSNs.** R3 (*"the Customer # is keyed on IDENTITY and reused, never allocated twice to one person"*) is **RECONSTRUCTED, not verbatim**, and CR-056 §0 says a reconstructed ruling must be confirmed before anything is built on it. A unique index is a one-way door that refuses a write in production on evidence a clerk may have typed wrong; R3's BEHAVIOUR is built in the service layer, where it is reversible, and the constraint is not. | `CR-056` W2 tasks 11-14 · `BR-CUS-089` (PO 2026-08-25, closing `OQ-364`) · `BR-CUS-092` (PO 2026-08-24) · `OQ-016` CLOSED · B2 §6 · migration `customers 0036` · `apps/customers/identity_match.py` | **CONFIRMED (the columns and the matcher implement signed law) — 🔒 HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr056_w2_customer_identity.py` |
| BR-CUS-1021 | **EVERY DOOR THAT OPENS AN ACCOUNT WRITES THE CUSTOMER LINK, AND A WEAK MATCH REFUSES TO LINK BY NAME.** `CR-056` §4, measured: *"Exactly one function writes a customer-to-account link and it runs only inside an import. **Every Master Account in the product has a blank customer column**, and an account opened at the counter shows a dash where its number should be."* `services.link_customer` stays the ONE writer and gains three arguments — the presented identity, the clerk's own explicit choice of customer, and the ACTOR — so the S18 wizard, the S19 move-in and the master-account door all reach it. **THREE OUTCOMES AND EACH IS A DIFFERENT ACT.** (a) The three signed facts agree with exactly one customer on file → the account LINKS to that person and `customer_accounts` records `match_tier='strong'` with its basis in words and the acting human as its confirmer, which is what lets `discounts.carry_forward_offer` carry the deposit. (b) Somebody looks related and the criteria do NOT all agree → **the new account opens under its OWN customer number, nothing is merged, and the candidates ride back for a human to look at.** `BR-CUS-019` (the duplicate check is ADVISORY) and `BR-CUS-089` (money moves only on `strong`) agree here: nothing is blocked and nothing is joined on a guess. (c) Nobody is found → a new customer, which is a real answer and not a failure. **THE CUSTOMER NUMBER IS STILL ALLOCATED INSIDE THE CREATE**, so no door can produce an unnumbered customer even for the width of a transaction. ⚠ **AN IMPORT LINK CAN NEVER EARN A TIER.** The bulk import matches on `external_ref` — the LEGACY system's id, a reconciliation key and not identity evidence — and it passes no actor, so the tier stays NULL and the carry-forward keeps refusing. A spreadsheet column may not move a deposit. **WHAT THIS CLOSES ON THE MONEY SIDE:** `discounts._stored_match` read `service_requests.match_tier` and nothing else, so *"a move-in that did not come from a service request has no stored tier at all"* — an account opened at the counter could never carry a returning customer's **$150.00** however well the clerk had identified them. It now reads both stores and takes the most recently CONFIRMED. | `CR-056` W3 tasks 15-17 · `BR-CUS-054` · `BR-CUS-019` · `BR-CUS-089` · `apps/customers/services.link_customer` · migration `customers 0036` | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr056_w2_customer_identity.py` |
| BR-CUS-1022 | 🔒 **IDENTITY IS PER KIND: A PERSON IS KNOWN BY SSN, LICENCE OR PHONE; A COMPANY BY ITS TAX ID — AND `BR-CUS-089` IS NARROWED ONTO PERSONS RATHER THAN REVERSED (PO ruling 2026-09-01).** PO verbatim: *"Customer can be person or company - in case of person , if we have SSN, or DL or Phone number, we create the Customer ID and attached these to custoemr ID ; if it is Company then we ask for Tax ID"*. ⚠ **THE COLLISION IS STATED, NOT SMOOTHED (law 13).** `BR-CUS-089` is signed and verbatim — *NAME + DATE OF BIRTH + ONE GOVERNMENT ID*, PO 2026-08-25 closing `OQ-364` — and **a company has no date of birth**. Read literally the signed rule made every company permanently unmatchable, so a landlord's limited company could present a perfect Tax ID and still be a stranger, and the **$150.00** deposit `BR-CUS-089` gates could never follow a business to a new account. **THE RULE:** a PERSON is strong on **name + date of birth + (SSN or driving licence)**, unchanged; a COMPANY is strong on **name + Tax ID**. **NEITHER KIND BORROWS THE OTHER'S CRITERIA AND NEITHER IS ASKED FOR THE OTHER'S EVIDENCE** — a company is never asked for a driving licence and a person is never asked for a Tax ID, refused BY NAME rather than dropped, because *asking for both and leaving one blank turns a rule into a habit and the blank field eventually gets filled with something*. A PHONE is capturable for both kinds and is a government identifier for neither: it SURFACES a candidate and never makes one strong. **THE KIND MUST BE STATED WHERE IDENTITY IS PRESENTED, AND IS REFUSED BY NAME WHERE IT IS NOT** — the test to apply depends on the answer, and a guess of `person` would judge a business by a date of birth it has not got and file its Tax ID nowhere. **A DOOR PRESENTING NO IDENTITY STILL OPENS THE ACCOUNT** and keeps the `person` fallback, which is the PO's own ruling of the same day — *a duplicate identifier refuses to LINK and never refuses to SAVE* — and the fallback is WRITTEN by the service and recorded as `kind_defaulted` on the audit row instead of arriving invisibly from the model's column default. **THE COLUMN:** `customers.tax_id`, app-layer encrypted with a keyed blind index beside it (`customers 0038`), exactly what `0036` built for a person's SSN — signed law under `OQ-016` CLOSED, not this lane's taste. ⚠ **`tenancy.ein` IS NOT IT**: that is the UTILITY's own tax number on another module's table, and it is the counter-example rather than the precedent — it holds nine plaintext digits against that same closed ruling. ⚠ **NO UNIQUE INDEX ON `tax_id_bidx`**, and this time it is a PO ruling rather than a reconstruction: a clerk who mistypes one digit must still be able to open the account. **NOT DECIDED, and stubbed rather than chosen:** whether a company gets an incorporation date (`OQ-1241`) and whether a sole trader is a person or a company (`OQ-1242`). | PO ruling 2026-09-01 (decision log) · `BR-CUS-089` (PO 2026-08-25, closing `OQ-364`) · `BR-CUS-092` · `OQ-016` CLOSED · B2 §6 · B2 r9 #1 (`kind person|company`) · migration `customers 0038` · `apps/customers/identity_match.py` | **CONFIRMED (the PO's own sentence; the narrowing of `BR-CUS-089` is stated in the decision log of 2026-09-01) — 🔒 HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr056_w2b_company_identity.py` |
| BR-CUS-1023 | **A CUSTOMER NUMBER IS CREATED ONLY FOR A NAME **AND** AT LEAST ONE IDENTIFIER. A NAME ALONE NO LONGER CREATES ONE (PO ruling 2026-09-01).** PO verbatim: *"Customer ID - minimum name and one ID - minimum - please make this rule and apply"*, elaborated in the same exchange as *"Customer can be person or company - in case of person, if we have SSN, or DL or Phone number, we create the Customer ID and attached these to customer ID; if it is Company then we ask for Tax ID"*. **THE MINIMUM, PER KIND:** a **PERSON** needs a name **AND** one of a Social Security number, a driving licence or a **phone**; a **COMPANY** needs a name **AND** its Tax ID. **A phone does NOT create a company** — the PO's list for a business names the Tax ID and nothing else, and a business's phone is the handle of whoever answers it. ⚠ **WHAT IS ENOUGH TO CREATE IS NOT WHAT IS ENOUGH TO MATCH, AND THE TWO MUST NEVER BE RECONCILED.** A **phone alone CREATES** (the PO's own list) and a **phone alone is a WEAK match that carries no deposit** (`BR-CUS-089`, `BR-CUS-1022`). Both are true at once. A lane that unifies them either refuses a customer the PO said to create, or moves the **$150.00** deposit on a telephone number. **THE REFUSAL IS SCOPED TO THE CUSTOMER NUMBER AND NEVER TO THE ACCOUNT.** A door presenting less than the minimum still OPENS the account, still writes its contact and its address, still bills; what it does not get is a Customer # — and every consequence (no deposit carry-forward, no recognition on return) follows from not having one rather than from a refusal to serve. **IT GOVERNS WHAT IS WRITTEN FROM NOW ON AND INVALIDATES NOTHING ON FILE** — customers already holding a name and no identifier stay lawful, exactly as hard rule 2's *existing 18,6 columns stay lawful containers*; no migration, no backfill, no screen refuses to display one. **IT GATES CREATION ONLY, NEVER LINKING**: a clerk naming an existing customer, a matching `external_ref`, and a `strong` identity match all still LINK, because those rows exist already and the rule is about minting a new number. **KIND DECIDES WHICH IDENTIFIER COUNTS** — a Social Security number does not satisfy a company and a Tax ID does not satisfy a person — so a door presenting an identifier with no kind cannot be resolved and is refused by name (`BR-CUS-1022`). **NO DOOR MAY SATISFY THE RULE BY INVENTING A VALUE**: no placeholder phone, no zero Tax ID, no account number reused as an identifier. The refusal is the product; a filled column that means nothing is worse than an empty one, because the matcher reads it. **THE REFUSAL NAMES THE MISSING THING IN PLAIN WORDS AT THE DOOR WHERE THE CLERK IS TYPING** — never a rule id or an internal code (B14 rule 32 clause 4), and never after the account has been created. ⚠ **SUPERSEDED AT ONE CLAUSE, THE SAME DAY, AND THE REST OF THIS RULE STANDS — PO 2026-09-01, verbatim: *"Without identifier we cannot create Customer ID or master account ot regular account"*.** The clause reversed is this rule's own **"THE REFUSAL IS SCOPED TO THE CUSTOMER NUMBER AND NEVER TO THE ACCOUNT"** and everything in it about the account still opening: the identifier is no longer a condition on the Customer # alone, it is a condition on **the account**. Name + one identifier → match-or-create the customer AND open the account; **name alone → NOTHING is created, no customer and no account of either kind**. **WHAT SURVIVES UNCHANGED AND IS ALREADY BUILT:** the minimum itself and its per-kind tables (a person by SSN, licence or phone; a business by its Tax ID), the create-versus-match distinction, the refusal wording at the door, the kind requirement, the no-invented-value prohibition, and *governs what is written from now on*. **ONLY THE CONSEQUENCE MOVED** — from *no number* to *no row at all*. **WHAT IS OWED AND IS DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT HERE:** the account-level refusal, and with it the ruling's own migration-blocking half — **the bulk import can then create no accounts at all**, which its recording lane states *"must be put to the PO before it ships, not discovered by a utility mid-migration"*. The build is that lane's; this stamp exists so no reader meets the sentences above believing they are still current.
