See the decision log of 2026-09-01, *AND NO IDENTIFIER MEANS NO ACCOUNT EITHER*. **THE BULK IMPORT IS THE DOOR THIS COSTS.** It carries a name and a legacy reference and no identity at all, so from this rule it creates NO customer: the ACCOUNT loads, no Customer # is minted, and the row is flagged for a human to identify — the fourth application of *the product never invents a party* (already ruled three times; not re-put to the PO, law 13). | PO ruling 2026-09-01 (decision log, `CR-056`) · `BR-CUS-1022` (identity is per kind) · `BR-CUS-089` (PO 2026-08-25, closing `OQ-364`) · `BR-CUS-054` (the entity and its N:M link) · `BR-CUS-019` (the duplicate check is advisory) · B14 rule 32 · CLAUDE.md hard rule 7 · `apps/customers/identity_match.creation_minimum` · `apps/customers/services.link_customer` | **CONFIRMED (the PO's own sentence) — 🔒 HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr056_w6_minimum_identity.py` |

### `BR-CUS-089` — AMENDED 2026-08-31: the criteria are no longer inert, and the tier has a SECOND store

That row records the criteria as *"signed and inert"* because *"the ruling names
evidence this product never collects"*. **Half of that is now false and the half
that remains is narrower.**

* **On a CUSTOMER the criteria ARE assessable.** `customers 0036` puts the date
  of birth, the Social Security number and the driving licence on the customer
  row, and `apps/customers/identity_match.py` computes exactly the PO's three
  facts — nothing added and nothing dropped.
* **On a SERVICE REQUEST they are still not.** `REQ-03.1` collects no date of
  birth and `DEF-638(2)` is open on precisely that, so a tier a clerk types on a
  request remains a human judgement that nothing machine-checks. The refusal
  payload in `discounts.CARRY_FORWARD_NO_MATCH_RULE` used to say *"nothing in
  this repository defines what makes a match strong … one is owed"* — both
  clauses were already false when written (`OQ-364` closed the same week) and
  they are corrected rather than left standing (hard-won law 1).
* **The tier now has two stores.** `customer_accounts` carries the same four
  columns `service_requests` does, so a link made at the counter records HOW.
  The gate is not weakened by that and is arguably better evidenced: a request
  tier is a typed judgement nothing checks, while an account-link tier was
  COMPUTED from the three signed facts and then committed by a named person.
  Both are read; neither is defaulted; an absent tier still refuses.

### `BR-CUS-092` — AMENDED 2026-08-31: the blind index exists, and the driving licence has a home

Two of that row's three stated absences move:

* ⚠ **"NO BLIND INDEX, so BR-CUS-089's matcher is still unbuilt and the column
  cannot be searched"** — `customers.ssn_bidx`, `driving_licence_bidx` and
  `phone_bidx` are built, and `service_requests.ssn`'s own docstring said the
  index *"is added by the lane that builds the matcher, which is the only lane
  that can prove it works"*. This is that lane.
* ⚠ **"CLAUSE (a) IS HALF DONE. THE DRIVING LICENCE IS NOT CAPTURED"** — it is
  captured **on the CUSTOMER**. It is still not captured on the SERVICE REQUEST,
  where `_clean_capture` refuses it by name, and that half of clause (a) stands.
  ⚠⚠ **TWO CORRECTIONS TO THAT BULLET, 2026-09-15, AND THE WRONG TEXT IS LEFT
  ABOVE ON PURPOSE BECAUSE IT WAS QUOTED ONWARD INTO THREE OTHER PLACES.**
  (1) The licence **IS** captured on the service request now — PO ruling of
  2026-09-15, built the same day. (2) **`_clean_capture` NEVER REFUSED IT "BY
  NAME" (`DEF-CUS-5410`).** Measured at the tip on 2026-09-15: no spelling of
  the licence was in `IDENTIFIER_FIELDS`, so `drivers_licence`,
  `driving_licence` and `drivers_license` all fell to the *unknown field* branch
  and answered `VAL_UNKNOWN_LOOKUP` — *"This form does not accept …"* — rather
  than `BIZ_STATE`'s *"We cannot store an identity number yet"*. **One says we
  hold this fact and may not store it; the other says we have never heard of
  this field**, which is the whole reason that list exists. The sentence was
  plausible because twelve of its neighbours WERE refused by name, and it was
  quoted verbatim into the decision log of 2026-09-15 and into this wave's brief
  before anybody ran the code.
* ⚠ **"THERE IS NO `ssn_last4` COLUMN"** is unchanged and must stay so. The
  customer row has none either: the last four is a read projection
  (`crypto.last_four`), and storing it would put four of the nine digits back in
  the clear while every test still passed.

### `BR-CUS-092` — AMENDED 2026-09-15: clause (a) is FINISHED, and the date of birth joins it

PO verbatim, shown the blueprint's step 1 beside the product's:

> *"ensure user is able to inputs both these on step 1 - Date of birth and
> driver's licence on step 1 ... rewrite this rules if required"*

⚠ **THE PERMISSION TO REWRITE IS RECORDED AND DECLINED AS UNNECESSARY — THE
SECOND TIME ON THIS ROW.** The PO offered the same on 2026-08-24 and this row
already records why it was declined then: accepting it would misrepresent the
position. **Nothing here is being overridden.** Clause (a) has said since
2026-08-24 that *both identifiers are captured on the service request*, and the
amendment of 2026-08-31 says in capitals what was left:

> *"CLAUSE (a) IS HALF DONE. THE DRIVING LICENCE IS NOT CAPTURED ... on the
> SERVICE REQUEST, where `_clean_capture` refuses it by name, and that half of
> clause (a) stands."*

**This amendment is that half landing.** A lane must not write a supersession
note; there is no superseded statement to name.

* **`service_requests.drivers_licence`** is an `EncryptedTextField` with a keyed
  blind index beside it, exactly as `customers.driving_licence` already is —
  the THIRD caller of `core/crypto.py`, not a new capability. Clause (c) binds:
  **last four only on every read path**, never in a list response, an export, a
  log line or an audit `details` blob.
* **`service_requests.date_of_birth`** is a **plain `date`**, and `BR-CUS-1020`
  already settled why: *"it must be COMPARABLE, `contacts.dob` is a plain date
  too, and `BR-CUS-092` names two identifiers of which it is not one."* It is
  not an identifier under this row; it is the middle clause of `BR-CUS-089`.
* ⚠ **THERE IS STILL NO `_last4` COLUMN OF ANY KIND AND THERE MUST NOT BE.**
  The last four is a read projection (`crypto.last_four`). A stored one puts
  four digits back in the clear while every test still passes.
* ⚠ **CLAUSE (d) STILL BINDS THE SCREEN.** The words *"stored encrypted"* may
  appear beside the licence only once the column is live. Until then the claim
  stays off the screen — hard-won law 1: a false security promise to a customer
  over a government identifier is worse than an absent one.
* 🔒 **Sensitive path.** `HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING` on the files, with a row in
  `12-governance/09-human-review-ledger.md` naming what was shown to the PO in
  plain English with a concrete figure.

**WHAT THIS UNBLOCKS, and it is the reason the gap mattered more than it looked.**
`BR-CUS-089` makes a strong identity match **name + date of birth + one
government ID**, and `BR-CUS-1020` records the cost verbatim: *"until the request
form captures a DOB, no match can be assessed against them."* Signed criteria
that **no request could ever satisfy**, because the form never asked. A service
request has been unable to recognise a returning customer for three weeks, and
nothing went red — an absence is invisible to every check this repo has
(`DEF-CUS-3653`'s lesson, one table over).

### ✅ BUILT 2026-09-15 — what landed, and the two judgments that were this lane's

`customers 0050` (`AddField` × 3 plus one btree index, no data step, no backfill,
no new RLS policy — a policy is per TABLE and `service_requests` has carried a
`FORCE` one since `0018`). The column shapes are exactly as this amendment
specifies them and there is no `_last4` column of any kind.

**The read payload, so a screen builds from the repo and not from a conversation
— `GET /service-requests/{id}` → `applicant`:**

```
"identifier": {
    "ssn_last4": "4471",                        "ssn_present": true,
    "stored_encrypted": true,                   ← the SSN's, under its old name
    "drivers_licence_last4": "4882",            "drivers_licence_present": true,
    "drivers_licence_stored_encrypted": true
},
"date_of_birth": "1988-03-14"                   ← in FULL, and NOT under identifier
```

Worked end to end: an applicant gives `TX-1234882` and `1988-03-14`; the column
holds `sbh1:insecure-dev:<nonce>:<ciphertext>` and no spelling of `1234882`;
staff see `•••• 4882`; `audit_log` carries `•••••4882`; the index token is
`bix1:<kid>:<HMAC>` and `tx1234882` finds the same row.

**FOUR JUDGMENTS THIS LANE MADE, recorded here because the rules ran out and a
judgment with its reasoning is the deliverable (CLAUDE.md law 13):**

1. **The column is `drivers_licence` and the customer's stays `driving_licence`.**
   Two spellings of one fact is a hazard, and renaming either moves every
   citation in every register, docstring and test at once. So neither is renamed
   and BOTH are answered: `ALIASED_FIELDS` refuses the other spelling BY NAME
   with the canonical one in the message, instead of *"unknown field"*.
2. **`dob` moved from *refused identifier* to *refused alias*.** It used to
   answer *"that identifier cannot be stored here"*, which was true and stopped
   being true; a refusal that has expired is worse than no refusal, because
   somebody believes it and goes away.
3. **`stored_encrypted` keeps its name and its meaning (the SSN's).** One
   consumer and one signed proof file read it; renaming it to
   `ssn_stored_encrypted` would edit a 🔒 proof's assertions for a cosmetic gain.
   The licence's flag is spelled out in full beside it. **If a third encrypted
   identifier arrives, rename it then.**
4. **`ssn` was NOT narrowed to persons in passing.** `BR-CUS-1022` forbids
   asking a company for a person's evidence and `service_requests.ssn` shipped
   four days before that rule; narrowing a shipped capture path has its own blast
   radius, so it is **`DEF-CUS-5409`** rather than a line added quietly here.

⚠ **AND `BR-CUS-1022` NOW BITES ON THIS SCREEN.** It says *"THE KIND MUST BE
STATED WHERE IDENTITY IS PRESENTED, AND IS REFUSED BY NAME WHERE IT IS NOT"*, and
this amendment puts identity on step 1. A **person** is strong on name + date of
birth + (SSN or licence); a **company** has no date of birth, is never asked for a
licence, and is strong on name + Tax ID. So step 1 must state the kind before it
asks for either identifier, and `CR-033` removed the control that did
(`applicant_kind` is no longer sent and the column takes its `person` default) six
days before `BR-CUS-1022` was ruled. Filed as a defect rather than assumed.

### `BR-CUS-089` — NARROWED 2026-09-01: it is a rule about PERSONS, and it always was

The row above is signed and verbatim and **not one word of it changes for a
person**. What the PO's ruling of 2026-09-01 exposed is that it was never a rule
about every customer: its middle clause is a **date of birth**, and a company has
none.

* **Read literally it made every company permanently unmatchable.** `tier_for`
  could not return `strong` for a business however completely it was identified,
  so the deposit carry-forward that rides on `strong` could never run for one.
  Nobody intended that; it is what a rule written from one worked case does when
  a second kind of party arrives.
* **So it is NARROWED, not reversed.** `BR-CUS-089` governs persons. A company is
  matched on **name + Tax ID** (`BR-CUS-1022`). No signed figure moves and no
  frozen B4/B5 number is touched: the deposit it gates is still **$150.00**
  principal (**$153.50** with `BR-CUS-057`'s interest), and it still moves only on
  `strong`.
* **The narrowing is DIRECTIONAL and must stay so.** It removes a clause a company
  cannot satisfy; it adds nothing to a person's test. A later edit that unions the
  two lists — letting a Tax ID satisfy *one government ID* for a person, or a date
  of birth stand in for a Tax ID — would turn a narrowing into a loosening of a
  signed money rule. `tier_for` is written as two explicit booleans over named
  constants for exactly that reason, and
  `test_neither_kind_borrows_the_others_criteria` asserts all four corners.


## CR-058 W4 — a transfer is a REASSIGNMENT, because a property was sold (2026-09-01)

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-1030 | **A TRANSFER REASSIGNS THE ACCOUNT TO A NEW CUSTOMER. IT DOES NOT CREATE A SECOND ACCOUNT AND IT DOES NOT CLOSE THE FIRST. ⛔ THIS SUPERSEDES THE BEHAVIOUR SHIPPED IN `apps/customers/transfers.py` SINCE `CR-030` W7, AT THIS SENTENCE, ON 2026-09-01.** **PO verbatim:** *"dont create new account - we reassing that account - bascailly I have sold my property - so account get transfer to new person or business; if I move to new location then it is new Service request and we follow the process"*. **WHAT WAS BUILT AND IS NOW WRONG:** `commit_transfer` ran `services.move_out` on the outgoing account and `services.create_account` at a NEW unit, in one commit, and linked the SAME customer to both — a customer MOVING. **WHAT THE RULE SAYS INSTEAD:** the account, its number, its unit, its meter and its ledger all STAY; the CUSTOMER changes. One account, one continuous history at one address, across a change of owner. **WHY IT IS NOT COSMETIC:** creating a new account splits the address's history in two and leaves the buyer's bills under a number the property has never had, which is the opposite of what a utility means by *the account transferred*. **WHAT MUST NOT MOVE, AND IT IS HARD RULE 4:** every invoice, invoice line, payment and journal entry already issued to the SELLER stays exactly as issued — a reassignment writes no correction to any of them and edits no history. A document issued to Mrs Alvarez still says Mrs Alvarez a year later, because that is who was billed. What changes is who the account belongs to FROM NOW ON. **THE BUYER'S CUSTOMER NUMBER IS RESOLVED, NEVER INVENTED** — match first, create only on no match, and only where an identifier is present (`BR-CUS-1023`, and ruling 0 of the same day). ⛔ **AND A BUYER WHO PRESENTS NO IDENTIFIER IS REFUSED — THE REASSIGNMENT DOES NOT COMPLETE.** The PO ruled later the same day, verbatim: *"Without identifier we cannot create Customer ID or master account ot regular account"*, superseding this morning's *"an account may still be opened"* clause. A reassignment CREATES no account, so the letter of that rule does not reach it — but its whole point is that **no account may exist with nobody identifiable behind it**, and handing a live ledger to an unidentified party produces exactly that. **A lane judgment, recorded rather than re-asked** (law 13; decision log 2026-09-01): the safe direction is to refuse, because a refusal is recoverable in thirty seconds by asking for a phone number and a wrong reassignment is a live account owned by nobody. **IT IS A MONEY ACTION AND IT WRITES `audit_log`** (hard rule 7): who it moved from, who it moved to, and the balance and deposit as they stood at the moment of the sale. ⚖️ **THE DEBT FOLLOWS THE PROPERTY — PO ruling 2026-09-01, verbatim: *"money follow property - new owner boutght everything property + debt also"* (`OQ-1252`, CLOSED the hour it was raised).** **The buyer takes the property and the debt**, and this rule is what makes that nearly free: an account carries its own ledger, so **the balance never moves, because the thing it sits on never moves.** The reassignment writes no journal line, raises no refund and touches neither `deposit_on_file` nor `balance`. ⚠ **THE FIGURE IS STATED, NEVER SILENT:** the balance and the deposit as they stood at the moment of sale ride the response and the audit row, so what the buyer took on is on the record at the moment it happened. **Whether the BUYER must be shown it, and where, is NOT ruled — `OQ-1253`**, and nothing here answers it by inference. ⚠ **AND IT DOES NOT REWRITE `premises.owner_id`.** That is metering's typed party record and a separate fact from any account (B1: no cross-module write); the reassignment says so out loud in its response so the clerk knows the owner record still wants matching on the PRM-04 surface. ⊕ **AMENDED 2026-09-20 (`DEF-CUS-5972`, `v2.319.5`) — WHO COUNTS AS *THE PERSON WHO ALREADY OWNS IT*.** A sale to this account's existing customer is refused `buyer_is_already_this_accounts_customer`, and until today that guard **could not fire**: it compared the id `link_customer` RESOLVED against the seller's, and a phone cannot reuse a customer (`identity_match.REUSE_ID_FIELDS` is `(ssn, driving_licence)` for a person), so a phone-identified buyer always got a NEW number, the ids always differed, the sale committed, the seller's link was deleted and **one person held two Customer #s for one property with their history on the unlinked one**. **THE TEST IS NOW EVIDENCE, ASKED BEFORE ANYTHING IS RESOLVED, AGAINST THIS ACCOUNT'S OWN LINKED CUSTOMERS: the presented buyer is refused when their NAME **and at least one other comparable fact** agree with one of them** — a name alone is never enough (`CR-056` §4: two landlords can share one), an identifier alone is never enough (a household shares a telephone line), and an explicit `customer_id` naming a linked customer short-circuits it. ⚠ **DELIBERATELY WEAKER THAN `BR-CUS-089`'s `strong`, which decides whether MONEY MAY FOLLOW somebody; this decides whether to refuse an ACT a clerk recovers from in thirty seconds, and the refusal names the Customer # it matched and what it matched on. Requiring `strong` would have left the guard exactly as unfireable as it was.** ⚠ **IT IS NOT A DUPLICATE CHECK and must not become one** — `BR-CUS-019` keeps that advisory and `BR-CUS-1024` deliberately lets a DIFFERENT returning person be minted a second number; the candidate set here is this account's own links and nothing wider. | PO ruling 2026-09-01 (decision log, *"SEVEN CORRECTIONS…"* §7) · `CR-058` tasks 14–18 · SUPERSEDES `CR-030` W7 task 7.1's built behaviour · `BR-CUS-1023` · `BR-CUS-054` · `BR-CUS-1011` · `OQ-1252` · CLAUDE.md hard rules 4 and 7 | **CONFIRMED (the PO's own sentence) — SUPERSEDES the shipped behaviour** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr058_w4_transfer_reassigns.py` |
| BR-CUS-1031 | **A CUSTOMER MOVING TO A NEW ADDRESS IS A NEW SERVICE REQUEST, NOT A TRANSFER.** **PO verbatim, the second half of the same sentence:** *"if I move to new location then it is new Service request and we follow the process"*. Transfer answers *the property was SOLD*; the new-request door answers *the person MOVED*. They were one door until 2026-09-01 and the door that existed did the second job. **WHERE IT HAS TO BE SAID: on the screen, where a clerk would otherwise reach for transfer** — a rule that lives only in a rules file is a rule the clerk meets as a refusal. **AND THE OTHER DOOR IS UNCHANGED:** a service request still creates a numbered request and nothing else — no account, no customer (`BR-CUS-1032`'s pinning test, and ruling 3 of the same day, *"this si also correct no chnges"*). | PO ruling 2026-09-01 · `CR-058` tasks 14 and 16 · `BR-CUS-1030` | **CONFIRMED (the PO's own sentence)** | `07-frontend/src/features/customers/TransfersTab.test.tsx` |
| BR-CUS-1032 | **THE NEW-REQUEST DOOR CREATES A NUMBERED REQUEST AND NOTHING ELSE — CONFIRMED, AND PINNED SO A LATER LANE CANNOT QUIETLY WIDEN IT.** **PO verbatim:** *"this si also correct no chnges"*. A service request is an ASK, not a party and not an account: `create_request` writes one `service_requests` row, allocates its number, and creates **no account, no customer and no `customer_accounts` link**. It is recorded as a rule rather than left as an absence for one reason — every other door in `CR-058` gained a customer resolution on 2026-09-01, and *"this one did not"* is a decision that has to be findable, or the next lane reads the pattern and adds it. ⚠ **A request may LINK to a customer later** (`service_request_service.link_customer`, the clerk's own act on `REQ-03`); what it may not do is MINT one at creation. | PO ruling 2026-09-01 (decision log §3) · `CR-058` task 19 | **CONFIRMED (the PO's own sentence)** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr058_w5_doors_create_nothing.py` |

## CR-058 W7 — the bulk accounts import asks who the customer is (2026-09-01)

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-1041 | **THE BULK ACCOUNTS SPREADSHEET CARRIES IDENTITY, AND A ROW THAT NAMES A CUSTOMER WITHOUT IDENTIFYING THEM CREATES NOTHING.** **PO ruling 2026-09-01, verbatim:** *"Without identifier we cannot create Customer ID or master account ot regular account"*, put to the PO again as a migration blocker on the accounts import and answered *give it identity columns too*. **THE RULE, in the two lines the ruling states:** a row carrying a customer NAME **and at least one identifier** loads its account **and** its customer is matched-or-created; a row carrying a **name alone loads NOTHING** — no account, no customer — and is **counted on the receipt as needing an identifier**, never silently dropped. **WHICH IDENTIFIER COUNTS DEPENDS ON THE KIND** (`BR-CUS-1022`, unchanged): a person by a Social Security number, a driving licence **or a phone**; a company by its Tax ID. **THE COLUMNS, and one of them is not new:** `customer_kind`, `customer_ssn`, `customer_driving_licence` and `customer_tax_id` join the accounts template, and **the phone identifier is the template's EXISTING `phone` column** rather than a second one beside it — the accounts file has carried the customer's phone since `BR-CUS-046` and two columns for one fact is a file where a utility fills one, the matcher reads the other, and the bill contact and the identity disagree. **EVERY NEW COLUMN IS OPTIONAL IN THE FILE**, so a spreadsheet saved yesterday still maps and still loads; what changed is what a ROW is allowed to create. **NOTHING IS INVENTED TO GET PAST IT** — no placeholder phone, no zeroed Tax ID, and **`customer_ref` is NOT an identifier**: the import's own code calls it a reconciliation key into the utility's old system, so it identifies a ROW in somebody else's database and not a PERSON, and pressing it into service would feed the matcher evidence about nobody. **THE RESOLUTION IS THE SHARED ONE:** the row hands its evidence to the account door and `customers.link_customer` matches first, creates on no match, and creates nothing with no identifier — the import writes no customer and runs no matcher of its own. **STAGE 3 SAYS IT ON THE OPERATOR'S OWN FILE** (`ACC.CUSTOMER_IDENTITY_REQUIRED`, `ACC.CUSTOMER_IDENTITY_MISMATCH`, both ERROR/reject) so a rehearsal and a commit cannot disagree about which rows will load. **AND WHAT IT DOES NOT CHANGE:** accounts and customers already on file with no identifier stay lawful — the rule governs what is WRITTEN from now on. No migration invalidates a row, no backfill runs, and no screen refuses to display one. ⚠ **A ROW WHOSE ACCOUNT IS ALREADY ON FILE IS EXEMPT**, because an *Insert & update* re-run creates no customer and no account: requiring identity there would refuse a correction re-run of a file that loaded lawfully. | PO ruling 2026-09-01 (decision log, *"THE BULK ACCOUNTS IMPORT GETS IDENTITY COLUMNS TOO"*) · `CR-058` W7 · `BR-CUS-1023` (the creation minimum, unchanged) · `BR-CUS-1022` (which identifier counts) · `BR-MET-1030` (the premises import's identical shape) · `BR-CUS-046` (the published template) | **CONFIRMED (PO ruling)** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr058_w7_accounts_identity_import.py` |
| BR-CUS-1042 | **THE ACCOUNTS TEMPLATE'S `Customer #` COLUMN IS READ.** It was published, auto-mapped, reported *Matched* at stage 2 and **dropped on the floor** — its own help text promised *"allocated from the tenant's customer series"*, which reads as *a value you supply is used*, and nothing read it. A utility migrating in with its own customer numbers lost every one and could not reconcile against their old system. **NOW:** a row quoting a customer number that is already on file **LINKS to that customer**; a row quoting one that is free **CREATES the customer with that number** instead of allocating a fresh one; a blank cell allocates from the tenant's series exactly as before. **`Customer #` IS OURS AND `customer_ref` IS THEIRS** (`BR-CUS-054`) — two facts, two columns, and a utility needs both. ⚠ **IT IS A KEY, NOT AN IDENTIFIER**: quoting a number satisfies `BR-CUS-1041` no more than a legacy reference does, because it names a row rather than evidencing a person. ⚠ **AND THE SERIES CATCHES UP AFTER THE RUN**, the same way `DEF-577` made the account series catch up: a file quoting `C-00500` against a counter at 12 would otherwise collide the first time a clerk adds a customer by hand. | `DEF-2593` · PO ruling 2026-09-01 (*"the template's Customer # column, which the help text promises is used and nothing reads"*) · `BR-CUS-054` · `BR-TEN-035` · `DEF-577` (the same catch-up) | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr058_w7_accounts_identity_import.py` |
| BR-CUS-1043 | **THERE IS ONE ACCOUNTS IMPORT DOOR, AND THE OLD THREE-STEP ONE IS CLOSED.** `POST /accounts/imports` and `POST /accounts/imports/{id}/commit` are the pre-`CR-030` importer. Its SCREEN was deleted when the five-stage runner replaced it (`DEF-611`), its endpoints stayed mounted and working, and they still carried the rules the PO reversed: they **allocate an account number when the cell is blank** (reversed 2026-08-27 — *"we use utility provdied account number here … if row dont have account number reject it"*) and they **require no customer name**. After `BR-CUS-1041` they would also have been the one door that creates accounts with no identity. **BOTH WRITE ENDPOINTS NOW REFUSE**, by name, pointing at the live import; `GET /accounts/imports/{id}` still reads a batch that was staged or committed before today, because history is not a write. **REFUSED RATHER THAN DELETED:** a route that 404s tells an integration nothing, and the batch tables hold real staged rows. ⚠ **THE TEMPLATE ENDPOINT REFUSES TOO** — it published a column set no live door reads, which is the same *"promised and honoured by nothing"* fault as `BR-CUS-1042`, one file along. | `DEF-2592` · PO ruling 2026-08-27 (the account number comes off the file) · `BR-CUS-1041` · `DEF-611` (the screen this door lost) · `CR-030` W1 (the runner that replaced it) | **CONFIRMED (lane judgment, decision log 2026-09-01)** | `06-backend/tests/test_us_cus_019_account_import.py` |

## CR-058 wave W1 — match first, create second, and never a duplicate (2026-09-01)

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-1024 | 🔒 **A DOOR ABOUT TO MINT A CUSTOMER NUMBER MATCHES FIRST, AND AN EXACT AGREEMENT ON THE NAME AND ONE GOVERNMENT IDENTIFIER REUSES THE NUMBER ALREADY ON FILE (PO ruling 2026-09-01, `CR-058` ruling 0).** PO verbatim: *"During regular and master account creation process - we check whether we ahve mathicng customer ID, if yes, we use it if no we create it, given we have identify atleast 1"*. **THE RULE, in the order a door asks it:** a matching customer is found → **USE IT, never a second Customer #**; no match and an identifier is present → **CREATE one**; no match and **no** identifier → **CREATE NOTHING — and since the PO's ruling later the same day, the ACCOUNT DOES NOT OPEN EITHER.** ⚠ **THAT LAST CLAUSE WAS WRITTEN THE OTHER WAY ROUND EARLIER ON 2026-09-01 AND IS CORRECTED HERE, DATED, RATHER THAN QUIETLY REPLACED.** It first read *"CREATE NOTHING, and the account still opens"*, which was `BR-CUS-1023`'s signed position that morning. **PO verbatim, the same day:** *"Without identifier we cannot create Customer ID or master account ot regular account"* — so the identifier is a condition on **the account**, not on the number alone, and `BR-CUS-1023`'s clause *"An account may still be opened… a person standing at the counter who will not give an identifier still gets their water"* is **SUPERSEDED (2026-09-01, decision log)**. **The cost is stated rather than discovered: a person who will not identify themselves cannot be given service at all**, and a phone number is enough to avoid it. ⚠ **WHAT COUNTS AS *MATCHING* HERE IS NARROWER THAN THE CREATION MINIMUM AND WIDER THAN THE MONEY TEST, AND THE THREE MUST NEVER BE COLLAPSED INTO ONE.** `BR-CUS-1023` answers *what is enough to MINT a number* (a phone counts, for a person). `BR-CUS-089`/`BR-CUS-1022` answer *what is enough to MOVE MONEY* — the `strong` tier, name + date of birth + one government id for a person, name + Tax ID for a company. **This rule answers a third question — *is this number already on file* — and its test is the NAME plus at least one GOVERNMENT-GRADE identifier for that kind: a Social Security number or a driving licence for a person, the Tax ID for a company.** **A PHONE IS NOT ONE OF THEM.** It creates (`BR-CUS-1023`), it surfaces a candidate (`BR-CUS-1022`), and it may not reuse somebody else's number: a phone is the handle of whoever answers it, two adults share a household line, and a wrong reuse hands a stranger an existing customer's file. **THE SIGNED MONEY RULE IS NOT WIDENED BY ONE INCH.** A reuse records **NO tier and NO confirmer**, so `discounts.carry_forward_offer` still refuses and **$0.00** of anybody's deposit moves; `BR-CUS-089` continues to govern money exactly as signed, and the collision with it is stated in the decision log of 2026-09-01 rather than assumed. What changes is only **how many rows exist**, never what they are permitted to do. **WHY IT IS NOT THE CONSERVATIVE CHOICE TO REFUSE.** Refusing to reuse writes three customer rows holding one Social Security number, which is the database asserting that three different people share it — measured, `DEF-2587`: a landlord at three buildings giving a name and an SSN and no date of birth got **C-00001, C-00002 and C-00003**. Under this rule the same landlord gets **ONE** number at all three. **AMBIGUITY STILL REFUSES AND STILL SAVES.** Where **two or more** customers agree on the name and an identifier, nothing is reused and nothing is merged: the door mints its own number and returns the candidates for a human, because the PO ruled the same day that *a duplicate identifier refuses to LINK and never refuses to SAVE* and picking one of two would be a coin toss over somebody's file. **IT REUSES A NUMBER; IT NEVER CORRECTS ONE.** The presented identity fills EMPTY columns on the reused row and overwrites nothing — asserting a stored identifier is wrong is a different act with a different audit story and is not a side effect of opening an account. **IT INVALIDATES NOTHING ON FILE:** customers **and accounts** already holding a name and no identifier stay lawful, no migration, no backfill, and no screen refuses to display one (`BR-CUS-1023`'s own clause, unchanged — it governs what is WRITTEN from now on). **AND THE DOORS IT CLOSES ARE THE ONES A CLERK PRESSES.** The refusal is wired at `POST /accounts`, `POST /accounts/{id}/move-in` and `POST /accounts/master` (and therefore at New Connect and the premises-owner door, which both reach the master door through the seam). **THE BULK IMPORT IS NOT WIRED TO IT AND THAT IS DELIBERATE:** the import carries a name and a legacy reference and no identity at all, so the ruling read literally refuses every account row of every migration file — **a migration-blocking consequence that is flagged for the PO rather than shipped quietly** (decision log 2026-09-01). Its call site passes the exemption in writing and names this sentence as the reason. **AND NO DOOR MAY SATISFY IT BY INVENTING A VALUE** — no placeholder phone, no zeroed Tax ID, no account number reused as an identifier. ⚠ **A LEGACY REFERENCE IS A CARVE-OUT ONLY WHERE IT RESOLVES (amended 2026-09-01, same day).** A door naming a customer outright, or handing over an `external_ref` that finds a row, is pointing at a party who IS identified and passes. The check first read *was a reference sent* rather than *does it resolve* — its own comment already claimed the stronger test, which is hard-won law 1 — so any unseen string walked an account past the rule. **Found by putting the tenant seed THROUGH the rule instead of around it:** every seeded row carries a `customer_key`, so all 48 would have taken that exit. **THE DEMO SEED IS NOT EXEMPT AND ITS IDENTITY IS NOT INVENTED:** each of the 48 entries presents the kind its account type already implies and the `555-` phone the fixture already writes onto its contact row — a person by phone, a business by the reserved `99-0001234` Tax ID — which is the identity lane's own ruling of the same day applied a second time rather than re-decided (decision log 2026-09-01). **An entry with no usable identifier presents none and gets no number**, because inventing a digit to keep a fixture tidy is what this rule forbids by name. | PO ruling 2026-09-01 (decision log, `CR-058` ruling 0) · `BR-CUS-1023` (the creation minimum) · `BR-CUS-1022` (identity is per kind) · `BR-CUS-089` (PO 2026-08-25, closing `OQ-364` — the MONEY test, unchanged) · `BR-CUS-019` (advisory) · `BR-CUS-054` · `DEF-2587` · `apps/customers/identity_match.reuse_candidate` · `apps/customers/services.resolve_customer_for_account` | **CONFIRMED (the PO's own sentence; what counts as *matching* is this lane's judgment, recorded in the decision log of 2026-09-01) — 🔒 HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr058_w1_one_customer_every_door.py` |

**WHY THIS RULE HAS ITS OWN TEST INSTEAD OF LIVING INSIDE `BR-CUS-1023`.**
The two rules answer questions that look alike and are not: one asks *may a number
be minted*, the other asks *is one already on file*. They were the same function
until 2026-09-01 and that is precisely how `DEF-2587` happened — every door reused
the MONEY test as the test for whether to create a row, so a landlord who could not
pass a date-of-birth check got a fresh number at every building. Three questions,
three tests, and a test file that asserts all three on one landlord.

**THE ONE THING A LATER LANE WILL BE TEMPTED TO DO, NAMED SO IT GOES RED.**
Reuse and recognition now both answer *this is the same party*, so unifying them
reads like tidying. It is not: a reuse that recorded a `strong` tier would let a
name and a Social Security number carry a deposit that `BR-CUS-089` says needs a
date of birth too, and the **$153.50** of `WE-7`/`GT-23` would move on evidence the
PO deliberately refused. `test_a_reused_number_carries_no_tier_and_moves_no_money`
is the assertion that goes red on it.

## CR-072 — `service_requests` widens so a passer-by's report can live in it (2026-09-04)

**PO ruling, verbatim, on the live deployment at
`smartbillhub.bynry.com/silver-creek-water1/portal`:**

> *"all the request coming from portal should go in Service request. if we have to
> icnrease the defination of Service request we should do it"*

| BR | Statement | Sources | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-1044 | **`service_requests` CARRIES A PROBLEM REPORT, AND THE WIDENING IS TWO NULLABLE COLUMNS.** `problem_kind` is what a passer-by picked off `CWP-006`'s radio list — a leak, no water, low pressure, a missed collection, a damaged meter. `interruption_id` is the confirmed interruption a clerk later decides the report is about, NULL until then. **Everything else a report needs already had a column and was already being written to it**: the reporter's name and phone are `applicant_name` / `applicant_phone` (`BR-CWP-027` — the reporter need not be a customer), their own words are `what_they_said`, and the place is `address_as_given`, which `BR-CUS-070` keeps verbatim. So this is a one-fact widening, not a new shape. ⚠ **`problem_kind` AND NOT `kind`:** this row already carries `applicant_kind` and `outcome_kind`, and a third bare `kind` would be mis-read by the next person who greps for it. ⚠ **THE VOCABULARY STAYED IN `metering`** (`interruptions.STORABLE_KINDS`) and is imported, never copied — the operational record is metering's (B2 §2) and two spellings of one list is how they drift. | `CR-072` §4 · PO ruling 2026-09-04 · `BR-CWP-100` | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr072_report_is_a_request.py` |
| BR-CUS-1045 | **A REPORT MAY BE ATTACHED TO A CONFIRMED INTERRUPTION, BY A CLERK, ONCE.** `attach_interruption` is `@require_role(SYSTEM_ADMIN, BILLING_CLERK)`, refuses a request that is not a report (`problem_kind` is the test) and refuses a request that already has one — the same refusal `link_child` makes on this row, for the same reason: a silent re-point moves a report off a record somebody has already been told about. **Many reports may attach to one interruption**; nothing here is unique. ⚠ **THE LINK REVERSED DIRECTION.** `interruptions.service_request_id` was one-to-one and is what forced a second interruption row per report; it survives one release UNREAD as `CR-072` §7's rollback, and the wave that drops it is named in `apps/metering/interruptions.py`. ⚠ **CORRECTING A MIS-ATTACHED REPORT IS NOT BUILT**, and that is deliberate: a clerk can only reach an interruption THROUGH a report today — there is no list of them — so the wave that builds that list owns the correction. Recorded in the decision log rather than guessed at. | `CR-072` §4 · `BR-CWP-101` · `BR-CUS-080` (the `link_child` precedent) | **CONFIRMED (lane judgment, decision log 2026-09-04)** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr072_report_is_a_request.py` |
| BR-CUS-1046 | **A LIST OPENS ON WHAT WAS JUST CREATED, NOT ON THE ALPHABET.** PO ruling 2026-09-10, verbatim: ***"Whenever any account is created or any entry is created in the system, it automatically sorts it by the alphabetical order. It should always sort it by the creation order. The new newly created entries should always show on the top."*** The default sort of the accounts list is **`created_at` DESCENDING** — newest first — and a clerk's own chosen sort still wins and still survives a refresh. **This SUPERSEDES `BR-CUS-053`'s `default sort name A→Z` clause and `ACCOUNT_DEFAULT_SORT = "name"` at `selectors.py:111`.** ⚠ **IT IS NOT ONE LIST — THE RULING SAYS *any entry*.** The same default is owed by the seven sibling tabs on that screen (Requests, New connects, Move-ins, Move-outs, Transfers, Disconnects, Imports) and by every list in the product that opens on a name; applying it beyond the Accounts menu is a separate walk, recorded as a scoping judgment and NOT a re-ask (law 13). ⚠ **AND IT MUST BE FIXED WITH THE ORDERING, NOT BEFORE IT.** The sort happens in PYTHON: `account_list` materialises every matching row (`list(qs.order_by("id"))`, `selectors.py:2747`) and sorts it in memory (`selectors.py:2803-2814`), so a new default is one more branch of an in-memory sort rather than an `ORDER BY`. That is the **fifth `NFR-3` breach** of the shape `CLAUDE.md` names four of, and `NFR-3` is the T4/T5 flexibility the PO ruled load-bearing — so **fix the default and the ordering together, or the fix makes the breach worse and hides it behind a feature.** Worked figure shown to the PO: opening `/accounts` untouched sends `?sort=name&dir=asc`, putting an account created a minute ago wherever its customer's surname falls — **for half the alphabet, page 2**. | CONFIRMED 2026-09-12 | decision log 2026-09-12; raised by `05-prototype/02 Accounts Menu Blueprint.pdf` §10 `ACC-S11` | ⚠ **EXTENDED 2026-09-12 BY PO RULING — IT IS ALL EIGHT LISTS, AND THE SIX WITHOUT A SORT CONTROL GAIN ONE.** PO verbatim, answering `OQ-CUS-1790`: ***"(c) All eight open newest-first and the six gain a sort control. Largest job: six server-side sorts."*** Measured 2026-09-12: only the accounts tab declares sortable columns; `requests`, `new-connects`, `move-ins`, `move-outs`, `transfers` and `disconnects` declare none because each server accepts no `sort` parameter and fixes one `.order_by(...)`. **So on those six, *newest first* would REPLACE the only ordering a clerk had rather than be a default they could override — and this rule's own second clause (*a clerk's chosen sort must still win*) is true of all eight only under option (c).** `transfers` and `imports` already comply. ⚠ **THE FOUR URGENCY SENTENCES ON SCREEN MUST BE REWRITTEN** — Requests *"Sorted by age, oldest first"*, New connects / Move-ins / Move-outs *"soonest first"* — because once the default moves they are comments claiming a guarantee (law 1). The urgency ordering is not lost: it becomes one click, and the click survives a refresh. ✅ **BUILT 2026-09-12 IN `2.238.0` — `CR-094-BRC01` step 2 wave C; `DEF-CUS-4996`, `DEF-CUS-5060` and `DEF-CUS-5061` are closed against it.** What shipped, so no lane re-derives it: all eight lists default to **`created` descending**, declared once per read model as `DEFAULT_SORT`/`DEFAULT_DIR` and carried to the endpoint through a new `default_dir` argument on `parse_list_params` — without which the grammar fell back to `asc` and the screen opened on the OLDEST row, *"which is the exact inversion the ruling exists to remove"*. The six gained a server-side `sort` plus sortable columns, and **every one of the seven sort maps is ONE implementation**, `core.pagination.order_terms`, rather than seven `if sort == …` ladders — the PO's standing instruction of the same day, *"dont create duplcate code"*. **The sort NAMES are each screen's existing column keys** (`received_at`, `target_activation`, `account`, `account_no`, `reference`, `out_date`, `in_date`, `state`), because `DataTable` keys a clerk's saved column set on `screenId` + column key (`BR-SHL-013`) and renaming a column would have discarded every saved set on this screen. **The four sentences were rewritten in all eleven catalogues** as a three-word FACT plus a five-word INSTRUCTION (B14 rule 33; the rationale that followed the dash is zero words on screen now), and the two move tabs' keys reached the catalogues for the first time — they had been rendering an English `defaultValue` in all eleven languages. ⚠ **AND THE ORDERING MOVED INTO SQL WITH THE DEFAULT, WHICH THIS RULE DEMANDED.** `account_page` takes the page with `COUNT(*)` + `LIMIT/OFFSET` for `created`, `account_no`, `name` and `status`; measured by rows BUILT, `?per=1` cost **73** rows before and **1** after on the 73-account demo utility. `balance`, `last_bill`, `f.ageing` and `f.balance_gt` still build every matching row and sort in Python, named in `ACCOUNT_DERIVED_SORTS` — those four are figures that live in the LEDGER and in BILLING rather than in a column (`BR-CUS-016`, `DEF-127`), and a second implementation of them in SQL could disagree with the cell the same response renders. **So the expensive path is now the exception a clerk opts into rather than the default every clerk lands on.** ⚠ **ONE CLAUSE OF THIS RULE IS NOT YET TRUE AND IS FILED, NOT HIDDEN: `DEF-CUS-5091`.** *"A clerk's chosen sort must still win and still survive a refresh"* — it WINS on all eight tabs; it SURVIVES A REFRESH only on the accounts tab, the one of the eight that keeps its table state in the query string. The other seven hold it in `useState` by `DEF-664`'s deliberate design, because all eight share ONE query string. **No `Created` column was added to any tab** — neither the blueprint nor B18 draws one — so while a list is on its default no header carries the glyph and the sentence under the pager is what states the ordering.
| BR-CUS-1047 | **AN ACCOUNT MAY BE RE-POINTED AT ANOTHER UNIT AS A CORRECTION — UNDER FOUR CONDITIONS, ALL OF WHICH ARE THE RULING.** PO ruling 2026-09-12, verbatim: ***"(a) Narrow the rule to allow exactly this correction: a reason from a fixed list, a supervisor's approval, effective today, never backdated, issued bills untouched."*** **(1)** the reason comes from a FIXED LIST, never free text; **(2)** a supervisor approves; **(3)** it is effective TODAY and **never backdated**; **(4)** **every issued bill is left exactly as it is** — hard rule 4 is untouched, and a bill that was wrong is credited and replaced through its own flow. **This NARROWS `BR-CUS-017`** (CONFIRMED 2026-08-08) and supersedes the blanket refusal at `apps/customers/services.py:3223` (`if field in ("unit", "account_type")`) **for the unit field only**. ⚠ **A MOVE IS NOT A CORRECTION AND STILL GOES THROUGH MOVE-OUT AND MOVE-IN.** **WORKED, on the blueprint's fixture `ACC-E07` (p185):** `A-11812 · Bramble Bakery`, `Suite 1 · U-000417` → `Suite 2 · U-000418` at the same address — plan `SCW-RES v1` → `SCM-COM v2`, base **`$20.00` → `$63.00` a month (`$43.00` more)**, meters `E-4417/W-4417` → `E-4418/W-4418`, **the reading history stays with Suite 1**, billing cycle unchanged, route changes, **the five already-issued bills stand**, and **the address does not move** because it belongs to the building (hard rule 8). Raised by the PO themselves: *"from the edit perspective, I cannot see the unit edit where I want to change unit a to unit b."* Without it the only fix was to close the account and open another, losing its whole history to correct a typo. ⚠ **BUILT 2026-09-13 — `POST /accounts/{id}/correct-unit` (`apps/customers/unit_correction.py`), the record in `account_unit_corrections` (migrations `customers 0045` + `0046` RLS), proved by `06-backend/tests/test_br_cus_1047_unit_correction.py`.** It was CONFIRMED for a day and reached no line of code, which nothing in this repository can see: a gate reads code against code and a ruling that never arrives leaves nothing to disagree with. **Two readings the build had to make, both recorded in the decision log of 2026-09-13 rather than assumed:** ***(1) WHO IS A SUPERVISOR** — `@require_role(SYSTEM_ADMIN, EXECUTIVE)`, because the PO has already answered twice (law 13, never re-ask): `OQ-042`'s closure names them verbatim (*"supervisor = SysAdmin/Executive"*) and A4's signed grid gives `Approve` to exactly those two on `adjustment_approval`. A Billing Clerk is refused. **(2) WHAT THE FIXED LIST IS** — the ruling requires one and enumerates none, so it is DERIVED: the three doors that write `accounts.unit_id` are the three ways a record can name the wrong one — `opened_on_wrong_unit`, `imported_onto_wrong_unit`, `moved_in_to_wrong_unit`. **None of them says a customer moved**, which is what keeps `BR-CUS-017` intact. ⚠ **AND ONE HALF IS DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT:** A4 gives a Billing Clerk `Request` on that same row, and nothing in this product implements a request that waits for an approval — `OQ-CUS-12355` asks whether a clerk should be able to raise one. | CONFIRMED 2026-09-12 · **BUILT 2026-09-13** | decision log 2026-09-12 + 2026-09-13; closes `OQ-CUS-1769`; unblocks `R5`'s `ACC-E07` and nothing else; opens `OQ-CUS-12355` |

The blueprint's own wording of the same ruling: ***"change the premises tab to accounts tab. the rows are the accounts at the same premises, and a tenant's account is its own — as is its money. **THREE THINGS ARE NOT THE DEFAULT AND ALL THREE ARE THE RULING'S:** **(1)** the master is IN the list, marked as the one you are on, and carries no `Open` action; **(2)** its `UNIT` cell reads *Common areas ×N* from `covered_unit_ids(account)` — its own OPEN service agreements, which is what billing prices — because a master names no unit at all (`BR-CUS-1010`); **(3)** there is **deliberately NO total under the balance column**. **WORKED, on the blueprint's own building (`1408 Elm Street`, p191):** six rows summing to **`$810.95`**, which is the owner's own **`$69.60`** plus **`$741.35`** owed by three unrelated tenants — a figure that means nothing and that, printed where an owner can see it, invites exactly the wrong conclusion. **The tab is shown ONLY where `kind='master'`**, and `?tab=accounts` on an ordinary account falls back to `Overview` (`BR-SHL-011`). **NO NEW TABLE, NO NEW RELATIONSHIP AND NO NEW ENDPOINT** — `GET /accounts?f.premises_id=` is `CR-056` W1's own filter, so the paging, the search and the status filter are all the SERVER's (`NFR-3`); | BR-CUS-1050 | **A DEPOSIT IS ONE CAPABILITY, DESIGNED ONCE, AND EVERY WIZARD THAT NEEDS ONE MOUNTS IT.** PO ruling 2026-09-12, verbatim: ***"(a) It is one concept — money held against an account — and add one more step of deposit into the move in stepper and copy the deposit step also. from the new account."*** and ***"Make it as account preferences in that user can also select paperless billing. that should also be available."*** **⚠ THE ACCOUNTING WAS ALREADY RULED AND IS NOT TOUCHED:** `BR-CUS-025` — a deposit is a **LIABILITY, never revenue** (2026-08-12) — and `deposit_liability` **2300** is one of the eleven designations the PO named account by account. **This rule is about the DOOR, not the books. ⚠ AND THE BOOKS' SIDE WAS ALREADY BUILT, WHICH IS THE FINDING RATHER THAN THE RULE:** `payments.services.record_payment(purpose="deposit")` has posted `DR undeposited funds / CR 2300`, moved the register through `BR-CUS-670`'s `customers.services.record_deposit_movement` and queued the customer's receipt **since 2026-08-27** — and **no screen in the product ever sent it.** Four wizards drew a deposit and all four refused it (`DEF-CUS-5112`). **THE SHAPE, and the whole of it is that there is one of each:** ONE customers-side module (`apps/customers/deposit.py` — the vocabulary, the validation, the handover), ONE `deposit` key in `services.CREATE_FIELDS` so `MOVE_IN_FIELDS` gains it in the same line and S18 and S19 cannot drift, ONE React component (`depositCapability.tsx`) whose only per-flow difference is a `carryForward` prop, and **NO second writer of `accounts.deposit_on_file`** — the figure is read back off the payment's own `deposit_held_after`. **THREE ANSWERS AND NOT TWO:** `required` · `not_required` (none was assessed) · `waived` (one WAS assessed and somebody decided not to collect it) — collapsing the last two would lose a decision that has a person behind it. ⚠ **AND THE THIRD ANSWER NEEDS A SUPERVISOR — AMENDED 2026-09-20, `DEF-CUS-5908`.** Blueprint **p073** draws the deposit dropdown's own help line as *"Yes — take one · No · **Waive it (needs a supervisor)**"*, over a screen whose actor block reads `Sam Ortiz · Billing Clerk`. **The parenthesis is a REQUIREMENT and not a caption**, and until 2026-09-20 nothing enforced it: `deposit.take()` returned the waived note for any actor at all, so **a billing clerk waived `$150.00` and the whole move-in committed** — account, reads and all. ⚠ **WHO COUNTS AS A SUPERVISOR WAS NOT PUT TO THE PO AGAIN, BECAUSE IT IS ALREADY ANSWERED TWICE (hard-won law 13).** This product's roles are exactly `system_admin`, `billing_clerk`, `executive` and none is called *supervisor*; **`OQ-042`'s closure** says verbatim *"supervisor = SysAdmin/Executive"* (built on since `CR-038` W7) and **A4's signed grid** row `adjustment_approval` reads System Admin **Approve** · Executive **Approve** · Billing Clerk **Request**. `BR-CUS-1047` already applied exactly this reading to the unit correction, so the waive gate is the SAME reading on a second control rather than a lane's pick. **IT IS A PER-DECISION GATE, NEVER `@require_role` ON THE DOOR:** a Billing Clerk may take, defer and decline a deposit all day — p073 draws them doing it — and only the third answer names an authority. **The refusal is `BIZ-APPROVAL` and not `AUTH-002`**, because it is *this answer needs a second person*, not *you may not use this door*; it cites `BR-CUS-025 · BR-CUS-1050`, carries `accepts` naming the two roles so the clerk knows who to ask, and rides `BR-SHL-019`'s one transaction so a refused waive rolls the account, the reads and the payment back together. **THE CLEARING DATE IS THE CONDITION:** a recorded deposit reaches the register and the 2300 liability **only when its payment clears**, so both move together or neither does; a blank clearing date leaves the account reading **`$0.00`** while the cash is in the drawer, and the step says so before the commit. **ONE TRANSACTION** (`BR-SHL-019`): the account, then the reads, then the payment — a refused payment rolls the account back and the clerk fixes the field with everything still on screen. **WHERE THE AMOUNT COMES FROM, measured:** `rating.plan_deposit_rules` (rate_plan × account_type → one fixed amount + its `CHAR(3)`, `BR-RAT-020/023`), tenant-editable on Plans & Rates — **so it is a tenant setting, not a constant.** The clerk TYPES the figure, which is the drawing's own ruling (*"Typed by hand today."* beside a dashed *"A credit tier would size this, and nothing works one out"* — `BR-CUS-058`'s tier is COMPUTED with no input path), and the server RESOLVES the plan's standard and **reports it beside what was typed** rather than overriding it. ⚠ **B14 rule 37 — the `$150.00` drawn on blueprint page 70 is an ILLUSTRATION and is stated nowhere in the product.** **WORKED, end to end on `brazosbend`:** a move-in takes **`$150.00`** by card with a clearing date, and one commit writes the account, one `cleared` payment of purpose `deposit`, the 2300 credit, `accounts.deposit_on_file = 150.00` and `deposit_received_on` = the clearing day; where the plan's rule says **`$120.00`** the result carries `deposit_amount_differs_from_plan_standard` and the **`$150.00`** still stands. Leave the clearing date blank and the same commit writes the payment and leaves the register at **`$0.00`**, carrying `deposit_payment_has_not_cleared`. **PAPERLESS AND TEXT REMINDERS ARE COLLECTED ON THE RENAMED STEP** — `accounts.paperless` and `accounts.sms_reminders`, writable since 2026-08-31 and refused by both screens for twelve days afterwards (`DEF-CUS-5113`) — and **`BR-BIL-036` is untouched: print is the FLOOR and both only ADD a channel**, which the step states once. ⚠ **`DEF-1713` IS NOT CLOSED BY ANY OF THIS** — a MIGRATED deposit written by the importer with no posting behind it is a different door, and the S36 tie-out's `deposit_liability` row stays DRIFTING for an estate seeded that way. ⚠ **`OQ-510` IS ANSWERED FOR THE COLLECTION HALF AND NOT FOR CARRY-FORWARD:** the wizards DO offer a deposit election now, and carry-forward stays a **stated absence** rather than a control, because `BR-CUS-024`'s mechanics are still unsigned and a control that saved nowhere would be a decision the customer believes was recorded. ⛔ **AMENDED 2026-09-23 — THE AMOUNT IS THE PLAN'S FIGURE FOR THE CATEGORY, NOT TYPED, AND *NO* IS OFFERED ONLY WHERE THE PLAN ASKS NONE (`OQ-CUS-12620`).** PO, verbatim: *"you need to take deposit amount as per plan ; plan will have deposite chanrge code as per customer categoris ;so that value should be atken as by default no override is required"* and *"no, we ahve to take depsoite if plans asked clark can waive it off - (a) When the plan asks a deposit: take it or waive it (supervisor). "No" only when the plan asks none."*. The figure comes from `plan_deposit_rules` (`BR-RAT-020`/`023`) via the unit's plan and the account's category; the server takes it from the plan; *Why this amount* is removed. Plan asks a figure → *Take it* or *Waive* (System Admin/Executive, unchanged, with a reason, now kept ON THE ACCOUNT rather than only in the pruned `audit_log`). Plan asks none → no deposit, nothing typed. Worked: Rosa, Residential, plan asks **$150.00** → the step shows $150.00, not editable; a billing clerk cannot skip it. ⛔ **AMENDED 2026-09-24 — A BILLING CLERK OR A SYSTEM ADMIN WAIVES (`OQ-CUS-12623`).** PO, verbatim: *"p3 - clark and sysem admin can do it"*. Supersedes the 2026-09-20 set (System Admin or Executive) and corrects the 2026-09-23 note above that read *"clark can waive it off"* as not widening it. A reason is still required and the waive is still kept on the account. | PO ruling 2026-09-12 (decision log, *THE ONE DEPOSIT CAPABILITY*); blueprint pages 63-64 and 69-72, `05-prototype/02 Accounts Menu Blueprint.pdf` at commit `033981d4f`; `BR-CUS-025` · `BR-CUS-037` · `BR-CUS-050` · `BR-CUS-058` · `BR-CUS-670` · `BR-PAY-012` · `BR-PAY-021` · `BR-PAY-026` · `BR-RAT-020/023` · `BR-BIL-036` · `BR-SHL-019` · `BR-SHL-046` · B14 rules 21, 32, 33, 37, 38, 39 | CONFIRMED 2026-09-12 | `06-backend/tests/test_cr094_wh_deposit.py` (21 cases — the move-in takes it and the register moves · the same capability serves S18 · the drawer case leaves it at `$0.00` · waived and not-required never collapse · the plan's standard is reported and never enforced · eight refusals each naming its OWN field · a refused deposit writes no account at all · **and one case that makes the register writer unreachable and asserts `deposit_on_file` does NOT move, so a second writer in customers goes red**) · `07-frontend/src/features/customers/depositCapability.test.tsx` (21 cases — the five payload branches, the gate's named field, the two-decimal total, the derived vocabulary, the ten drawn controls by id each with a bound label, and a `null` currency rendering a sentence rather than `USD`) · `MoveInWizard.test.tsx` (the six-step rail, step 4's paperless, step 5's deposit) |
