moving a control that cannot act would be a dialog that opens onto nothing. | PO 2026-09-13 · `ACC-S14` · `CR-094-BRC01` R4 · B14 rule 30 (declined) · finding F12 | CONFIRMED · **BUILT 2026-09-13, `CR-094-BRC01` wave L, product `2.242.0`** — the eight are CHILD ROUTES of `/accounts/:accountId` and `AccountDetailPage` renders whichever one the URL names inside the shared `ModalShell` through an `<Outlet/>`. **8,838 lines of existing screen were MOUNTED and none of it copied**; the whole wave is one 290-line container (`07-frontend/src/features/customers/accountActionOverlays.tsx`), two OPTIONAL props on `ModalShell` (`size="wide"`, `titleHidden`), a three-line path fallback in `DisconnectWizard`, and the route table. The nesting is what keeps `useParams().accountId` resolving for the five screens that read it, `?step=` surviving a reload for the three that park there, and each screen's existing `navigate(accountDetailPath(accountId))` exit CLOSING the dialog — so no exit was rewired and no address changed spelling. **Three of the eight keep their own door**: `/billing/bills/{id}/document`, `/billing/fix?bill=` and `/disconnects/new?account=` are all still declared, still mounted and still what every other screen links to. | `07-frontend/src/features/customers/accountActionOverlays.dom.test.tsx` (20 cases — every door by ADDRESS and by COMPONENT, both doors, park-and-resume, and the dialog head measured against each mounted screen's own source) · `accountQuickActions.test.ts` (22 cases — eleven of thirteen open over the account, the two that do not are HELD) |
| BR-CUS-1052 | **A DOCUMENT FILED ON AN ACCOUNT CAN BE REMOVED BY A CLERK, AND *REMOVED* MEANS IT LEAVES THE ACCOUNT — NOT THAT THE BYTES ARE ERASED.** PO ruling 2026-09-13, verbatim: ***"We should have this functionality"***, answering the `CR-094` wave I report that an account's Documents tab shipped with no delete of any kind — *"a clerk who files the wrong scan cannot take it back"*. ⚠ **IT COLLIDES WITH A CONFIRMED RULE AND THE SUPERSESSION IS NARROW AND STATED.** `BR-SHL-031` (CONFIRMED, PO DF-1..4 2026-08-08) makes the legal class **WRITE-ONCE, SEVEN YEARS**, and `stored_documents` enforces it with a database trigger; `NFR-35` says the same. **Neither is reversed.** What the clerk's control does is WITHDRAW the filing: the document stops being listed, opened or counted on that account, the withdrawal writes an audit row naming who did it and when, and the stored object stays under its retention lock where the law requires it. **A misfiled scan therefore stops being part of the customer's file the moment it is withdrawn, which is what the ruling asks for**, and no signed retention promise is broken to do it. ⚠ **AND THE AUDIT-LOCKED CLASSES STILL REFUSE:** `BR-CUS-036` makes system and contract documents audit-locked, so a withdrawal there is refused BY NAME with its reason on screen rather than hidden — a clerk may take back a scan they filed, not a contract the system attached. ✅ **BUILT 2026-09-13** (`CR-094-BRC01` wave M, `2.245.0`) — **the same day it was ruled.** `customers/0044` adds `withdrawn_at` and `withdrawn_by` to the FILING CARD and nothing else; `DELETE /accounts/{id}/documents/{docId}` stamps them; `documents_page`, its `COUNT(*)` and `document_link` all stop seeing the row, so a withdrawn filing is **not listed, not counted and not openable** and answers the same 404 as one that never existed (`BR-TEN-003`, HR-1). ⚠ **THE STORED OBJECT IS NOT TOUCHED AND THAT IS ASSERTED RATHER THAN CLAIMED:** the withdrawal makes no call into `document_store`, and `test_the_stored_object_is_untouched` FETCHES the object and re-hashes it AFTER the filing has gone. `BR-SHL-031`'s write-once trigger and `NFR-35`'s seven years are untouched. ⚠ **THE AUDIT-LOCKED CLASSES REFUSE WITH 409 `BIZ-LOCKED`, NEVER 404** — a 404 would teach a clerk the row had gone when it had not — and `BR-SHL-025` puts the same reason on the disabled bin before they press it. **`contract` is UPLOADABLE and NOT WITHDRAWABLE**, which is the rule and not an oversight: a countersigned lease arrives on paper and has to get in; the lock is that nothing takes it out. ⚠ **NOTHING RESTORES A WITHDRAWN FILING, AND THAT IS A RECORDED JUDGMENT RATHER THAN AN OVERSIGHT** — nothing was ruled about it, so the narrower thing was built: gone from the clerk's view, recoverable only by somebody holding the audit trail. The reasoning is in the decision log of 2026-09-13. A restore control is a second decision and it is unmade. ⚠ **AND `OQ-1340` IS OPEN OVER THE AUDIT ROW** — `audit_log` is pruned on a retention schedule, so **no document, screen or docstring may describe that row as a permanent record** of what the account used to hold. | PO 2026-09-13 · `BR-CUS-036` · `BR-SHL-031` (unreversed) · `NFR-35` · `ACC-S8` | CONFIRMED (PO 2026-09-13) · **BUILT 2026-09-13** | `CR-094-BRC01` wave M · `2.245.0` · `customers/0044` · `06-backend/tests/test_cr094_m_account_document_withdrawal.py` (13 cases) · `07-frontend/src/features/customers/AccountDocumentsTab.test.tsx` (21 cases) |
| BR-CUS-1053 | **AN ACCOUNT'S DETAILS ARE CORRECTED FROM THE ACCOUNT, AND EVERY GROUP ON THE OVERVIEW CARRIES ITS OWN EDIT CONTROL.** PO instruction 2026-09-13, verbatim: ***"Build the account edit screen"***, and, listing it among the work to start, ***"Correcting an account's details from the account … Every group on the Overview carrying its own edit control"***. It builds `ACC-S1` and `ACC-S15` together because they are one surface: `R5` draws SIX edit surfaces, one per Overview group, which is the asset module's answer to the same problem (`AST-B-25`, six panel pencils) rather than one form over twenty-three fields. ⚠ **WHAT THE SERVER ACTUALLY ACCEPTS IS THE SCOPE, AND IT IS NARROWER THAN THE SCREEN LOOKS.** ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-10-06 (`DEF-CUS-9820`, PO ruling D4 (a), verbatim: *"Yes: add an editable "PO on bills" field to the account's Billing preferences. Bills drafted after the change print the new PO; bills already issued keep the old one"*): `PATCH_FIELDS` holds TWELVE — `po_number` joined, offered on the BILLING PREFERENCES form, trimmed, blank clears it, longer than 60 characters (the move-in's bound) refused by name; the eleven below are the count as of 2026-09-13.** ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-13 (wave N, measured against the code): THE TWO FIGURES BELOW WERE WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS AND ARE PINNED NOW BY `06-backend/tests/test_cr094_n_account_edit_scope.py::…tc6`.** `PATCH_FIELDS` holds **ELEVEN** names and not seven — the three this rule omitted are **`tax_exempt_ref`, `tax_exempt_expires` and `preferred_language`**, and all three are fields `R5`'s own dialog DRAWS, so a lane trusting this sentence would have shipped a form missing the certificate number, its expiry and the language, green all the way. `REFUSED_FIELDS` holds **THIRTY-FOUR** entries and not eleven; the eleven named here are a real subset — the columns owned by another path. ⚠ **AND THE *silent skip* BELOW IS NOT WHAT HAPPENS:** `unit` and `account_type` are refused at the door with `VAL-002` before that branch can run (it is dead code — `_validate_account` populates those keys only under `if not partial:`), and the real fault is that they come back **`unknown_field` instead of naming their rule**, which `_reject_unknown`'s own docstring promises never to do. Walking every column made it **SEVEN columns wide, not two** — `DEF-CUS-5127` — and among the five nobody had looked at is **`external_ref`, the utility's own legacy account id, settable on create and on import and correctable from nowhere** (`DEF-CUS-5130`). **BUILT 2026-09-13 as `ACC-S1` + `ACC-S15` (`2.249.0`):** eight groups on the Overview carry a control, exactly one is live (`Account`), the other seven are disabled with their reason under `BR-SHL-025`, and `status`, `opened_at` and `custom_data` are writable and deliberately NOT offered — each recorded in the decision log of 2026-09-13 rather than dropped. — `PATCH /accounts/{id}` (`services.update_account`) takes **`account_subtype`, `autopay`, `opened_at`, `paperless`, `sms_reminders`, `status`, `tax_exempt`** and the tenant's custom fields; **eleven columns are refused BY NAME with their own rule id** (`REFUSED_FIELDS` — `account_no` is allocated by the series, `balance` is the ledger's, `credit_tier` is computed, `currency` is the tenant's, both deposit columns are the deposit path's, `bad_address` and `nsf_count` are written by payments, `closed_at` is the move-out flow, `kind` is the master-account door, `unit_id` is `BR-CUS-017`). ⚠ **THIS ROW SAID `unit` AND `account_type` WERE *SKIPPED SILENTLY* AND THAT WAS NEVER WHAT HAPPENED — MEASURED 2026-09-13 (`DEF-CUS-5127`).** Both were refused at the door with `VAL-002`, because `_reject_unknown` checks the payload against `PATCH_FIELDS` before anything else; the `continue` a function later is DEAD CODE, since `_validate_account` populates those two keys only under `if not partial:` and `update_account` always passes `partial=True`. **What was actually wrong is the REASON GIVEN:** a real column answered *"unknown field"*, so a clerk correcting a miscategorised account was told the field does not exist. **Both now refuse BY NAME with their rule** — `unit`/`unit_id` cite `BR-CUS-017 · BR-CUS-1047` and point at `POST /accounts/{id}/correct-unit`; `account_type` cites `BR-CUS-012` and says plainly that no rule states how a category may be changed (`OQ-CUS-12356`). **Neither is offerable on this form.** ⚠ **THE UNIT IS A SEPARATE CAPABILITY AND IS NOT FOLDED IN:** `OQ-CUS-1769` closed 2026-09-12 on option (a) as `BR-CUS-1047` — a unit correction is allowed with a reason from a fixed list, a supervisor's approval, effective today, never backdated, issued bills untouched. ⚠ **IT WAS BUILT ON 2026-09-13 AS ITS OWN WAVE, exactly as this clause said it should be** (`POST /accounts/{id}/correct-unit`) — this row read *"the code still refuses it, so that ruling is unbuilt"* until then, and that sentence is the one a lane would have trusted. **It is still NOT folded into this form:** a supervisor approves a unit correction and a Billing Clerk may not, so it cannot sit among the Overview's edit pencils. | PO 2026-09-13 · `ACC-S1` · `ACC-S15` · `R5` · `services.update_account` · `REFUSED_FIELDS` · `BR-CUS-1047` (unbuilt) | CONFIRMED | CR-094 wave N |
| BR-CUS-1054 | **A CUSTOMER'S OWN DETAILS CAN BE CORRECTED, AND TODAY THEY CANNOT BE CORRECTED ANYWHERE IN THE PRODUCT.** PO instruction 2026-09-13, verbatim: ***"Start the customer edit one also - we will need this"***, given immediately after being told that `GET /customers/{id}` is READ-ONLY and that no PATCH exists for a customer on any door. **The account edit surfaces (`BR-CUS-1053`) do not reach these fields** — a misspelt name, a wrong telephone number or a wrong email belong to the CUSTOMER, and `update_account` neither accepts nor refuses them because they are not its columns. ⚠ **IT IS A PII PATH AND THAT IS THE WHOLE DIFFICULTY.** A customer's identity is split across two tables and several encrypted columns: `customers` holds `display_name`, `kind` (person|company), `customer_no`, `external_ref` (the legacy id), `date_of_birth`, and **`ssn`, `driving_licence`, `phone` and `tax_id` as `EncryptedTextField`s, each with its own `crypto_context` and a `_bidx` BLIND INDEX column beside it**; `Contact` holds the `email` (citext, `DB-15`). **A correction therefore re-encrypts and RE-COMPUTES the blind index in the same write, or search silently stops finding the row** — the value is right and the index points at the old one, which no test that reads the field can see. ⚠ **THE MAILING ADDRESS IS HARD RULE 8'S NAMED EXCEPTION AND STAYS ONE:** `customers.addresses` `kind='mailing'` is where the BILL goes and is the only address a customer owns; `kind='service'` is RETIRED and a write asking for it is refused BY NAME. ⚠ **THE PORTAL SIGN-IN IS A DIFFERENT COLUMN AND MUST NOT MOVE AS A SIDE EFFECT.** `apps/portal` holds its own `email`, which **is the sign-in identifier, unique per tenant**, normalised through `credentials.normalise_email`. Correcting the contact email does not and may not re-point a credential: that is an identity change, it is another module's table (B2 §2 forbids the write), and doing it quietly would lock a customer out of the portal or hand their account to a new address. **Whether the two should be linked is unruled and returns to the PO as a question, not as a default.** ⚠ **ISSUED BILLS DO NOT RESTATE** — a bill's customer name is locked into its snapshot at issue (hard rule 4), so correcting a name today changes what the NEXT bill says and nothing already sent. | PO 2026-09-13 · `GET /customers/{id}` (read-only) · `models.Customer` · `models.Contact` · hard rule 8's mailing exception · hard rule 4 | **CONFIRMED — ✅ BUILT 2026-09-13 (version `2.247.0`).** `PATCH /customers/{id}` over `apps/customers/customer_edit.update_customer`, reached from a pencil on each row of the customers list (`CustomerEditDialog.tsx`). **Five fields move:** `display_name`, `external_ref`, `date_of_birth` (a PERSON's only), `phone` — through `identity_match.set_identifier`, so the ciphertext and its `*_bidx` token are written in ONE act — and `email`, onto the primary `contacts` row of **every account the customer holds** (`BR-CUS-054`'s landlord case; measured at **2** accounts from one save). **The proof that matters is a SEARCH and not a read-back:** `512-555-0142` corrected to `512-555-0199`, then the clerk's own search finds her by the new number and finds nobody by the old one — and a kept case performs the naive `customer.phone = …; save()` and asserts the search goes BLIND on it, so the assertion is proved capable of failing. **Refused BY NAME, each with the rule that owns it:** `kind` (`BR-CUS-1022` — a flip would strand every identifier on file in a column the new kind's matcher never reads → `OQ-CUS-12351`), `customer_no`, `postal`/`address`, `portal_email`, `match_tier`/`match_basis`, `is_sample` and every `*_bidx`. **The audit masks the three government identifiers at the boundary** (`•••••6789`) and deliberately does NOT mask the phone — decision log 2026-09-13. **The portal sign-in does not move** (`BR-CWP-004`, B2 §2) and the screen says so — ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-10-22: `OQ-CUS-12350` is CLOSED.** PO, verbatim: *"give option to clark to make it log in email also if they choose it make login and bill email sent otherwise keep them separate"*. A tick box *"Also use this as the portal sign-in email"*, unticked by default and shown only with a portal login, moves the login too (old address told, reset link to the new one); unticked, nothing about the sign-in changes. The same box is on the account's *Customer* pencil when exactly one login holds the account (decision log 2026-10-22, lane SIGNIN-EMAIL). ⚠ **THE MAILING ADDRESS IS STILL UNWRITABLE BY ANY CLERK DOOR** — `contacts` and `addresses` are created at account-create and thereafter written only from the customer PORTAL; this wave opens the first clerk door onto a contact and moves the EMAIL only. 🔒 `HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING`. | CR-094-BRC01 wave R · `06-backend/apps/customers/customer_edit.py` · `06-backend/tests/test_br_cus_1054_customer_edit.py` (28 cases) · `07-frontend/src/features/customers/CustomerEditDialog.dom.test.tsx` |
| BR-CUS-1070 | **EACH SERVICE ON A REQUEST CARRIES ITS OWN REQUESTED START, AND IT IS NOT THE JOB'S `Connection date` UNDER ANOTHER NAME.** The blueprint draws the two as separate facts on one screen — capture step 2 puts a `SERVICE · REQUESTED START` table under the services multiselect (*"Each service carries its own start. A block that needs electric on the 12th and sewer a fortnight later is one request and not two, so the date sits per service rather than once on the form."*, p076) and puts `Connection date` — *"The whole job, where the applicant has a date in mind."* — separately beneath it. **THE FALLBACK IS SETTLED BY p080 AND IT RUNS IN NEITHER DIRECTION:** step 6's review draws `Requested start → 05/12/2026, all three` and `Connection date → — not given` in adjacent rows of the same request, which no fallback can produce. So **`service_request_services.requested_start` NULL means *the clerk gave none*, never *read the request's*,** and `service_requests.requested_start` is never derived from the services. ⚠ **THIS SUPERSEDES THE BUILT BEHAVIOUR AT ITS OWN SENTENCE, RATHER THAN A SIGNED RULE.** `set_services` created every row with `requested_start=request.requested_start` from CR-030 W4 until 2026-09-15 — a copy, never a clerk's statement — so no clerk could ever give two services different dates; the column existed, was written, and was not theirs to set. **Measured on `brazosbend` the day it changed: 8 service requests, `service_request_services` 0 rows**, because nothing in `sample_data.py` called `set_services`; no backfill was written and none was owed. ⚠ **THE SEEDER HALF IS BUILT AS OF 2026-09-16 (`DEF-CUS-5423`, PO: *"can you add this in seeder data"*).** `sample_data._drive_cr030_request` now drives capture step 2 like every other step, so a freshly seeded utility carries **12 service rows across 7 of its 8 sample requests** — `SR-26-0043`, the 25-unit build, asking for **electric on the 4th and water on the 18th**, a fortnight apart on one request, which is p076's own argument and the only case that proves this column is a clerk's answer rather than a copy. The other six cover every shape the read has to tell apart: all services agreeing on one date over a BLANK connection date (p080's drawn pair), all of them undated, one dated and one not, a single service, a job date with no service dates, and **no services at all** on the eighth, deliberately, because `count: 0` is a real answer. The set is narrowed to what the tenant SELLS — `seams.enabled_service_codes`, never a list of names (`BR-RAT-024`, `G47`) — so a utility is never asked for a service it does not run and none is switched on to make the data look richer. **The backfill statement is unchanged**: the demo tenant planted before that date still holds none. **The write door is unchanged and stays one door** — `POST /service-requests/{id}/services`, `@require_role(SYSTEM_ADMIN, BILLING_CLERK)` — and it now accepts `{"code", "requested_start"}` objects beside the bare codes it always took, a bare code meaning *no date given*. `available` is untouched and still starts NULL (*not yet asked*, REQ-03.3). **The read serves `requested_start_agreement` = `{count, agreed, unset}` beside the service list**, derived once on the server so two screens cannot disagree about whether the dates agree: `agreed` is the single ISO date when every service carries the same non-null one, else `null`. | blueprint entry 06 p076 + p080 (read as images, scale 2) · `DEF-CUS-5195` · decision log 2026-09-15 | CONFIRMED (lane decision — nothing here is a fact only the PO holds, law 13) | `06-backend/tests/test_cr094_per_service_starts.py` |
| BR-CUS-1075 | **A REFUSAL AND AN ASK-FOR-MORE EACH RECORD THREE THINGS FOR THREE DIFFERENT AUDIENCES, AND ONLY ONE OF THEM REACHES THE APPLICANT.** The Accounts blueprint's entry 06 draws both dialogs (p084–p086, read as images) and the product shipped one field each. **REFUSING.** `Reason *` is a SELECT of five — *Not our service area · Documents not provided · Applicant withdrew · Duplicate of another request · Other* — and it is what the UTILITY files the refusal under: a closed list so that *why does this utility turn people down* is a question a report can answer, which five hundred typed sentences cannot. `What to tell them`, captioned *"Goes into the refusal letter."*, is the APPLICANT's and is the body of the letter. The free-text reason stays the REGISTER's and stays internal. **ASKING FOR MORE.** `WHAT IS NEEDED` is a tick list of four — a readable site plan · proof of identity checked and verified · a connection date · something else — and it is the CLERK's checklist: stored on the request, kept on its history, and **not sent**, because the drawing attaches *"Sent to them"* to the message box alone. `Message to the applicant`, captioned *"Sent to them and kept on the request's history."*, is what goes out, and it replaces one fixed sentence — *"More information is needed before this can be reviewed."* — that every request in the product sent alike and that no applicant ever received. **NO CODE OF OURS REACHES AN APPLICANT.** B14 rule 32 keeps a rule id or an internal code off a clerk's screen, and the argument is stronger for a letter, which outlives the screen: an applicant reading *duplicate_of_another_request* is reading our database. The letters carry the clerk's own sentence and nothing else of ours. **THE LETTER RIDES ALONG AND MAY NEVER UNDO THE ACT.** It is queued after the decision has committed, and a queue that refuses is reported to the clerk as an outcome rather than swallowed or raised — a refusal that could not be posted is still a refusal. **AND THE DRAWN FIRST TICK IS AN ILLUSTRATION IN PART**: it reads *"A readable site plan — the one attached was rejected"*, and the clause after the dash is true of the one request in the picture and false of a request with nothing attached (B14 rule 37), so the label is the first half. | blueprint entry 06 p084–p086 · `DEF-CUS-5389` · `CR-094-BRC01` lane C · B14 rules 32 and 37 | CONFIRMED | `CR-094-BRC01` |
| BR-CUS-1055 | **HOW MANY UNITS ARE ON THE PROPERTY IS WHAT THE APPLICANT SAYS, AND IT NOW HAS A COLUMN — WHILE THE TOWN, THE STATE AND THE POSTCODE ARE THE MATCHED PREMISES' AND ARE READ BY WALKING TO IT.** Two PO rulings of 2026-09-15, an hour apart, and the second one REVERSED work already written. **(1) THE UNIT COUNT.** Verbatim: ***"we need to build this"***, on the Accounts blueprint's `How many units are on this property? *` (p077, helped by *"What the applicant says is there"*) and its `Units on the property · 6` on the request's own screen (p082). Before this there was **no column**, and the drawing's own annotation says where the number went instead: *"the number lives only in whatever the clerk typed into the notes, which no screen and no rule can read"* (`DEF-CUS-5385`). It is a **CLAIM, not a count** — the blueprint's match table draws the two side by side, `Units on the property · 6` against `6 units on PR-0731`, confidence `Agrees`, so what the applicant says and what the register holds may disagree and the request stores only the first. **No maximum is declared** (`NFR-5`); a value that is not a whole number is refused and a negative one is refused, and **zero is accepted**, because vacant land with a connection request on it genuinely has none. It stays **editable after submission**, unlike the address — `BR-CUS-070` names the address and not this, and a clerk who learns a building has six flats rather than four should be able to say so (lane judgment, decision log 2026-09-15). **(2) THE ADDRESS IS A SEARCH, NOT FIVE BOXES.** Verbatim: ***"if we have unit or street address then we should take it from premises/Unit - it means its a search and if not foudn them alow user to add them"***. The blueprint draws five address boxes on capture step 3 and prints the whole line back on the record (`As given → 212 Balcones Drive, Unit 3, Austin TX 78704`, p083) — and the town, the state and the postcode in that line are **the matched premises' own facts**, reached by walking, never columns on `service_requests`. That is `CLAUDE.md` hard rule 8 / `BR-MET-1161` applied to this screen rather than a new rule, and it is `DEF-CUS-3653`'s fix on a second screen: the read serves `premise_locality` through `seams.premises_locality`, under a name that says whose it is. ⚠ **`address_city_as_given`, `address_state_as_given` AND `address_postal_code_as_given` WERE WRITTEN AND TAKEN OUT AGAIN THE SAME HOUR, BEFORE ANYTHING WAS PUSHED** — the model, the migration, the capture allow list and a fifth `G85` home entry all existed for about an hour. The near-miss is recorded in all four files because the argument FOR them was a good one (a request records what a stranger said before any premises exists, which is the shape of `interruptions.location_text`) and it was still the wrong answer. ⚠ **`address_as_given` AND `address_unit_as_given` ARE UNTOUCHED AND STAY** — they are what the applicant said, kept verbatim and never overwritten by the resolver (`BR-CUS-070`, which p083 draws as its own footnote: *"What they typed is kept beside what we matched, never replaced by it."*). ⚠ **AND THE MATCHED PLACE IS NAMED RATHER THAN KEYED** (`DEF-CUS-5386`, B14 rule 31): the read serves `resolved_premises_no` and `resolved_unit_no` beside the ids, falling back to the premises' address and then to a stated absence — **never to the id**. Worked on the demo tenant: `SR-26-0001` resolved to `01a0a444-5ad2-7e88-be8a-bdb8aa86c05e`, which the screen printed raw, and now reads **`P-00052` · unit `SCW-U-207` · Silver Creek, TX 78611**. The drawn `PR-0731` / `U-001884` are illustrations (B14 rule 37) and our formats do not move to match them. | **PO 2026-09-15** (both rulings) · blueprint `05-prototype/02 Accounts Menu Blueprint.pdf` entry 06 p077, p082, p083 read AS IMAGES (B14 rule 42) · `BR-CUS-070` · `BR-MET-1161` / hard rule 8 · B14 rules 31 and 37 · `NFR-5` · `DEF-CUS-5385` · `DEF-CUS-5386` | **CONFIRMED — BACK END BUILT 2026-09-15; THE CAPTURE SCREEN BUILT 2026-09-19.** ➕ **Capture step 3 now asks the question.** `The property` → `How many units are on this property?`, helped by *"What the applicant says is there."*, writes `units_on_property`, and the drawn evidence table gains the **third** row this rule is about — `Units on the property · 6` against `6 units on P-00045`, confidence `Agrees`, with `Differs` built beside it because a confidence column that can only ever say one word is a check that cannot fail. **No asterisk and no gate:** `_clean_capture` accepts `None` and no signed rule makes it blocking, so an asterisk would be a promise `Continue` does not keep over a number the applicant may not know — the drawn `*` is a recorded divergence. ⚠ **AND THE THREE ADDRESS COLUMNS WERE REFUSED A SECOND TIME, BY THE LANE THIS ROW PREDICTED.** The capture lane's own brief asked for `City`, `State` and `ZIP code` as editable fields with *"new payload keys"*; they stay WALKED and read-only, their drawn layout is built, and the browser walk now counts the inputs in that block and demands zero so it cannot be quietly undone. Proof and every divergence: `04-docs/05-modules/03-customers/conformance/CR-094-BRC01-capture-steps-1-and-3.md`. `customers/0049` adds `units_on_property` and nothing else; `CAPTURE_FIELDS` accepts it; `service_request_reads.resolved_place` serves the two numbers, the premises' address and `premise_locality`, and `_header` carries the locality so the drawn address line can be composed above the tabs. ⚠⚠ **BOTH HALVES OF THIS ROW ARE SUPERSEDED IN PART ON 2026-09-22, IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS, AND BOTH ARE PO RULINGS.** **(1) THE UNIT COUNT IS REMOVED FROM BOTH SCREENS.** PO report of that date, row 12: *Remove Units on site*. The QUESTION on capture step 3, the `Units on site` row on the request's own screen, and the evidence table's THIRD row — the claim weighed against the register, which is the half of this rule the drawing argues hardest for — are all off the product. **The COLUMN, `CAPTURE_FIELDS`'s entry and every row already holding a number are untouched**, so a caller that knows the fact can still send it and nothing already captured is lost; what went is the question and the two places that displayed the answer. It was built on the PO's own instruction of 2026-09-15 (*"we need to build this"*) and taken off a week later. **(2) THE THREE ADDRESS COLUMNS ARE ADDED, AND THE REFUSAL THIS ROW RECORDS TWICE IS WITHDRAWN.** PO ruling 2026-09-22, verbatim: *"correct address will be on presmies but first time you will ahve to take the details to match it"*. `address_city_as_given`, `address_state_as_given` and `address_postal_code_as_given` exist on `service_requests` (`customers/0065`), `CAPTURE_FIELDS` accepts them, and capture step 3 asks for them **as typed boxes, until a premises is picked** — at which point they disappear and the register's own city, state and ZIP take their place, read-only, exactly as this row describes. ⚠ **`BR-MET-1161` IS NOT NARROWED AND NO FOURTH HOME IS CREATED**, which is why the reversal is narrow: the rule governs where an address is STORED AS THE TRUTH, and these are a record of **what a member of the public said** — the `_as_given` posture this row already defends for `address_as_given`, and the one `interruptions.location_text` carries. **The lesson is the one the decision log states:** a hard rule about STORAGE was read as a rule about CAPTURE, by a lane that had the rule in front of it and quoted it accurately, and a request that cannot take a city and a ZIP cannot find the premises it is about. ⚠ **THE BARE SPELLINGS ARE STILL REFUSED** — a `city` on this table is the fourth home `G85` catches by name, and a backend case asserts all three are refused in the same breath as the `_as_given` three are stored. ⚠ **AND `G85`'s DECLARED BLIND SPOT MOVES FROM 3 TO 6 IN THE SAME COMMIT**: the gate matches by NAME and cannot see an `_as_given` spelling, so the NUMBER is what does the work, and a number that does not move when its subject does is indistinguishable from a current one. ⛔ **THE SCREEN IS NOT BUILT AND `DEF-CUS-5386` STAYS OPEN** — `ServiceRequestDetailPage.tsx` still renders `loc.resolved_premises_id`, so a clerk still sees a UUID. Wave 2 owns that screen; the payload it needs is published in the conformance file below with a worked example. 🔒 `HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING` (migration ordering). | `CR-094-BRC01` wave 1 lane B · `06-backend/apps/customers/migrations/0049_br_cus_1055_units_on_property.py` · `service_request_reads.resolved_place` · `06-backend/tests/test_cr094_request_place_and_units.py` (11 cases) · `04-docs/05-modules/03-customers/conformance/CR-094-BRC01-request-screen-lane-B-the-place-and-the-unit-count.md` |
| BR-CUS-1076 | **A CAPTURED REQUEST REMEMBERS WHICH MESSAGES IT WILL SEND AND UNDER WHICH WORDING, AND THE ONE THAT FIRES ON A DECISION IS THE REASON THE BLOCK EXISTS.** The Accounts blueprint's entry 06, capture step 6 (p080–p081, read AS IMAGES) draws `What goes out to the applicant`: five rows under `SEND · WHAT IT SAYS · TO · CHANNEL · TEMPLATE · WHEN`, **three ticked and two not**, and beneath them a template picker. Three ticks against two is what makes it DATA rather than a picture. **THE FIVE OCCASIONS AND THEIR MOMENTS:** the request is in and its number (`on submit`) · what we still need from them (`on submit`) · a receipt for the money taken (**`already handed over`**) · that a balance is still owed (`on submit`) · **the decision, when it is made (`on approve or refuse`)**. `WHEN` carries three values and they are three different kinds of thing: one fires now, one fired before the clerk got here, and one fires on a day nobody has reached. **A ROW WHOSE MOMENT HAS PASSED MUST NOT FIRE AGAIN** — p081, *"The receipt is already in their hand … this row records what was given rather than queueing a second copy."* **WHAT IS STORED IS ONLY THE CHOICES.** The applicant's name, the request number, the CHANNEL (the template class's own fact) and the MOMENT (the occasion's) are read, never copied — hard rule 8's reasoning one table over. **THE DECISION ROW IS ONE OCCASION WITH TWO CLASSES AND THE OUTCOME PICKS BETWEEN THEM**, because a template body has no conditional in it and *"we are going ahead"* and *"we are not able to go ahead"* are two letters (the reasoning that split the acknowledgement in two under `BR-CUS-090` clause (3)). What the clerk picked and what actually rendered are both recorded, and where they differ the row says so. **A PICK IS A CLASS AND A VARIANT, NEVER A VERSION PIN** — resolution happens when the message is queued, through `BR-BIL-025`'s three axes and two fallbacks, so wording the utility has since corrected cannot go out in its withdrawn form and `BR-BIL-027` keeps a draft unsendable. **⚠ THE TICK IS A STANDING INSTRUCTION AND A CLERK IN FRONT OF THE REQUEST OUTRANKS IT.** Refusing already queues the clerk's own letter (`BR-CUS-1075`) and that letter **IS** row five on that path; a second one would tell one applicant twice about one decision. So refusing RECORDS the row against the letter it already sent, and approving — where nobody is typing anything — is where the tick decides. **Asking for more fires nothing here: it is not a decision.** **⚠ AND NOTHING IS SENT.** `BR-NOT-013` — no provider adapter exists on any channel, print included — so every surface built on this block says QUEUED and never *sent*, which is the drawing's own last line: *"Everything here is queued, and nothing is sent. This product writes a row per message and no sender picks it up — no email, no text, no print file."* | **PO question 2026-09-15** — *"at the time of approval we are also linking notification and templates to the modal - are you ensuring it will work?"* · blueprint `05-prototype/02 Accounts Menu Blueprint.pdf` entry 06 p080–p081 read AS IMAGES (B14 rule 42) · `BR-CUS-1075` · `BR-CUS-079` · `BR-BIL-025` · `BR-BIL-027` · `BR-NOT-013` · B14 rules 31/32/35/37 | **CONFIRMED — BACK END BUILT 2026-09-15.** `customers/0051` adds `service_request_messages` and `0052` its RLS policy; `applicant_messages.py` owns the five occasions, the block read and write, the picker and the firing; `notifications/service_request_events.py` gains a **fifth** class `service_request_approved` with its wording and `applicant_templates` for the picker; `approve_and_raise` fires row five and `refuse_request` records it. ⛔ **THE SCREEN IS NOT BUILT** — `ServiceRequestCapturePage.tsx` is `CR-094-BRC01` lane D's, and the payload it needs is published with a worked example in the conformance file below. ⚠ **TWO OF THE FIVE OCCASIONS HAVE NO WORDING** (`documents_outstanding`, `balance_outstanding`); the drawn defaults stand and every row carries `available: false` — `DEF-CUS-5417`. 🔒 `HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING` (migration ordering, RLS policy). | `CR-094-BRC01` wave 1 lane J · `06-backend/apps/customers/applicant_messages.py` · `06-backend/apps/customers/migrations/0051_br_cus_1076_service_request_messages.py` + `0052_rls_br_cus_1076_service_request_messages.py` · `06-backend/tests/test_cr094_applicant_messages_block.py` · `04-docs/05-modules/03-customers/conformance/CR-094-BRC01-capture-lane-J-what-goes-out-to-the-applicant.md` |
| BR-CUS-1077 | **APPROVING A MOVE-IN RAISES A PENDING MOVE-IN JOB — A ROW OF ITS OWN, IN `move_in_jobs`, WHICH THE MOVE-INS TAB SHOWS BESIDE THE DRAFT ACCOUNTS IT HAS ALWAYS SHOWN. THE ACCOUNT STILL DOES NOT OPEN AT APPROVAL.** PO ruling 2026-09-16, verbatim: *"it should do B"*, choosing from three options put to them with each cost named. **WHAT IT FIXES:** the end-to-end clerk walk found that approving a move-in did not complete at all — `SR-26-0024` sat at `Under review` with `outcome=move_in` already saved, so the request looked decided while being nothing of the kind (`DEF-CUS-5398`). **WHERE THE ROW LIVES, AND THE THREE PLACES IT DOES NOT.** ⛔ Not `accounts`: the Approve drawing forbids opening one on all five outcomes — *"Not now — No account opens. Nothing is energised."* ⛔ Not `move_records`: that is the record of a move that HAS HAPPENED, `BR-CUS-152` says nothing ever updates a row of it, and its `account_id` is NOT NULL — the very account the job exists to create. ⛔ Not the `service_requests` row itself: an application's life ends at `approved` and a job's life starts there, and folding two lifecycles into one row also fixes the queue to requests, so a move-in raised by any other door could never join it. ✅ **A row of its own, which is the shape the product already uses one outcome over** — a new connection raises a **draft** `connections` row that appears on its tab at once, carries `request_id`, and whose own wizard asks for the rest. **WHAT IT CARRIES: NOTHING THAT ALREADY HAS AN OWNER.** The applicant, the address, the requested start, the services, the money and the unchecked documents are all on the REQUEST, and the job names the request — `move_in_jobs.request_id`, NOT NULL and unique per tenant, which is also what makes a double-click raise exactly one job. The address is WALKED to the premises (hard rule 8) and no address column exists here. `outcome_ref` is deliberately NOT taken: for a move-in that column already means *the account this application produced* and the tab's `request #` search leg reads it that way. **TWO STATES, `pending` → `done`**, and `moves.record_move` closes the job inside the wizard's own transaction when a move-in record names its request. **NO STEP COLUMN** — nothing in this product writes a step, so `move_reads.STEP_HAS_NO_STORE` stays served on every row (the `DEF-921` posture: a column with no writer is a slot nothing fills). ⚠ **THIS SUPERSEDES B18 §5.5.2's FOOTNOTE FOR THE MOVE-IN, AND THE SUPERSESSION IS STATED (B14 rule 41).** *"A move-in is short enough that the wizard is the record"* never weighed where an APPROVED move-in waits before any wizard is opened, and the PO's own reason for rejecting option C was that *"the queue stays empty until somebody finishes the wizard"*. ⚠ **THE MOVE-OUT IS UNCHANGED** — the ruling names the move-in, and widening it would be a lane widening a ruling. ⚠ **WHAT IS STATED RATHER THAN HIDDEN:** a move-in committed through some other door passes no request, closes no job, and leaves it on the tab beside the account it produced (`pending_job_closes_on_the_recorded_move`); and **the pending row has no door into the move-in wizard yet** — the walk back is to the request. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-10-05 (`CR-171-BRC10` S2, task `P1.01`): A JOB MAY NOW BE `cancelled` — `pending` → `done` OR `pending` → `cancelled`.** The PO ruled a door into existence on mock-up v22 (*"update details view button accrdongly"*, over the table giving a *Not started* move-in **More: Cancel move-in…**). `customers/0080` widens `ck_move_in_job_state`; the door that writes it is `P1.22`. Nothing else in this rule moves. | PO ruling 2026-09-16 · `06-backend/apps/customers/move_in_jobs.py` · `06-backend/apps/customers/migrations/0053_br_cus_1077_move_in_jobs.py` · `06-backend/tests/test_br_cus_1077_move_in_job.py` · `DEF-CUS-5398` · B18 §5.5.2 (footnote superseded) · `06-backend/apps/customers/migrations/0080_cr171_p1_01_move_in_redesign.py` · `CR-171-BRC10` §2 S2 |
| BR-CUS-1078 | **AN UNCHECKED REQUIRED DOCUMENT NO LONGER BLOCKS AN APPROVAL. IT IS TOLD TO THE CLERK, BEFORE THEY PRESS, AND THE APPROVAL PROCEEDS.** PO ruling 2026-09-16, verbatim: *"see anyway we are creating draft move in request , in such case clark can add document later; it is good that documents are available when request is submitted and aporve but lets not make it mandetory"*, and on how to say so, *"if you want ywe can show the messge documents will be required"*. ⚠ **THE PO'S OWN REASONING IS WHY IT IS ALL FIVE OUTCOMES AND NOT THE MOVE-IN ALONE.** Every approval now raises a DRAFT job whose own wizard asks for the rest (`BR-CUS-1077` for a move-in, a draft `connections` row for a new connection), and **a draft job is precisely a place where a missing document can still arrive**; holding the approval to protect a job that has not started yet protects nothing, and the reasoning does not distinguish between outcomes. ⚠ **IT SUPERSEDES THE SECOND HALF OF `BR-CUS-095` AND A DRAWN STATEMENT THE BLUEPRINT MAKES TWICE**, and both drawn sentences are quoted here so nobody ships them: p083 *"The money does not gate approval and the documents do"* and p084 *"approval is exactly the thing it blocks"*. **The first half of p083 survives** — money never gated approval and still does not. ⚠ **WHAT REPLACES THE BLOCK, because *not mandatory* is not the same as *not mentioned* — this half is the ruling too, not a courtesy.** `documents_condition` keeps its state (`blocked`, so the condition is still NOT clear and the summary still counts it against the total) and its message, and sets `blocking=False`; `outstanding_conditions()` is the new list beside `blocking_conditions()` and is what a screen reads to say what is still wanted. The request keeps its `waiting_on` so the queue still shows it, and the job the approval raises carries the outstanding list into its own wizard. **A silent approval over missing documents is the worse failure of the two.** ⚠ **AND THE CONDITION IS EVALUATED AGAINST THE OUTCOME THE CLERK IS CONSIDERING, NOT THE ONE ALREADY STORED (`DEF-CUS-5427`).** The checklist is keyed on the outcome, so a request with nothing decided reports every condition clear over a `move_in` that is about to want two documents; the decision tab publishes each option's own conditions beside its consequence table so the dialog redraws both as a radio moves. ⚠ **WHAT THIS DOES NOT TOUCH:** the ARREARS condition still blocks — it asks whether anybody has LOOKED, which is a question about a person and cannot be answered later by a job — the fee condition still blocks (`DEF-CUS-5399` is the open disagreement about that one), and no money rule moves. **Worked, on `SR-26-0024` (brazosbend, 2026-09-16):** two required documents unchecked for `move_in` → before the ruling `can_approve: false`, blocked on `documents_verified`, and the request could not be approved at all; after it, `can_approve: true`, and the clerk reads `Still needed · 2 required documents are still unchecked. · The job will ask for them.` The request approved in a browser at 1440 on 2026-09-16. | PO ruling 2026-09-16 · `DEF-CUS-5427` · supersedes half of `BR-CUS-095` and blueprint p083/p084 · `06-backend/apps/customers/service_request.py` (`documents_condition`, `outstanding_conditions`) · `06-backend/tests/test_cr094_approval_raises_the_job.py` · `10-scripts/def_cus_5427_approve_walkthrough.mjs` | CONFIRMED | CR-094-BRC01 |
| BR-CUS-1079 | **A UTILITY DEFINES ITS OWN CONSUMER CATEGORIES. `account_types` IS A SEEDED, TENANT-EXTENSIBLE, RLS-SCOPED LOOKUP LIKE THE TWO TABLES EITHER SIDE OF IT.** PO ruling 2026-09-17, verbatim: ***"utility can get their own account types"***, answering a straight either/or — *do utilities get their own account types, or do the seven stay the signed vocabulary and the screen simply stops pretending they're editable?* ⚠ **THIS SUPERSEDES `BR-CUS-094`'s PLATFORM POSTURE AND THEREFORE B2 r5 #12 [D5] AND r5's RLS-EXCLUSION LINE.** Until this ruling `account_types` was the ONE customers table with **no `tenant_id`, no RLS policy at all** and a GLOBAL `UniqueConstraint(Lower("code"))`, and `customers/lookups.py` refused create, update, set_active and delete by name. **The seven stop being the vocabulary and become the SEED.** **WHAT MADE THE QUESTION REAL IS A DEFECT THE PO FOUND THEMSELVES** — *"if they add entry in this table - look up it does not reflect in these places"*: the S8 card rendered **Add · Edit · Deactivate · Delete** over a server that refused all four (`DEF-CUS-5647`, hard-won law 2's mirror), and the refusal's message key `customers.lookups.account_types_are_platform` existed in the backend **and in no catalogue**, so the clerk got no sentence at all (`DEF-CUS-5648`). Its twin `account_subtypes` behaved the opposite way, which is most of why this read as a wiring fault. **THE SHAPE IS THE HOUSE PATTERN, NOT AN INVENTION:** `BR-TEN-030` (codes unique per tenant, case-insensitive; **a code is FROZEN once used**, because `rating` stores the code as TEXT in charge narrowing and in the plan/tax targeting lists and no handler can rewrite those — the NAME stays editable), `BR-TEN-034` (a used row is not deletable; a deactivated row stops being pickable and every account keeps billing under it) and `BR-TEN-036` (a seeded row is deactivatable, **never** deletable). `legacy` remains `BR-CUS-094`'s THIRD axis and is **served, not writable**: who may retire a code from new business has not been ruled (`OQ-CUS-12407`). **WHERE A NEW ROW GOES IN A PICKER THE PO DREW** is the one question the ruling opens, and it is answered with a stored fact rather than an iteration order: `account_types.sort_order` carries `BR-CUS-094`'s ruled order as 10..70, the legacy slot is 900, and a tenant-added row is appended at `max + 10`. The S8 CARD still sorts by `code` like every other reference card (IS-2); `sort_order` is the PICKER's. **WHAT IT COSTS, SAID RATHER THAN LEFT TO BE FOUND:** two utilities may now use ONE code for DIFFERENT meanings, so **every hardcoded consumer is a real bug** where before it was a defensible reading of a platform vocabulary — the portal's `code__iexact="residential"`, notification rules offering two of seven, and the notification/billing template constants that spell the fourth category three different ways (`govt` · `government` · `municipal`). **THE PATTERN TO COPY IS ALREADY WRITTEN AND TESTED:** `metering._resolve_unit_type` reads the tenant's own rows FIRST and falls back to its tuple only for a tenant that has none, with a deactivated row deliberately NOT resurrected. **WHAT IS NOT DECIDED HERE:** whether a tenant-added type may be targeted by a plan, a charge or a tax is the same question as before and unchanged. | **PO 2026-09-17** · `12-governance/02-decision-log.md` 2026-09-17 · supersedes `BR-CUS-094`'s platform half + B2 r5 #12 [D5] + r5's RLS-exclusion line · `DEF-CUS-5647` · `DEF-CUS-5648` | **CONFIRMED — BUILT 2026-09-17.** `customers/0054` fans the 8 rows out per tenant, repoints every `accounts`/`account_subtypes`/`service_requests` FK at **its own tenant's** clone and swaps the global unique constraint for a per-tenant one; `customers/0055` adds RLS `ENABLE`+`FORCE` and the B2 §3 policy on both `USING` and `WITH CHECK`; `AccountTypeLookupHandler` has full CRUD; `service_request_categories` offers every active non-legacy row of the tenant instead of iterating `SEVEN_CATEGORIES`; the platform fixture goes 4 → 8 behind bump `V2.48.0.12`. 🔒 `HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING` (migration ordering, RLS policy). | `06-backend/tests/test_po_20260917_account_types_are_the_tenants.py` · `06-backend/tests/test_us_cus_schema_and_seed.py::test_po_20260917_rls__account_types_are_the_tenants_own_and_not_shared` · `10-scripts/po_20260917_account_types_walkthrough.mjs` |
| BR-CUS-1080 | **THE MOVE-OUTS LIST SHOWS THE MOVE-OUTS THAT HAVE NOT FINISHED YET, AND THE WORD FOR A FINISHED ONE IS `Completed`.** PO ruling 2026-09-19, verbatim: **`yes`**, asked whether the Move-outs list should show move-outs that have not finished yet. ⚠ **THIS IS A WINDOW, NOT A MISSING COLUMN, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE RULE.** `move_reads.py` had said since W5 that a move-out before its close *"is not recorded anywhere"*, and **that had been false since `CR-035`**: `wizard_parks` carries `flow`, `step`, `subject_ref`, `values` and `waiting_on` for all five flows INCLUDING `move_out`, and `move_record_reads._in_flight` already served such a row with a real step number. The list could not see them because its window was `accounts.closed_at IS NOT NULL`. **THE WINDOW IS NOW TWO SOURCES** — `accounts` closed and terminal, UNION `wizard_parks WHERE flow='move_out'` — paged as ONE order in SQL (`_move_out_page_keys`), never two lists merged in Python, which is `NFR-3` and is the shape `BR-CUS-1077` already gave the Move-ins tab. **EVERY ROW NAMES ITS KIND** in `row_kind`: `account` or `parked_move_out`. **THREE OF B18 §5.6.2's FOUR STATE SEGMENTS ARE FILLABLE NOW** — `closed`, plus `in_progress` (a draft, empty `waiting_on`) and `waiting` (a park, a reason recorded), the two told apart by the ONE column that distinguishes them. ⛔ **`scheduled` STAYS UNHOLDABLE and its reason is narrowed to exactly that**: a move-out somebody intends and nobody has started writes nothing, and `accounts` has no `scheduled_move_out_on`. ⚠ **THE STEP HAS TWO ANSWERS AND BOTH ARE RIGHT.** A parked row prints `2 of 6` off the draft's own column; a CLOSED one prints an absence and MUST, because **four code paths write `accounts.closed_at` and only one is the wizard** (`services.move_out`, against the disconnect close-out, the account import and the import rollback) — `6 of 6` on the other three would claim a clerk walked six steps they never saw. ⚠ **`f.closed` EXCLUDES EVERY UNFINISHED ROW**: it is a window on `closed_at` and a draft has closed nothing. ⚠ **AN UNFINISHED ROW'S `↗ Open` RESUMES THE WIZARD** at `/move-outs/new?parked=<id>` through `resumePath` — the same builder the parked panel and the move record use — because there is no record and no reference to address one with; a FINISHED row's opens `/moves/{ref}` as before. ⚠ **AND THE SECOND HALF IS ONE DISPLAY WORD** (`DEF-CUS-5901`): a finished move-out reads **`Completed`** and not `Closed`. **The stored value is still `closed`, there is no migration and no state value changed** — the blueprint p117 draws `Completed` and `move_record_reads.STATE_COMPLETED` already served it, so the record page spoke the blueprint's vocabulary and only the LIST did not. **WHAT IS STATED RATHER THAN INVENTED ON AN UNFINISHED ROW:** no reference (only a move-IN draft is numbered while it is in hand — `CR-122-BRC06`, `OQ-CUS-12489`), no settlement (nothing is settled until the account closes), no date where the step asking for one has not been reached, and no account where nobody has picked one. **WHAT IS NOT DECIDED BY THIS RULE:** the three KPI tiles still count the CLOSED rows only and say so in `open_means`, and the parked-drafts panel above this table is still mounted — `OQ-CUS-12488`. | PO ruling 2026-09-19 · `DEF-CUS-5919` · `DEF-CUS-5901` · supersedes `move_reads.MOVE_OUT_NOT_YET_CLOSED_HAS_NO_STORE`'s general claim and B18 §5.6.2's one-state column · `06-backend/apps/customers/move_reads.py` (`move_out_parks_queryset`, `move_out_park_row`, `_move_out_page_keys`) · `06-backend/apps/customers/seams.py` (`wizard_park_queryset`) · `07-frontend/src/features/customers/MoveOutsTab.tsx` · `06-backend/tests/test_w5_move_worklists.py` · blueprint p117 | CONFIRMED | — |

## `BR-CUS-1081` — the transfer's buyer carries what an account needs (2026-09-20)

**PO ruling, verbatim**, having walked the transfer wizard in the live product:

> *"step 2 dont allow to take kYC documents - DOB, all detaisl we need to create
> account - Similar to Move in request we will need all details for account"*

and, when told a backend lane would be needed:

> *"we have it - we have created it for service request move in - use it"*

| BR | Statement | Sources | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-1081 | **A transfer's BUYER is captured with the same set a move-in application captures, and the set is REUSED rather than restated.** `transfers.BUYER_CAPTURE_FIELDS` is derived from `service_request_service.CAPTURE_FIELDS` — the eleven of them that are about a PARTY rather than about a property or an application — and `_capture_vocabulary()` **raises** if any name has left that set, so the two cannot drift. ⚠ **AN INTERSECTION IS THE WRONG SPELLING AND IS NAMED HERE SO NOBODY "TIDIES" IT INTO ONE:** it shrinks silently, and a capture set that quietly stops carrying a date of birth is a match that quietly stops being strong. **REQUIRED: three, and the ruling added none** — a name, a stated kind, and one identifier lawful for that kind (`BR-CUS-1023`, `BR-CUS-1030`). **OPTIONAL: everything else**, because the PO named the move-in request as the benchmark and every added field is optional there; making a SALE stricter than the application they pointed at would refuse a buyer for a field the benchmark does not ask for. ⚠ **THE COST OF THE DATE OF BIRTH BEING OPTIONAL IS STATED RATHER THAN HIDDEN:** `BR-CUS-089` needs name + date of birth + one government identifier for a `strong` tier and only `strong` moves money, so a returning customer holding **$150.00** of deposit carries **$0.00** of it and gains a SECOND customer number (`DEF-2587`'s measured shape — one landlord, three rows, one Social Security number). **It is not silent:** `buyer_match.missing_for_strong` names the absent fact before the commit. **WHERE EACH PIECE LANDS, AND `transfers` GAINS NO COLUMN:** identity and the date of birth on `customers` through `services.link_customer`; **the buyer's NAME, and their email, phone and date of birth, on `contacts` through `services.update_account_contact`** ⊕ **AMENDED 2026-09-20 (`DEF-CUS-5971`, `v2.319.5`): THE NAME GOES ON EVERY SALE, AND UNTIL TODAY IT WENT ON SOME.** The write sat behind *if anything else was captured*, and the other three are OPTIONAL by this rule's own sentence below, so a lawful sale to a buyer identified by a phone and nothing else left `contacts` reading the SELLER — and `primary_contact_name` is every display name in the product, so the Accounts list's Name cell, the unit's Occupant column and every meter's customer all went on naming the person who sold the house, beside the BUYER's Customer #. **The name is now written whether or not an optional field came with it.** ⚠ **AND THE HALF THAT DOES NOT MOVE IS STATED RATHER THAN SILENT:** a buyer who gave no email and no telephone number leaves those columns the seller's, so the bill still goes to the seller — the commit returns `contact_details_still_the_sellers` beside the mailing-address absence that was already there. **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-23 by (T3) below — the seller's email, phone and mailing address are removed at every sale, and neither absence is stated any more.** ⚠ **THIS IS NOT THE `contacts` RE-HOME.** `DEF-CUS-5971` remains OPEN against B2 r9 #1's Eng-Lead disposition — the name-bearing selectors reading the LINKED CUSTOMER — which is 42 call sites in four modules and is not one lane's to take; the language on `accounts` through `services.update_account`; **the mailing address on `customers.addresses` `kind='mailing'`** — CLAUDE.md hard rule 8's first named exception — through `services.update_account_mailing_address`. **KYC documents ride `account_documents` `doc_type='kyc'`** and NOT `service_request_documents`, whose `request_id` is a non-null `PROTECT` FK a sale has no row for. ~~**A CO-APPLICANT HAS NOWHERE TO GO** and is reported rather than dropped — `OQ-CUS-12514`.~~ **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-23 by (T4) below** — it is stored. **AND A CLERK IS TOLD ABOUT A MATCH BEFORE THE COMMIT**, through `POST /transfers/buyer-preview`, which writes absolutely nothing. ⛔ **AMENDED 2026-09-23 — FOUR PO RULINGS ON WHAT A SALE DOES (transfer questions T1–T4).** **(T1) The BUYER is searched for on step 2, and an account with no customer linked is not refused.** ⊕ **CORRECTED 2026-09-23 (the same day, decision log *"CORRECTED BY THE PO: T1's SEARCH IS FOR THE BUYER, ON STEP 2"*) — this clause first read as a search for the SELLER's customer on step 1.** PO, *"my bad search custoemr id should be on step 2 - not on step 1"* and *"no we have to search custoemr id for buyer hence on step 2"*: step 2 opens with the shared customer finder (`CustomerFinder.tsx`, New Connect's, extracted — B14 rule 43): one match → its details with *Link*; several → a table, one *Link* per row; none → *Create customer*. A linked buyer is sent as `customer_id` and needs no identifier (the customer already has one); an id that is malformed, unknown or another tenant's earns no exemption and falls through to the ordinary identifier rule (`DEF-CUS-5974`: *not recognised, never a 500*). **The seller with no customer linked is RECORDED BY NAME** (`transfers.seller_name`, the account's contact; `transfers.customer_id` is nullable from `customers/0068`), and `from_account_has_no_customer_entity` is RETIRED — `DEF-CUS-6387`. **(T2) The property's owner moves only for a MASTER account:** PO, *"in case of master account - not in case of regualr account - (a) The sale makes the buyer the property's owner."* — selling the premises' master account makes the buyer the premises' owner (through metering's own service; customers writes no metering table); selling a regular account leaves the owner as it is. **(T3) The seller's reach ends at the sale:** PO, *"(a) At the sale, the seller's email, phone and autopay are removed. The buyer's are used if given; otherwise bills go by post to the service address."* — the seller's email, phone, mailing address and autopay enrolment are removed; the buyer's are written where given; with none, bills go by post to the service address. **(T4) A co-applicant is stored:** PO, *"a) Store a second person on the account (name, email, phone), shown on the account page"* (`OQ-CUS-12514` closed). Worked: A-11512 (Hollis, Marcus, no customer linked) sold to Tim Aiden with no email given → the clerk searches for Tim on step 2, finds nobody and presses *Create customer*; the sale goes through and Marcus is recorded as the seller by name; Marcus's email, phone and autopay are gone; Tim's bills go by post to 61 Larkspur Way; the property's owner changes only if A-11512 is the premises' master account. ⛔ **AMENDED 2026-09-24 — THE CAPTURE IS THE PRIMARY PATH AND THE SEARCH IS OPTIONAL BESIDE IT (`DEF-CUS-6549`). NOTHING ELSE IN THIS ROW MOVES.** T1 above says step 2 *opens with* the customer finder, and it was built so that it opened with NOTHING ELSE: from `2.351.0` the buyer's whole capture was drawn only after a clerk had searched, found nobody and pressed *Create customer*, and the step refused meanwhile with *"Search for the buyer first."* **The PO reported it, having already ruled this row on 2026-09-20**: *"you need to take all buyer detaisl the way were taking- full name, email, phone, ids etc, document, cateory sub category - all - then allow provide a button to search for customer id; what you did is you removed all buyer details and only provide search - this is wrong"*. **THE RULING, option (a) of two put to them, verbatim:** *"I'd go with (a): the clerk has the buyer in front of them, and a wrong phone caught at the counter is cheaper than one caught by a returned bill. - thissi correct - please correct this"*. **SO, THREE CLAUSES.** **(1) The form is the primary path:** every field this row names is on the step from the moment it opens, and a buyer who is not on file is typed straight in — **a search is never required to reach any field**, and a lane that puts a condition round the capture is rebuilding this defect. **(2) The search is a convenience:** a button beside the form that pulls an existing customer by number or name. There is no *Create customer* button, because the fields ARE the create form — New Connect's own posture with the same component. **(3) A hit FILLS the form and LEAVES IT EDITABLE:** the found customer's name, kind, telephone number and email are written into the controls the clerk is looking at, nothing is disabled, and the edited value is what the commit sends. ⚠ **THE ONE FIELD A HIT WITHDRAWS IS THE IDENTIFIER, AND THAT IS NOT A LOCK:** a linked buyer rides the commit as `customer_id` with `identity: {}`, so a Social Security number typed against one reaches no column at all — a field that renders and is silently dropped (`DEF-CUS-6487`'s shape), and `BR-CUS-1020` refuses a second place a government identifier is typed. ⚠ **AND THE CATEGORY IS NOT CAPTURED HERE, WHICH IS SAID OUT LOUD BECAUSE THE PO'S LIST NAMES IT.** The category and sub-category are the ACCOUNT's and not the buyer's: the blueprint draws them on step 1 (p127) and not on step 2 (p128), a sale creates no account so there is nothing to classify, and `services.REFUSED_FIELDS` refuses `account_type` on every door with **`OQ-CUS-12356` OPEN** against it — *"No rule states how it may be changed afterwards and no door validates such a change"*. A picker on step 2 would resolve an open question by choosing. **If a sale should be able to re-classify an account, that is `OQ-CUS-12356` and it is the PO's to rule.** | **PO ruling 2026-09-20** (the live-product walk, decision log of that date) · the conformance file `transfer-against-its-blueprint.md` §2 · `BR-CUS-1023` · `BR-CUS-1022` · `BR-CUS-1020` · `BR-CUS-089` · CLAUDE.md hard rule 8 | **CONFIRMED 2026-09-20 — built the same day** | `06-backend/tests/test_br_cus_1081_transfer_buyer_capture.py` (26 cases) |

**THE ONE THING A LATER LANE WILL BE TEMPTED TO DO, NAMED SO IT GOES RED.**
Making the date of birth compulsory reads like tightening identity, and it is
not: it refuses a genuine first-time buyer who has nothing on file to match
against, for a question that cannot help them. If it is ever wanted it is the
PO's to rule, because it makes a sale stricter than the application the PO
pointed at. `test_a_buyer_with_no_date_of_birth_is_accepted_and_told_what_it_costs`
is the assertion that goes red on it.

**THE SECOND TEMPTATION: a second `contacts` row for a co-applicant.** It would
be a row no screen shows, no door reads back and no bill prints — hard-won law 2
exactly. `OQ-CUS-12514` is where that decision belongs.
| BR-CUS-1082 | **A MASTER ACCOUNT WITH OPEN SUB-ACCOUNTS IS *WARNED* WHEN IT IS MOVED OUT — NEVER REFUSED, AT THE COMMIT OR ON STEP 1.** ⚠⚠ **REVERSED 2026-09-20 BY PO RULING, CLOSING `OQ-CUS-12515` ON OPTION (b). THIS ROW SAID *"CANNOT BE MOVED OUT — REFUSED BY NAME"* FOR ONE DAY AND IT WAS A LANE JUDGMENT THE WHOLE TIME.** PO, verbatim: *"option B - give a warning and allow it; if owner has sold the property to new owner then we are transfering all libalities and ownership to new owner"*. ⚠ **THE REASON IS A FACT ABOUT THE PRODUCT RATHER THAN A JUDGMENT ABOUT RISK, AND IT IS THE HALF WORTH CARRYING.** The build argued from asymmetry — refusing wrongly costs a support call, permitting wrongly writes a NUMBERED move record that hard rule 4 forbids deleting — and that reasoning was sound. The PO answered it with something the lane did not have: **a landlord leaving is not a landlord abandoning their tenants.** Where the property has been SOLD the act is a **TRANSFER**, which moves the account, its liabilities and its ownership to the new owner with the tenants untouched beneath it. So the case the refusal protected against **already has its own flow**, and blocking the move-out blocked only the legitimate remainder — an owner leaving for some other reason entirely. **A product that refuses a lawful act to prevent a mistake the adjacent flow already prevents is not failing closed; it is failing shut.** **WHAT THE RULE IS NOW.** A move-out of a master account with open children **COMMITS** — the account goes terminal, `closed_at` is written, every open agreement ends and the numbered record is allocated, exactly as any other close — and the response carries a WARNING row, `code: master_closed_over_open_sub_accounts`, `br_ref: BR-CUS-1082`, with the count. **The children are untouched: closing a landlord has never been a cascade and still is not.** **WHERE THE DRAWING SAYS OTHERWISE:** `B19 §15.8` — the capture of the PO's 2026-08-24 walkthrough — settles *"a move-out is **refused**, not warned"* and **item 1 of its three is SUPERSEDED by this ruling**; its other two (the children's balances and ageing are shown; a sub-account may hang off a **purpose** rather than a unit) stand. That section also disclaims itself in as many words — *"THIS IS NOT IN ANY SIGNED RULE … It needs ratifying before W5 builds step 1"* — which is why the question was asked, and the answer was no. ⚠⚠ **IT IS NOT `BR-CUS-171`, AND SAYING SO IS HALF THE REASON THIS ROW EXISTS (`DEF-CUS-5950`).** `BR-CUS-171` signs the LINK — who a master's children are, DERIVED from the premises, closing `OQ-531` — and its row contains no move-out clause of any kind. **`BR-CUS-171` is still cited here for the one thing it does sign: WHO COUNTS AS AN OPEN CHILD.** **ONE DEFINITION, NEVER A SECOND — AND THE REVERSAL DID NOT TOUCH IT.** The count is `selectors.sub_accounts_for_master`, the same call `move_out_settlement.settlement_for` makes for `open_sub_accounts`, so the number the screen shows and the number the warning carries cannot disagree. "Occupying" keys off the status's BEHAVIOUR CLASS and never its label (`BR-CUS-055`), so a tenant drops out of the count on the day they close and a pending one never appears; the master itself and any other master on the premises are excluded. Two definitions of one word is `DEF-CUS-5901`'s defect class and this rule refuses to mint another, whether the answer refuses or warns. **WHERE IT LIVES — BOTH PLACES, AND THEY ARE NOW THE SAME KIND OF THING.** **(1) `services.move_out`** appends the warning before any write, above `_commit_event_reads`, where the raise used to stand. **(2) STEP 1 OF THE WIZARD** draws it beside the existing `not an occupant` strip, because the blueprint puts the fact on step 1 (p110, *What is open on this account* → `Sub-accounts`). ⚠ **THE SCREEN DRAWS ONLY ON A KNOWN COUNT** — the close-impact read may be in flight or may have failed, and a strip drawn over an unknown count would appear on EVERY account in the product while passing a naive test exactly as a correct one does. **WHAT THE CLERK READS — one catalogue key for both halves, in all eleven catalogues:** **`2 sub-accounts are still open.`** A FACT of five words and **nothing else** (B14 rule 33). ⚠ **THE INSTRUCTION HALF IS GONE, AND ITS REMOVAL IS THE RULING IN COPY.** It read *"Close or reassign them first."* — an instruction that stopped being true the moment the clerk was allowed to continue, and leaving it would have been a screen telling a person to do something the product no longer requires. **No rule id and no reason on screen** (B14 rules 31/32): the id travels in `br_ref` and the WHY — that a sale is a transfer, and a transfer carries the tenants — lives in the decision log and on this row. ⚠ **THE TIER MOVED WITH THE WORDS.** It was a `RefusalStrip` — danger, `role="alert"`, `data-refusal` in the markup — and is now the kit's `WarningBox` (`tone="soon"`, `role="status"`, `data-warning="customers-open-sub-accounts"`), because a warning left inside a refusal wrapper still reads as a refusal to a clerk and still announces itself as one to a screen reader, whatever the sentence says. B14 rule 36 — the shared component kit is how a screen complies. ⚠ **THE CLERK IS NOT ASKED TO ACKNOWLEDGE IT, AND THE RULING LEFT THAT TO US.** There is no tick-to-continue: that would be a refusal with an extra click, which is the thing the PO removed, and five further steps plus a named commit button already stand between reading this and closing anything. **NOTHING EXISTING IS REWRITTEN.** Move-outs already recorded against a master stay as they are — hard rule 4, corrections are new rows — and no migration was written in either direction. | `B19 §15.8` item 1 (SUPERSEDED 2026-09-20; items 2–3 stand) · `BR-CUS-171` (who an open child is — the LINK only) · `BR-CUS-055` · `BR-CUS-030` · `BR-SHL-019` · B14 rules 31/32/33/36 · hard rule 4 · `OQ-CUS-12515` (CLOSED 2026-09-20, option (b)) · `DEF-CUS-5930` · `DEF-CUS-5950` · `06-backend/apps/customers/services.py` (`move_out`) · `07-frontend/src/features/customers/MoveOutWizard.tsx` (step 1) | **CONFIRMED — PO RULING 2026-09-20 (`OQ-CUS-12515`, option (b)). Shipped in `2.317.0`.** ⚠ It was `PROPOSED — LANE JUDGMENT` from 2026-09-20 until the same day; what is signed is the WARNING, and the refusal it replaced was never signed by anyone. | `06-backend/tests/test_br_cus_1082_parent_with_open_children.py` · `06-backend/tests/test_move_out_scenarios.py` scenario `E1` |

| BR-CUS-1083 | **A CREW THAT ATTENDS AND DOES NOT CUT IS RECORDED, AND THE RECORDING MOVES THE CASE.** PO ruling 2026-09-20, verbatim: *"The register reflects reality. Today a failed visit is invisible and the case looks untouched."* **THE FOUR OUTCOMES ARE THE PO'S OWN WORDS AND THE SET IS CLOSED:** `locked_gate` · `access_refused` · `paid_at_door` · `disputed`. A fifth is refused BY NAME, for the reason `STOP_REASONS` is closed one function over — each of these ends with a household either keeping or losing supply, and a fifth way to arrive there is a decision, not a string. ⚠ **`locked_gate` AND `access_refused` ARE NOT ONE OUTCOME WEARING TWO WORDS.** They make the same move and they are different FACTS: a padlock is an obstacle, a person standing in the doorway is a refusal, and a regulator reading the record a year later is asking which one happened. **WHERE EACH ONE SENDS THE CASE — the PO ruled *"each must move the case somewhere, not merely annotate it"* and WHICH somewhere is DERIVED (hard-won law 13, decision log 2026-09-20):** the first three **clear `cut_scheduled_for` and return the case to `notice_sent`** — the cut is still lawful and still owed, what failed was the visit, and the case must stop advertising a date that has passed; `disputed` goes to **`stopped` with reason `dispute`**, which blueprint p137 already draws as one of the three reasons a ladder is stopped by hand. **THE TIER IS UNTOUCHED, so a re-schedule resumes rather than restarts, and NO NEW DATE IS INVENTED** — a date is what `schedule_cut` computes from a notice period, and a lane guessing *"try again next Tuesday"* would be writing a notice period nobody served. ⚠ **`paid_at_door` DOES NOT STOP THE LADDER AND RECORDS NO AMOUNT.** A crew holding a cheque is not a posted payment; `apps/customers` writes no financial table (`BR-CUS-037`), and a stop on an unposted payment closes a case on a crew's word. When the money posts, `halt_reason` answers `nothing_owed` by itself and `schedule_cut` refuses — the ladder stops itself, which is `BR-PAY-036`'s design. `PAID_AT_DOOR_TAKES_NO_MONEY` says so on every payload. ⚠ **THE OUTCOME IS A STORED ROW AND THE MOVE IS AN EXPLICIT WRITE — THE OPPOSITE ANSWER TO `on_hold`, WHICH IS DERIVED.** The difference is whether the fact has a CLOCK in it: a protection expires while nobody is looking, so a stored state would be stale with nothing to re-read it; a crew attended on a day and found a padlock, which never stops being true. Deriving it would be a computation over history and would give the wrong answer the moment the cut is re-scheduled. ⚠ **ONE TRANSACTION, AUDIT ROW INCLUDED** — the visit row, the case move and `disconnect.visit.recorded` are one act, `commit_cut`'s shape. A `disputed` outcome writes TWO audit rows, its own and `stop_by_hand`'s, because two acts happened. ⛔ **ONLY ON A SCHEDULED CASE**, refused by name otherwise: a case with no date on the calendar had nobody at its meter. ⛔ **IT RAISES NO WORK ORDER, RE-DISPATCHES NOBODY AND NOTIFIES NO ONE** (`VISIT_RAISES_NO_FOLLOW_UP`) — a new visit is a new cut date, which is the wizard's act. **NO BACK-FILL:** cases already sitting `scheduled` with a date in the past get no row, because nobody recorded what happened at those meters. | **PO ruling 2026-09-20** (decision log of that date, verbatim) · `OQ-383` (CLOSED 2026-09-20; raised 2026-08-25) · `DSC-02.3` CREW banner · B18 §5.8.3 / §5.8.7 · `BR-CUS-039` · `BR-CUS-037` · `BR-PAY-036` · CLAUDE.md hard rules 1, 4, 7, 8 · `06-backend/apps/customers/disconnect_visits.py` · `06-backend/apps/customers/migrations/0062_br_cus_1083_disconnect_visits.py` · `07-frontend/src/features/customers/disconnect/record/RecordVisitDialog.tsx` | **CONFIRMED — PO RULING 2026-09-20. Built the same day.** | `06-backend/tests/test_br_cus_1083_visit_outcomes.py` |

**THE THING A LATER LANE WILL BE TEMPTED TO DO, NAMED SO IT GOES RED.** Folding the
four outcomes into a DERIVED state, because `on_hold` is right there as a precedent.
It is not a precedent — read the clock paragraph above, and
`disconnect_visits.py` §2, before changing it. If the change still looks right,
the thing to change is the DECISION, in the decision log, and not the code.
`test_a_recorded_visit_moves_the_case_and_the_row_is_the_evidence` is the
assertion that goes red on it.
