| BR-CUS-1090 | **A MOVE-IN IS NUMBERED THE MOMENT IT IS APPROVED, NOT WHEN IT COMPLETES — `MVI-{YY}-{0000}` ON THE JOB ROW, AND THAT NUMBER IS ITS IDENTITY FOR THE REST OF ITS LIFE.** PO ruling 2026-09-22, verbatim: *"you would ahve created a new move In request ID and add all details from servcies request to it and save it as a draft - do you ahve move in request ID in numbering?"*, and *"fix all these issues"* over a table whose row 16 reads *A move-in gets `MVI-…` at creation and its own page*. **WHAT IT FIXES:** approving a move-in raised a `move_in_jobs` row with no reference of its own, so the Move-ins tab printed the SERVICE REQUEST number in a column headed `Reference` and that link walked the clerk BACK to the application. Two rows beside it on the same seeded utility printed `MVI-26-0001` and `MVI-26-0002`. **THE PRECEDENT IS THE PO'S OWN, AND IT IS FOLLOWED EXACTLY — `BR-CUS-093` (2026-08-24):** a service request is numbered at CREATION and not at submission, *"the clerk can quote the number to the customer on the phone while the form is still half-filled"*, and an abandoned draft **BURNS its number**. ⚠ **SO GAPS IN THE `MVI-` SERIES ARE NORMAL AND MUST NEVER BE REPORTED AS LOSS.** A rolled-back approval keeps its allocation; `BR-TEN-035` permits the gap and prefers it to a number issued twice. ⛔ **`BR-CUS-152` IS NOT NARROWED AND `move_records` IS NOT TOUCHED.** The fork — *is a draft a different thing, or does the immutability rule loosen* — is answered the first way, and it was already answered before anyone asked: `move_in_jobs` is **the job waiting to happen** (two states, `pending` → `done`) and `move_records` is **the job that happened** (`account_id` NOT NULL, nothing ever updates a row). **What travels between them is the NUMBER, never the ROW** — `moves.record_move` INSERTs a new record carrying the reserved string, and no row changes table, is promoted, or is updated. ⚠ **THE MECHANISM ALREADY EXISTED: `record_move(reserved_move_no=…)`, from `CR-122-BRC06` — *"A RESERVED NUMBER IS ADOPTED, NOT RE-ALLOCATED … allocating a second one here would file the finished job under a number nobody was told."*** The parked wizard has reserved since 2026-09-20; the approval reserves now. One code path, two callers. **ONE NUMBER ACROSS THREE TABLES, AND THE CHAIN IS THE RULE:** `move_in_jobs.move_no` (allocated once, at approval) → `wizard_parks.reference` (CARRIED, via `move_in_no` in `CARRIED_REFERENCE_KEYS`, burning no counter) → `move_records.move_no` (adopted, the draft's first and the job's second, never minted afresh). A move-in raised by any other door is unchanged and still mints at commit. ⛔ **THE JOB STILL COPIES NOTHING** — `move_in_jobs` §2 stands word for word. The applicant, the unit, the requested start, the services and the outstanding documents are all READ through the NOT NULL, `PROTECT`ed request at the moment anybody asks, because *"a second copy of a fact that already has an owner is how two numbers come to disagree"*, and because an address column here would be a fourth home hard rule 8 forbids and `G85` fails. **The services were the one detail genuinely missing and they are now served the same way.** ⚠ **B18 §5.5.2's FOOTNOTE IS NOT SUPERSEDED A SECOND TIME.** `BR-CUS-1077` superseded it for the move-in on 2026-09-16 and said so; what this rule reverses is narrower and unsigned — `move_reads.MOVE_IN_IS_STILL_A_DRAFT`'s own sentence *"The record and its number are written when the account opens"*, which `CR-122-BRC06` had already falsified for parked drafts two days earlier. That sentence is narrowed in the same commit as the code. **NO STEP COLUMN, still** — nothing writes one, so `STEP_HAS_NO_STORE` goes on riding every job row. **IDEMPOTENT AT THE DATABASE:** `uq_move_in_jobs_tenant_move_no_ci` beside the existing `uq_move_in_jobs_tenant_request`, so a double press mints one number even if the service check races — `BR-CUS-093` clause (e)'s posture. **NULL IS A REAL ANSWER:** a tenant with no `move_in` series row gets a job with no number and the reason is SERVED (`moves.MOVE_NUMBER_UNAVAILABLE`), because *"a household must not be refused a move-in because nobody configured numbering"*. | **PO ruling 2026-09-22** (decision log of that date, verbatim) · `CR-133-BRC07` · `BR-CUS-093` (the numbered-at-creation precedent, including the burned number) · `BR-CUS-1077` (the job row this numbers) · `BR-CUS-150`/`151`/`152` (untouched) · `BR-TEN-035` (allocation, and the lawful gap) · `CR-122-BRC06` (`reserved_move_no`, the mechanism reused) · B14 rules 31/32/33 · CLAUDE.md hard rules 1, 6, 8 · `06-backend/apps/customers/move_in_jobs.py` · `06-backend/apps/customers/migrations/0064_br_cus_1090_move_in_job_number.py` · `07-frontend/src/features/customers/MoveInsTab.tsx` | **CONFIRMED — PO RULING 2026-09-22. Built the same day, `2.328.0`.** | `06-backend/tests/test_br_cus_1090_move_in_job_number.py` |
| BR-CUS-1091 | **A REQUEST TAKES THE ADDRESS IN PARTS SO IT CAN FIND THE PREMISES — AND THE PREMISES IS STILL THE ONE ADDRESS.** PO ruling 2026-09-22, verbatim: *"correct address will be on presmies but first time you will ahve to take the details to match it"*, over a report whose row 16 asks for **Address line · Unit, flat or suite · City · State · ZIP** on capture step 3. **WHAT IT FIXES:** step 3 asked for a street line and nothing else, so a clerk holding a full address had nowhere to put most of it and the premises search — the one thing that step exists to do — had almost nothing to match on. **THREE COLUMNS, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS NAMED FOR WHAT IT IS:** `address_city_as_given`, `address_state_as_given` and `address_postal_code_as_given` on `service_requests` (`customers/0065`), accepted by `CAPTURE_FIELDS`, served by `location_and_services`. ⛔ **`BR-MET-1161` IS NOT NARROWED AND NO FOURTH HOME IS CREATED, WHICH IS WHY THE REVERSAL IS THIS NARROW.** Hard rule 8 governs where an address is **stored as the truth**; it says nothing about what a clerk may TYPE. What these three hold is **a record of what a member of the public said**, which is the `_as_given` posture `address_as_given` has carried since it was written and the one `interruptions.location_text` carries — *what a stranger typed; a report, not a record*. **THE BARE SPELLINGS STAY REFUSED BY NAME**: a `city` on this table is the fourth home `G85` catches, and a backend case asserts all three bare names are refused in the same breath as the three `_as_given` names are stored, because an implementation that accepted a bare `city` would satisfy *the parts are captured* and be exactly the thing the rule forbids (hard-won law 7 — ask what would make the check pass wrongly). **THE SECOND HALF IS THE PO'S OWN AND IS BUILT:** the boxes are typed **until a premises is picked**, and the moment one is they disappear and the register's own city, state and ZIP take their place, read-only — so a matched request shows the premises and never the claim. **NOT REQUIRED, DELIBERATELY.** `Continue` asks for the street line and nothing else, exactly as it did: a new connection at a field with nothing on record is the job this wizard exists for, and an asterisk here would refuse a clerk who has a street and no town. ⚠ **THIS REVERSES A REFUSAL THIS MODULE RECORDED SEVEN DAYS EARLIER, AND THE CHAIN IS PART OF THE RULE.** Three columns of these exact names were built on 2026-09-15 and removed the same hour under the PO's ruling of that day (*"if we have unit or street address then we should take it from premises/Unit - it means its a search"*); `BR-CUS-1055` records that near-miss and `CAPTURE_FIELDS` carried *"Do not re-add them"* until today. **The warning is AMENDED rather than deleted**, with the whole chain written out, because a lane that finds these columns and then finds a warning against them has no way to tell which came first. ⚠ **AND `G85`'s DECLARED BLIND SPOT MOVES FROM 3 TO 6 IN THE SAME COMMIT** — the gate matches by NAME and is deliberately not taught the `_as_given` spelling (teaching it would make it refuse `address_as_given` itself, which `BR-CUS-070` requires), so the NUMBER is what does the work, and a stale number is indistinguishable from a current one. | **PO ruling 2026-09-22** (decision log of that date, verbatim, and the implementation entry beneath it) · `BR-CUS-1055` (the refusal this reverses, amended in both directions the same day) · `BR-MET-1161` / CLAUDE.md hard rule 8 (untouched) · `BR-CUS-070` (`address_as_given`, the posture reused) · B14 rules 32/33/38/42 · `06-backend/apps/customers/migrations/0065_br_cus_1091_address_parts_as_given.py` · `06-backend/apps/customers/service_request_service.py` (`CAPTURE_FIELDS`) · `07-frontend/src/features/customers/ServiceRequestCapturePage.tsx` step 3 · `10-scripts/gates/g85_one_address_home.py` | **CONFIRMED — PO RULING 2026-09-22. Built the same day, `2.328.3`.** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr031_service_request_api.py::test_the_address_parts_are_captured_as_a_statement_and_are_not_the_service_address` · `07-frontend/src/features/customers/ServiceRequestCapturePage.test.tsx` (*step 3 sends the address PARTS, every one of them `_as_given`*) · `10-scripts/po20260922_reqa_walkthrough.mjs` (row 16, both halves, in Chromium) |

a test asserts `25.0000` and goes red on `25.00`). **ONLY PRICEABLE ONE-TIME CODES ARE OFFERED, AND BOTH HALVES ARE PRECEDENT RATHER THAN TASTE:** `one_time` because a recurring base charge or a penalty is something that happens to an ACCOUNT over time while an application fee is taken once at a counter against a request that has no account yet; and priceable because of the PO's ruling of 2026-09-21 (`DEF-MET-6110`) — *"Step 6 offers only charge codes a build can actually price"*, after a clerk picked `TAP`, which states no amount, and lost thirty-three acts to *"Something went wrong on our side."* The predicate is `canBeRaisedOnItsOwn`, IMPORTED rather than re-written, because two copies of *can this be priced* drift. **Measured on the seeded utility: 29 charge codes, 19 of them one-time, 15 of those priceable** — `BULK-WATER`, `DAMAGE`, `IMPACT` and `TAP` state no amount and no rule and are left out. ⚠ **THE FILTER IS LAYER 1 AND IS NOT THE CHECK.** A tenant edits a code on Settings this afternoon, so a list filtered when the panel mounted can be wrong by the time the clerk presses Add; | BR-CUS-1094 | ⛔ **AMENDED 2026-10-02 BY `BR-CUS-1116` (PO, option A): who pays a unit's base is now decided PER DAY of the period, not once at billing start — a tenant who moves in on the 16th takes the base from that day and the owner keeps the days before it (15/30 × $8.00 = $4.00).** ⚠⚠ **READ THIS FIRST: MOST OF THIS RULE IS ALREADY BUILT, AND THE ROW ORIGINALLY IMPLIED OTHERWISE (corrected 2026-09-23, the same day).** `BR-CUS-170` (PO 2026-08-27) already bills a master account for its premises' empty units, and `apps/billing/gather.py` already PRICES them — `_unbilled_unit_tariffs`, *"the base charge of every empty unit"*. **So this rule largely RE-RULES what exists**; what it adds is the occupied/vacant split being decided at run start and the no-payer refusal below. **A lane must not rebuild the billing path** — read `gather.py`'s answer, never derive a second one, or the screen and the bill will disagree on a money path. **A VACANT UNIT'S BILL GOES TO THE PREMISES' MASTER ACCOUNT; AN OCCUPIED UNIT'S GOES TO ITS OWN TENANT — AND WHICH IS WHICH IS DECIDED WHEN THE BILL RUN STARTS, NOT STORED (PO ruling 2026-09-23).** PO verbatim: *"if we have 10 units on premsies, and 5 are vancant and 5 are occpied by tenants. then 5 teannt bill will go to them to their regualr account, but for 5 vacant units - bill will be generated against master account. also every month this is dynamic siaution and hence unit and account valid for billing are decied at the time of billing start only"*. ⚠ **THIS ANSWERS `OQ-CUS-12609` AND SUPERSEDES THE EXCLUSION IN THE 2026-09-12 RULING**, whose row is titled *"A MASTER ACCOUNT NOW LISTS THE ACCOUNTS AT ITS ADDRESS, AND THE ONE THING IT WILL NOT DO IS ADD THEM UP"*. **It is superseded in its BILLING clause only**: the `Accounts` tab (`BR-CUS-1048`) still LISTS and still does not TOTAL. What changes is that a vacant unit now has a payer. ⚠ **THE TENANT HALF DOES NOT MOVE.** An occupied unit is billed to its own account exactly as today — this rule adds a payer for units that have none, it does not re-route anybody. So this is **not** *one bill carrying every unit's lines*: the master receives the vacant units' charges, and five tenants still receive five bills. ⚠⚠ **THE DYNAMIC CLAUSE IS THE LOAD-BEARING ONE AND IT IS A PROHIBITION.** Occupancy is resolved **at bill-run start** and only then. **No column may cache which units a master account pays for**, because a cached answer is wrong the first time a tenant moves in and nothing would go red — the same shape as a refusal with a clock in it (hard-won law 2's mirror). The run takes its own snapshot, the way it already freezes a tariff at issue. ⚠ **RULED THE SAME DAY — A VACANT UNIT WITH NO MASTER ACCOUNT IS A REFUSAL THE RUN STATES, NEVER A UNIT IT SKIPS (PO 2026-09-23).** PO verbatim: *"we should say it and use this rule during billing"*, answering the clause this row had deliberately left open. ⚠ **THIS ROW READ *"WHAT IS NOT YET RULED, and must not be guessed"* FOR ABOUT AN HOUR ON 2026-09-23** — kept here rather than deleted, because the next reader meeting the open form in `git log` or in the decision row of that date must be able to see WHAT changed and WHEN. **WHAT IT MEANS IN THE RUN:** a vacant unit whose premises names no master account has no payer, and the bill run SAYS SO — the unit appears in the run's own exceptions with its premises and the reason, exactly as a missing read does. It is **never** silently skipped, **never** billed to a tenant who does not live there, and **never** given an invented account. **THE SHAPE THIS TAKES IS ALREADY IN THE PRODUCT:** `billing.models.DECISION_CASES` carries `base_only_billing` — *"include | exclude the base-only accounts this run"* — minted for the PO's own ruling of 2026-08-11 that this is *"a decision user make hence during pre billing process, you have to give option to user to opt in or opt out"*. A vacant unit with no payer belongs in that same pre-billing list, where a clerk sees it BEFORE the run prices anything, rather than in a log nobody opens after. ⚠ **AND THE REFUSAL MUST NAME THE UNIT, NOT A COUNT.** *"3 units could not be billed"* is a number a clerk cannot act on; the premises and the unit label are what lets somebody attach a master account and re-run. This is `G32`'s lesson — a refusal that reaches a person as silence, or as an unresolvable code, is not a refusal. **WORKED CASE:** a 10-unit building, 5 occupied and 5 vacant at the moment the October run starts. Five tenant bills are raised as today; the five vacant units' base charges are raised on the premises' master account. In November two tenants have moved in: the run recomputes and the master pays for three. ⚠ **THE SCREEN THIS RULE IS CHECKED WITH IS BUILT (2026-09-23, `2.345.0`) AND THE PO ASKED FOR IT FIRST, BEFORE THE BILLING CHANGE.** PO verbatim: *"show all units with its status what wil be billed to master account - can we do that?"*. It is a fourteenth, conditional tab on `S21` — `Units`, third, straight after `Accounts` — served by `GET /accounts/{id}/units`, and it is **the other half of `BR-CUS-1048`**: that tab lists every OCCUPYING account at the address, and a vacant unit has no account, so **a vacant unit appeared on no list anywhere in the product.** **IT READS THE PAYER FROM THE TWO FUNCTIONS THE RUN USES AND RE-DERIVES NEITHER** — `covered_unit_ids` and `base_charge_only_coverage`, the same pair `apps/billing/gather` resolves through the seam — so the screen and the bill cannot disagree. **IT STORES NOTHING**, which is this row's own prohibition: `test_br_cus_1094_tc5` reads a building, moves a tenant in and reads it again in the SAME process, and the unit changes hands. **AND IT STATES A PAYER, NEVER A PRICE** (`BR-CUS-1040`): a vacant unit with no plan is billed here and charged nothing — `gather._unbilled_unit_tariffs` skips it on `not has_plan` — and the row SAYS SO rather than promising a charge the run will not raise. ⚠ **ONE THING THE SCREEN DELIBERATELY DOES NOT SHOW, because it does not exist:** `occupancy_statuses`' *vacant base-only* versus *vacant no-billing* distinction. **No table in this product carries a foreign key to that list**, so no unit declares one (`DEF-CUS-6306`, and `DEF-BIL-6296` one layer up). | PO 2026-09-23 | **CONFIRMED · SCREEN BUILT 2026-09-23** | `apps/customers/selectors.master_unit_billing_page` · `apps/customers/api.account_units` · `07-frontend/src/features/customers/MasterUnitsTab.tsx` · `06-backend/tests/test_br_cus_1094_master_units_tab.py` · `10-scripts/br_cus_1094_master_units_walkthrough.mjs` |

**THE THING A LATER LANE WILL BE TEMPTED TO DO, NAMED SO IT GOES RED.** Copying the
applicant, the unit, the start date or the services onto `move_in_jobs`, because the
PO's sentence says *"add all details from servcies request to it"*. **They are already
all on the row** — read through the link, which is what `move_in_jobs` §2 requires and
what makes a queue and an application incapable of disagreeing. The PO's sentence is
about what a clerk SEES, and a clerk sees no difference.
`test_the_job_stores_no_fact_the_request_owns` is the assertion that goes red on it.

## BR-CUS-1096 — A PROTECTION WARNS THE CLERK; IT NEVER STOPS THEM (2026-09-25)

⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-25 (lane RECON) — THIS IS ONE OF FIVE RULINGS OF ONE PRINCIPLE, NOT A LOCAL
EXCEPTION.** The principle: **the product tells the clerk what is true and does not stop them
acting.** The other four were ruled the same day: **`BR-CUS-1097`** (reconnection shows what is
still owed and never requires it clear) · **`BR-CUS-1098`** (an occupant's protection does not block
a cut of the master account — the case where the person affected owes nothing) · **`BR-CUS-1099`**
(no recorded proof of certified service warns, it does not refuse the schedule — the case where the
record protects the UTILITY rather than the customer) · **`BR-CUS-1100`** (the block on new service
marks the ACCOUNT and the CUSTOMER, never the address). **A sixth case of this shape is the
principle applying again, not a question to raise** (hard-won law 13), and **friction before
proceeding has now been declined FOUR times** — option C here, and again on each of the others.



**PO ruling, verbatim, 2026-09-25:** *"so please show the warining - but dont
stop clark from doing his work; we have to tell there is an issue but we ant
stop them"*. Closes `OQ-680`, open since 2026-08-27 from `DEF-744`.

**WHAT IT SAYS.** Where a protection is in force on an account — a **medical
certificate**, a **weather moratorium**, an **active payment plan**
(`disconnects.PROTECTIONS`) — the disconnect wizard **SHOWS the clerk a warning
and lets them proceed**. No protection refuses a schedule, and there is no
override to grant because there is nothing to override.

**THE CLERK IS ASKED FOR NOTHING FIRST.** A middle path — proceed, but type a
reason — was put to the PO and **declined**; so was refusing outright. *"we ant
stop them"* rules out a typed reason, a tick-to-acknowledge and a supervisor.

**THE CONSEQUENCE, AND IT IS PART OF THE RULE RATHER THAN A SECOND ONE.** If
nothing blocks, **the record of the override is the only protection that
exists** — for the customer and for the utility. The product captures, itself,
without asking the clerk for anything: **which protections were in force** (by
kind, with their dates) · **that the warning was shown** · **who proceeded** ·
**when**. Hard rule 7 already puts a money act in `audit_log`; this is the same
shape for an act that takes away a household's supply.

⚠ **AND NOTHING OF THAT REASONING APPEARS ON SCREEN** (B14 rule 33 — rationale
is zero words). The warning states the protection and its dates and stops.

⚠ **WHAT WAS SHOWN BEFORE THE RULING, BECAUSE A SIGNATURE COVERS ONLY WHAT WAS
SHOWN.** The PO was told plainly that in much of North America a medical
certificate or a weather moratorium is **law, not process**, and that a recorded
protection is a different kind of fact from a ticked checklist. They ruled
against blocking over that concern. It is written down here so the next lane
does not re-raise it (hard-won law 13).

⚠ **WHAT THIS RULE DOES NOT REACH.** The **unattended** ladder halt —
`disconnects.HALT_PROTECTED` freezing reminders, notices and the certified final
letter — is a machine declining to act, not a clerk being stopped, and it stands
exactly as signed under `BR-CUS-039`. `record_protection`'s three lifetimes and
its refusal of a fourth kind are untouched. `BR-CWP-091` (*"a hold stops a
disconnection, not the debt"*) is a portal rule about the DEBT; its first clause
now reads against this rule and belongs to whoever owns it.

⛔ **WHAT THE PRODUCT DOES TODAY IS THE OPPOSITE, MEASURED.**
`disconnects.schedule_cut` → `_require_running` → `halt_reason` returns
`HALT_PROTECTED` and the schedule is **refused**. The rule is CONFIRMED and the
wiring is owed; nothing in this repository implements it yet.

| BR | Statement | Sources | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-1096 | **A protection in force WARNS the clerk and never refuses the act.** The three protections are shown with their dates on the disconnect wizard; the clerk proceeds without supplying anything. The product records, automatically, which protections were in force, that the warning was shown, who proceeded and when — that record is the only protection that exists once nothing blocks. The unattended ladder halt is not touched. | PO ruling 2026-09-25 (verbatim above) · `OQ-680` CLOSED · `DEF-744` · `12-governance/02-decision-log.md` 2026-09-25 · hard rule 7 · B14 rule 33 | **CONFIRMED 2026-09-25** | ⛔ **NONE — and the code does the opposite.** The test is owed by the lane that wires it, and it must assert BOTH halves: the schedule succeeds over a live medical certificate, AND the four facts are on the recorded act. |

## BR-CUS-1097 — THE BALANCE NEED NOT BE CLEAR TO RECONNECT (2026-09-25)

**PO ruling, verbatim, 2026-09-25, choosing option (a) of two:** *"Show what's
still owed and let the clerk proceed"*.

**WHAT IT SAYS.** On reconnection the screen shows **what is still owed — the
arrears plus the reconnection fee — and what has been paid against them**, and
**the clerk restores service anyway. Nothing blocks.**

**WORKED, AND IT IS THE CASE PUT TO THE PO.** **Brett Calloway owes $1,204.50
plus the reconnection fee. He pays $400.00 and agrees a plan for the rest. The
clerk restores water the same day.**

**DECLINED:** option (b), requiring the balance to be clear first — which is what
the code does today. **And no friction replaces it:** no typed reason, no
tick-to-acknowledge, no supervisor. The PO declined friction **twice on
2026-09-25**, here and on `BR-CUS-1096`.

⚠ **IT SUPERSEDES HALF OF `BR-CUS-104`, AND THE SUPERSESSION IS STATED RATHER
THAN ASSUMED (hard-won law 13).** The fee's TIMING is untouched — it still lands
**at the cut**, it is still a real receivable, and `A-11340`'s position is still
**$8,315.00**. What is superseded is the GATE: *"a customer must clear the
arrears PLUS the fee before `mark_reconnecting` will accept them"*. **$8,315.00
is now what the clerk is SHOWN, not what they must collect first.**
`BR-CUS-105` is unaffected.

**THE CONSEQUENCE, AND IT IS PART OF THE RULE.** With nothing blocking, **the
record is the only protection there is.** At reconnect the product captures,
itself, without asking the clerk for anything: **what was still owed** at the
moment service was restored · **what had been paid** · **who restored it** ·
**when**. Reconnecting on a payment plan is normal practice; reconnecting with
no record of what was outstanding is how a dispute six months later has no
answer.

⚠ **NONE OF THAT REASONING APPEARS ON SCREEN** (B14 rule 33).

⚠ **IT IS THE PAIR OF `BR-CUS-1096`, AND THE TWO ARE ONE PRINCIPLE: the product
tells the clerk what is true and does not stop them acting — and because nothing
stops them, it records what was true when they acted.** Two independent PO
rulings, hours apart, on unrelated screens. A lane finding one alone will read it
as a local exception.

⚠ **AND IT INVALIDATES A DERIVATION IN `OQ-1170`'s CLOSING NOTE**, which settles
what status a restored account returns to by arguing that *"a case reaches
RECONNECTING → RESTORED only once the balance is clear"*. A restored account can
now owe money. The conclusion is not reversed here; the argument for it is gone.

✅ **THE PRODUCT DOES THIS NOW — WIRED 2026-09-25, lane CUTPATH (`DEF-CUS-6735`
CLOSED).** ⚠ **THIS PARAGRAPH READ *"WHAT THE PRODUCT DOES TODAY IS THE
OPPOSITE"* UNTIL THAT DAY** and described `reconnect_blockers` returning
`not_settled` whenever `balance > 0`. It no longer does: `reconnect_warnings`
carries `money_still_owed` with the same figure from the same read, and `ready`
does not look at it. **Measured on the running product:** case `DSC-26-0007`
owes **$223.90**, `Mark reconnecting` is live, and the screen says *"Money is
still owed."* ⚠ **ONE REFUSAL WAS DELIBERATELY NOT SWEPT UP WITH IT** — an
**unreadable** balance still refuses, because this rule is about a balance that
is KNOWN and not clear, and moving a case to *"paid, awaiting a visit"* on a
reading that FAILED would put that sentence on screen with nothing behind it.

| BR | Statement | Sources | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-1097 | **A clerk may restore service on an account that still owes money.** The screen states the arrears plus the reconnection fee and what has been paid against them; nothing refuses, and the clerk supplies nothing. The product records automatically what was still owed, what had been paid, who restored it and when. Supersedes `BR-CUS-104`'s *clear-before-reconnecting* gate and no other clause of it. | PO ruling 2026-09-25 (verbatim above) · `BR-CUS-104` (amended) · `BR-CUS-1096` (its pair) · `OQ-381` · `OQ-1170` · `12-governance/02-decision-log.md` 2026-09-25 · hard rule 7 · B14 rules 33, 41 | **CONFIRMED 2026-09-25** | ✅ **WIRED AND TESTED 2026-09-25 (lane CUTPATH, `DEF-CUS-6735` CLOSED).** `06-backend/tests/test_br_cus_1097_1099_warn_do_not_stop.py` asserts BOTH halves: Brett Calloway's $1,204.50 reconnects, AND the record carries what was owed, when and who. ⚠ **THE FOURTH FACT — WHAT HAD BEEN PAID — IS NOT RECORDED AND IS NOT INVENTED**: nothing on the disconnect path reads it (`DEF-PAY-6074`), so it travels as the named absence `paid_against_arrears_not_read`. The rule is NOT fully satisfied until that read lands, and this column says so rather than claiming it. Proved in a browser at 1440 and 1280 by `10-scripts/br_cus_1097_1099_case_record_walkthrough.mjs`. |

⚠ **`BR-CUS-104` IS AMENDED, NOT WITHDRAWN.** Its row above still states the
fee's timing correctly and that clause is signed law. Its sentence *"a customer
must clear the arrears PLUS the fee before `mark_reconnecting` will accept
them"* is **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-25 by `BR-CUS-1097`** and must not be built to,
cited as current, or restated in a docstring. The same sentence is repeated in
`04-docs/05-modules/03-customers/06-epics/EP-CUS-10-transfer-disconnect-wizards.md`
and in `07-frontend/src/features/customers/DisconnectWizard.tsx`; neither was
amended by this lane.

⚠ **AND `BR-CUS-1096`, ITS PAIR, IS ABOVE IN THIS FILE.** A protection warns and
never blocks; a balance is shown and never blocks. Read them together.

## BR-CUS-1098 — **A MASTER ACCOUNT IN DISCONNECT AND RECONNECT; AN OCCUPANT'S PROTECTION DOES NOT BLOCK THE CUT** (2026-09-25)


⚠ **ONE PRINCIPLE, FIVE RULINGS, NOT FIVE EXCEPTIONS: *the product tells the
clerk what is true and does not stop them acting.*** `BR-CUS-1096` (a protection
warns) · `BR-CUS-1097` (the balance need not be clear to reconnect — **recorded
by lane PROT; this lane recorded the same ruling independently and defers to
that id**) · **`BR-CUS-1098`** · **`BR-CUS-1099`** · **`BR-CUS-1100`**. A lane
finding one alone should read all five before deciding it is a local exception.
**A sixth case of this shape is the principle applying again, not a question to
raise** (hard-won law 13), and **friction before proceeding has been declined
four times.**

### (a) The table, confirmed in full

**PO, 2026-09-25, verbatim:** *"this si correct you should ocnsider these while
building"*. The three rules underneath are **cited, not restated**:
**`BR-CUS-1010`** (a master account names a premises and need not name a unit),
**`BR-CUS-171`** (an occupying account is its child), **`BR-CUS-097`** (a
sub-account's unpayable residue transfers up).

| | Regular account | Master account |
|---|---|---|
| What gets cut | One unit's services | **A building.** Common supply, and occupants who owe nothing can be affected |
| Who is affected | The account holder | **The owner and every occupying account under them** |
| Protections | Checked against one household | **Checked against EVERY OCCUPANT** |
| The debt | What that account owes | **Their own plus residue transferred up** (`BR-CUS-097`) |
| Reconnect | One visit, one address | **May need several** — common supply plus each affected unit |
| The work order | One | **One per affected SERVICE POINT** |

**And for the four steps, confirmed in the same message:** step 1 carries **one
line** — *"Master account · 25 occupying accounts"* — **that single line is the
whole warning** · step 2's protections table gets **a row per occupying account
that has a protection**, not only the master's own · step 3's field-work table
gets **a row per affected SERVICE POINT** rather than per service, which may be
twenty-five · step 4 names **WHO LOSES SUPPLY** rather than only what the account
owes, and shows **transferred residue separately** from the owner's own debt.

### (b) The ruling, asked separately and answered separately

The confirmed table contained the words *"one medical certificate downstairs
protects the whole building"*. **Those were our words, not the PO's**, and they
are ambiguous between the protection being **SHOWN** and it **BLOCKING**. So it
was put to them directly — *"Can a clerk cut a master account while an occupant
is protected?"* — and answered, option **(a)**, verbatim:

> *"Warn and let them proceed — consistent with your two rulings"*

**THE RULE: a protection held by an OCCUPANT does not block a cut of the MASTER
account. It is SHOWN to the clerk, who proceeds or does not.**

**THE WORKED CASE PUT TO THEM: 500 Main Street.** The owner owes **$14,390.00**
across **25 units**; one tenant in **unit 12** holds a **medical certificate**.
**The clerk may cut.**

⚠ **WHAT MAKES THIS ONE DIFFERENT, AND IT WAS SHOWN TO THE PO IN THESE TERMS
BEFORE THEY RULED.** `BR-CUS-1096` and `BR-CUS-1097` are about **the person who
owes the money**. **This is about cutting off someone WHO OWES NOTHING and has a
certificate on file.** That distinction was put to them explicitly and they ruled
anyway. **A signature covers only what was shown, and what was shown here
includes the distinction.**

⚠ **AND THE CONSEQUENCE IS SHARPER HERE THAN ANYWHERE ELSE: THE PEOPLE AFFECTED
ARE NOT THE PEOPLE THE RECORD IS ABOUT.** What is captured at the cut must name
**EVERY OCCUPYING ACCOUNT THAT HELD A PROTECTION, by kind and by date** — not
merely that the master's own checks were seen. **The owner's case file is the
only place a protected tenant downstairs will ever appear.**

| BR | Statement | Sources | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-1098 | **A master account's cut is a building's cut, and an OCCUPANT'S protection warns rather than blocks it.** Protections are checked against every occupant and shown; the debt includes residue transferred up; reconnect may need a visit per affected service point, and one work order per service point. The record must name every occupying account that held a protection, by kind and date. | PO ruling 2026-09-25 (both messages verbatim above) · `BR-CUS-1010` · `BR-CUS-171` · `BR-CUS-097` · `BR-CUS-1096` · `12-governance/02-decision-log.md` 2026-09-25 · hard rule 7 | **CONFIRMED 2026-09-25** | ⛔ **NONE.** Nothing in the product distinguishes a master account on this path today. Owed by the wiring lane; the disconnect rebuild is PAUSED. |

## BR-CUS-1099 — **NO RECORDED PROOF OF CERTIFIED SERVICE WARNS; IT DOES NOT REFUSE THE SCHEDULE** (2026-09-25)

⚠ One principle, five rulings — see `BR-CUS-1098`'s header.

**PO ruling, verbatim, 2026-09-25:**

> *"So we warn user , but we dont stop user - We can't send a certified notice —
> a final-tier cut isn't lawful without one"*

**WHAT IT SAYS.** Step 3's *"No proof, no cut"* becomes a **WARNING, not a
gate**. A clerk may schedule a cut with **no proof of certified service
recorded**. **The screen still asks for it; it no longer refuses.**

✅ **WIRED 2026-09-25, lane CUTPATH (`DEF-CUS-6736` CLOSED).** ⚠ **THIS
PARAGRAPH READ *"VERIFIED IN THE CODE — THE PRODUCT REALLY REFUSES"* UNTIL THAT
DAY**, and it was true when written: `schedule_cut` and `commit_cut` both called
`assert_service_proved`, which raised `cut_on_unrecorded_service`. **That raise
is gone.** The fact is carried by `service_proof_warnings` and lands on the
payload both verbs audit, so `audit_log` records **that no proof was there, who
acted and when** — the capture this rule requires, taken automatically, asking
the clerk for nothing.

⚠ **A SECOND REFUSAL SITS BESIDE IT AND IS REPORTED, NOT ASSUMED.**
`assert_service_proved` also raises **`tier_3_proof_not_certified`**
(`BR-CUS-039`) when the recorded channel is not certified mail. **The PO's words
are about the ABSENCE of proof and say nothing about another channel**, so that
refusal is NOT read as ruled on. `signed_for_on` stays unread and its question
stays open, exactly as before.

⚠ **WHAT MAKES THIS ONE DIFFERENT — IT REVERSES WHO THE RECORD PROTECTS.** The
other rulings are about **protections**: the record shows the utility considered a
**CUSTOMER'S** circumstances. **This is a legal precondition on the utility's OWN
act** — a final-tier cut without proved certified service is a cut **the utility
may not lawfully have made**. **Here the record protects the UTILITY**, and its
absence is the utility's own exposure. *(Recorded as the consequence of the
ruling. It was not put to the PO in these words, and it is not a caveat on the
ruling — they have applied this principle five times and the pattern is
deliberate.)*

**WHAT MUST BE CAPTURED**, automatically, asking the clerk for nothing: **that no
proof of certified service was recorded at the moment the cut was scheduled** ·
**who** scheduled it · **when**. For the other rulings the record names what the
clerk was told; **for this one it names what the utility could not show.**

⚠ **THE PRODUCT GAP IS A SEPARATE FACT AND IS NOT FIXED BY THIS RULING. We
cannot SEND a certified notice at all** — the notice is served outside the
product and its proof is typed in by a person. **That limitation exists today, is
already filed, and the ruling is only about whether the ABSENCE of proof blocks.**

| BR | Statement | Sources | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-1099 | **A cut may be scheduled with no proof of certified service recorded.** The screen asks for the proof and warns when it is absent; it does not refuse. The product records automatically that no proof was recorded, who scheduled the cut and when. The separate refusal on a NON-certified recorded channel is not ruled on here, and the inability to SEND a certified notice is a separate, already-filed gap. | PO ruling 2026-09-25 (verbatim above) · `BR-CUS-100` · `BR-CUS-039` · `12-governance/02-decision-log.md` 2026-09-25 · hard rule 7 · hard-won laws 1, 2 | **CONFIRMED 2026-09-25** | ✅ **WIRED AND TESTED 2026-09-25 (lane CUTPATH, `DEF-CUS-6736` CLOSED).** `assert_service_proved` no longer raises `cut_on_unrecorded_service`; `service_proof_warnings` carries it and `schedule_cut`/`commit_cut` put it on the audited payload, so `audit_log` records the absence, who scheduled and when. `06-backend/tests/test_br_cus_1097_1099_warn_do_not_stop.py` asserts both halves **and** carries one case whose only job is to go RED if a later lane removes `tier_3_proof_not_certified` with it. |

## BR-CUS-1100 — **THE BLOCK ON NEW SERVICE MARKS THE ACCOUNT AND THE CUSTOMER — NEVER THE ADDRESS** (2026-09-25)

⚠ One principle, five rulings — see `BR-CUS-1098`'s header.

**PO ruling, verbatim, 2026-09-25:** *"you ahve to mark account and Customer ID
also"*. **Closes `OQ-384`**, open since 2026-08-25.

**WHAT IT SAYS.** `DSC-02.4`'s *"blocks new service elsewhere until it is
settled"* has a SUBJECT at last: **the ACCOUNT and the CUSTOMER record. Not the
premises.** The third reading the question offered — the **PERSON**, catching a
spouse on a different customer record — was **not chosen**.

⚠ **THE SPLIT IS THE LOAD-BEARING PART.** Marking the **customer** catches them
applying for service at a **different address**; **not** marking the **address**
means **a new tenant moving into that same unit is not punished for the previous
occupant's debt**. **That is the difference between chasing a debtor and
blacklisting a property.** A premises-level mark **reverses** this ruling.

⚠ **TWO CONSEQUENCES ARE OUR READING, NOT THE PO's — OPEN TO CHEAP REVERSAL.**
They have been told we will build these unless they say otherwise:
**(1)** the mark **WARNS and does not refuse** — a new-service request from a
marked customer shows the clerk what is outstanding and the clerk decides, the
fifth application of `BR-CUS-1096`'s principle; **(2) "settled" includes an
AGREED PAYMENT PLAN**, not only a zero balance, because `BR-CUS-1097` lets a
clerk reconnect on a plan and a plan that did not clear the mark would make the
two rules contradict each other.

⛔ **THE PROMISE IS UNKEPT TODAY AND CLOSING THE QUESTION DID NOT WIRE IT.**
Measured on the tip: `disconnects.new_service_block_for()` computes the block and
is three-valued (`None` when AR is unreadable — a failed reading is not a
clearance), and **its only callers are `tests/test_w7_cut_reconnect.py` and one
API docstring**. `NEW_SERVICE_BLOCK_UNCONSUMED` says so in the module's own
words, and a grep test asserts the absence on purpose. **A customer cut for
non-payment can sign up again today and nobody would know** (hard-won law 2).

| BR | Statement | Sources | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-1100 | **The post-cut block on new service is carried by the ACCOUNT and by the CUSTOMER record, and never by the premises.** A new tenant at the same address is not blocked. Whether the mark warns rather than refuses, and whether an agreed payment plan counts as settled, are this repo's reading and not the PO's words. | PO ruling 2026-09-25 (verbatim above) · `OQ-384` CLOSED · `DSC-02.4` · B18 §5.8.7 · `disconnects.NEW_SERVICE_BLOCK_UNCONSUMED` · `12-governance/02-decision-log.md` 2026-09-25 | **CONFIRMED 2026-09-25** | ⛔ **NONE — the block is computed and consumed by nothing.** Owed by the wiring lane, and it must assert the negative too: a new tenant at the cut premises is NOT blocked. |

## BR-CUS-1101 — **AN ACCOUNT TAKES A SUBSET OF ITS UNIT'S SERVICES** (2026-09-25)

**PO ruling, 2026-09-25, in their own scenario.** A premises offers **six**
services. The unit has **six** service points. **Five** of those points carry a
meter or an asset. **And the account takes only three.**

**WHAT IT SAYS.** An account's services are a CHOICE made at move-in, not an
automatic consequence of standing on the unit. Which of the unit's service
points the account takes is asked on the move-in wizard, pre-ticked with all of
them, and the account is opened with **one `service_agreements` row per service
taken and no row at all for the rest**.

⚠ **WHAT WAS TRUE BEFORE, MEASURED.** `customers.services._open_service_agree
ments` looped **every** service point on the unit and opened one agreement per
point, unconditionally — nothing was filtered and nothing was skipped. So in the
PO's own scenario the account came out with **six** agreements where they wanted
**three**, and there was no field on any door and no control on any screen that
could say otherwise. The step was impossible rather than awkward.

⚠ **NO COLUMN WAS ADDED AND NONE MAY BE.** `service_agreements` already carries
everything this needs: taking a subset is **writing fewer rows**. There is no
migration, and the chain the account reaches its world through is unchanged —
`accounts` → `service_agreements(service_point_id)` → `service_points(unit_id,
service_id, route_id, cycle_id)` → `units(rate_plan_id)` and `assets`
(`BR-CUS-029`, `BR-CUS-672`: every inherited fact is held by reference).

⛔ **THE COST THE PO WAS SHOWN AND ACCEPTED: AN ABSENT AGREEMENT CANNOT BE TOLD
APART FROM A DELIBERATE UNTICK.** Nothing records *the clerk considered gas and
said no*, because nothing records anything but the rows that exist. So **no
document, screen or docstring may claim to know which of the two a missing row
is**, and B14 rule 33 means the screen does not explain this either — it simply
never claims it. A later ruling that wants the distinction is a new column and a
new rule, not a reading of this one.

⚠ **THE UNIT OF CHOICE IS THE SERVICE CODE, NOT THE SERVICE POINT ID**, and the
second reason is the load-bearing one: a clerk chooses a *service* and a refusal
can name one out loud, and **`service_points` carries no unique key on (unit,
service)** — a sub-metered unit may hold two water points — so taking *water*
takes every water point at that unit and cannot half-connect one.

⚠ **A SERVICE THE UNIT HAS NO POINT FOR IS REFUSED, BY NAME.** A clerk cannot
buy a connection that does not exist, and a code silently dropped would open an
account the clerk believes has gas: no gas agreement, no gas route, no gas plan,
no gas meter, and every check green (hard-won law 2's mirror). An EMPTY list is
refused for `final_reads`' reason — a screen that sent the key sent it because a
clerk ticked boxes, and `[]` is a payload that lost them on the way.

⚠ **THE DEFAULT IS EVERY POINT, AND THAT IS WHAT MAKES THIS SAFE.** `services`
absent means *all of them*, so `POST /accounts` (S18), the S68 bulk import,
`unit_correction` and `activate_account` are byte-for-byte what they were. Only
the move-in door names a subset, and only when a clerk unticked something.

⚠ **MOVE-OUT AND CLOSE-OUT NEEDED NO CHANGE AND THAT WAS VERIFIED, NOT ASSUMED.**
`_end_service_agreements` filters `account=account`, so it closes the agreements
the account HOLDS and has never closed by unit or by service point. An account
that took three closes three. The assertion that can tell a correct close from a
wrong one needs a second account holding agreements at the same moment, and the
test carries one.

⛔ **BILLING NARROWING ITSELF TO THE AGREEMENTS IS A SEPARATE PIECE OF WORK AND
IS NOT THIS RULE.** Until it lands, this rule governs what the account HOLDS and
what every screen reading the agreements shows; what a bill run charges for is
owed by the money lane.

✅ **IT LANDED THE NEXT DAY — `BR-BIL-1048`, 2026-09-26, and the paragraph above
is history rather than a live limit.** The gap it describes was real and it was
`DEF-BIL-6823`: measured on a seeded six-service tenant on real PostgreSQL 16,
an account left holding **three** of its unit's **six** agreements was billed
**`$475.68` for all six**, `$189.60` of it water, sewer and trash it never
bought. It now bills **`$282.29`** and those three services appear **nowhere on
the document** — not as a line, not as a `$0.00`. The read is
`customers.selectors.billed_service_codes_for`, published here and asked by
`apps/billing/gather`; it is **dated**, because `BR-CUS-030` closes every
agreement at move-out and the FINAL bill is prepared afterwards.

⚠ **ONE CONSEQUENCE OF THIS RULE THAT ONLY APPEARED WHEN BILLING STARTED
READING IT — `OQ-BIL-12739`, OPEN.** A clerk may untick water and keep sewer,
and sewer costs 90% of the water the customer used (`BR-RAT-1030`). That
account now produces **no bill at all**: the engine refuses by name and the
account waits on the run's exception list. Nothing is guessed and nothing is
built; the question is whether the move-in screen should refuse the
combination at the door.

| BR | Statement | Sources | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-1101 | **An account takes a SUBSET of its unit's service points, chosen at move-in and defaulting to all of them.** One `service_agreements` row per service taken and none for the rest; no column is added, so an absent agreement cannot be told apart from a deliberate untick. A service the unit has no point for is refused by name; an empty list is refused. Move-out closes only what the account holds. ⚠ **BILLING NOW NARROWS TO THESE ROWS — `BR-BIL-1048`, 2026-09-26** (`DEF-BIL-6823`: three of six held, six billed, `$475.68` → `$282.29` on a measured account). One consequence is open: sewer without water has no basis to take 90% of and the bill refuses by name (`OQ-BIL-12739`). | PO ruling 2026-09-25 (six offered · six connected · five metered · three taken) · `BR-CUS-029` · `BR-CUS-015` · `BR-CUS-030` · `BR-CUS-672` · `12-governance/02-decision-log.md` 2026-09-25 · hard rule 4 · hard-won law 2 | **CONFIRMED 2026-09-25** | ✅ **WIRED AND TESTED 2026-09-25 (lane MOVEIN).** `06-backend/tests/test_br_cus_1101_service_subset.py` asserts BOTH sides of the subset — three agreements exist AND the other three were not created — plus the unchanged default, the named refusal, the empty-list refusal, and a move-out that leaves a neighbouring account's six untouched. Proved in a browser by `10-scripts/br_cus_1101_move_in_services_walkthrough.mjs`. |

## BR-CUS-1102 — New Connect asks a customer the same questions move-in asks, and finds them by CUSTOMER ID (2026-09-28)

**PO rulings of 2026-09-28, on `CR-150-BRC09`.** Two doors create the same customer record and they
had drifted apart: the move-in wizard takes a full identity and New Connect took a sub-category, an
email, a phone, a prefix and an external reference. **Each door's own test was green over the
difference**, because each asserts the fields its own screen renders — a missing field is an
ABSENCE, and an absence is invisible to every check this repo has.

⚠ **THE RULE IS *REUSE THE COMPONENT*, NOT *ADD THESE FIELDS*.** Adding the missing fields one at a
time leaves two hand-maintained captures that will drift again on the next change, which is the
`SignupWizard.tsx` failure in another module — and the second copy always drifts.

| BR | Statement | Sources | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-CUS-1102 | **A NEW CONNECT COLLECTS THE SAME CUSTOMER IDENTITY A MOVE-IN COLLECTS, THROUGH THE SAME SHARED COMPONENTS, AND LOOKS AN EXISTING CUSTOMER UP BY CUSTOMER ID.** Full name, **customer category with its sub-category under it**, gov-ID, **date of birth**, co-applicant and a **mailing address**, built from the shared `CustomerFields` / `applicantCapture` components rather than field by field. **The lookup is a CUSTOMER ID search run on the details already entered** — PO verbatim: *"this should be search Customer ID - and based on what information is inputed above it will search whether customer ID exist or not"* — answering either the customer's number, name and accounts held, or *"No customer ID found. A new customer is created."* ⚠ **THE *"Becomes a customer"* CHIP IS REMOVED**, and a removed control is recorded as removed so no lane restores it as a missing feature. **The mailing address uses the product's existing `UsAddressFields` block** — PO: *"this should be in line with USA addess standards"* — street, apt/suite/other, city, state, ZIP; **nothing required**, ZIP checked for five digits or ZIP+4. ⚠ **A MAILING ADDRESS IS THE ONE LAWFUL SECOND ADDRESS** (`CLAUDE.md` hard rule 8: `customers.addresses` `kind='mailing'` is a named exception, because where the BILL goes is a different fact from where the service is); **nothing here gives any other entity an address column.** **The utility's own account fields merge into the Customer ID block and the CLERK fills them** — a list field offers the utility's own list, a text field is typed, nothing appears if the utility set none up, and none is required unless the utility said so; when the search finds an existing customer, anything already held arrives filled in and can be changed. **The external system reference stays FREE TEXT** (PO: *"it should free text"*) and **Mode becomes a dropdown** (*"and this one also"*). **Communication preferences — language, paperless bill, SMS reminders — are collected here, beside the email and phone**; autopay and tax exemption go on the Charges step with the money (`BR-CUS-1103`). | PO rulings 2026-09-28 (`12-governance/02-decision-log.md`, *THE NEW CONNECT REDESIGN — THE PRODUCT OWNER'S EIGHTEEN ANSWERS*, answers 4, 5, 6, 7, 9 and 17) · `CR-150-BRC09` · `DEF-CUS-7601` · `CLAUDE.md` hard rule 8 · `BR-CUS-017` · `BR-MET-112` (nothing is written until the last button — unchanged) | **CONFIRMED 2026-09-28** | ⛔ **OWED.** `07-frontend/src/features/customers/NewConnectWizard.test.tsx` must assert the SAME shared component renders on both doors — a test beside the code that goes red the day one door grows a field the other does not. Nothing asserts it today. |
| BR-CUS-1103 | **THE CHARGES STEP KEEPS ALL THREE PAYER OPTIONS ON SCREEN, DISABLES ONE ONLY BY A REAL CHECK, AND TAKES A PAYMENT.** PO verbatim, reversing a proposal to remove the two that cannot be chosen: *"this is wring - keep previous options"*; and on which is offered today, *"owner account only"*. **All three — the owner's account, each unit's bill, the connection — stay on screen; only the owner's account can be chosen today; and a disabled option is disabled by a check against what EXISTS, never by a value typed into the code.** ⚠ **THAT SECOND HALF IS THE DEFECT THE PO WAS ASKING ABOUT** (`DEF-CUS-7610`): `NewConnectChargesStep.tsx` passes `available={false}` as a hardcoded literal at both sites, under two sentences stating facts nobody computed — *"Those accounts do not exist."* and *"Nobody can settle this debt."* It is hard-won law 2's mirror, a live screen behind a dead control, and **`G13` cannot see it**: G13 asks whether a prop is PASSED, and a literal `false` is passed. **PAYMENT FIELDS FOLLOW THE PAYMENT TYPE** — PO: *"based on payment type we nedd to give all paymnet fields"* — payment option (*No payment now* · Cash · Cheque · Card · Bank transfer), amount, payment date, and a reference whose LABEL follows the option. ⚠ **PAYMENT TERMS KEEP NO DEFAULT — `BR-BIL-024`, PO 2026-08-23, UNCHANGED, and restated here because four new fields now surround it:** a guessed due date starts a late-fee clock on a real invoice. **Autopay and tax exemption (certificate number, expiry) are collected here**, with the money rather than with the contact preferences. | PO rulings 2026-09-28 (decision log, answers 16, 17 and 18) · `CR-150-BRC09` · `DEF-CUS-7610` · `DEF-CUS-7611` · `BR-BIL-024` (unchanged) · `BR-MET-1182` (the books entry) · `CLAUDE.md` hard-won law 2 | **CONFIRMED 2026-09-28** | ⛔ **OWED.** The hold that disables an option must carry the test that fires the day it expires — assert the option becomes selectable the moment the accounts it names exist. `CLAUDE.md` law 2 requires it in the same commit as the hold, and no such test exists today. |

The file is filed right after creation and both columns then show the real uploader and time **on the record**. ⚠ **THE DATA BEHAVIOUR IS RATIFIED UNCHANGED AND ONLY THE PRESENTATION MOVES** — which is why this is written down: the previous behaviour was DELIBERATE and defended in the code, so a lane reading only the defect row would "fix" the wrong half. ⚠ **`draftDocumentRows()`'s own comment saying New Connect asks no document number is STALE and goes with this change** — hard-won law 1: a comment is a note, never a guarantee, and nothing re-reads it. | PO rulings 2026-09-28 (decision log, answer 8) and 2026-09-22 (the two verification words) · `CR-150-BRC09` · `DEF-CUS-7603` · `CLAUDE.md` hard-won law 1 | **CONFIRMED 2026-09-28** | ⛔ **OWED.** The documents table renders exactly the columns that can carry a value in this state — asserted in the test rather than claimed in a comment. |

## BR-CUS-1105 — **THE ACCOUNTS REGISTER SHOWS FOUR STATUS TILES, AND EACH ONE IS ALSO THE FILTER** (2026-09-28)

**PO ruling, 2026-09-28, given as a table.** The Accounts list (`S17`, the
Accounts tab) draws **exactly four** tiles, one word each, each with a short
line under it:

| Label | Counts | Line under it |
|---|---|---|
| **Active** | accounts on status `active` | billing normally |
| **Pending** | `pending_activation` | no bill can be raised |
| **Disconnecting** | `marked_disconnect` | still consuming |
| **Suspended** | `vacation_hold` | base charges only |

**AND EACH TILE SETS THE LIST'S FILTER.** PO, verbatim: *"each tile SETS the
list's filter when pressed, through the same state the FilterBar reads, so the
filter bar shows it as applied afterwards"*. Pressing the applied tile again
clears the filter, and either way the list returns to **page 1**.

⚠ **`Disconnecting`, NOT `Disconnects` — RULED, AND THE REASON IS ON THE SAME
SCREEN.** `Disconnects` is already a TAB here and it lists the disconnect
REQUESTS, not the accounts. Two controls one word apart, counting different
things, is the drift this naming exists to avoid.

⚠ **WHAT IS DROPPED, AND ONE OF THE FOUR WORKED.** `Accounts 79 · live and
closed`, `Outstanding`, `Past-due 60+` and `Closed this year` all go. The last
two never counted anything and are `DEF-CUS-7700`. **`Outstanding` was a real,
served figure**, and the ruling does not include it — so this screen now states
**no tenant-wide receivable anywhere**. The BALANCE column still answers for one
account; the utility-wide figure is Finance's Revenue & AR, which is the surface
`DEF-127` always tied `rollup.outstanding` out against. The server still serves
the field: what changed is the renderer.

⚠ **THE HEADLINE NUMBER FALLS AND THAT IS THE RULING.** The old tile counted
live AND closed accounts together; `Active` counts only the accounts billing
normally. On the demo utility that is **79 → 71**.

**HOW THE COUNTS ARE TAKEN, AND WHY IT MATTERS TO `BR-CUS-055`.** One grouped
query, server-side, tenant-scoped, keyed on the status **CODE** —
`selectors.account_status_counts`, a LEFT JOIN **from** `account_statuses` so
that a status holding nobody still comes back at `0`. Never one query per tile,
and never counted in the browser.

`BR-CUS-055` makes the status list **tenant-extensible**, and this rule states
what the four tiles do about it:

* **A RENAMED STATUS IS STILL COUNTED.** The tile keys on the code, so a utility
  that calls `vacation_hold` *Snowbird hold* still sees its accounts under
  **Suspended** — and the tile keeps the PO's ruled word, because the tenant's
  own name would break the one-word ruling the first time somebody wrote a
  sentence into a lookup row.
* **A STATUS THE TENANT DOES NOT HAVE DRAWS A DASH AND CANNOT BE PRESSED.** The
  strip stays four tiles wide. `0` would say *nobody is suspended* about a
  utility that has no such status, and a filter on a status no account can hold
  is a control that returns an empty list for ever.
* **A STATUS THE TENANT ADDED IS COUNTED AND SERVED AND DRAWS NO TILE.** The
  read is the tenant's whole status census, not the tiles' private index, so the
  next screen that wants a fifth figure reads it rather than adding a query.

| Rule | Source | Status | Built |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BR-CUS-1105` — four status tiles on `S17`, each of which sets `f.status`; renamed statuses still counted, absent statuses drawn as a dash and not pressable; `Outstanding` and the two uncounted tiles withdrawn | PO ruling 2026-09-28 (the four-row table, verbatim) · `BR-CUS-055` · `BR-CUS-053` · `NFR-3` · `B14` rules 31 · 33 · 43 · `CLAUDE.md` hard rule 1 · hard-won law 2 and its mirror · `12-governance/02-decision-log.md` 2026-09-28 | **CONFIRMED 2026-09-28** | ✅ **BUILT AND TESTED 2026-09-28 (`2.392.0`, lane CUS-TILES).** `06-backend/tests/test_def_cus_7700_account_status_tiles.py` — 9 cases, including the empty-status zero, the absent-status gap, the rename, the one-query count and a tenant-isolation case run on real PostgreSQL under `sbh_rls_probe`. `07-frontend/src/features/customers/AccountsStatusTiles.dom.test.tsx` — 10 cases that PRESS a tile and read the chip back out of the filter bar, mounted under the real `/:tenant/accounts` route. |

## BR-CUS-1110 — **THE TENDER IS SPELLED `check`, AND THAT IS THE STORED VALUE AND NOT A LABEL** (2026-09-29)

**PO ruling, verbatim, 2026-09-29**, given with the approved four-step drawings
of `REQ-03`: *"use american english check = not cheque"*.

**What it moved, and why it is a rule rather than copy.** The word was not only
printed: `service_requests.fee_method` STORED `cheque`, and it was the last
British spelling of a tender anywhere in the product. `payments` has said
`check` since `BR-PAY-026`, `tenancy.PaymentMethod` seeds `check`, and
`payments.counter_receipts.FEE_METHOD_TO_TENDER` existed for no purpose other
than translating one vocabulary into the other at the boundary. A stored value
that two modules spell two ways is a join waiting to be written wrong.

**The three parts, all of them enforced:**

1. `ServiceRequest.FEE_METHODS` is `cash · card · check`. `cheque` is not in it.
2. `cheque` is still ACCEPTED on the wire, normalised at the write door
   (`service_request_service.normalised_fee_method`) and **never written
   again**. Refusing the old word would break a caller for a spelling;
   retiring a value without grepping its consumers is `DEF-CUS-3653`.
3. Rows already written are corrected by `customers/0075`. The reverse of that
   migration is a deliberate no-op: un-correcting is not a rollback.

The tender-detail object a counter sends with a check is keyed `check` —
`{"number", "bank", "date"}`, drawn on step 3 as `Check number · Check bank ·
Check date` — and `cheque` is accepted there too. The keyword going the other
way into payments stays `cheque=`, because that is **payments'** published
contract and this module does not rename a neighbour's parameter.

⚠ **ONE PLACE STILL SPELLS IT THE OLD WAY AND IT IS NOT THIS MODULE'S.**
`tenancy.published_content.CENTRE_PAYMENT_METHODS` is `cash · cheque · card ·
money_order` and its validator refuses `check` BY NAME, so a utility cannot say
in American English that a service centre takes checks, and four seeded centres
publish the British word to the customer portal. Measured 2026-09-29 and filed
as `DEF-TEN-7861`, owned by tenancy.

| Rule | Source | Status | Built |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BR-CUS-1110` — the application-fee tender is stored, served and accepted as `check`; `cheque` is a legacy INPUT that is normalised and never written; existing rows are migrated | PO ruling 2026-09-29 (verbatim above) · `BR-CUS-090` · `BR-PAY-026` · `12-governance/02-decision-log.md` 2026-09-29 · CLAUDE.md hard rule 4 (`service_requests` is not one of the four immutable tables) | **CONFIRMED 2026-09-29** | ✅ **BUILT AND TESTED 2026-09-29** (lane REQ03-SERVER, `2.395.0`). `customers/0075_req03_american_english_check.py`; `06-backend/tests/test_req03_four_steps_server.py` sends both spellings at `POST .../fee-collection` and reads `check` back off the stored row, and asserts `cheque` is absent from `FEE_METHODS`. Shown RED first by putting the old spelling back. |

## A portal request carried to an ACTIVE customer — `CR-159-BRC10` (PO 2026-10-01)

PO, verbatim: *"complete the move in process or new conenct request coming from portal -
extend it until entire process is completed and customer is activated; you will ahve to
create regular or master customer; you will ahve to create or link customer ID - those
cases also should be tested"*. The rules below are what that took; the judgments behind
them are in `12-governance/02-decision-log.md`, 2026-10-01, *"A PORTAL REQUEST CARRIED TO
AN ACTIVE CUSTOMER — THE FOUR JUDGMENTS"*.

| Rule | Source | Status | Built |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BR-CUS-1111` — **what the clerk decided on the request reaches the account that fulfils it.** The customer linked on the Decision tab (`link_customer`) is the account's customer, and the class picked at capture step 2 is its class, wherever the move-in or master payload names neither. A payload that names one wins — the request is a default, never a lock. *Searched, no prior customer* adds nothing. Where the master form sends no customer at all, the APPLICANT is the party (name, kind, and a person's phone). Worked: Harbor Lane Property Mgmt (a company, `C-…`) linked on a request a manager sent as a person, Jo Marsh → the move-in opens on Harbor Lane's number and the customer count does not move. | PO 2026-10-01 · `BR-CUS-1024` · `BR-CUS-094` · `DEF-CUS-9035` | **CONFIRMED** (implements the ruling) | `services._carry_request_decisions`; `MoveInWizard` class prefill; `tests/test_portal_request_to_active.py` |
| `BR-CUS-1112` — **an approved request may be fulfilled by the OWNER's master account.** An owner or manager taking a whole building on file is approved as a move-in and fulfilled by `POST /accounts/master` with `service_request_id`: the account opens ACTIVE, the pending move-in job closes with `closed_reason = master_account_opened`, no move record is written (no unit was occupied), and `service_request.fulfilled_by_master_account` is audited. A build's owner account (New Connect) passes its build's request the same way. | PO 2026-10-01 · `BR-CUS-068` · `BR-CUS-1077` · `DEF-CUS-9036` | **CONFIRMED** | `services.create_master_account` / `_fulfil_request_with_master`; *Owner's master account* link on the request page → property's Common areas with `?request=` |
| `BR-CUS-1113` — **a portal application's ticked services are the request's service rows.** One `service_request_services` row per service the utility sells, dated the applicant's requested start; a code the utility no longer sells writes no row and is not refused (it stays in the applicant's words). | PO 2026-10-01 · `BR-CUS-1070` · `DEF-CUS-9034` | **CONFIRMED** | `service_request_service.create_from_portal(service_codes=…)` |

## A master account only on a multi-unit building — PO ruling 2026-10-02

PO, verbatim: *"we have to raise master account on multi pairtment building where we can
have multiple units. single family hime, single unit, we should not do master account - how
to handle this"*. **RULED: a master account only where the premises has two or more units.**
How we handle it is ours under law 13 and is in `12-governance/02-decision-log.md`,
2026-10-02, top row, (a)–(e). It NARROWS `BR-CUS-1010` (*"every address gets its owner a
Master Account"*, 2026-08-31) and `BR-MET-116` (*"a house or a duplex does get one"*,
2026-09-21) to addresses of two or more units; both rows are stamped the same day.

| Rule | Source | Status | Built |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BR-CUS-1114` — ⛔ **AMENDED 2026-10-02 BY `CR-162-BRC10` (PO, Q1, the same day): THE PREMISES TYPE DECIDES, NOT THE COUNT.** PO verbatim: *"Q1–Q8 as recommended, go"* on *"apartment complex, commercial building, mixed-use and any type able to hold several units get a master account (and the owner customer) even with one unit entered … a single-family home gets none"*, reframed the same day: *"when we say premises type has account type - master or regular attached … then when you create account dont you want to use this field when you are creating accounts"*, and *"we will need this on screen in setting so user can provide the it and set it"*. **So every premises type in the utility's own list carries an ACCOUNT TYPE — Master account or Regular account (`premises_types.account_kind`, `metering/0066`) — shown as the *Account type* column on Settings and asked, REQUIRED with no default, when a type is added (*"Choose the account type."*).** Seeded: apartment complex, commercial building, mixed-use, mobile-home park, government building → Master; single-family → Regular. Every master door reads it through the one guard below: a Regular type refuses a NEW master with *"Single-family homes have no master account. Open a regular account at Move-in."* (`customers.account.master_not_for_single_family`); a utility's own type nobody has answered yet falls back to the count rule as first built (the sentence below), so such an address behaves exactly as on 2.442.0. Changing the answer moves only what is created afterwards; no master account is ever created or removed retroactively. **New Connection:** the owner's master opens, takes the build's one-time charges inside the commit, and is then HELD Pending (`customers.services.hold_new_master_pending`) until the build's first activation makes it Active. Worked: *Haustin Towers*, apartment complex, one unit entered → master account Pending, Active on approval; *7 Wren Lane*, single-family, two units → refused, no master. *The rule as first built (2.442.0), now the fallback only:* **a master account is raised only on a premises with two or more live HOMES (units that are not common areas).** Every master door refuses a NEW one below that, before writing anything, with *"Single unit. Open a regular account."* (`customers.account.master_needs_two_units`, B14 rule 33): the property's master form (`POST /accounts/master`), the portal approval's *Owner's master account* (the same form with `service_request_id`), New Connect's owner step (`metering.connections.create_owner_account`) and the premises-owner / import door (`metering.premises_owners`), all through `customers.create_master_account`. The count is `customers.services.dwelling_units_at_premises` — metering's live units minus its live common areas (`BR-MET-101`'s flag), a judgment recorded in the decision log 2026-10-02 (built row, call (a)): a house with a laundry flagged common is still one home. A premises of 2+ homes is unchanged (a duplex qualifies); a building that later gains its second unit may then have one. `BR-CUS-1011` runs first, so an address that already HAS a master still answers with it. **Existing masters on single-unit premises are counted and reported, never changed.** Worked: *14 Alder Run*, one home → refused, 0 master rows; *18 Alder Run*, one home + a Laundry common area → refused; *400 Heron Court*, three homes → opens ACTIVE. | PO 2026-10-02 (verbatim above) · decision log 2026-10-02 (a)(d)(e) · `DEF-CUS-9113` | **CONFIRMED** | `customers.services._refuse_master_on_single_unit_premises` · `MASTER_ACCOUNT_MIN_UNITS = 2` · `CommonAreasTab` (button disabled with the reason) · `ServiceRequestJobLink` (door closed, served `resolved_premises_units`) · `tests/test_br_cus_1114_master_only_multi_unit.py` |
| `BR-CUS-1115` — **on a single-unit premises the applicant gets a REGULAR account on its one unit, which holds the services and goes ACTIVE; a single-unit new build raises no owner's master account.** New Connect sends `create_owner_account` only where the address will hold two or more units (`newConnectDraft.buildOpensOwnerMaster`; units added to an address on file join the ones there) and its review step says *"Single unit. Open a regular account."* where an owner is named on one unit. The applicant's account opens through Move-in on the unit, as every regular account does. Worked: *81 Juniper Bend*, battery, Kestrel Energy Co-op — one regular ACTIVE account holding `battery`, no master (the 21-walk's W11 had opened an owner master holding nothing). | PO 2026-10-02 · decision log 2026-10-02 (b)(c) · `DEF-CUS-9113` | **CONFIRMED** | `newConnectDraft.buildOpensOwnerMaster` · `NewConnectReviewStep` · `tests/test_br_cus_1114_master_only_multi_unit.py::test_a_single_unit_new_build_ends_with_one_regular_active_account_and_no_master` |
| `BR-CUS-1116` — 🔒 **an empty home's BASE charge on a building with an owner's master account is billed to that master account, by the day, until a tenant moves in.** PO 2026-10-02, option A (decision log *"AN EMPTY UNIT'S BASE CHARGES ON A MULTI-UNIT BUILDING ARE BILLED TO THE OWNER'S MASTER ACCOUNT UNTIL A TENANT MOVES IN"*): *"in our case master account - this is correct"*. Base only, never usage. Only where a master account exists (two or more homes, `BR-CUS-1114`); an empty single home bills nobody. The owner pays for the days nobody occupied the home in the period — `vacant days / days in period`, unrounded until the line (B4.3, hard rule 2) — so a move-in stops it from the move-in day and a move-out restarts it the day after. Each home is its own line, naming it: *"Unit 4 · water base charge"*. A master with no common area is billed for its empty homes (it was never in the run before). New Connection's unit choice is two options: *Vacant · service off* and *Vacant · base charges only*; *Landlord-billed between tenants* merged into the second and *Open an account now* removed (a parked build holding either reads as its successor). **Worked:** Oak Court, 4 homes, Unit 4 empty, water base $8.00 → the owner's bill carries $8.00 a month ($16.00 over two months); a move-in on 16 September (30 days) → owner 15/30 × $8.00 = **$4.00**, tenant **$4.00**. It amends `BR-CUS-170` (the owner's bill carries the empty units) and the clause of `BR-CUS-1094` that decided a unit's payer once, at billing start — the payer is now decided per day. | PO 2026-10-02 (verbatim above) · `DEF-CUS-9050` · `DEF-CUS-9082` | **CONFIRMED** | `customers.selectors.vacant_days_in_period` / `base_charge_only_coverage(period_start=, period_end=)` · `_unit_less_masters_covering` · `billing.gather` (`_vacancy_factor`, `borrowed_anchor`) · `rating.pricing.stages.price_usage._base_lines` (`occupancy_factor`, `unit_label`) · `billing.resolution_switch.apply` · `tests/test_def_cus_9050_empty_unit_base_to_master.py` |

## The portal applicant's address in parts, and their own category — `DEF-CWP-9817`, `DEF-CWP-9818` (2026-10-06)

`BR-CUS-1091` (the address in PARTS, PO 2026-09-22) was built on the staff capture only; the
public *Start service* door still took one box. **Amended 2026-10-06:** it now binds the portal
too — see `BR-CUS-1117`. The category rule is the PO's ruling of the same day, verbatim:
*"How should a portal applicant give their customer category? Show the full Category and
Sub-category dropdowns from Settings on step 3, and let the applicant pick from all seven
categories and their sub-categories. The clerk can still change it."* Judgments are in
`12-governance/02-decision-log.md`, 2026-10-06, *"THE PORTAL ASKS THE ADDRESS IN PARTS AND THE
APPLICANT'S CATEGORY"*.

| Rule | Source | Status | Built |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BR-CUS-1117` — **a portal application takes the service address in PARTS — Street address, Town, ZIP code, and the unit — and is matched on all three through the staff side's own matcher.** The parts land in the request's `_as_given` columns (`address_as_given`, `address_city_as_given`, `address_postal_code_as_given`, `address_unit_as_given`) as what was SAID; no state is asked (the blueprint draws none) and none is invented on the request. The match is `metering.address_match.premises_matching_address` — the one the Approve window uses — and only ONE exact row is a match; a near match or two rows is *a person will look at it*. **Every step after step 1 shows ONE line in US form** — `1841 Quarry Hill Ct, Apt 12, Silver Creek, TX 78611` — the matched PREMISES' address when there is one (`BR-CUS-1091`'s second half), the typed parts with the utility's own state otherwise; a bare unit number is written `#12`. Hard rule 8 is untouched: a unit is a label and the parts are a statement. | Blueprint p31 (*15 Start service · 1 Address*) · `BR-CUS-1091` (PO 2026-09-22) · PO report 2026-10-06 (*"Haustin Towers 2"*) | **BUILT 2.555.0** | `tests/test_def_cwp_9817_9818_portal_address_and_category.py` · `StartService.test.tsx` · `CheckAndSend.dom.test.tsx` |
| `BR-CUS-1118` — **a portal applicant picks their own customer Category (REQUIRED) and Sub-category (optional) on step 3, from the same offered list the clerk uses, and the clerk can still change it.** Offered = Settings › Account types' rows that are active and not stopped (`legacy`, `DEF-CUS-9808`), with their active sub-categories, in the clerk's order; codes and names travel to the guest, never ids (B14 rule 31). The pick is judged on every save (a stopped category or a sub-category of another category is refused by name) and at Send is written to the request's `account_type` / `account_subtype` by the customers module's own portal door — not the clerk's `set_customer_category` — with a `service_request.category_set` audit row. A pick that stopped being offered between step 3 and Send writes nothing and does not refuse the application (it arrives *Not yet classified*, as before). On approval it reaches the account exactly as a clerk's pick does (`BR-CUS-1111`). | **PO ruling 2026-10-06 (b)**, verbatim above · `BR-CUS-094` (offered vs legacy) · `BR-CUS-1111` | **CONFIRMED — PO RULING 2026-10-06. BUILT 2.555.0** | `tests/test_def_cwp_9817_9818_portal_address_and_category.py` · `tests/test_portal_request_to_active.py` (the clerk's change still wins) · `CheckAndSend.dom.test.tsx` |
| `BR-CUS-1119` — **a returning customer at move-in is recognised, shown what they still owe, and may have their OWN closed account on the SAME unit reopened; the clerk chooses whether the old balance is paid first or carried.** (1) *Search customer ID* adds two levels BELOW New Connection's Q5 (whose *found* / *possible* stand, and whose own answer is unchanged): **same name and phone**, and **lived here last** — the move-in's unit's most recently closed account's customer, when the name typed is theirs; each with what their accounts owe from the books. (2) The account on THIS unit may be **reopened** — same number, history kept — when it is the linked customer's, closed (not a cancelled move-in), closed within **`reopen_within_years`** (S66 *Billing defaults*, default **5**, 0 = never) and the unit is empty; another unit always gets a new account and BR-CUS-017 is unchanged. Reopening changes only the account row (status, `opened_at` = the move-in date, `closed_at` cleared, the move-in's preferences) and adds new service agreements; no issued bill, payment or journal line moves (hard rule 4). (3) When it still owes money (the books' AR net of credit held), the clerk chooses **Must pay first** — Complete is refused (`customers.moveIn.reopen.payFirst`) until a payment through Payments' own form brings it to $0.00 — or **Carry it** — the balance stays on the account for the next bill; the choice and the figure are recorded on the move-in (`move_in_reopenings`) and audited. **Worked:** Viktor Valdez, C-00022, AC-2026-00022 on SCW-U-022, closed owing $84.20 → reopened, *carry, $84.20*; or refused until $84.20 is paid, then *pay first, $0.00*. | PO 2026-10-13, verbatim: *"Q1 C both; Q2 - Reopen, for the same unit only ; Q3 - give opton to use whether pay it now or carry itr - Must pay first · Carry it onto the reopened account · Clerk chooses"* and *"Q4 - keep 5 years right now"* (decision log *"RETURNING CUSTOMER AT MOVE-IN — FOUR PO RULINGS"*; built per *"RETURNING CUSTOMER — HOW THE FOUR RULINGS WERE BUILT"*). ⚠ SUPERSEDES IN PART the 2026-10-05 ruling (1) *"if match based on our logic then we say match if it is not then we dont say it"* for the move-in's search only. | CONFIRMED 2026-10-13 (`CR-181-BRC10`) | Built (lane REOPEN): `customers/owner_lookup.py` · `customers/move_in_reopen.py` · `customers/move_in_reopen_models.py` · `customers/0083` · `tenancy/settings_families.py` · the move-in wizard's `StepCustomer` / `ReopenChoice` / `StepUnit` / `StepReview` · `tests/test_cr181_returning_customer_reopen.py` |
| `BR-CUS-1120` — **a TEMPORARY STOP pauses an account's service between two dates and keeps the account open: while it lasts the account is on `vacation_hold` and the bill carries the base (standing) charge only, by the day; the clerk may tick *Field visit needed*; the customer may end it early.** (1) A portal *stop my service* ask is stored with its account, dates and reason and is typed *Temporary stop* on arrival; approving it is its own act — New connection and Move-in are never offered for it. (2) On the stop date the account becomes `vacation_hold`, on the restart date `active` again, by the daily clock; idempotent; audited; no service agreement ends. (3) B2: with *Field visit needed* a shut-off work order is raised for the stop date and a turn-on for the restart date. (4) B4: *Restart my service now* in the portal sends a request; when a clerk approves it the account is active, and billing normal, from that day. (5) A clerk may start, move or end a stop from the account page. (6) A period partly on hold bills the base for every day and the meter's usage; a period wholly on hold bills the base only. | **CONFIRMED** — PO rulings B1, B2, B4, 2026-10-14 (decision log *"TEMPORARY STOP — FOUR PO RULINGS (B1–B4)"*, verbatim there); judgments of ours in *"TEMPORARY STOP — HOW THE FOUR RULINGS WERE BUILT"*. | `CR-183-BRC10`: `customers/temporary_stop.py` · `temporary_stop_models.py` · `temporary_stop_api.py` · `temporary_stop_schedule.py` · `customers/0084` · `billing/gather.py` (`_behavior_class_for_period`) · `work/from_flows.py` (`raise_temporary_stop_visits`) · `portal/services.py` · `TemporaryStopApproveModal` · `TemporaryStopAccount` · portal `TemporaryStopCard` · `tests/test_cr183_temporary_stop*.py` |
| `BR-CUS-1121` — **a temporary stop lasts at least `temporary_stop_min_days` (default 10) and at most `temporary_stop_max_months` (default 12) calendar months, both S66 *Billing defaults* settings; outside them approval is refused by name.** Days = restart date − stop date. Worked: SR-26-0017, 13 Oct → 16 Oct, is **3 days** and is refused (*At least 10 days.*); 10 days is accepted; a restart more than 12 months after the stop is refused (*At most 12 months.*). | **CONFIRMED** — PO ruling B3, 2026-10-14, verbatim: *"No minimum, 12 months maximum, set in Settings. - this is correct - also set min limit to 10 days for time being"*. | `CR-183-BRC10`: `customers/temporary_stop.py` (`date_problems`, `limits`) · `tenancy/settings_families.py` · `settingsRulePages.ts` |
